# BroTown UI design notes: why each screen is the way it is

BroTown is a multiplayer action RPG played in the browser, mostly on an
iPhone held upright; it also plays on a phone held sideways and on a computer.
Its owner has reworked most of its screens many times, trying each change on
a phone. These notes are the reasons: a section for each part of the game.

Every UI review bundle (`tools/qa/ui-review/capture.mjs`) carries this file as
`DESIGN-NOTES.md`, and each screen in the bundle's README names the sections
about it.

## How to read these notes

Each section has:

- **Screens**: the screens of the UI review it is about (their file names
  without the number: `dash-bag` is `NN-dash-bag.jpg`; `dash-*` is every
  screen whose name starts that way, `all` is every screen).
- **What it is for**: the job of that part of the game.
- **Decisions, and why**: what the owner chose and the reason, often in their
  own words (in quotation marks). "v2.3.N" is the version that made the
  change: a higher number is newer.
- **Tried before, and changed**: earlier designs and why they went.
- **Please don't suggest**: changes already tried and turned down, or ruled
  out by a decision above. If you still think one is right, say which note
  you are going against and why: the owner may change their mind, but should
  know they are being asked to.
- **Still open**: what is not decided yet. Suggestions are most welcome here.
- **Sources**: where the game's own code and documents say it.

The pictures show the game as it is today; these notes say how it got there.
If a picture and a note disagree, the picture is the game as built: say so,
it may be a bug.

For the people building the game (first compiled at v2.3.3460, 2026-10-18):
these notes are a digest of the specs in
`docs/specs/`, the UI law (`docs/UI-BIBLE.md`, `docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md`),
`docs/TRAPS.md` and the owner's words as the code and `CLAUDE.md` record them.
The code and the specs win where they differ. A new screen in
`tools/qa/ui-review/states.mjs` gets a section here or its name on a
section's `Screens:` line, and a new decision about a screen gets a line in
its section; `node tools/qa/ui-review/capture.mjs --check-notes` lists the
screens with no notes.

## The short version

What holds on every screen, from the sections below:

- **The world comes first.** The painted world is the picture; the interface
  is a quiet, dark "field kit" around it (Lantern Slate). Every pixel of
  interface covers the world on a phone, so less is more, and gold is kept
  for the one main action and what is selected or special, never a plain
  border.
- **An upright iPhone first.** Everything is designed at 390 x 844 for a
  thumb. Sideways and on a computer are the same game re-laid, never a
  different one, and no screen size sees more of the world than another.
- **The game never stops for a menu.** It is one shared world on a server,
  so nothing pauses: menus open over, under or beside the world while it
  keeps playing.
- **The server decides.** Gold, items, XP and every result come from the
  server, so a button often waits for its answer rather than guessing.
- **Pictures cost memory.** An iPhone tab dies at about 250 MB, so a screen's
  art is loaded when it is needed and let go after; a new big picture has to
  earn its place.
- **The owner's own drawings stay.** Where the owner drew a screen (the
  player card, the quest windows, the stat pills, the touch controls'
  rings...), it is kept as drawn, and not spread to other screens.

## The look: Lantern Slate, icons and type
Screens: all

**What it is for:** One visual system for everything inside the game, so the painted world stays the star and the interface reads as a quiet field kit around it. The owner approved it as "Lantern Slate" (v2.3.1227–v2.3.1240).

**Decisions, and why:**
- Lantern Slate's north star: "the world is the illustration; the interface is the field kit". Dark slate surfaces, warm-white text, about 85% neutral, 10% content colour, 5% brass. The painted world must stay the brightest, most colourful thing on screen.
- The order every UI choice follows (UI Bible): understood in under 0.2 s without reading; then as little screen as possible (iPhone first: every pixel of chrome covers the world); then hierarchy; consistency; beauty last. "Icons, not words": a word may sit beside an icon but is never the main signal. "Apple, not Blizzard."
- Scope: this covers the game once the world is on screen. The login door and the creator are a separate title look on purpose (painted backdrop, gold BRO TOWN logo, gold plates) — owner directive (v2.3.2359). Their gold stays in those two screens.
- Colour always means something. Brass/gold = focus, selection, premium and the one main action — never a default border. HP red, mana blue, stamina amber, XP green, success green, info blue, magic purple. On the touch controls only, sky blue means a cooldown. In the quest windows green is the reward, the gold bar the one thing to press, grey "not yet".
- Rarity is a thin edge on a slot, never a filled tile: Normal grey, Rare blue, Elite orange, Godly a turning rainbow ring and a rainbow name. The owner: "rare is blue, elite is orange, godly is prismatic" (v2.3.3142), matching the pets. Lantern Slate names the godly ring its one always-running animation. A selected item gets a separate inner gold ring, so it still shows its rarity.
- The only light surface is the trade window's item wells: light brown on your side, light grey on theirs, so you can tell whose pile is whose (owner, v2.3.2283 / v2.3.2294). The light ground means whose pile it is, never a lighter step for depth.
- Depth comes from four dark roles (sheet, raised card, well, deep well), soft shadows and hairline edges. The rule is no blur (backdrop-filter): it misbehaves on iPhone Safari over the game's canvas, so things over the world use near-opaque fills instead.
- Many buttons wear the owner's carved, chiselled frames (v2.3.1331–1337, owner: "use them on all the buttons everywhere"): selected = a double gold ridge, the main action = a brass face with dark text, destructive = a red face. The UI Bible's rule for anything tappable is at least 44 pt.
- Type is Source Sans 3. Nothing in the UI is smaller than 11 px (v2.3.2466; owner: "Make all font in the game at least the equivalent of size 8 font."). Text drawn in the world itself (NPC names, loot names, pickup timers) was left out of that floor by the owner's choice. Changing numbers use even-width digits so they don't jitter; capitals only for titles and short headers.
- Icons: a 90-icon set made with ChatGPT from one master prompt replaced the emoji (v2.3.1223–1224) and is kept unchanged. Since then the owner's own art: the player card's social icons (v2.3.2926), the quest windows' painted art (v2.3.3030), "small icons" for party badges, chat lanes, buffs, the PvP skull, the townsfolk's talk bubble and the ability buttons (v2.3.3279), 53 bag icons (seeds, crops, dishes, trap, woods, essences; v2.3.3281), one special-attack picture per weapon (v2.3.3336), and the pictures the owner picked from thirteen more sheets ("Icon picks: 1b, 2a, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9", v2.3.3481): the eight lands' scenes (on the land banner, then since v2.3.3511 small under the minimap: "look bad enlarged"), the quest pins over townsfolk, the arena's helmet, the trade request's handshake, the jackpot's sack, the pet house, the free spin's prize wheel and the empty Journey page's treasure map. Most of those sheets were ChatGPT redrawing icons the game already had, which were left as they are. Emoji remain only as a fallback if a picture fails. The small icons are cut at the size they are drawn (64–160 px), because a bigger file costs phone memory (about 2 MB instead of 7).
- Eight documented exceptions, each drawn or asked for by the owner and allowed only where it is: the dashboard's wells and toolbar bevels; the trade item well; the player card's corner brackets and ◆ dividers and the inspect card ("Change of plans. like this better."); the touch controls' gold rings with pictures ("Make the on screen buttons look more like the improved mockup"); the quest windows' painted frames ("Add these for the new quest windows."); the hero sheet's stat pills ("Add pill design for hero equipment menu stats.").
- Motion is short and finite (90–320 ms). No looping pulses except finite alerts and the godly ring; the phone's reduce-motion setting removes shimmer.
- Sounds, the owner's rule (v2.3.2658): "Use the click sound for navigating through the menus ... Use the close sound for closing the dialog window". One sound for equip and unequip. The click is quieter on purpose (you hear it on every tap), every button gets a sound automatically, and one tap never plays two.
- A rare-or-better drop is revealed by a short spin (2 s a stage; the owner asked for "a short celebration") that is deliberately not a modal: the world keeps playing, so you are not hit while you watch (v2.3.1925).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- A "light & airy" parchment/beige palette was built, previewed and rejected by the owner (v2.3.1227); the old dark-navy theme and the red-leather bag texture went too.
- Per-row frames in the trade window: the owner rejected them as "too many boxes within boxes" (v2.3.2290).
- Elite was purple and godly a gold ring until v2.3.3142.
- The text floor was 8 px, then 10 px (v2.3.1239), now 11 px (v2.3.2466).
- Heavier chip frames went back to the simpler ones (owner: "I don't like the thick black border around the sort buttons", v2.3.1335); the dark lip round button tracks went 6 → 3 → 2 px after it read as "a big black border" (v2.3.1336–1337).

**Please don't suggest:**
- A light theme, or a new light surface anywhere to add depth; the trade wells are the only light ground, and only to say whose pile is whose.
- Blur / frosted glass, or CSS filters to grey out or darken controls. Greyed states are baked grey copies: a filter once made the block button's icon vanish (TRAPS §42).
- Gold as a general border, or a consistency pass that carries the gold rings, brackets, pills or quest frames into other panels; or cleaning those owner-drawn exceptions up into plain panels.
- Diamonds as ornaments or on dividers. The owner has taken them out three times: around the creator's Create button (v2.3.2207: "a dead giveaway of AI produced content"), the creator's plate ("Remove this diamond separator thing.", v2.3.2388) and the Points tab's weapon rows (v2.3.3559: "It just muddles the nice crisp image"). The player card's ◆ dividers stay only because the owner drew them.
- Repainting the icon set, swapping icons for text labels, or bringing emoji back.
- Boxes inside boxes (nested cards); a third level inside the trade well.
- A second rarity palette (the reveal and the bag must agree), or turning the drop reveal into a modal.
- Raising the world's own labels to the 11 px floor (the owner chose to leave them).
- Removing the dialogue backdrop's 2 px blur (the owner kept it, below).

**Decided (2026-10-19):**
- The one blur the game keeps, by the owner's choice: the dark backdrop behind a character's dialogue and the quest windows blurs the world slightly (2 px, `.bt-npcdlg-scrim` in game.css). Asked after the ChatGPT review of 2026-10-19, which suggested removing it, the owner: "Keep blur" (v2.3.3481). It stays the only exception to the no-blur rule.

Sources: docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, docs/UI-BIBLE.md, docs/specs/small-icons.md, docs/specs/bag-icons.md, docs/specs/owner-icons.md, docs/specs/ui-sounds.md, docs/specs/rarity-reveal.md, docs/specs/armor-grade-look.md, docs/TRAPS.md (§42, §48), src/styles/game.css, src/ui/mobile/dash/common.js, CLAUDE.md

## Phone first, then sideways, then computer
Screens: all

**What it is for:** The game is built for an iPhone held upright in Safari. Holding it sideways and playing on a computer are other views of the same game, adapted so that every button can be reached and nobody sees more of the world than anyone else.

**Decisions, and why:**
- iPhone Safari, upright, is the primary platform (the whole UI was laid out against a 390-wide iPhone). Touch is tested first, not a mouse.
- **Since v2.3.4152 every screen has the dashboard along the bottom**, the upright band: a sideways phone, a tablet and a computer as an upright phone (the owner, 2026-11-03: "Move the left side menu to the bottom of screen on computer and all other devices"). It is never taller than today's upright band (the owner: keep today's dashboard height on every layout; on a wider screen at most 243 px and a third of the screen: 130 on a sideways phone, 240 to 243 on a computer), its slots counted from its own box, the world above it with bars at its sides past 2.2:1; a computer keeps its keys and key caps, its controls list folded to the ⌨ until H opens it (docs/specs/one-band.md). This supersedes "No I don't want an overlay over the world" (v2.3.2157), "You can actually remove that whole bottom length bar now" (v2.3.2168) and "base desktop off the landscape mode" (v2.3.3687): the side column those built, described in the sideways and computer bullets below, is the OLD layout, kept for one device with `?oneband=0` or the Test panel's "Side column (old layout)".
- **A phone on its side rests with the band folded** to its button row (v2.3.4152; the owner: the dashboard "will need to adapt to the new redesign we're adding for "gear" and "bro." Go recommended on default behavior going into landscape mode"): a 130 px band left a 260 px world, too short to play under the controls (they sat on the player frame and the minimap). A button opens its page; CLOSE, or closing the page any way, folds it again. Turning sideways, or loading sideways, folds it each time without touching the upright's remembered fold, and a player who opens it keeps it open until they fold it or turn the phone. While the band is open sideways the joysticks and every combat button step aside, hidden and untouchable, and come back when it folds: no control is ever over an open band. Folded, they take the sideways layout inside the world, the attack disc a little smaller (80 px) and the left thumb's cluster 20 px lower, so nothing covers the player frame, the minimap or its plate (docs/specs/one-band.md).
- **On a computer or a tablet the band is open at rest and its content centred**: the nav row, the page and the resting columns are as wide as the world above them and never wider than 1200 px (v2.3.4152; v2.3.4182: 840, below). The Gear and Bro mockups are three panels side by side; across a 1920 px window they would be three thin strips.
- **...and the band stops where the bars begin** (v2.3.4172, the owner on #1386: "Make the edge of the dashboard stop at the black bars width (hopefully that's an easy change and won't mess up anything)"). Where the world has black bars at its sides, the band is exactly the world's width, its edges in line with the bars', and the bars run black to the bottom of the screen; until then its background was the window's width, under the bars too. It takes the two numbers the world's box already uses, so nothing over the world moved (measured by mp-bandshots: the weapon pill, the keys, the chat bar, the player frame and the minimap). Where there are no bars (an upright phone, a tablet whose world fits) the band is the full width as before. A phone on its side keeps its band the screen's width: open, its world is 572 px over an 844 px band on purpose, so its slots keep the room.
- **...and the buttons and the open tab are one assembly** (v2.3.4182, the owner at a computer: "I noticed the gray dashboard still exceeds the black bars that I wanted fixed. Also look at where the dashboard buttons are relative to the content area. They're all shifted to the left. They should be part of the dashboard assembly the same way it appears on mobile"). On a phone the row (CLOSE and the five) and the open tab are one block the screen's width; on a computer or a tablet they are now one centred block of 840 px (or the band's width when that is less; `BAND_ASSEMBLY_MAX_W`): the row spans exactly the tab's width, CLOSE at its left edge and the five buttons sharing the rest, and every tab (Gear, Bro, Quests, Codex, More, the band at rest) has those two edges. 840 because the Bro card's three panels were laid out on a sideways phone's 844 px band, and a 1280 px laptop's 1056 px world holds it with room either side; 1200 left the row floating in the middle of a wider tab. The gray past the bars: measured on this build at six computer sizes, three pixel ratios, folded, every tab and after a resize, nothing of the band is drawn past the world's edges, so it did not reproduce (main before #1386 merged drew the band the window's width, the likeliest picture the owner saw); mp-bandshots now reads the screen's pixels just outside the world. The Gear tab filled the block once its own 560 px cap went (v2.3.4191).
- Everyone sees the same slice of the world, drawn at whatever size fits: a bigger screen zooms in instead of seeing further, a narrow phone zooms out instead of seeing less (v2.3.1768b, v2.3.2021). Fairness is standing policy: seeing a monster or another player before they see you is the game's tactical layer, so a cheaper or smaller phone must not see less. Sideways was built to show about the same area, wider and shorter (v2.3.2156); the owner later chose a wider view of town held sideways ("landscape zoom: town/worldview only; combat zones stay.", v2.3.2497).
- No screen sees more AREA of world than an upright phone (v2.3.4084; a design review the owner shared: "a consistent maximum gameplay visibility range regardless of screen dimensions"; the owner: "Cap it for everyone now"). A sideways phone or a wide computer is zoomed in until it sees about an upright phone's 850 x 1330 game px, never so far its short side drops below 680; the view keeps the screen's shape, and upright phones do not move. A sideways phone went from 2078 x 960 to 1564 x 723, a 2560 x 1080 computer from 1627 x 960 to 1384 x 817 (docs/specs/view-cap.md). Off for one tab with `?viewcap=0`, for every phone from the Test panel ("Same view for every screen").
- (The old layout, `?oneband=0`, from here to the computer bullets.) Sideways is optional (owner: "Landscape would be an optional view. You can play in portrait or landscape."). At rest the world gets the whole screen: no bottom bar at all (owner: "You can actually remove that whole bottom length bar now", v2.3.2168), gold is a small chip at the world's bottom centre, and one ▴ chip opens the menus. The owner weighed the extra tap and chose it (v2.3.2176; the buttons "should've been hidden inside the main dashboard screen when it's minimized").
- An open menu sideways sits in a column BESIDE the world, which shrinks to make room — never over it (owner: "No I don't want an overlay over the world"). The game keeps running: "You should be able to play the game with the menus open. That's the idea." The column is the upright dashboard stood on end (the bag 2 slots wide and 4 tall, the combat skills and the dashboard buttons at its foot): "equivalent dashboard navigation space as the portrait mode" (v2.3.2165–2166).
- The column takes the side away from the iPhone's Dynamic Island ("The iPhone has a punch hole that's awkward since it goes right through the menus", v2.3.2174). The phone's rotation decides; the owner checked both ways on a real iPhone: "The mapping is correct." (v2.3.2177). Settings → "Landscape menu side" (Auto / Left / Right) stays for people who want the menus under one thumb.
- A sideways phone is only 300–430 px tall, so the creator is laid out in three columns there (v2.3.3216), and windows are held to the screen and scroll inside when taller (the rule since v2.3.3407, after Ace's Flip button was found below a sideways screen).
- Adding the game to the iPhone Home Screen is the recommended way to play (no browser bar). A card shows how, drawing the share icon because Safari's button has no label and moves; owner: "there needs to be some kind of instruction on the game itself on how to do this" (v2.3.2159). Settings → "Play full screen — hide the browser bar" brings it back.
- On a computer (a mouse and a window at least 1000 px wide), in the old layout (`?oneband=0`), the game plays the SIDEWAYS layout (owner, 2026-10-28: "Yes base desktop off the landscape mode but fix the menu sizes", v2.3.3687): three columns the window's height — the pages at one edge, 390 px, an upright phone's width, the width they were drawn for; the world in the middle; and the controls at the OTHER edge (owner: "I meant the right side of the screen consists of the black strip with the shortcuts. Not just on the right side of the left black strip."). The world stays fair by the sideways rule (its height), so an ultra-wide monitor gets bars at the sides past 2.2:1, never more world. Every page is laid out for that column ("Every menu needs to look like it fits the space"): the bag five across and ten down, the Equipment tab's figure and gear twice the size with Damage and Defense open under the bars, the Points tab a list (each stat a row with its whole name and what it gives now) under the three weapons and the character, bigger skill tiles and codex cards, More two across with each tile's status line. Down the pages it is a phone's order (owner: "the dashboard buttons need to be at the top of that black bar section, not at the bottom. It's more logical arrangement that way and matches with the way it's set up for mobile ... [the three combat skills] should all be in the top row beneath the graph dashboard button. Then beneath that the filters for the inventory/bag"), then, a cut later, "change the 3 primary combat level row back to the bottom": the buttons, the filters, the bag, the weapons at the foot. The buttons' pictures are 42 px, nearly twice a phone's 24 (owner: "The dashboard button thumbnail icons can be almost twice as large as they are now"), and the chip that folds the pages says CLOSE, then OPEN (owner: "Instead of the down arrow chip I think you should just have a "close" button that says close for hiding the display menus" -- the upright phone's chip since v2.3.2266); folded, the column is a strip with that chip at the top. A phone on its side keeps the arrows: a word does not fit its 44 px chip beside six buttons in 238 px. `?narrow` is the old upright column. Phones never see this rule.
- On a computer the touch controls are hidden and keys and the mouse do everything (WASD, click attack, Space, X, Shift, Q, E, R, F, T, V, Tab and 1–3, C, Esc). Since v2.3.3711 the left hand stays on WASD and the right on the mouse (owner: "Do your recommended keyboard layout. I still think there are better layouts if you consider more creative combos (like keyboard presses while a click is held down, double clicking, etc)"): the right mouse button HELD is the shield (a left click while it is held is the shield bash; with no shield the right click is still the special), Q the weapon's special, F the Element Burst (G still works), the mouse wheel the next or last weapon, a direction key tapped twice a dodge roll that way, and a double click on a monster locks on to it (with the sword, the lunge). The browser's right-click menu never opens over the game (a text box and a link keep theirs). The list shows them as R-Click, +Click, 2×WASD, 2×Click and Wheel (docs/specs/desktop-keys.md "v2.3.3711"). **What changed since** (v2.3.4794, from the code: `deskKeyList.js`, `desktopControls.js`): Space jumps and X rolls (v2.3.3844, the owner: "spacebar I think should be jump"); the right button toggled the shield (v2.3.3847) until **Q became the shield's toggle and the right click the special again** (v2.3.4323, the owner: "I think right click should be special attack instead. Block should be moved to something else"), so the list reads `Q Shield ↕` only with a shield and `R-Click Special` always; and in the game's combat since v2.3.4396 (docs/specs/manual-combat.md) each left click is one attack at the cursor with a sword or a bow (a staff fires while the button is held), the right click ARMS a sword's or a bow's special for the next attack, and the double click locks nothing: `2×Click Lock on` is listed only in the old combat (`?manual=0`). Every key is listed in its own column at the screen's far edge, in four groups (Move, Fight, Other, Menus), the menus' letters too (owner: "find a good place to keep a list of all the controls", v2.3.3687). H or its ⌨ folds it to the ⌨ alone, and the world takes the room (v2.3.1715's rule: a toggle with no way back would be a delete). A key is listed only while it works (owner: "Keyboard instructions need to be dynamic since "whirl" doesn't work if you don't have your sword equipped"): R-Click (Q before v2.3.3711) only with a shield, R only with the sword in hand, T only with a trap, V only with food. Each group's rows are packed with a gap between groups, the words 15 px bold (owner: "don't space out the controls evenly make them in chunks without spaces within chunks but space between chunks ... The labels for the controls needs to be more readable too"). In the upright column (`?narrow`) the old strip in the black margin stays (v2.3.3526). Since v2.3.4198 the list shows only the essentials (WASD, Shift, Space, Click, 1-3, E, M, Esc, H; owner: "I'm wondering if the menu shortcuts are overwhelming and just the essential commands should stay listed"), with a last line "More keys" that opens the full list in place and "Fewer keys" that folds it back, remembered per browser (bt_kb_more). The full list stays reachable because this is the only on-screen record of a computer player's keys; every key works either way.
- Each button with a key wears its letter as a small key cap, on a computer only (owner: "just put the letter next to the icon or action in a tasteful way", v2.3.3407): I bag, P character, J quests, L life skills, K codex, O more, M map, Enter on Next / Accept Quest, Esc on every ✕, B emotes, T trap. A key opens its page and closes it again; Esc closes the window in front, one at a time. The strip lists only keys no button wears.
- A computer can finish a harvest: the round harvest button comes back saying "Drag ↔ or hold E"; the mouse can make the gesture anywhere on the world, or you hold E (v2.3.3407).
- A laptop window (1366 × 768) is checked too: the creator sizes itself from the game's column, not the window, so ENTER BRO TOWN is on the screen (v2.3.3407).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- On a computer: full width ("the layout is super stretched") → 50% → 25% of the width (v2.3.1715, "force shrink the game area another 50% ... including the dashboard") → the phone's shape (v2.3.1768: "the view showed way more of the world all at once") → filling the window's height with black bars (v2.3.1768b) → the sideways layout across the window, fair by height (v2.3.3687) → the upright band across the bottom of the window, as every screen (v2.3.4152). The controls first went under the bag, then in a strip beside the pages (owner: "If you split the keyboard shortcuts on its own strip to the right and kept the inventory on the left it might look better"), then at the screen's right edge, their rows first spread evenly down it, then packed in groups. The dashboard's buttons first stayed in the bottom corner as on a sideways phone, the weapons under the bag; then at the top, the weapons under them; then the weapons back under the bag, the buttons' pictures nearly twice the size and the chip a word, CLOSE / OPEN.
- Sideways first kept the upright band (67% of the screen, about three tiles of world), then a slim strip, then no band (v2.3.2156–2168), then, from v2.3.4152, the upright band again, at most a third of the screen, resting folded to its button row (the open band left a 260 px world under the controls). The old tiny-slot bag pane sideways was retired: "must be a legacy view that needs to retire" (v2.3.2173).
- The menu column always landed on the left (iPhones report the same inset on both sides) until the rotation was used (v2.3.2177).
- An invisible strip down the left edge (the iOS swipe-back guard) ate taps on controls there; it became an invisible listener instead (v2.3.3407).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Menus that slide over the world sideways, or pausing the game while a menu is open.
- Putting the menu buttons on the world at rest. ("Bringing back a bottom bar sideways" was on this list until the owner chose the bottom band for every screen, v2.3.4152.)
- Swapping which side the menus take for a rotation (checked on the owner's phone).
- Letting a bigger screen (a computer, a tablet, a bigger phone) see more of the world than a phone does.
- Letting a computer's wider screen show more of the world than its height gives (the world is fair by height; past 2.2:1 the sides are bars).
- Listing a key that does nothing with what you hold, or moving the controls back under the bag (the owner wanted the inventory left and the controls right).
- Spreading the controls' rows to fill the column, or the dashboard's buttons at the column's foot on a computer.
- Key letters on a phone, or typed into a button's words (they are drawn only for a mouse).

**Still open:**
- Many buttons are smaller than Apple's 44 px guide; none is unreachable.
- The NPC dialogue, the quest offer and the world map are drawn over the whole window on a computer.
- Building windows on a computer are still the phone's 360 px card, low in the world; they fit, but are small on a big window (docs/specs/desktop-wide.md "Not done").
- The pages other than the band's resting row (Character, Quests, Life Skills, Codex, More) lay out as on an upright phone on every screen; on a sideways phone, opened, they scroll inside a short band (docs/specs/one-band.md "Not done"). On a computer the doubled minimap stays the old layout's (under the band the world is a third shorter and the doubled map lay over the skateboard's button). Sideways, Diego's drawer covers the world above the open band (it sells from the bag, so the band must be open); a shorter drawer is the owner's call.

Sources: docs/specs/landscape-mode.md, docs/specs/view-cap.md, docs/specs/desktop-wide.md, docs/specs/one-band.md, docs/specs/desktop-keys.md, docs/specs/desktop-harvest.md, docs/specs/reach-every-button.md, docs/specs/ui-review.md, docs/specs/control-redesign.md, docs/TRAPS.md (§175, §210–§214), src/styles/game.css, src/game/worldViewport.js, src/ui/panels/KeyboardHintsPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/InstallHint.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/SettingsPanel.jsx, CLAUDE.md

## The login door and your Login Key
Screens: login-door, more-account

**What it is for:** The first screen asks who you will play: a bro you already have (Continue) or a new one (Create Character). The Login Key is the only way to take a character to another phone or computer, so the game says so at the door and keeps the key one tap away in More.

**Decisions, and why:**
- The door is its own screen in front of the creator (v2.3.1814; owner: "a new login screen needs to be made (re use the same splash screen). It should have button for Login (put in key) or create new character."). Coming back is the common act and creating the rare one, so the creator sits behind a choice. It reuses the creator's painted backdrop so the two never look like different apps.
- Every load stops at the door, and you tap Continue or Create Character (v2.3.2447; owner: "Player should need to tap continue or create a character."). It goes straight into the world only after a choice you just made (a bro picked, a key typed) or a crash-recovery reload.
- The door is the owner's painted art, not CSS buttons (v2.3.1823; owner: "the title feels game-like but the buttons feel like standard mobile-app controls... your background and title currently say fantasy RPG, while the buttons say website login form"). The logo lockup and the CONTINUE plate are the owner's own files (v2.3.2161–2167, v2.3.2458, v2.3.2642). Continue is the one gold main button and breathes gently; gold motes drift and the logo shimmers now and then (off with the phone's reduce-motion setting). It is the title flow, so Lantern Slate's in-game rules don't apply.
- One line under the buttons, in the owner's own wording: "Your Login Key lets you access your character on any device." A longer one ran to three lines and competed with the buttons. The version and build code sit at the bottom, the quietest thing there (owner: "add the version back to the home splash screen somewhere", v2.3.2185). The number is the game's own, the newest v2.3.N in the build's commit titles (v2.3.3481); package.json's number, kept only as the art's cache key, had read 2.3.2324 on a v2.3.3439 game.
- Continue opens "CHOOSE YOUR BRO" (owner: "I like CHOOSE YOUR BRO here."): up to 10 bros a device ("n / 10" always shown), highest level first ("sort by highest level character on top"), each card a portrait ("In an RPG, I should recognize my character visually before I even read the name."), name, gold LEVEL and "Played 3d ago". One tap plays; the list scrolls past 4–5 bros instead of growing; the cards are gunmetal blue to stand apart from the sheet ("Make the buttons like a gunmetal blue").
- It is a character select, not an account manager (owner: "it currently feels like an account-management modal, not a character-selection screen", v2.3.2193): no delete button and no Create card ("Remove the delete character button from this menu.", "Remove the create bro from there."). Each card's ••• holds Restart at level 1 and Remove from this device (frees a slot; the Login Key still brings the bro back), and both need you to type "yes" (owner's request, v2.3.2194). The "This device is full" card points there in those words (v2.3.3481).
- The key box is folded to one line, "Playing on another device? USE LOGIN KEY" (owner: "The Login Key input is consuming almost half the panel despite being an occasional action."). A key typed at the door joins at once (owner: "make it so that when you enter your character key you just immediately join the game", v2.3.1823). It is checked with the server first, so a typo can neither wipe this device's key nor quietly make a fresh level-1 bro (v2.3.1143).
- Login Keys are now eight short words made by the server (the owner chose "8 short words", v2.3.3355), shown as eight numbered words, good on any device. Older short keys work only on devices the character was played on (v2.3.3344).
- A refused key is never treated as a wrong key and never makes a new character. The right old key on a new device shows "This character is played on another device" with a code to send the owner, Copy, Use a Login Key and Try again. Since v2.3.5716 (`oldkeyheld`) an old key is never let in on a new device, so the words send the player to a new Login Key (the Account card of a device they play on, or one the owner gives); Try again stays for a pass with `oldkeyheld` off (card RV, decided for the owner while away). A key the owner took back shows "This Login Key no longer works" and a way to type a new one. Too many wrong guesses by anyone only makes the phone wait a minute and retry.
- In game: More → "Login Key" tile ("Save it — it is your only way back"), promoted from three taps deep in Settings (v2.3.2038; owner: "is the character key retrievable once inside the game?") and named for the key, which is what someone about to wipe a browser looks for. The page: the key with Copy, "This Login Key is the only way to get your character back ... There is no email recovery.", then a form to continue another bro, which here asks "Continue as your Lv N character?" because it signs this one out.
- The in-world exit says what it does: one bro, "Leave the world?" / Log Out (red); two or more, "Switch bro?" / Switch Bro, not red, "This one is saved", opening onto the bro list (v2.3.2421). **Since v2.3.4123 it always logs out** (the owner, 2026-11-03: "Change just the exit door icon on top left to "logout" (bring to splash screen) not switch character"): **since v2.3.4221 a pill that says LOGOUT** (the owner, 2026-11-04: "The top left button needs to just say 'logout' as a pill button."; the dashboard CLOSE pill's font, weight, tracking and colour, no door picture; aria-label "Log out" and the hook `.bt-zone-header__logout` kept), labelled "Log out", the same confirm ("Leave the world?", red Log Out, "Your bro is saved"), and it lands on the splash screen whatever number of bros the device holds. A device with several bros picks another there: Continue opens the list of bros.
- Nothing pops up at login: the daily chest is gone (owner: "I find the login page with the chest intrusive", v2.3.3140).
- On a computer the painting is one picture across the window (v2.3.3836; owner: "The loading screen (ocean scene) and the splash page both look like they have effects that only suit the portrait mode"): the dark castle on the left, the golden one on the right, the river under the buttons, and the gold motes across all of it. The logo and the buttons keep the phone's column. Before, the column showed the whole painting small, over the same painting twice the size: two hard seams and two of every castle. The creator behind the door shares the backdrop and changed with it. A phone is unchanged.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The creator used to be the front door, with an "Already have a character?" link (until v2.3.1814).
- The key button said "Log in with your Key" until "Continue" (owner: "'Continue' is a better button ...", v2.3.1923, the plate repainted v2.3.1954).
- An overwrite warning on Create (v2.3.1861) was retired once a device could hold ten bros (v2.3.1923).
- Until v2.3.2447 a known key walked straight in, and the door opened the list by itself (v2.3.2111).
- Painted diamonds around Create and a static shine on the logo were removed (owner: "remove the diamonds framing create character ... it's a dead giveaway of AI produced content", v2.3.2207–2209).

**Please don't suggest:**
- The creator as the first screen again, or opening the list or entering the world on load by itself.
- Plain CSS, web-form-style buttons on the door, or holding the door to Lantern Slate's in-game rules.
- A visible delete button or a Create card in the bro list; dropping the typed "yes".
- Showing the key box open by default, or a second confirm at the door.
- Email, password or social-login recovery: there is none by design (v2.3.1143), and recovery goes through the owner.
- Treating a device refusal or a lockout as a wrong key that starts over.

**Still open:**
- Asked for in the v2.3.2193 redesign and not built: each bro's last location under its name ("LEVEL 18 · Bro Town"), as nothing stores a last place yet.
- A one-tap human check for new characters (Cloudflare Turnstile, "One quick check") is built but off until the owner turns it on (v2.3.3275).

Sources: docs/specs/account-login.md, docs/specs/login-keys.md, docs/specs/device-lock.md, docs/specs/identity.md, docs/specs/account-check.md, docs/specs/character-restart.md, docs/specs/switch-bro-labelling.md, docs/TRAPS.md (§209, §215), src/ui/panels/LoginScreen.jsx, src/ui/panels/CharacterPicker.jsx, src/ui/account/AccountKeyCard.jsx, src/ui/account/AccountLoginForm.jsx, src/ui/DeviceScreen.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/MorePanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/ZoneHeader.jsx, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/styles/game.css, CLAUDE.md

## The character creator
Screens: creator, creator-tab-*, creator-paint, creator-paint-designs

**What it is for:** Making a new bro: a look and a name, then ENTER BRO TOWN. The look is made only here and stays with the character, so the screen puts the bro first and keeps every picker in view beside him.

**Decisions, and why:**
- "The character is the star" is a standing owner call (v2.3.798, v2.3.1524). Every layout change is weighed by what it costs his size; trading his size away is the owner's decision.
- Upright the screen is two columns (v2.3.1524): on the left the logo, the bro on his pedestal between two braziers, the BRO NAME plate, ENTER BRO TOWN and Back; on the right the Trait Picker, always open. The owner's reasoning: the picker is the purpose of the screen, so it gets the room instead of hiding behind a button.
- It wears the title screens' gold, not Lantern Slate (owner's mockups: the picker v2.3.2358; the name cluster v2.3.2359, "the left column should come across into the fold too"). The gold stays inside the creator and the door. ENTER BRO TOWN is the one gold button.
- The picker is labelled "Trait Picker" because the owner wanted the pane to say what it is (v2.3.1527); its sparkles went at the owner's word ("you can remove the sparkles framing TRAIT PICKER too", v2.3.2360). It has nine flat tabs in a 3×3 grid, each with painted art: Hair, Hats, Skin, Eyes, Eyewear, Beard, Shirt, Pants, Shoes. They are flat because the old "Head" group took two taps to reach a choice (v2.3.1525). A tab with nothing to choose is dropped, never shown empty.
- Bro type is its own button, wide across the top of the grid. It was named "Species" until v2.3.3772 (owner: "Change species to bro type"); the code and the tests' state ids still say species. Its icon is the three bro types' heads side by side, Human, Monkey and Alien, the same heads as its tiles (v2.3.3772; owner: "for bro type just include 3 small heads of each type instead of just the weird shaped alien one"); it was an alien's head. The heads stand over the words, as on every other tab, not beside them: beside them, the strip took the whole button on an upright phone and squeezed "Bro type" into a sliver at its right edge (v2.3.3777, seen in the review's portrait shot). (v2.3.3589; owner: "I think species should be its own button on character creator screen", then "Yes make species it's own button. And add alien."): Human, Monkey and Alien. A tenth tab in the 3×3 would have stood alone on a fourth row, two columns cost five rows (115 px of options at 390×844), and four or five columns cut "Species", "Eyewear" and "Shoes" off, so it is one 44 px row over the grid and the nine stand where they did; its icon is the Skin tab's head made an alien, and since v2.3.3590 it wears the Hemi Bros collection's alien face (owner, of the eyes: "Biggest difference for alien is the alien eyes. Those weren't made the same"): the collection's small checker eyes with its nose and mouth, as the eyes alone were two specks at the tab's 30 px. Sideways it is simply the first of the two narrow columns. Picking a species puts on its skin (Monkey Brown, Alien Cyan) and the Alien's eyes, but never over a colour or eyes the player chose. It has no colour row: the skin is the next tab, and the same swatches on two tabs would be two controls for one colour. The Skin tab is the skin tones (at the colour row's size, four a row: 26 of them since the fur colours) and the tattoo card.
- Each strip's None tile shows what None means: the Hemi Bros collection's plain bro, its head with nose, mouth and ear and no eyes, on Bro type (Human), Hair, Hats, Beard and Shirt, and with the collection's Normal Eyes on Eyes (Normal) and Eyewear (v2.3.3592; owner: "the 'None' option should show a thumbnail of what that means - for example human should just show a hemi bro head (maybe the one without the eyes to focus on just the shape)"). It replaced v2.3.3526's no-entry sign and the painted bald head (tools/ui/make_none_tiles.py).
- Every tab keeps the same height, so the bro never jumps as you switch tabs (owner reports, v2.3.1252–1253). Options scroll down under a ▾ fade (v2.3.2205); colours show up to four rows before they scroll (v2.3.2396; owner: "the color picker is dimming the colors even when there's more room to display"). A tab with no colour row (Hats with no hat on, a bald bro, no beard) gives that empty space to its options; the bro stands in his own column, so this cannot move him (v2.3.3481).
- On a computer the Trait Picker is drawn 1.3 times larger in a creator 1.4 columns wide, the bro's column as it was (v2.3.3769; the owner: "For the trait picker desktop only you can make the trait picker area and buttons proportionally larger"), and it is as tall as its tab (v2.3.3769; the owner: "As you find improvements you can make to the empty space on the desktop version go ahead and make your changes to better it"): on Species it ended three tiles in with some 60% of the pane an empty navy box, so it stops under its content and the painted backdrop shows below; a long tab (Hair, Hats, Shirt) still reaches the screen's foot and scrolls inside. The bro does not move: he is in his own column, whose height the picker does not set. Phones keep the full-height pane.
- A "Default" text button above the swatches gives the item back its own colours (v2.3.2035; owner: "make the default color its own text button above the color options (right now it's a random color like blue even if it doesn't match default item color)"). A swatch would have to be some colour, and would lie about the item.
- Skin, Shirt, Pants and Shoes carry a drawing card: a live picture of your own bro, framed on what you are editing, with the editor's real tools under it (16 ink colours and 3 brush sizes; patterns on Shoes). Any of them opens the editor with that tool ready. Owner: "I don't really want a button to launch the editor anymore. Every time somebody playtests the game they always never notice it", then "I'd rather you just have the tools for tattooing right there beneath the character" (v2.3.2414–2415); "Add shirt editor under shirt" (v2.3.2416).
- You turn him by dragging a finger across him. The rotate buttons went (owner: "Remove the two buttons for turning the bro on trait picker page and just keep behavior for using finger to turn", v2.3.2006); the owner's arc-and-dots cue at his feet teaches the drag at half strength ("make the new touch spin art 50% transparent though so it doesn't take up too much attention") and fades once he has been turned (v2.3.2391–2393). A tap zooms in close and back.
- The name, Randomize Bro and Reset sit in one carved plate from the owner's mockup (a lion medallion on top, v2.3.2361); ENTER stays outside it. BRO NAME is a centred heading (owner: "Make the name your character area more obvious. Maybe center and large and in all caps put the BRO NAME label.", v2.3.2151), and the name field is the biggest control there ("Make name larger to fill the space", v2.3.2416), with a gold reroll icon inside it. The icon sits inside the field's right end on purpose (v2.3.1251), the name's text kept clear of it, and the owner asked for it bigger ("randomize button for name needs to be larger it looks small and awkward", v2.3.2453); the automatic check's "partly covered by Generate a random name" on the name field is that icon.
- Randomize Bro (green) rolls the whole look; Reset (russet) gives the blank slate the owner asked for ("bald shirtless character is what I wanted"), drawings included. Different colours at the owner's request (v2.3.2151); "Randomize Bro", not "Look", at the owner's word (v2.3.2456).
- ENTER BRO TOWN can always be pressed, so it can say why you can't go yet, in one short line under the name: "Name your bro first", "Type at least 2 letters", "Pinch back to 100% zoom" (owner: "if someone tries to press the shared 'join brotown button' make it give the reason it can't join (need name first) etc.", v2.3.2388; one line each since v2.3.3430). Its label gently grows and shrinks (owner: "make the Enter Bro Town text subtly grow and shrink", v2.3.1577), and only once you are able to leave.
- The name follows the worker's name rule, and the same line says why before you press (v2.3.5622, card MOD; the red team's RT-3 S2): "No emoji or symbols" (an emoji in the world's outlined name is a known iPhone crash, and a hidden or right-to-left mark can disguise a name), "Pick a name of your own" (a name that reads as the game or its staff: BroTown, Admin, Mod, Owner...), "20 letters at most". Each fits the one-line slot at 320 like the others. The rule is the worker's own (src/game/charName.js, held to it by mirror-audit), so a name the box takes is never refused at the join.
- The name field does not grab focus when the screen opens: on iPhone that opens the keyboard and hides the bro (owner, v2.3.1818). While you type, the bro shrinks and the logo steps aside so the keyboard doesn't shove the screen (owner's iOS report, v2.3.2391).
- It opens on the Bro type tab, "Species" until v2.3.3772 (v2.3.3589). It opened on Skin from v2.3.3526, when Skin held the species too: the first ChatGPT review asked for it so a new player sees the drawing card (a tattoo) at once, and the owner said yes ("Go recommended on everything"), the species and skin being what the rest is built on. With the two apart it opens on the species, and the Skin tab with its card is the next tab. The camera still opens on the whole bro. Until v2.3.3526 it opened on Hair, and a player who never visited Skin, Shirt, Pants or Shoes never saw the card.
- Back returns to the door (owner: "Create a character need a back button to main menu.", v2.3.2219). The "already have a bro?" link was removed here (owner, v2.3.2006): the door's Continue does that.
- Sideways the same controls sit in three columns (bro, name plate, picker), since a sideways phone is 300–430 px tall and the creator cannot scroll on a touch screen (v2.3.3216). On a laptop it sizes from the game's column so ENTER is on screen (v2.3.3407), and so does its drawing editor, whose Done had run off the column (v2.3.3481). On a short upright phone the plate gives height back so the bro stays full size (the owner's option B, v2.3.3430).
- The drawing editor names itself ("Tattoos", "Shirt design", "Pants design", "Shoe pattern"; owner: "find room to make a title label somewhere so users know what editor they're in"). You tap the part of the little bro you want to ink and flip him to draw his back, a separate drawing (v2.3.2422, v2.3.2503); any visible skin takes a tattoo, and pants and shirts are drawn on the garment itself. Tools: pen, eraser, fill, shapes with a corner handle, letters, mirror, Select to recolour, resize or re-layer, ready-made designs (owner: "a catalog of pre done designs to choose from"), Undo/Redo, a trash can, Done. Every stroke is kept as you go. Each ready-made design you pick is ADDED as its own piece, on top of what is there, never replacing it (v2.3.4167; owner: "I wasn't able to put two flames on the pants it would erase the previous one when I tried to add another one"); the same design picked twice steps two cells aside so the second is plainly a second. Holding a design, a shape or a letter, the Select row has Copy beside Place (owner: "a copy button (for selected design) would be great"): the copy lands two cells away, on top, and is the one you hold, so your next drag moves the copy. The layer row's fifth button, Delete, takes off just the piece you hold (the bin still empties the whole canvas), and a palette tap on a held design repaints its main colour, never its dark outline. What is saved and worn is still the one finished picture per canvas, so the world shows every design you placed.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- An always-visible sheet under the bro, then a slide-up drawer the owner picked from three models (v2.3.1276), then the permanent column (v2.3.1524).
- Grouped tabs (Head with sub-tabs, Shirt, Pants, Shoes) until the flat tabs (v2.3.1525).
- A Build tab for height and frame (v2.3.1953), removed: "I changed my mind on the build sizes ... It looks bad. Use the medium (default) character only." (v2.3.2268).
- The species inside the Skin tab, its strip over the skin tones (v2.3.2682, "Make sure it's available in trait picker"), until it got its own button (v2.3.3589).
- Picking a ready-made design replaced the whole drawing (v2.3.2444: "Replacing is what 'choose a design' plainly means"), reasoned when a design was flattened into pixels; once a design became its own movable piece (v2.3.2455) that only erased the first flame when you added a second, and it adds since v2.3.4167.
- The default colour was a swatch, then no tile at all (re-tapping your pick undid it, v2.3.1253), then a tile again (v2.3.2007; owner: "I don't see a way to get a hat color back to its default color"), then the Default text button (v2.3.2035).
- A large brass button to open the editor (v2.3.1946; owner: "Make it more noticeable to go into the new editor"), which playtesters still missed, replaced by the live card (v2.3.2414).
- The painted scroll-and-die reroll icon (the owner: "Whatever that is") became a stroked recycle mark (v2.3.2464), then smaller ("Shrink the randomize icon though it's too large", v2.3.2469).
- A diamond divider in the plate ("Remove this diamond separator thing.", v2.3.2388); the editor's save-to-slots row (owner: "too confusing between saving load out vs saving your current work", v2.3.2416).
- The bro was moved and shrunk several times to stand on the pedestal and keep his head clear of the logo (v2.3.2151–2204).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Hiding the pickers behind a button, a drawer or a second screen, or shrinking the bro to make room for chrome.
- Bringing back the Build tab, showing empty tabs, or rotate buttons on the stage.
- A swatch for the default colour, or a second gold button (gold means ENTER here).
- A separate button to open the editor instead of the live card, or save slots in the editor.
- Focusing the name field on open, making the creator scroll on a phone, or fitting it to the keyboard by shrinking the whole screen (that lets iOS push the creator off the top with no way back: TRAPS §64).
- Holding this screen to Lantern Slate's in-game rules.

Sources: src/ui/panels/NameModal.jsx, src/ui/panels/PlayerPaint.jsx, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/styles/game.css, src/data/joinGate.js, src/rendering/traits/speciesCatalog.js, docs/specs/SPECIES-PLAN.md, docs/specs/character-build.md, docs/specs/creator-colour-picker-fit.md, docs/specs/creator-ink-card.md, docs/specs/creator-keyboard-and-spin-cue.md, docs/specs/editor-round-four.md, docs/specs/tattoo-front-and-back.md, docs/specs/reach-every-button.md, docs/TRAPS.md (§64, §175, §214, §227), docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, CLAUDE.md

## Arriving in BroTown
Screens: welcome

**What it is for:** The first minute in the world: one loading screen, then you stand in the Wheel's BroTown with one clear job (find Mayor Bro), and the first quest teaches the controls one at a time as it hands you the gear they are about.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Nothing for the world while a loading screen is up (v2.3.4136).** The owner: "Before the loading screen is finished I'm seeing pop up messages meant for the main screen area (like the star on minimap etc). Needs to wait until it's done." Every card, arrow, banner, toast and the minimap's big star waits until the ocean clip (or a zone's veil) has fully gone, then comes in its usual order, as if the world had just appeared (docs/specs/loading-gate.md).
- **The first steps: five, and nothing more (v2.3.4106).** The owner: "First sequence of events when first creating character should be welcome to Brotown, talk to mayor bro, equip weapons, see minimap with star marker and quest progress in top left pill. Don't include the non-essential stuff anymore like tap to talk etc". So a brand-new character sees exactly these, in order, each once: (1) WELCOME / BroTown / "Your adventure starts here." (with Skip tutorial); (2) "Talk to Mayor Bro: He has your first quest." ringing him, and the big gold arrow pointing at him, until his dialogue opens; (3) "Gear up" on the glowing sword and shield, with the arrow, until both are worn; (4) "Your map: The gold star is where your quest leads." ringing the minimap; (5) "Your quest: Your quest, and how far along you are." ringing the quest card at the top left. Steps 4 and 5 go by themselves after 7 s, or on a tap. The five follow each other a breath apart (1.5 s), not the 20 s other cards wait. Every other first-minute card is retired: the folded dashboard and its OPEN ring, drag to move, attack, the special, the reward's way to the bag, the bow and staff, swapping, the guard, and tap yourself to chat. The first points (a level-up) stay as they were; the Login Key card waits until the five are done and the first quest is handed in; the iPhone's "Play full screen" card waits for the five too. The glow on the gear, the weapon-swap hint after the first hand-in, the NEW MONSTER card and the townsfolk's lines are not cards of the first sequence and are unchanged (docs/specs/quest-coach.md; tested by mp-firststeps).
- **No tutorial message over the controller (v2.3.6791, card UI3 PR 11).** The owner, 2026-11-27, after the new controller landed: *"Needs to fix tutorial messages over new controller area."* One rule places every teaching message (`src/game/tutorClear.js`, asked through `src/ui/tutorSpot.js`): its WORDS cover no housing of the controller (the stick's lobe, the low rail, the right lobe and its tier, as boxes from the layout the controls are placed with), upright, sideways and in a computer's window (there the controller is not drawn and nothing moves). A message that would meet one goes straight up, 8 px over the highest housing it spans, or (the coach's card, which points at its target with its ring) slides up to 160 px sideways into the open middle when that keeps it nearer; never over the top bar: a card too tall for the room left is cut to it and scrolls. An arrow or ring may touch what it points past; its words may not. Found on the phone page before the change (mp-tutorclear): sideways, the coach's TALK TO MAYOR BRO card on the right lobe and the big arrow's "Tap to open" on the rail; and on every screen the "Next: ..." toasts and the Add to Home Screen card, hung 12 to 14 px over the band inside a shell that stands 114 to 131 px over it. `tools/guards/tutor-clear.test.mjs` holds the rule and that each message asks it.
- **The marks on Mayor Bro are on him every frame (v2.3.4360).** The owner: "the reticle around mayor bro is pretty obnoxious with movement (the frames are gritty)". The gold box round him, the card over his head and the big arrow are page elements laid over the canvas, and he is in the world: he moves on the page whenever the camera does, and he strolls about on his own. The box and card found him 12 times a second and the arrow 4 (then glided a fifth of a second), so all three stuttered after him as you walked. Now each is put where he is after every frame the game draws, from the camera that frame was drawn with, on whole device pixels (`src/ui/afterFrame.js`); the arrow no longer glides while it points at him. The card moves by its own rules (inside the world's span, clear of the band, under the cap at the top), so it still changes side of him when he nears the top of the screen: that one jump is the rule, not the grit. Nothing about what the marks say, when they show or when they go has changed (tested by mp-mayormark).
- One loading screen: the owner's "washed ashore" beach clip, its LOADING bar painted into the film. It plays once and holds on its full bar (owner, of the loop: "shows it load all the way then replays it loading halfway", v2.3.1347), is shown whole rather than cropped (owner: "on the web app view the ocean screen loading bar gets clipped", v2.3.2199), and shows its first frame at once so it never starts small (v2.3.3025). The trip through the old town happens hidden behind it (owner: "players are starting in the old town and getting routed to the wheel on the loading screen", v2.3.3025).
- On a computer the upright beach stands whole in the middle, over a blurred, darkened copy of its own first frame instead of black bars, and the server's words sit under it rather than across its painted bar (v2.3.3836; owner: "The loading screen (ocean scene) and the splash page both look like they have effects that only suit the portrait mode"). A phone is unchanged.
- The loading screen is allowed to take longer: the animations are loaded before it lifts, so nothing hitches on first use (owner directive, 2026-07-19; in the Wheel, monsters' looks load as you walk toward them, by the owner's yes, v2.3.2989). The world never appears without the server; if the server is slow, the screen says what it is waiting for (v2.3.2439).
- You arrive in the Wheel's BroTown square (owner: "Just have players spawn in town.", v2.3.2990). A new bro starts with no weapon and cannot leave the safe ground until he has spoken to Mayor Bro (owner: "not be allowed to leave town without speaking to mayor bro first. He'll give you the sword and shield", v2.3.1676); walking out shows "Speak to Mayor Bro first — He's beside the Town Hall — he'll arm you". It holds at full strength while you lean on the gate, on foot or on the board (v2.3.3839: it froze almost clear on a quick computer, and the owner met an "invisible wall").
- The first thing said is a short plate at the top: WELCOME / Bro Town / "Find Mayor Bro — he wants a word. Follow the gold road on your map." (since v2.3.3812 "The gold line on your map leads to him; then tap him.": lane A's judge pass found players looking for a road in the world). On a computer the plate stands lower, its top at 58% (v2.3.3812): at 26% it lay over the Town Hall and Mayor Bro, the one it tells you to find, at 1280 x 720; a phone keeps 26% (owner: "a message about welcome to bro town and find the mayor because he wants to speak with you", v2.3.2121). The plate carries the only button, Skip tutorial (owner: "add just a 'skip tutorial' button on the very first dialog box when you join the game. No pop ups should be scheduled after that.", v2.3.2890).
- The way is a gold road on the minimap only, from you to Mayor Bro (or the edge of the map, toward him) — nothing is drawn on the ground (owner: "I think I want to remove the footsteps and just rely on the gold road on the minimap of where to go", v2.3.2992).
- The dashboard started folded on a brand-new bro's first join, with a ring on the OPEN chip (owner, v2.3.2495); retired with the first steps (v2.3.4106): the band is as you left it.
- Quest marks over a giver are pictures, not emoji (the owner asked for a green checkmark "above his head (not emoji)"): since v2.3.3481 the owner's own pins from their icon sheets ("Icon picks: ... 4"): a gold "!" pin offers a quest; a still GREY "?" pin means accepted, still waiting (a waiting quest never looks ready); a gold ring with a green check means you have everything, hand it in; a finished giver wears nothing (v2.3.3047). The waiting "?" was grey (a teal grey that read as green on grass), then yellow at the owner's "have the question mark above mayor bros head be yellow instead of green. Green implies something is done." (v2.3.3336), and is a neutral grey pin now, the one the owner picked.
- The pinned quest card (top left) leads with the quest's picture and turns into a painted green check when you have everything (owner: "Make the quest notification turn to a green checkmark if you have everything you need to complete the quest.", v2.3.3048). Since v2.3.3918 the check shows ONLY once it is ready (the adopted review: "The green checkmark next to 'Into the Wild' makes the objective look completed, while the instruction below still says to kill five monsters"): in progress the card is the title, the count at its right ("3/5") and the objective; ready, the check, "5/5" and "Done! Go back to Mayor Bro". A tap opens that quest's page (the v2.3.1714 fold to its title is retired: the owner's tracker is "tappable", and one compact card needs no folding).
- Accepting the first quest puts a sword and shield in your bag, not your hands, so the controls that lead to them glow: OPEN on a folded band, then the sword and shield tiles; after the hand-in, the bow and staff (owner's sequence, v2.3.3049). Only until the second quest is handed in, and it is the buttons themselves glowing, not another card.
- The first hand-in pays 25 gold and the bow and staff, with no "choose where to train it" step (owner: "Makes no sense to have to choose xp in something you've never used yet. Just remove that little xp bonus.", v2.3.3335).
- (Until v2.3.4106, replaced by the five first steps above.) The quest coach teaches each control when the quest has just given you the thing it is about (owner: "the controls need to be taught in the quest line by highlighting (flashing) the sequence you should be learning", v2.3.1796): Move; Gear up; Attack; Special attack ("a quick swipe"); after the hand-in, Your reward, Three weapons, Swap weapons and Guard with the bow; and, once you hold a shield, "Everyone here is a real player. Tap yourself to say something." and Save your Login Key (v2.3.2147–2306); and at your first level-up, the way to your points (v2.3.3551, the one lesson that also comes after the Mayor's tutorial). One card at a time, beside a ring on the real control; the card never covers the combat buttons (owner, v2.3.2564), steps aside while a window is open (v2.3.3407) or a harvest runs, goes up over your bro's name plate rather than lie on him when there is room above him (both v2.3.3481), and a tap on it puts it away (owner, asked three times, v2.3.2123–2312).
- Pacing (owner: "The tutorial onboarding is too heavy on window pop ups right after you join the game.", v2.3.2766): the welcome goes first and alone, no card over a plate, at least 20 s between tutorial pop-ups (owner: "Put a minimum 20 second timer on the onboarding tutorial between pop ups", v2.3.2888), and the iPhone "Play full screen" card only after 75 s, on a quiet screen.
- A returning player is never welcomed or coached again (owner: "Sometimes when you rejoin a game from a saved character it brings up the tutorial again as if starting a new character", v2.3.2765), even on a new preview build.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- A talking-head Mayor Bro video that played over the dashboard (v2.3.1219), removed by the owner ("remove the tutorial and the mayor bro pop up and greeting", v2.3.1593); the welcome came back as two lines of text, not the clip.
- A gold road on the ground (footprints, arrows, ribbon or beads) until v2.3.2992; now the minimap's only.
- Earlier loading clips (an ocean, a reef, an island vista) before the beach (v2.3.1220); the clip used to be cropped to fill the screen.
- Four onboarding pop-ups in about three seconds on arrival, then a 2.6 s gap between cards, before the referee and the 20 s gap (v2.3.2766, v2.3.2888).
- A coach card that took no taps, then a 22 px ✕ (v2.3.2123), a 44 px one after demo reviewers said the tips "just won't go away no matter what I do" (v2.3.2284), then the whole card tappable (v2.3.2312).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Bringing back the Mayor's video greeting, or a path drawn on the ground.
- A dimming, step-by-step tutorial that stops play; the coach teaches one thing at a time and the game keeps running.
- More pop-ups on arrival, or tutorial cards closer together than 20 s.
- Starting a new bro with weapons, or letting him leave before meeting Mayor Bro.
- Emoji quest marks, or green for a quest still in progress (green means done).
- An XP-choice window on the first quest's reward.
- Lifting the loading screen before the art and the server are ready, or looping its clip.
- Showing the welcome or the tutorial again to a returning player.

**Still open:**
- A drag that starts on a coach card is still answered by the card rather than the joystick under it; keeping tap-to-dismiss over that is the owner's trade to make (v2.3.2577).

Sources: docs/specs/onboarding-pace.md, docs/specs/quest-guidance.md, docs/specs/quest-coach.md, docs/specs/quest-path-guide.md, docs/specs/quest-windows.md, docs/TRAPS.md (§220), src/ui/mobile/QuestCoach.jsx, src/ui/onboardingPace.js, src/game/welcomeBanner.js, src/game/wheelHome.js, src/ui/IntroVideo.jsx, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/data/gameSystems.js, src/styles/game.css, CLAUDE.md

## The world screen (HUD) and the controls
Screens: hud, hud-band, hud-folded, hud-controls, hud-weapons, topbar-gold

**What it is for:** The screen you play on. The painted world fills almost all of it; a thin top bar, the minimap (top right), the dashboard band (bottom) and, on a phone, touch controls that appear only when they can do something sit around it. On a computer, keys replace the touch controls.

**Decisions, and why:**
- The world is the star and the interface stays quieter than it: "the world is the illustration; the interface is the field kit", "icons, not words" (Lantern Slate, UI Bible). Code notes record that the owner has asked more than once to keep the HUD clear, and the fun & social plan the owner approved ("Go recommended") rules out any new always-on button on a phone because the screen is full.
- Top bar: a door chip on the left (log out to the splash screen, always, since v2.3.4123; from v2.3.2421 it read "Switch Bro" when the device held more than one bro); **since v2.3.4183 the door is all that stays on it** (the owner, 2026-11-02: "I don't like the placement of where the gold count is (top right) and the placement of the achievement points (top left)"): the gold is in the Gear tab's header and your achievement points on the Bro card, and a gain shows briefly as a "+12 gold" or "+5 AP" word rising under the bar's right end, where the purse was. The purse and points described below are how it was until then; in the world nothing drawn in the middle since v2.3.4113 (the place and its safety are on the minimap's plate: see "Where you are and whether you're safe"; the time-of-day icon of v2.3.2892 is gone, night being off); gold on the right ("Move gold amount display to very top right on the top bar that lists the zone name.", v2.3.2320). It is the only gold count on screen, because two counts disagree the moment one lags. Your achievement points sit beside the door, opposite the gold: the owner's medal and the server's number ("Achievement points should be a prominent part of the experience in brotown", v2.3.3553). A rise pops a "+10" beside the number that slides into it, as gold spins its coin, and a tap opens the Achievements page. In the Wheel the name is just "BroTown" (v2.3.3108), and the icon always shows day for now ("Yeah make it daylight only for now", v2.3.2963). A "TEST MODE" tag beside the name marks a character the owner's test switch has set (v2.3.3430). A long press on the name opens the owner's test panel, on purpose not a HUD button. **While you earn combat XP the middle of the bar is your XP bar** (v2.3.3695, the owner: "The game needs an XP Popup and an Xp bar to appear with the icon of whatever primary combat skill you're earning Xp in someplace at the bottom (or maybe top) depending on what works best. Then disappears again."): the skill's picture (the owner's Points icons), "Lv 7", a gold bar filled to the next level and "+3 XP" popping, the worker's numbers, in place of the name for 3 seconds after the last XP, then the name again. In the top bar because it is the one place every layout shares (upright, sideways and a computer playing wide), never under a thumb and never over the world, and the bar where the AP already pops its "+N"; the bottom is the dashboard upright but a side column sideways and on a computer. The name stays in the page under it (the zone banner docks into it, and the long press still reaches it). Since v2.3.3739 (the owner: "put the plus xp to the left of the weapon icon so you can still see the bar. Also use a white bar to show how the xp will grow and fill it up to that point. It should also show the number of xp needed for the next level somewhere on the bar."): the order is "+3 XP", the picture, "Lv 7", the bar, the pop in a slot of its own so the row never shifts; the XP just earned shows white at once and the gold grows over it in half a second (a level-up runs to the end, then the new level from empty); "45 / 120" on the bar, cream with a dark outline to read over gold and white alike. On a 320 px phone the words and gaps shrink, never the bar (docs/specs/xp-bar.md). Since v2.3.3796 ("show the white bar longer ... make the fill up to that white portion slower. Also instead of changing the whole xp number make it count up"): the white stands alone 0.6 s, the gold climbs 1.2 s eased out, the number counts with it (a level-up to the old level's end, then the new level's), and the bar stays 3.5 s (5.2 s after a level-up). Since v2.3.3992 (the owner: "Make the plus xp without the dark container background. Make the text small then pop up before fading and the numbers do a jump to bag effect but just to the number on the xp bar (keep all the icons at the top of the bar as it is now)"; "Also don't include any decimals in numbers for xp"): "+3 XP" is the words alone with a flat dark outline, no pill; it starts small, pops past its size and settles, then fades; a kill's "+N" flies into the bar's NUMBER ("45 / 120"), which pulses as it lands (it flew to the skill's picture before), the pictures untouched; and every XP number on the bar is whole: a fraction of a gain is held and added to the next, a gain under 1 pops nothing, never "+0 XP", and a kill whose blow was worth under 1 shows "+1 XP" from what was held. **Since v2.3.3765 everything on the bar is larger** (the owner: "Make the info on the top ribbon larger (icon for achievement points, numbers, titles, larger). It can fit more"): the medal and its number, the name, the hour's icon, the purse, the door and the bar's height all grow by one scale, the largest each layout fits with nothing cut or overlapping (measured by mp-topbar with a 2,550,000 purse, 12,400 points, the No man's land pill and TEST MODE): 1.05x on a 320 phone, 1.1x at 360, 1.15x at 375, 1.2x at 390, 1.1x on a 430 phone, 1.3x sideways (it would fit 1.6x, but a sideways screen is short and the world needs the height), 1.45x on a computer. To fit, a phone narrower than 430 shows big numbers short ("2.55M" gold from 100,000, "12.4K" points from 10,000; the full number is in the button's spoken label), and while the XP bar is up there the points step out for its 3 seconds. The pieces placed just under the bar (gold popups, the achievement banner, the new-monster card) move down with it.
- The minimap is top right with a thick slate-and-brass frame "so it's not confused with game screen area" (v2.3.3009), and the land and its levels sit on a name plate under it (v2.3.3108). Details in the world-map section.
- The bro is drawn about 64 CSS px tall with the band folded: "I think char 64 pixels tall was probably best" (v2.3.3020). The view had been zoomed out twice; drawn smaller, the pixel art looked "gritty" as the camera moved (and the owner once called 37 px "too small", v2.3.2249).
- Since v2.3.6580 the bro is about 66 CSS px with the band folded (the default view 0.77 -> 0.79; `?zoom=0.77` is the view before): the owner tried 0.926, 0.85, 0.81 and 0.88 and said *"I think the sweet spot might be between that zoom you just had me test and the more zoomed out default ... seeing player detail vs world around you for just general play"*, then *"Ok I like 0.79"*. The view takes in 97% of the world each way.
- The dashboard band is at most one third of the screen (owner hard lock, v2.3.1258). The OPEN/CLOSE pill at its far left folds it down to its button row, and the choice is remembered ("make it possible to minimize the entire dashboard with a button on that empty space next to gold", v2.3.2119; a pill because the arrow was "a little too subtle. Make it a pill that says CLOSE.", v2.3.2266). A new character starts with it folded and the tutorial rings the pill (the owner's call, v2.3.2495). Six buttons: Dashboard, Character, Quests, Life Skills, Codex, More (v2.3.3215). The sticks keep working above an open band: "You should be able to play the game with the menus open. That's the idea."
- **Since v2.3.6786 your gold is on the top bar again, between "BroTown" and the daily spin** (card UI3, PR 7). The owner's three words on the purse, in order: v2.3.2320 (its date is not in the repository's notes), "Move gold amount display to very top right on the top bar that lists the zone name."; 2026-11-02, v2.3.4183, "I don't like the placement of where the gold count is (top right)" (it left for the Gear tab's header); 2026-11-26, v2.3.6786, "Move gold amount in between daily spin and brotown on top bar". The last names the place: a coin and the number LEFT of the spin's button, never the corner. The number is the purse the page already holds, read where Gear reads it and said in Gear's form ("9,999", "12.4K", "2.55M"; the spoken label says the whole number, "Gold: 9,999,999"), so the two never disagree; nothing shows before the worker's record has come (never a false 0). A gain spins the coin and bumps the number, a spend bumps the number (the old purse's own pops, transform only). It takes no tap. Its place is fixed: the spin's slot keeps the button's width when the button is not shown (no spin today on this worker, or its window open), so the gold never slides into the corner the owner disliked; and the door's column is made as wide as the right one, so the logo and the name stay centred on the screen (what the empty right column was made for, v2.3.1333). On an upright phone there is no room for the coin beside its number (at 375 x 667 the logo and the spin leave 37 px, the gold needs about 72, and twice that with the door's column matched), so there the coin stands over its number in a smaller size and the logo shrinks with the middle (about 85 px of its 102 at 375): the orchestrator's pick (A) of 2026-11-26 while the owner was away; the owner's to change for (B) the spin's button as the wheel's round picture, or (C) the centring given up. Sideways and on a computer the coin is beside its number at full size. While the XP bar holds the middle on an upright phone (only under `?hud=0`) the gold steps out for its seconds, as the AP chip did. Gear still shows the gold too: "move" was read as "show it on the bar", and whether Gear's goes is the owner's call. `?topgold=0` takes it off the bar on one device (remembered; `?topgold=1` forgets). mp-topgold holds the place, the centring, a sale at Diego's against Gear, and the switch.
- On a computer in the old layout (`?oneband=0`; since v2.3.4152 the dashboard is the band at the bottom on every screen) the dashboard is a column at one edge and the keys list a column at the other; CLOSE folds the dashboard's column to a strip at the edge. Since v2.3.3769 that strip is the top bar's grey with one arrow at its middle pointing into the world, and a click anywhere on it opens the dashboard (the owner: "Make the left long strip black bar the same gray color as the top bar but have an arrow centered in the middle pointing towards the screen play area"). A toolbar of the six buttons there was built first and replaced by the owner's design the same night. Since v2.3.3793 the folded strip has no OPEN at its top (the owner: "Also you can remove the 'open' button on desktop since the arrow takes the place of that (redundant with both)"): the arrow alone, wearing I's key cap, and the coach's "open the dashboard" lessons ring the arrow ("Tap the arrow."). CLOSE stays on the open column.
