Every page of the open column but the dashboard's own ends in the three weapons and their levels (v2.3.3769: "As you find improvements you can make to the empty space on the desktop version go ahead"); the Quests page with nothing active lists the quests on offer under its empty line; the Skills tiles keep their spread down the column (v2.3.3687, "Every menu needs to look like it fits the space": packed at the top was tried and left a third of the column empty) and carry their XP to the next level in numbers; a building's window is 470 px wide and up to 82% of the world's height (the Blacksmith keeps its low place so the smith is seen above it); and while Diego's shop strip or a trade's drawer is across the world's foot, the world's buttons under it (a door's E prompt, the skateboard) step aside, their keys still working (v2.3.3793). Phones are unchanged.
- On a computer a window's ✕ wears its "Esc" key cap to its left, level with it (v2.3.3813): hung under the ✕ it fell into the window's words ("Blue slimes explode, by [Esc] the way").
- Sideways in the old layout (`?oneband=0`): no bottom band. A ▴ chip opens the dashboard as a full-height column and the world narrows beside it, never under it ("No I don't want an overlay over the world"). At rest only the chip shows; the owner weighed the extra tap and chose it (v2.3.2176). The column takes the side away from the iPhone's camera cut-out, checked by the owner on the device ("The mapping is correct."), with Settings → Landscape menu side (Auto / Left / Right).
- Left stick: shown only while your thumb drives it ("Hide the joystick overlays. Just show the left joystick when you're moving the character. Just show the right contextual button when there's input that can be interacted with.", v2.3.2246). A swipe on the left half is the dodge; a double tap swaps weapon. The round weapon button sat in the corner under the stick ("Add a little icon of current equipped weapon in bottom left above dashboard but beneath left joystick. If you tap on it it switches to the next equipped weapon.", v2.3.3005), and the bell moved one place right for it.
- **The weapon pill** (v2.3.4130), the owner sending a picture of it: "I uploaded ... different pill shape weapon swap type bar. Put it centered bottom of screen area." One dark pill with a gold rim, centred at the foot of the world just over the dashboard band, on a phone upright and sideways and on a computer alike, with three round wells always in the same order: sword, bow, staff. The weapon in your hand's well is ringed in glowing gold and wears the Character sheet's own coloured picture; the others wear the same pictures baked grey (never a CSS filter over the game: the iPhone grain). A tap or click on a grey well takes that weapon; a weapon you do not have yet is an empty well that says "No bow yet". On a computer the wells wear their keys, 1 2 3, which still work. Upright the bottom of the screen was the thumbs': Jump turned west on its ring (about 12 degrees below west; it was 45) to stand 8 px over the pill, Block beside it, both keeping their size. On a 320 px phone the pill stands between the bell and Block (under the attack disc there), its wells 34 px. Before it: the weapon triangle beside the movement stick (v2.3.3921: "I need a button on screen for switching between weapons. Maybe a triangle or wheel where the active weapon is highlighted on top and the others are greyed out", moved left by the owner: "the right joystick is already crowded with buttons"), and before that the corner chip (v2.3.3005). The left stick's double tap still swaps.
- The right control is both a stick and a contextual button: "I want both joysticks back ... BUT I also want the right joystick to keep its contextual button properties that exist now" (v2.3.2258). Hold to auto-attack, drag to aim, quick swipe for the special; next to a resource it is the harvest button. **Since v2.3.4396 that is the old combat** (`?manual=0`, one device). The game's own attacks by hand (the owner: "only making an attack after manual input is better than auto-attacking at precisely the correct interval"; docs/specs/manual-combat.md): a sword swings once for each swipe across the disc or anywhere on the right side, a bow shoots once for each flick, and only a staff still casts while the thumb is held (its tap a bolt, its swipe the special). A tap or a hold with a sword or a bow attacks nothing; near a monster it floats "Swipe to swing" or "Flick to shoot" over the bro (`mcTeach`). A sword's or a bow's special is armed by its button and made by the next attack. Since v2.3.4655 the sword's swipe swings at once, for its share of a full swing; since v2.3.4800 its readiness and the perfect strike's moment are shown on the blade itself (see "Readiness on the weapon"; the timing bar is gone); `?manual=1` is the plain swipes.
- Its picture is what a tap would do (v2.3.3105, "I'd like the right joystick button to have an icon that represents the action ... maybe chat bubble for speaking [to NPCs], door for entering door"): the weapon in your hand, the harvest tool, a speech bubble (until v2.3.4105: the stick never talks now, see "Talking to characters"), a door, the moon at the farm bed, otherwise a see-through JUMP arrow. A tap jumps only when nothing else applies ("prioritize other contextual uses for the tap instead of jump first if any apply"). Beside a prop a tap jumps and a hold attacks ("a tap should jump"). Since v2.3.3712 a tap on the stick NEVER jumps: the JUMP button (X on a keyboard) is the jump, and a tap with nothing else to do ATTACKS ONCE with the weapon in hand, the way you face (one swing, arrow or bolt; a bow still looses only at something). Where the JUMP arrow was, the stick wears that weapon's picture. (Since v2.3.4396 that free tap attacks only with a staff, or in the old combat: a sword or a bow attacks by a swipe or a flick, `BroTown.jsx` `rE`, `mcSwipes`. A keyboard's jump is Space since v2.3.3844.) The owner: "the contextual jump button gives the jumps only to the jump button and what to replace it with (maybe just an attack in its place if nothing else is there?)".
- Targeting is automatic: always the nearest monster, and a tap on a monster pins it ("Change the auto targeting system to always be nearest enemy. Only way to pick target and lock it on is to tap on the monster", v2.3.2251). A faint ring at monsters' feet shows when Attack can engage. **Since v2.3.4396 there is no tap lock** (the owner: "Remove the tap to target system for now ... I think this system will be easily botted and abused"; "Yes duels lose tap lock"): the sword still faces the nearest monster by itself, a bow and a staff aim where the thumb sends them, and `targeting.js` drops any other lock each frame (`mcLockAllowed`). The tap lock lives on only in the old combat (`?manual=0`). **Since v2.3.6820 the nearest is plainly the nearest** (the owner, 2026-11-26: "I'm often running into an issue where it will lock on the wrong target. I think it should go towards the nearest enemy or at least let you switch to nearest enemy"): inside the sword's reach the nearest takes the lock after a 120 ms dwell instead of having to be 12% nearer, and pushing the stick toward one of two monsters about as near (within 25%, in front of the push) gives it the lock at once; the stick at rest changes nothing by facing (`src/game/lockPick.js`, `docs/specs/lock-pick.md`; `?locknear=0` the old rule).
- In a fight the attack disc turns warm orange and see-through, and almost vanishes over a monster under it. The owner first asked for see-through ("Attack button sometimes covers monster"), then said a faded button looked switched off: "Maybe only during combat it changes color (like to orange) keeping its transparency" (v2.3.2263–2264).
- Every touch button follows the owner's mockup: a gold ring, a picture, no words ("Make the on screen buttons look more like the improved mockup", v2.3.3018). Words appear only as instructions (AIM, WAIT / PUMP / CHOP / REEL / FLIP). The base attack has no cooldown ring: "No cooldown for base attack though".
- The skateboard button (v2.3.3776, the owner: "You can tap it to ride (maybe a button somewhere on screen). When outside of town it makes you get off"): a round button with the owner's board on it, only in BroTown's safe ground, in Shield Bash's place (beside Sprint upright, beside the disc sideways) because it never shows while a fight is near (the shield up, Block showing, a monster targeted). Lit warm while you ride; Z on a computer. On the board he stands sideways like a skater and pushes with the jog's legs when he sets off and every 2 s (`RIDE_LOOK`, one switch). You get off with one short line at the edge of town ("Off the board: boards stay in BroTown."), when hit or when you fight (docs/specs/skateboard.md).
- **The owner's own icons for the reticle, jump and sprint** (v2.3.4130): "Also different icons for jump, sprint, and reticle", with a picture of each. The attack disc's reticle is theirs, drawn in code: a thin grey circle broken into four arcs and a small white cross in the middle. Jump wears a leaping figure and Sprint a winged foot, light on the dark button: the game's OWN drawings of those ideas (tools/ui/draw-control-icons.py, 256 colours), because the owner's references were someone else's pictures (a well-known game's class logo, a stock silhouette). The buttons' frames, sizes and states are as they were.
- **The attack disc wears an aiming reticle, Jump a leaping figure, Sprint a glow and an arc** (v2.3.3920). The owner: "The grayed out large staff button on the right hand side is used to rotate the character and point the direction of attacks"; "The up arrow with the line beneath it is used to make the character jump. I'm not a huge fan of the icon art and I'm not sure the button is large enough"; "The boot with the wing button is tapped once to make the character sprint until their energy runs out". From the design review they adopted: a neutral four-direction reticle that "should look like an aiming joystick, not a disabled button" (a thin ring, four ticks, a gold centre dot, drawn in code, always bright, lit with its rod while a thumb is on it; the weapon in hand is the weapon triangle's now, and a harvest, a door, a talk or the farm still show their own pictures); "a simple leaping silhouette or a motion arc with an airborne figure ... Slightly enlarge the button" (a parchment figure mid-leap over a gold arc; 64 px upright, 68 sideways, was 60 and 64; `?jumpicon=arrow` brings the arrow back for a tab); and for Sprint "give its border a strong glow and perhaps a circular energy indicator ... an unmistakable exhausted state" (a slow gold glow while on, a 3 px gold stamina arc outside the button, and tired: a grey face, an empty red arc and the shake).
- Sprint sits just above the attack disc ("put the sprint button near the right joystick instead of the left maybe just north of it", v2.3.3106), and its gold ring is your stamina running down. Special and Whirlwind made room for it ("Above, fights move up"): a row higher upright, beside it sideways. They stay on a diagonal, from the owner's "enough space between them for not accidentally pressing the other one", but close: "Whirlwind action button needs to be placed closer to melee special attack (too far away)" (v2.3.3522). Their centres are 57 px apart upright (77 before) and 64 sideways (86), about 9 px of air between their rings.
- Block is its own toggle at the attack disc's lower left, apart from the attack buttons "as a mental separation for combat purpose" (v2.3.2562). It wears the owner's round gold shield, cut from their UI sheet like the other ability pictures, with a baked grey copy while it waits ("Block button needs a new icon", v2.3.3522); it had drawn the shield you carry in the world, stretched. Since v2.3.3677 the shield stands on a calm steel-blue disc, lifted off the gold starburst it was painted on ("I want a different icon for the shield block (the light explosion makes it look like an attach)"): the gold burst is what the attack buttons wear, so Block is the one blue button on the right, guarding, not striking. It shows in a fight with a shield on, and stays up until you attack, tap it again or run out of stamina ("Instead of dropping the shield at first hit I want it to keep being held", v2.3.2248). You cannot swing and block at once: "you can both swing and block at the same time. That is not right." Shield Bash appears beside the disc only while the shield is up.
- Whirlwind shows only in a fight (the owner chose hiding over greying out, v2.3.2561). Special stays and greys out when you lack mana, so it can still be learned, and shows your weapon's own special picture (v2.3.3336).
- The left thumb's cluster above the movement stick: Element Burst (enchanted weapons, level 6+) and Quick Eat (v2.3.3260). Quick Eat eats the right heal in one tap, because eating from the bag took four to six seconds of taps, longer than most fights between players. It shows only while you carry a heal and are missing health.
- No man's land badge: a red skull and the ring number in a red pill, a tap gives the rules ("If you tap it it gives you info on what it means", v2.3.3107). Since v2.3.3765 it stands in the top bar just right of "BroTown" ("I think the no man's land pill would work better just to the right of the Brotown name at the top (you can cut it away same as when earning xp in a weapon)"): beside the title, never inside it (the title is the zone banner's landing spot), cut away with the title while the XP bar holds its place and back when the bar goes. The same place upright, sideways and on a computer. On a narrow bar it is the smaller of its two sizes, and with the owner's TEST MODE tag there too the hour's icon gives way: the title and the purse never do. Your own skull is a small mark on its corner. The slowly blinking red line along the band's top stays. Skull and number only: the minimap's name plate has the words. The "Attack <name>?" window comes up at the top, under the minimap, out of the thumbs' way (v2.3.3253). **The badge's explainer comes up by itself once, before you step in** (v2.3.3825): a judge died reading it among the first quest's slimes, because it is a window over a world that keeps running. So the first time a character nears the line, on the safe ground where nothing can hurt them, it opens by itself with a line over the rules ("Just ahead is No man's land. Read this here, on safe ground: out there, players can fight you."), once per character; after that the pill opens it as before. And while it is open, a hit closes it, so a player who opens it mid-fight is never left reading behind it (docs/specs/no-mans-land.md).
- Numbers and bars: damage numbers 1.75x bigger ("anywhere from 1.5-2x bigger", v2.3.3033); messages like "No mana!" the same size ("the same font size as the combat damage numbers", v2.3.3192), at a fixed size on screen ("Yes do fixed size instead"). Other players' health bars are as big as yours ("should be the same size as mine", v2.3.3267). The harvest bar is green, numbered and over the resource, "not the player head" (v2.3.3035), and under the rock for mining; your own plate and health bar hide while you gather (v2.3.3027).
- A level-up plays the owner's medallion and LEVEL UP banner in the middle of the screen, then holds. The level's number is in the medallion's cream centre, in the owner's gold numerals from the Points sheet ("on the level up badge the number just needs to be inside that big paper colored center space. You can use numbers from sprite sheets I've given you", v2.3.3693). The plate under the banner is one row: the skill's icon (your portrait for a character level) and whose level it is. Before that the number stood big under the banner ("put the number of the level more prominently just below the icon label of the level up", v2.3.3676), and the icon was in the centre. It stays about 1.9 s: the owner first halved it ("reduce the time the level up message stays on screen by 50%", 2.6 to 1.3 s, v2.3.3608), then asked for half again ("about 50% longer", v2.3.3676). Two at once share the screen side by side. Since v2.3.4055 a melee, bow or staff level-up's medallion sends 5-7 small glowing bubbles as it starts to fade, in staggered arcs to the Character button, or to its Points tab when the Character page is open (else the dashboard's fold handle), each landing with a small pop ("When a level up on one of the 3 primary combat all weapons happens do a 'jump to bag' effect that just shows bubbles in representing xp or something jump to the character button or the character button's points section if that menu already open"). CSS circles, no picture; a life skill's level-up sends none; `?pointsfly=0` turns it off for one tab (docs/specs/points-fly.md). Since v2.3.5004 the burst's art is drawn from 256-colour numbers on a small canvas as each frame comes up, in the same place, size, timing and layer (the owner saw the before and after and said yes); it stays the page's own overlay, over the HUD and over a building's window, which the game's renderer could not be (`?burstnumbers=0` is the old picture; docs/specs/pictures-256.md §7a).
- The JUMP button stands under the attack disc, centred on it (v2.3.3681, the owner: "I want a dedicated button for jumping"). On an upright phone since v2.3.3740 it stands LEFT of the disc instead, 18 px clear, and the disc a little lower and nearer the edge: "move the contextual button slightly down and to the right more. Move the jump button more to the left. The way the thumb rests on the screen it will result in less accidental taps between jump and the contextual button". Not along the bottom: that strip is the doors' Enter button's. TRAP and Shield Bash stack over it beside the disc, Block stays diagonal below and left (docs/specs/jumping.md). It is always there on a phone, the one always-on button the owner asked for by name; a tap on the stick still jumps too when it has nothing else to do. Since v2.3.3721 it is larger (60 px upright, 64 sideways; it was 48 and 54) and the attack disc above it smaller (84 and 94; it was 96 and 108): the owner, "the contextual button should shrink in size a bit and the jump button made larger". It stands half a button clear of Block beside it, a few px toward the screen's edge of the disc's centre, and wore a plain parchment arrow over a short gold ground line on the slate face Sprint and Block wear (since v2.3.3920 a figure mid-leap over its arc), lit warm in the air: "a more generic jump button icon on the button would be more appropriate because the icon now for jump looks like a special ability" (it wore the owner's round blue arrow on its own gold ring, v2.3.3526). The owner chose the arrow of four marks drawn ("I like option B for jump better"); the others, a chevron, a double chevron and a hop's arc, stay for a tab (`?jumpicon=`, docs/specs/jump-button/). A second press in the air is a flip, the roll's somersault (v2.3.3682, "press jump then press jump again to do a little flip"): timed well (coming down, just before landing) it carries you about twice as far and says "Perfect!"; sprinting into the jump makes it bigger.
- Since v2.3.3799 JUMP orbits the attack disc at its SOUTH-WEST, on the same ring Sprint stands on (10 px off the disc's edge): the owner, "It should be positioned orbiting southwest of the large contextual button." Upright it sits exactly south-west; sideways it turns a little further south, to stay 8 px clear of Shield Bash and the board above it. Its touch area is its circle, not its square, so its corner does not reach into the disc. Block stays the disc's diagonal bottom-left, beside Jump on its left (on a 320 px phone, where the bell leaves no room, it stands up beside Jump under TRAP instead). The doors' Enter button, whose strip Jump now stands in, rises above Jump while Jump is up, between the move stick and the disc (on a 320 px phone it draws small, without its picture), and goes back down when Jump is not drawn. No button's touch area overlaps another's at 320, 360, 375, 390 and 430 upright or 844 sideways (the `thumbbtn` phone scenario asserts it, with pictures). Computers have no Jump button and are unchanged.
- At a building's steps a small "Enter" over the sign's name appears, placed so it covers no other control (v2.3.3032). New players get one coach card at a time pointing at the control the questline just gave them; it never blocks and moved off the combat buttons ("Coach card move off the combat band", v2.3.2564). It never covers the pinned quest card either: sideways (v2.3.3481) and on a computer playing wide (v2.3.3782, the QA play-through of the judges' path: GEAR UP lay across "Into the Blue"'s body line) a card that would cross it steps right of it.
- Computer: WASD and the mouse, a keys strip in the black margin beside the game (v2.3.3526; in the lower left right of the bell when the window has no room beside it; a tap or H shrinks it to a chip), and each button wears its key letter ("just put the letter next to the icon or action in a tasteful way", v2.3.3407). Phones never show the letters.

- **Swapping weapons is taught once, by the big arrow, after the first quest** (v2.3.4130, the owner: "Weapon swap instruction from tutorial should only become relevant after receiving new weapons from turning in first quest. Instructions will need to change to accommodate the new pill design. I like the big arrows to teach where things are on screen."): the guide arrow's third lesson (after the weapon lesson, before the points). Once Mayor Bro's first quest is handed in (it pays the bow and the staff) and a second weapon is in your hand, the arrow points at the weapon pill with "Tap a weapon to switch" ("Click a weapon or press 1 2 3" on a computer), until your first switch or 10 s, never again. Not before the hand-in, and not while the new weapons are still in the bag (an empty well is nothing to switch to; the bag's glow leads you to equip them first). It replaced the gold callout of v2.3.3877 ("nothing said anything to me about swapping weapons"), which came on as soon as you held two weapons (docs/specs/guide-arrow.md).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The right stick became a plain button (v2.3.2242), then a stick and a button again (v2.3.2258).
- The shield was a hidden double-tap-and-hold gesture, then a button that dropped after one block (v2.3.2242), then held (v2.3.2248). Bow and staff lost the button for a gesture (v2.3.2446) and got it back (v2.3.2472).
- Arrow buttons for switching targets (v2.3.2243) went when targeting became automatic (v2.3.2251).
- The right stick's double tap swapped weapons (v2.3.2472) until the owner reverted it: "Weapon swapping goes back to the LEFT joystick only." (v2.3.2542).
- Special and Whirlwind went right (v2.3.2542), left (v2.3.2562), right again ("Spec and swirl need to be on the right joystick. It was put on the left.", v2.3.2574), then up above Sprint (v2.3.3106). Sprint began right of the left stick (v2.3.3006). Half a button apart until v2.3.3522 ("too far away").
- The Block button drew the 64 px shield sprite from the world, stretched (until v2.3.3522), then the owner's shield on its gold starburst, which read as an attack (until v2.3.3677).
- A JUMP button under the attack disc (v2.3.3017) became the right stick's tap ("I think this would work well instead of the smaller dedicated jump button", v2.3.3105). Its first full-strength picture was "way too intense", the mockup's faint arcs round the disc went ("remove the strange lines to the left and right of the button"), and the "Too far away!" message that ate taps near distant resources was removed (v2.3.3145). The button came back for everyone in v2.3.3681 ("I want a dedicated button for jumping"), in its old place under the disc; the tap still jumped beside it until v2.3.3712, when the tap became one attack and the button the only jump.
- The top bar showed the land and level band (v2.3.3009) before they moved under the minimap (v2.3.3108). The No man's land badge was on the top bar (v2.3.3058), then centred over the band (v2.3.3107), then left of centre (v2.3.3268, it covered the Block button), centred again stepping aside for the shield (v2.3.3608), twice the size (v2.3.3600), beside the minimap's name plate (v2.3.3629; sideways by the minimap's top, or in the top bar's row on a short screen), and since v2.3.3765 back on the top bar, right of "BroTown".

**Please don't suggest:**
- A taller dashboard band (one third of the screen is the owner's ceiling), or a sideways menu laid over the world.
- New always-on buttons on a phone. Buttons that appear and vanish with the situation are deliberate.
- Words or labels on the touch buttons (the owner's mockup has none), beyond the instruction words.
- The speech bubble back on the right stick, or its tap talking to whoever stands beside you (v2.3.4105: "Make talking to a npc player and entering a building always two different things").
- Bringing back the right-stick double tap (swap or shield), the target-switch arrows or the arcs round the disc. (The JUMP button was on this list until the owner asked for it back, v2.3.3681.)
- A burst, rays or sparks behind the Block button's shield: they made it read as an attack (v2.3.3677).
- Moving Special and Whirlwind to the left again, or moving Sprint (it stays put; fight buttons make room). Or spreading Special and Whirlwind apart again: the owner found half a button "too far away".
- Greying Whirlwind out of combat, a cooldown on the base attack, or fading the whole attack disc (it read as switched off).
- Zooming the view further out.
- Blur, frosted glass or drop shadows over the game view: they cause grainy static on iPhone Safari and the design system bans them.
- The No man's land chip back at the bottom, or the Attack? window back at the bottom where the thumbs are. ("Words on the badge" was on this list until the place chip, v2.3.3974: the owner adopted Safe / PvP / Outlaw in words.)

**Still open:**
- Many buttons are smaller than Apple's 44 px guide; none is out of reach (UI review, v2.3.3407).
- The coach's weapon-swap lesson still teaches the left stick's double tap, the owner's own wording, not the weapon button (v2.3.3005: a one-line change if wanted).
- Upright, the weapon pill's place made Jump turn west of the south-west the owner asked for (v2.3.3799, "orbiting southwest"): still on the disc's ring, down and to its left (v2.3.4130). If it reads wrong in the hand, the pill could stand higher instead.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/control-redesign.md, docs/specs/levelup-number.md, docs/specs/sprint.md, docs/specs/jumping.md, docs/specs/landscape-mode.md, docs/specs/no-mans-land.md, docs/specs/desktop-keys.md, docs/specs/reach-every-button.md, docs/specs/wheel-doors.md, docs/specs/quest-coach.md, docs/WORLD-MAP-PIPELINE.md, docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, docs/UI-BIBLE.md, docs/FUN-SOCIAL-PLAN.md, docs/TRAPS.md, src/ui/mobile/ZoneHeader.jsx, src/ui/mobile/BottomDashboard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dashMinBus.js, src/ui/mobile/WorldChatFeed.jsx, src/ui/panels/TouchControls.jsx, src/ui/panels/ShieldButton.jsx, src/ui/panels/SprintButton.jsx, src/ui/panels/SpecialButton.jsx, src/ui/panels/QuickEatButton.jsx, src/ui/panels/WeaponTriangle.jsx, src/ui/panels/KeyboardHintsPanel.jsx, src/game/quickEat.js

## Where you are and whether you're safe (the minimap's plate), and the player frame (gone)
Screens: hud-pvp, nml-popup, hud-outlaw

**What it is for:** Saying where you are and whether you are safe there, at a glance, and your own level, HP and energy without opening a page.

**Decisions, and why:**
- The owner, 2026-11-02: "The top bar is underwhelming (says 'brotown'). The red skull with the 1 is supposed to mean your character is in the wilderness and any character 1 level below you, equal to, or above you can attack you ... if you are the one to initiate an attack on another player you get a red skull above your head". He adopted the review's "replace the top bar with a location and safety HUD ... Safe Zone / Wilderness / Outlaw ... reserve the red skull for the actual aggressor state ... make the status tappable so players can view the exact death penalties" (v2.3.3974).
- **Since v2.3.4113 none of this is on the top bar** (the owner, 2026-11-03: "Remove name of town and "safe" or other designation from top bar (currently centered). The minimap label area already does this."). In the world the bar's centre is empty: the place's name and the safety chip below are gone from it on every layout. The minimap's name plate carries both: the land (or BroTown) and "safe" as before, and in No man's land "No man's land 12" in red (the number is the monster level there, so the level band it replaces is not lost), or "Outlaw · 18 min" in red while you carry the red skull. A tap on the plate then opens the rules window the chip opened; elsewhere the minimap opens the world map as before. What the centre still does: the XP bar takes it while you earn, TEST MODE shows there for the owner's test characters, and the owner's long press on it still opens the Test panel (the name stays in the page, drawn clear, as an invisible hold area). A land's banner docks into the plate (it has since v2.3.3108). Off the world (a dungeon, a room) there is no plate, so the zone's name is still drawn in the centre and its banner docks there. The paragraphs below are how it was from v2.3.3974.
- The bar names the land you stand in (as the minimap's plate does), and "BroTown" at home, the commons and the sea between; every phone test knows the live world by "BroTown" (TRAPS §244).
- Right of the name, a chip always up: green **Safe** (a shield) everywhere no player can attack you; amber **PvP · Lv 6–8** in No man's land, the character levels that can attack you there (the worker's rule: levels at most N apart, N the lower of the two No man's land numbers; from your side the widest is your own number, so the chip never promises a fight the worker refuses; a narrow phone drops "PvP"); red **Outlaw** with the skull while you carry the red skull (you attacked first). The skull means outlaw only now; the white skull (you were attacked) is a small mark on the chip's corner; the over-head skulls are unchanged.
- A tap opens a window that says it plainly with your own numbers first: "Players Lv 6–8 can attack you here. If a player beats you, you keep what you're wearing, but you drop your 2,306 gold and the 8 things in your bag. Put things in the Bank in BroTown to keep them safe." Then the rules, shorter. An outlaw reads the minutes left and that a player kill now takes everything, worn gear too.
- Still NOTHING said or played on the way into or out of No man's land (v2.3.3853, v2.3.3887: "just get rid of the no man's land gigantic warning message. I'd rather not have anything"): the risk words live only in the window you tap.
- **Since v2.3.4163 there is no player frame** (the owner, 2026-11-03: "No remove them from the game screen: they're already seeing it on the dashboard and whenever they enter combat or have any resource depletion it appears above their character head anyway"). The level badge and the HP and energy bars are gone from the top-left on every layout; nothing replaces them there. Your HP shows over your bro's head when it changes, energy and mana as their spend bars, and all of it on the dashboard; the Bro button opens the Bro page the frame's tap opened. The quest card and the status chips sit just under the top bar again, following it as it grows (`--bt-hud-top`). The paragraph below is how the frame was, v2.3.3974-v2.3.4093.
- The player frame, top-left under the bar: your level, an HP bar with its number and an energy bar, a thin mana bar too while a staff is in hand. The owner asked for it on 2026-11-02 ("Yes, always on: compact: your face, level, an HP bar and an energy bar at the top-left; the quest card moves just under it. Tap it to open your Bro page.") after the desktop playtest's "player status not showing". An earlier identity strip was cut as "way too busy" (v2.3.1848–1857) because it carried the name, XP and gold too; this one is kept to the level and the bars. **Since v2.3.4093 there is no picture in it** (the owner, 2026-11-03: "Remove the character pfp in the top left corner if you haven't already"): the face (64 px on a phone, 96 on a computer, v2.3.3974) is gone on every layout, the level is a round gold badge beside the bars, the frame is as tall as the bars need (40 px on a phone), and the whole frame still opens the Bro page. Your portrait still shows on the dashboard's Character button. The quest card and the status chips start under the frame (`--bt-hud-top`). The bar over your own head stays.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Health or energy back on the game screen's top left, or the gold in anything there (the owner took the player frame away, v2.3.4163; their later word, 2026-11-26, brought back the face, the name, the combat level and one weapon's XP bar as the character HUD, and no more: see "The character HUD (top left)", v2.3.6781).
- The gold or the achievement points back on the top bar (the owner moved them to the Gear tab and the Bro card, v2.3.4183).
- A banner, a chat line or a sound on crossing into No man's land.
- The place's name or a safety chip back in the top bar's centre (the owner moved them to the minimap's plate, v2.3.4113).

Sources: src/ui/mobile/NmlBadge.jsx, src/ui/mobile/placeName.js, src/game/placeSafety.js, src/ui/PlayerFrame.jsx, server/src/nomansland.js, docs/specs/no-mans-land.md

## The character HUD (top left)
Screens: charhud, charhud-side, charhud-desk, hudart, hudart-side

**What it is for:** Who you are and how you are progressing, always, whatever menu is open: the owner, 2026-11-26 (docs/design/UI_FEEDBACK_2026-11-26.md part 1): "I strongly agree with moving the character portrait into the top-left corner. It gives players a persistent sense of identity and progression, regardless of which menu they have open." "Portrait: Always visible and tappable. Name and combat level: Immediately adjacent to the portrait. XP bar: Persistent gold progression bar. Quest: Smaller, single-line tracker underneath." "Since combat level is determined by the highest weapon level, I would display the XP progress of whichever weapon is closest to increasing the overall combat level. Label the XP bar with that weapon so players understand the relationship." "Tapping the portrait should immediately open the character dashboard." And: "the upper HUD tells you who you are and how you're progressing, while the bottom dashboard lets you manage your character. Those two areas shouldn't unnecessarily repeat each other's information."

**Decisions, and why:**
- Top left under the top bar, opposite the minimap (v2.3.6781, card UI3 PR 2; src/ui/hud/CharacterHud.jsx, its rules src/game/charHud.js). The face is the portrait the dashboard already bakes (no second bake, no new picture in memory); beside it the name (13 px, one line) and "Lv N" in gold, the combat level; under them the weapon's small picture, its word and a gold bar with "45 / 120" in it. 184 x 52 upright and on a computer; sideways 176 x 48, the face drawn 40 in a 44 px tap box.
- Which weapon: the one whose next level raises the combat level soonest. With one level (the level is your best weapon's) only a weapon AT the top level can raise it, so it is the one of those with the largest share of its next level; a weapon below the top is never shown, however close it is to its own next level (a Sword at 90% of level 10 beside a Bow at 10% of level 12 shows the Bow). At the last level the bar is full and says "Max". Before the worker's record comes, the face and the name only, never a guessed "Lv 1".
- The weapon's XP moves in the HUD's bar now, so the top bar's passing XP slot is quiet while the HUD is up (it only ever showed weapon XP): never both. A gain on another weapon shows only its "+N XP" with its small picture over the bar's end; the bar stays on its weapon. A kill's "+N XP" flies to the HUD's number.
- The whole HUD is one button: a tap (a click on a computer, which never swings) opens Bro, the Menu's Bro page.
- **The corner holds the HUD and nothing else that is always there** (v2.3.6790, card UI3 PR 10; the owner, 2026-11-27: "Also move stuff out of the way. Maybe just keep quests in quest area for now. Leave only character hud in top left corner."). No quest tracker is drawn over the world: the tracked quest, its steps and its "ready" are on the Quests page, and a quest ready to hand in still lights the Quests door's dot and the Menu button's (they were there already, so no new mark was added). The world's quest marks, the minimap's, the gold road and the coach's lessons are unchanged. The rule is src/game/hudCorner.js (the coach keeps its cards off the HUD alone now).
- `?questline=1` (remembered on the device; `?questline=0` forgets) brings back the one line of v2.3.6781 under the HUD: "title · count · objective" (the owner's own "[Into the Wild · 3/5 · Defeat 5 monsters]"), never wrapping; between quests "Next: <quest> · Talk to <giver>" and "+n more"; its tap opens that quest.
- The things that show only at times take the line's place: the status chips (the snowflake, the flame, the buffs, the trap's button) right under the HUD, the same left edge, and the party chip under their two rows; sideways the party chip stands BESIDE the HUD, under the top bar, as before. With `?questline=1` they sit under the line as v2.3.6781 had them. Nothing crosses at 375 x 667 or 667 x 375 (on the test box). Where these sit is the owner's to change.
- `?hud=0` (remembered on the device; `?hud=1` forgets) gives back the old top left exactly: the full quest card with its fold, the top bar's XP, the chips where they were.
- **In the owner's pictures** (tag 6787, card UI3 PR 8; src/ui/hud/HudArt.jsx, its rules src/game/hudArt.js, its pieces public/ui/hud/ cut by tools/art/cut-hud.mjs from assets/icons-source/owner-2026-11-26-hud/sheet.png). The owner, 2026-11-26 (20:3x UTC), with one picture: "Use these art assets for a new compact hud in top left and weapon xp bar"; and (20:4x UTC): "The tiny pill on the right of weapon xp bar should have weapon level. Inside character badge goes combat lvl"; and, asked which weapon the bar follows (20:5x UTC): "Weapon in hand". So: your own face in the steel ring, the COMBAT level in the gold badge hanging under it, and to its right one steel bar: the weapon IN HAND in its small ring (the sheet's sword; the bow and the staff wear the game's own small pictures), its progress to its next level the green fill, its LEVEL in the pill at the bar's right end (100 at the cap, the fill full). Laid out as the sheet's top row (the bar centred on the ring's middle, the badge over the ring's bottom), the ring 48 px and everything to it, in UI3-hud's own box (184 x 52; sideways the same at 92 %, 169 x 48), so nothing under it moves; the sheet's bar would be 162 px at that size, it is drawn 134 (its middle stretches, its round ends never). The name stays above the bar (11 px) and the "45 / 120" count in it (10 px): neither is on the sheet, both kept so nothing is lost. Two buttons: the face opens Bro, the bar the weapon's page (Combat, then the weapon's; Back goes to Combat). The fill is green as on the sheet, where the panel's bar was gold (the owner's morning word, "Persistent gold progression bar": the picture is their later word). Before the worker's record the badge and the pill are empty, never "1". `?hudart=0` (remembered; `?hudart=1` forgets) gives back the drawn panel above, with its own rule for the weapon; `?hud=0` hides both.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- An identity strip with the name, XP and gold, cut as "way too busy" (v2.3.1848–1857).
- The player frame, asked for 2026-11-02 ("Yes, always on: compact: your face, level, an HP bar and an energy bar at the top-left", v2.3.3974).
- Its face out, 2026-11-03 ("Remove the character pfp in the top left corner if you haven't already", v2.3.4093).
- The frame out, 2026-11-03 ("No remove them from the game screen: they're already seeing it on the dashboard and whenever they enter combat or have any resource depletion it appears above their character head anyway", v2.3.4163).
- The reversal, 2026-11-26: the face, the name, the level and one weapon's XP bar back as this HUD, by the owner's later word (v2.3.6781). Health and energy stay off.
- The drawn panel (a square face, "Lv N" in words, a gold bar of the weapon closest to raising the combat level), dressed the same evening in the owner's pictures with the weapon in hand (tag 6787); the panel stays under `?hudart=0`.
- The quest line under the HUD (v2.3.6781, the owner's "Quest: Smaller, single-line tracker underneath"), out of the world a day later by their next word (v2.3.6790), kept behind `?questline=1`.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Health, energy or mana in the HUD (the owner's later word lists the face, the name, the level, the XP bar and the quest line, no more).
- A quest tracker back in the top left by default (the owner, 2026-11-27: "Leave only character hud in top left corner"; `?questline=1` is the way back for one device).
- The online player count on the HUD (it left in v2.3.1227; the owner suggests the Social menu or a small mark in the top bar, a later step's).
- A second XP bar for weapon XP in the top bar while the HUD is up.

Sources: docs/design/UI_FEEDBACK_2026-11-26.md, docs/plan/cards/UI3-hud.md (on claude/plan-orc), docs/plan/cards/UI3-hudart.md, src/ui/hud/HudArt.jsx, src/ui/hud/hudArt.css, src/game/hudArt.js, tools/art/cut-hud.mjs, tools/guards/hud-art.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-ui3hudart.mjs, src/ui/hud/CharacterHud.jsx, src/ui/hud/charHud.css, src/ui/hud/hudColumn.js, src/game/charHud.js, src/ui/QuestCard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/ZoneHeader.jsx, src/ui/panels/PartyHUD.jsx, src/ui/mobile/QuestCoach.jsx, src/styles/game.css, tools/guards/char-hud.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-charhud.mjs

## The HUD and the Attack button, bigger (`?bighud=0` today's)
Screens: bighud, bighud-side

**What it is for:** The owner, about 07:05 UTC 2026-11-28, after playing the new menus on their iPhone: *"Attack button and HUD need to be about twice as big."* With the menu design of 2026-11-27 they had also set the frame: *"Do not compromise readability, available screen space, gameplay visibility or device fairness to accommodate a design. Flag unresolved requirements rather than inventing solutions."* So both grow TOWARDS twice, as far as the screen holds them, never over a neighbour, and what gave way is said here and in the PR (tag 6847, card UI4; the rule src/game/bigHud.js, its suite tools/guards/big-hud.test.mjs, the phone test tools/qa/mp/mp-bighud.mjs).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The HUD** is the top-left character HUD in the owner's pictures (above: the face ring, the badge, the name, the weapon's bar), the only thing that corner holds. The whole group is drawn at one scale (its `--ha-k`), so its words, its numbers and its bar all grow together: up to twice today's, as wide as the room left of the minimap allows (the minimap is the world's top right, 132 px a side on a phone, with 8 px of air), and sideways as tall as the room above the controller's stick lobe allows. Never under today's. The status chips and the party chip move with it (under it; sideways the party chip beside it).
- **Attack** is the controller's Attack (its well in the right lobe, padSkin.js): it grows from its well's centre as far as it stays clear of the big left button's box (Jump, or Block in a fight), the top row's pictures (Mobility, the middle well, Special; a box there rises off it as the controller already lifts them off the big buttons, never past its picture's foot), the column's right edge and the band's top. Its touch box is its face, so the place a thumb can press grows with it; how a press is read is unchanged. No other control moves.
- **What fits** (measured on the test box, the real game in BroTown): upright at 390 x 844 the HUD 241 x 68 (1.31 times today's 184 x 52, the name 11 to 14.4 px) and Attack 71 px (1.20 times today's 59); at 375 x 667 the HUD 226 x 64 (1.23) and Attack 68 (1.19); at 320 x 568 the HUD stays today's (no room: today's already reaches 4 px over the minimap) and Attack 60 (1.22); sideways at 844 x 390 the HUD 339 x 96 (twice today's sideways 169 x 48, the name 10.1 to 20.2 px; its foot at 155, the controller's top at 211) and Attack 78 (1.20). Each touch box is its face.
- **A computer keeps today's sizes**: the owner played a phone, a computer has no controller (a mouse) and its HUD has the whole wide window.
- `?bighud=0` (remembered on the device; `?bighud=1` forgets) gives today's sizes exactly.

**Still open (the owner's call):**
- **Twice does not fit upright, either one.** The HUD at twice is 368 x 104, wider than the 242 px left of the minimap at 390 wide (the minimap from 258, 8 px of air); the ways to twice would be the minimap smaller, the HUD on two lines, or the bar shorter than the rest of it. Attack at twice is 119 px in a right lobe 119 px tall, between Jump/Block (72 px from its centre) and the top row (50 px above it); the way to twice would be the right lobe drawn taller with the top row lifted about 47 px and Jump/Block about 30 px further left: the controller's redesign.
- At 320 wide the HUD today already reaches 4 px over the minimap's left edge (184 + 8 = 192 against 188); kept as it was.
- "HUD" was read as the top-left character HUD. Other readings: the weapon wells on the controller's rail, the top bar (the logo, the gold, the spin). Not grown.
- With `?padskin=0` (the older buttons) or `?hudart=0` (the drawn panel) nothing grows.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Covering the minimap, the top bar or another control to reach twice (the owner's frame of 2026-11-27).
- Changing the band's heights, the camera or the world's scale for this.

Sources: src/game/bigHud.js, src/ui/panels/padSpot.js, src/ui/hud/HudArt.jsx, src/ui/hud/hudArt.css, src/game/renderRes.js, tools/guards/big-hud.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-bighud.mjs

## Townsfolk, quest badges and the keepers
Screens: near-mayor

**What it is for:** The six characters standing in the Wheel's BroTown give quests, run shops and games, and make the town feel lived in. A mark over each head says at a glance whether he has something for you.

**Decisions, and why:**
- Who stands where: Mayor Bro beside the Town Hall's steps, near where you arrive; Lil Bro on the square on the other side of the arrival, so a new player meets him; Diego west of the General Store's steps; Ace west of the Gambling Den's; Blacksmith Bro east of the forge's; Beastmaster Bro east of the Woodworker's, where the box traps his quests ask for are made (v2.3.3032, v2.3.3067, v2.3.3121). Ace came over from the old town because his coin flip opens only from him, so while he stood there nobody could play it.
- They walk around and work (v2.3.4058: "Make NPCs walk around and do stuff now since it doesn't make much memory anymore"; docs/specs/living-town.md). Since v2.3.3809 they are drawn live from their looks, as players are, so a stroll is the jog and a job is one of the player's own work poses, from body sheets every phone already holds. Keepers and locals walk a short loop by their door and pause, some to work: the blacksmith's hammer (the mining swing), Gavel Greg holding up a lot, Sterling's coins, Glint's gem, Benny's luggage (the pickup), Old Man Reel fishing by the river, and Chef Blaze cooking with his pan. Each works with his own tool (v2.3.5460, the owner: "chef blaze should cook, not hammer like an anvil"): the mining swing holds a pickaxe, so only the blacksmith swings it; the cook, the woodworker and the farmer work in the player's own skill figures, from the look palette's colour numbers, nothing baked (v2.3.5462; docs/specs/townsfolk-work.md). While working a tee shows its colour without its print (a printed tee in a work pose would be a picture of its own, more memory). At most six walk near the screen at once. Every phone sees the same: where each is comes from the clock and his name.
- A quest giver still stays findable ("a quest giver who wanders is one you have to hunt for", the rule before): he keeps his spot and only shifts a step or two round it (the blacksmith working there, the woodworker chopping, Farmer Bro kneeling, hoeing and watering: docs/specs/townsfolk-work.md), and he stops and turns to face you when you come within about 200 px. With a window of yours open, anyone near stops and faces you. A tap on a walking townsperson talks to him as before; the map pins and the quest star follow him.
- On a computer they stand still and face the street, as before: a computer draws their photographs (the look palette is not built on Windows or a Mac), and a photograph cannot step or work. The Test panel's "Townsfolk drawn live" off does the same on every phone; `?folkwalk=0` on one.
- One mark over a head, never two (v2.3.3047, v2.3.3279, v2.3.3336), never as emoji. Since v2.3.3481 the quest marks are the owner's own pins from their icon sheets (their pick 4 of the review: "Icon picks: 1b, 2a, 3, 4, ..."), a little taller than the old discs (32 x 38 world px), the drawn discs kept only as a fallback:
  - **gold "!" pin, bobbing:** he has a quest for you;
  - **grey "?" pin, still:** you took his quest and he is waiting. The owner's pin baked grey (a neutral grey, its own picture, never a filter): a waiting quest wears a grey "?" so it never looks ready, as the review showed it. It was a teal-grey disc from v2.3.3047, which on grass read as sage green, then yellow ("have the question mark above mayor bros head be yellow instead of green. Green implies something is done", v2.3.3336). It holds still because there is nothing to come for yet;
  - **gold ring with a green check, bobbing:** you have everything, hand it in ("a green checkmark above his head (not emoji)", v2.3.3047);
  - **the owner's talk bubble:** someone to talk to with no quest for you (v2.3.3279, from the owner's own small-icon sheets; it replaced an 8 px gold star). It stays where the old disc sat.
  - The small map in today's town, the farm and the dungeons marks a waiting quest in the same grey, so the map and the world agree.
  - A giver whose whole chain is handed in wears no quest badge. The old green done-badge was retired because green now means "hand it in".
- Every townsperson wears the same name plate as a player, below the feet, with a gold role line where a player's level goes: Mayor, Kid, Gambler, Shopkeeper, Blacksmith, Beastmaster ("Make every persons name or title as a consistent name plate", v2.3.2071; "Also call him the gambler", v2.3.2617). The plate is a step smaller than the monsters' so the town does not shout over the field (v2.3.2513). No health bar: on a townsperson it would read as an invitation to fight him.
- **The townsfolk go by nicknames** (v2.3.4048). The owner: "Instead of names like 'gavel Greg' it would be more humorous to just have them go by nicknames. I didn't mean to remake all the NPCs to be like gavel Greg. The way their appearance looks now is fine." So the eight with a job-and-first-name name read as a nickname everywhere a player sees one (the plate, a dialogue's title, a map pin, a quest's "Talk to ...", the computer's "E Talk to ..."), the way Ace, Slim, Glint, Bean and Sterling always have: Going-Going (Gavel Greg), Fish Tales (Old Man Reel), Selfie (Tourist Terry), Badge (Sheriff Buck), Tryhard (Guildmaster Gary), Swampy (Deed Dave), Snail Mail (Postmaster Pete), Sizzle (Chef Blaze). Their looks did not change. The old names stay the keys in the code; the map is `src/data/folkNames.js`, one line a nickname.
- **...and the old names came back** (v2.3.4088). The owner, 2026-11-03: "Revert the nicknames and go back to the other names, I liked them better other way." Every townsperson shows and says his own name again (Gavel Greg, Old Man Reel, Tourist Terry, Sheriff Buck, Guildmaster Gary, Deed Dave, Postmaster Pete, Chef Blaze); `FOLK_NICKNAMES` is empty. Do not bring nicknames back unless the owner asks.
- Walking up to a quest giver (within 90 px) opens his dialogue by itself ("Created a new character and first quest didn't trigger by walking up to mayor bro. It should activate by proximity.", v2.3.1886); walking up to Diego opens his shop (v2.3.2050). A tap on him, E on a computer or a tap on the right stick (which then shows a speech bubble) opens it too. Since v2.3.4105 only the tap on him (a click on a computer) talks (see "Talking to characters").
- Ace opens only on a tap ("you have to tap on the joker to open up his dialog window", v2.3.2620): his window is a bet, and walking past a man who takes your gold should not put the stake screen in front of you.
- Beastmaster Bro opens by himself only to offer or pay a quest. His "how it's going" line waits for a tap, because his quests send you past him to the Woodworker's door again and again (v2.3.3121). Since v2.3.3525 Blacksmith Bro and Diego do the same: the tour gives them quests, and their doors are right behind them.
- Woodworker Bro (west of the Woodworker's steps) and Farmer Bro (by the Feed & Seed) joined in v2.3.3525 for the tour, the townsfolk's quests that open after the Mayor's first. Each keeps his building: once his quests are done, talking to him opens it.
- The keepers (v2.3.3258): instead of walking into buildings, you talk to the building's person ("Yes I like your idea of interacting with the building's NPC instead of having the building interiors"). Blacksmith Bro opens the forge; Mayor Bro opened the Town Hall (leaderboard and world map) once he had no quest for you, until v2.3.3899. A keeper's quest always comes first. Every door's Enter button still works.
- **Mayor Bro always talks (v2.3.3899).** The owner: "After finishing mayor bro quest that awards sword and shield it wasn't apparent what to do next. I tried tapping on mayor bro and kept opening the town hall door which was confusing ... apparently I couldn't talk to him." He keeps no building now. With a quest to offer or hand in, as before; with nothing of his own, he says the next step in his speech bubble ("Good work! Next, see Blacksmith Bro at the forge. Follow the gold road.", from the tour's next step, where the gold road and the star already point), or the quest you have, or a friendly word pointing at the town board. Never "has nothing for you" (game/mayorTalk.js).
- A tap on a townsperson with nothing for you floats the game's line "… has nothing for you right now" over him, rather than doing nothing.
- They react to emotes near them (v2.3.3394, on your phone only): Lil Bro copies the nearest player's move, Blacksmith Bro flexes back, Diego objects to dancing by his door (in Halloween week, "No zombies in my store!"), Ace calls a dance-off, Mayor Bro cheers a crowd. At most one such line every 20 s each, so the town stays fun alone (the fun & social plan's "fun alone, better together").

**Tried before, and changed:**
- Waiting on a quest wore no badge, and a ready quest wore a "?" (before v2.3.3047). Then waiting was a teal grey (v2.3.3047), then yellow (v2.3.3336), and now the owner's "?" pin in a neutral grey (v2.3.3481).
- The quest marks were discs drawn in code (gold "!", the "?", a green disc with a drawn check) until the owner's pins (v2.3.3481).
- Townsfolk without a quest wore an 8 px gold star (until v2.3.3279).
- Only two townsfolk had name plates; the others kept a floating name over the head (until v2.3.2071).
- The dialogue opened only within 56 px, so walking up to Mayor Bro often did nothing (until v2.3.1886).
- Ace had a walk-up opener like Diego's (v2.3.2618) until the owner made him tap-only (v2.3.2620).
- Rooms you would walk into were offered; the owner chose keepers and a picture of the room behind each menu (v2.3.3258).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Making Ace open when you walk up, "for consistency" with Diego. The owner rejected exactly that.
- Green for the waiting "?" (green means done), or emoji for the quest badges.
- A health bar on townsfolk, or names floating over heads instead of the plate below the feet.
- Walk-in building interiors (the owner chose the keepers and the room pictures).
- Two marks over one head (a quest badge and the talk bubble).

**Still open:**
- Nine buildings have no keeper outside yet (Bank, Saloon, the Gem Works' two, Cookhouse, Land Office, Post Office, Sheriff's Office, Guild Hall, Auction House). Each needs a picture of the person; the prompts are ready. Once a building has its keeper, its door's Enter button could go, if the owner wants.
- Beastmaster Bro's, Woodworker Bro's and Farmer Bro's pictures are stand-ins made by a script until the owner's own.
- Whether the Auction House's clerk (already drawn, inside its room) should also stand outside is the owner's call.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/quest-guidance.md, docs/specs/small-icons.md, docs/specs/owner-icons.md, docs/specs/building-rooms.md, docs/specs/wheel-doors.md, docs/specs/emote-moves.md, docs/specs/living-town.md, docs/FUN-SOCIAL-PLAN.md, src/data/wheelBuildingDoors.js, src/data/gameDisplay.js, src/data/folkNames.js, src/data/ownerIcons.js, src/rendering/systems/entityRenderer.js, src/game/townsfolkReact.js, src/game/desktopControls.js, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/game/townsfolkWalk.js, src/game/townsfolkDay.js, src/rendering/folkPose.js

## Talking to characters and taking quests
Screens: mayor-dialogue, quest-offer, quest-accepted, guide-arrow

**What it is for:** How a character talks to you and hands out work: first he speaks, a line at a time; then a separate window lets you decide; then the game shows you took it and where to go.

**Decisions, and why:**
- Two screens, one job each (v2.3.1820): "I'd rather have an NPC message window that has a larger picture of him on the left side of the window and just the text of what he's saying in sequential order chunks. Then a new 'Accept quest' window pops up with the items shown that will be handed over on accepting it". The old single card mixed reading with deciding, and on a phone the deciding half was often below the fold.
- The dialogue shows his head, not his whole body ("I wanted just the head of Mayor bro in his profile pic while talking for quest dialog.", v2.3.1828). His lines come in the chunks they were written in, with dots for how many are left. The button says Next; on his last line it says See the quest (an offer), Claim reward (a hand-in) or Close. A tap anywhere on the card also goes on. The text box keeps a steady height so the button does not jump under your finger between lines, and the button keeps to the right on a one-line answer with no dots too (v2.3.3481: its Close fell to the left).
- The portrait never moves (v2.3.3915; owner: "Fix all the portrait pics from slightly moving around from one NPC message to the next. Right now they're inconsistent"). Its frame is a square sized from the card's width alone, the same for every townsperson and every message, and the whole head shows in it, standing on its floor. The card's top is pinned a little above the screen's middle, and a long message grows it downward. Before, the frame stretched to the card's height, and the card, centred, grew with each message's words, so every message cropped, zoomed and moved the head afresh (`mp-portraitframe` measures it).
- Walking away and coming back starts the conversation over, since you may not remember what he said. The cards block iPhone's press-and-hold copy menu ("remove the long press option on the quest dialog close button.", v2.3.2311).
- The quest offer shows the title, the errand, and two captioned groups: "He hands you now" (the owner's own phrase, v2.3.1704) and "For finishing “<quest name>”". Captions say WHEN you get each thing because the owner found it "a little confusing what's rewards for the next quests vs what's rewarded for the current quest". Both groups show on the offer because what you get for finishing is half the decision (v2.3.1827).
- Accept Quest is the big gold bar; Not now and a tap outside the window decline, since declining an offer costs nothing.
- **Accepting closes the window (v2.3.4166).** The owner: "after accepting the quest mayor bro says again to kill the slimes (this message is unnecessary they already know they just accepted his quest)." QUEST ACCEPTED! says it, over the world; his "I still need ..." line is his answer only when you talk to him again. A giver with something to do at once for the quest keeps the window, with only that button and no words above it: Lil Bro's Race!, "Open the forge" and the other building buttons (`questAction` in `src/game/tour.js`). Closing would only cost a tap to open it again. (Since v2.3.1828 the card had turned to the quest's in-progress line on Accept.)
- The claim window has no Not now, and a tap outside does nothing: the reward is already earned, and a stray tap under the button used to throw the screen away (v2.3.2289). A small ✕ in the corner closes it, and the reward stays waiting for you.
- The quest windows wear the owner's painted art (v2.3.3030: "Add these for the new quest windows."): a navy panel in a gold frame with a crest, the gold button (grey until it can be pressed), painted item slots, green banners. This is Lantern Slate's seventh licensed exception, for the quest windows and banners only.
- A claim says what you got ("Rewards claimed!"), the coins fly to the purse and the items to the bag, then he offers his next quest after 1.9 s or a tap. The button says Claim Rewards, not Turn In, because "it needs to be more obvious that you're redeeming a reward" (v2.3.1764).
- Choosing where XP goes (Melee, Bow or Magic) is shown as part of the prize, on a green panel with the owner's weapon chips ("should feel more obviously like a reward", v2.3.1793). The first quest pays no XP and asks nothing: "Makes no sense to have to choose xp in something you've never used yet. Just remove that little xp bonus." (v2.3.3335).
- After Accept: the QUEST ACCEPTED! banner on top, above everything; the quest pinned at the top left with its title, picture and next step; a gold road on the minimap to where it leads (v2.3.2992: "just rely on the gold road on the minimap of where to go"; nothing is drawn on the ground). For the first quest the dashboard's OPEN, then the sword and shield, flash until worn ("After accepting first quest make "OPEN" on dashboard flash. Then make sword and shield both flash.", v2.3.3049).
- **The big arrow, the first time (v2.3.3925).** The owner: "The first time you get handed a weapon put an arrow pointing to either the bag dashboard button or the weapon if the dashboard is already open. The flashing light isn't obvious enough. Also the first time you level up and have enough points to allocate ... that same big arrow." A gold arrow with a dark edge, about 56 px, bobbing toward its target, with a short line beside it ("Tap to open", "Tap Gear", "Tap to equip"; "Click" on a computer). For the sword and shield it points where the glow is (the fold handle, the Gear button, the weapon's tile) and follows as that changes, until the weapon is worn or a minute passes. At the first points it points at Character ("Tap Bro: points to spend"), then the Points tab, until a point is spent (or three minutes of showing, so a player keeping their points is not followed for ever). Once per character; `?guidearrow=0` turns it off for one tab. It takes no touches and never moves its target (ui/guideArrow.js). It is never drawn over an open card (v2.3.4166, the owner: "Remove the arrow in the first screenshot", where it stood on the Mayor's NEW QUEST card): when its target is covered by a dialogue, an offer or any window, it waits until that closes.
- The pinned card: tap to fold it to its title. Text sizes were raised when the owner found it "a bit too small to be legible" (v2.3.1711). It shows the quest's next step for quests with steps ("a lot of people get stuck on the quest for cooking 2 fish", v2.3.2820), and turns to the painted green check when everything is in hand ("Make the quest notification turn to a green checkmark if you have everything you need to complete the quest.", v2.3.3048).
- A brand-new player gets one welcome line (since v2.3.4106 "Your adventure starts here.", the first of the five first steps; it said "Find Mayor Bro — he wants a word. Follow the gold road on your map.") with a Skip tutorial button: "add just a 'skip tutorial' button on the very first dialog box when you join the game. No pop ups should be scheduled after that." (v2.3.2890). Onboarding speaks one voice at a time, because "The tutorial onboarding is too heavy on window pop ups right after you join the game" (v2.3.2766).
- On a computer, Enter presses Next, Continue and Accept Quest, and Esc is Not now or the ✕; each button wears its key (v2.3.3407).
- **Talking and entering are always two different things (v2.3.4105).** The owner: "Make talking to a npc player and entering a building always two different things." You talk to a townsperson ONLY by tapping him (a phone) or clicking him (a computer), from up to 300 px. E, the right stick's tap and a door's button never talk: they are the door's (and the bed's, a chest's, a harvest's). E beside someone with nothing else to do floats "Click <name> to talk" and opens nothing. The keys list says "Click Talk" and "E Use" (it said "E Interact", which is what sent the owner to E beside Mayor Bro). docs/specs/tap-to-talk.md.
- THE TOUR (v2.3.3525): the Mayor's slimes are the only quest a new player is offered; once they are in, six townsfolk offer theirs AT ONCE, each wearing the gold "!" over his head, on the minimap and on the world map ("Maybe mayor bro is the only quest available for the first quest then after completing that other NPCs begin having quest icons appear above their head", then "Make the quest icons appear on the minimap and map too"). Each giver's second quest follows his first; the Mayor's snowmen wait for the whole town. Every line says why he wants it ("Keep the dialog witty and funny (it's an unserious game bro)") and works in any order. With nothing in hand the gold road still suggests ONE giver, in the tour's order, and the pinned card says "Next: <quest>", "Talk to <him> <where>" and "+5 more: look for a gold ! on your map". A giver with nothing more to offer puts his window away after the claim.
- PLAIN WORDS (v2.3.4025): the owner, "Just make the characters (all of them all dialog) a simple and easy to understand personality and directions. No cryptic jokes or anything that could confuse someone." This replaces the "witty and funny" ask above. Each townsperson says who he is in one friendly sentence, then the job: what, how many, where, and who to bring it to. The eight with nicknames (Sizzle, Snail Mail, Swampy, Tryhard, Badge, Going-Going, Fish Tales, Selfie) say them. docs/specs/plain-words.md.
- A step done at a giver's own building puts a button on his "how's it going" line that opens it (Open the forge, Open the bench, Open the Feed & Seed), the brass button beside a quiet Close; Lil Bro's says Race!. The same window and the same counted visit as walking to the door.
- Woodworker Bro's swap (a pine bow or staff remade in softwood) asks which one on the claim window when you hold both, the way the XP choice is asked, and the claim waits until one is picked.
- Lil Bro's race: a big 3, 2, 1, GO! in the middle of the play area that takes no taps (you are held on the line through it), then a slim pill at the top with the flags touched, your clock and his, then a small result card in the same place with both times, a hint on a loss and Race again; OK (Esc on a computer) or a few seconds closes it. At the top, under the minimap, where the "Attack <name>?" card goes: out of the thumbs' way. The flags stand in the world, the next one gold, touched ones green, the finish chequered, and the gold road leads to the next.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- One scrolling card with a 40 px head, all his lines, the objective, both reward groups and Accept (until v2.3.1820).
- The whole figure as his picture (v2.3.1820), then the head (v2.3.1828).
- Captions "He gives you" / "You receive", which did not say when (until v2.3.1704).
- "Turn In Quest" (until v2.3.1764). An XP chooser on the first quest (until v2.3.3335).
- The first quests were all the Mayor's, four lands of monsters to kill for their remains, with the snowmen first (until v2.3.3525, the tour). "Remnants" became "Remains" everywhere a player reads it (v2.3.3525).
- The tour's first cut was one line handed from giver to giver, only the next step ever offered ("Mayor bro would hand off the quest to the next person"); the owner then asked for the whole town to open after the Mayor's first quest (v2.3.3525). The road's one suggestion is what is left of the hand-off.
- A path drawn on the ground (beads from v2.3.2121; then arrows, a ribbon or footprints, chosen in Settings) until the owner chose the minimap's gold road alone (v2.3.2992). It is kept behind `?questpath`.
- The quest banners drew under the dialogue's dark backdrop until they were moved on top (v2.3.3030).
- A talking-head video greeting from the Mayor, removed on the owner's instruction ("remove the tutorial and the mayor bro pop up and greeting", v2.3.1593).
- One control that both talked and entered, by who stood where: E asked a townsperson with a quest before a door (v2.3.3583), then the door wherever its prompt was up (v2.3.3834, the judge pass: "E ENTER TOWN HALL" talked to the Mayor), then anyone in talk range before a door, the computer's button swapped for "E Talk to <name>" (v2.3.3868: "When you're next to mayor bro ... you would actually open the door"); the right stick asked for the character first (v2.3.3105). Each order surprised someone; all went in v2.3.4105.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Merging the dialogue and the offer back into one window.
- A Not now on the claim window, or a backdrop tap that closes it.
- A "choose one reward" pick on quest one: it gives the bow and the staff both, by the owner's rule that all three combat styles land on quest one.
- Drawing the quest road on the ground again, or bringing back the Mayor's video greeting.
- Spreading the quest windows' painted frame to the dialogue box or other windows, or flattening the quest windows into the plain slate style. Both are bounded owner decisions.
- Rewording "a quick swipe" in the tutorial lines as "flick": the owner reported "flick" as wrong (v2.3.1681).
- E, the right stick or a door's button talking to someone again, in any order ("the character first", "the door first", "the nearer of the two"): v2.3.4105's rule is that only a tap or click on the person talks.

**Still open:**
- On a computer the dialogue and the quest offer are drawn over the whole window, a little wider than the game's column (UI review, v2.3.3407).
- Quest rewards show no rarity tag yet: every quest reward today is a plain item, and a "Common" label used only by these windows was avoided (v2.3.3030).

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/quest-windows.md, docs/specs/quest-guidance.md, docs/specs/quest-path-guide.md, docs/specs/quest-coach.md, docs/specs/onboarding-pace.md, docs/specs/desktop-keys.md, docs/specs/reach-every-button.md, docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, src/ui/panels/NpcDialogue.jsx, src/ui/panels/QuestOfferPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/QuestPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/QuestBannerLayer.jsx, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/ui/mobile/QuestStepNudge.jsx, src/ui/onboardingPace.js, src/game/welcomeBanner.js, src/styles/game.css

## The minimap and the world map
Screens: world-map, world-map-pin

**What it is for:** Finding your way round the Wheel, a big world of eight lands around BroTown. The minimap in the top-right corner says where you are and which way to go; a tap on it opens the full world map, which shows more the further you zoom in.

**Decisions, and why:**
- The Wheel's minimap was made bigger and richer on purpose: "I'm thinking the minimap will need to be larger and the most informative and intuitive it can be for navigation purposes. Maybe tapping it brings up an overlay of a labelled world map. It would probably help to have areas labelled so players can start memorizing the territory." (v2.3.2966). On a phone it is a 132 px box showing about three zones across, centred on you.
- **On a computer it is twice as big** (v2.3.3986, the owner playtesting on a computer: "Map system is confusing... make the mini map 100% + bigger/ double the size... and change the look of the mini map"): 264 px, showing 1.5 times the world (4,800 game px across against a phone's 3,200), every mark in it twice as big. A phone keeps 132 px: 264 would be 68% of an upright phone's width. The size is in one place (`src/ui/wheelMiniSize.js`), and what keeps clear of the box asks it.
- **A slim, rounded frame** (v2.3.3986; the owner: "Questioning the minimap border shape, placement, size, and minimap quality as well", and from the review they took "a thin charcoal or muted gold border, gentle corner rounding, and no attached oversized location plaque"): a rounded square (corners of 12), a 3 px frame of a dark keyline, charcoal and a thin muted-gold line inside, over a soft shadow, so it still reads as not the game area. It stays in the top-right corner, and the box is opaque. The lands are softened toward the world's ground (a light wash and a gentle shade at the window's edge), so the flat colours read calmer. This reverses the earlier "thick 7 px slate-and-brass frame ... so it's not confused with game screen area" (v2.3.3009): the owner's new words win.
- The name plate under the map, inside the box and slimmer since v2.3.3986 (the map's window 98 px of the 132, the plate 29): the land's element icon and name in the land's colour, over the level band in gold ("Lv 6–10"), or "safe" at home. The top bar is to say the place and its safety (a later change); the plate goes once it does. The owner asked to "move the zone name and level band beneath the minimap but I want to reduce the size of the minimap to make room for it (rather than enlargen it further)"; offered a shorter rectangle, a smaller square or a round map with a banner, they chose the shorter rectangle (v2.3.3108). The stage's name ("the thaw line") did not fit at the 11 px text floor, so it stays on the world map.
- Each land is painted one flat colour on both maps, with its element icon on the plate and a banner as you cross into it: "There might need to be flat colors on the minimap to help orient you to what elemental zone you're in" (v2.3.3024).
- The banner as you cross into a land is the owner's own animated banner again (v2.3.3609: "I also don't like the intro graphics to each zone. The older ones were better."): for Frost Ridge, the Flame Fields, the Wind Dunes and the Verdant Wilds their ornaments build either side of the game's name plate in nine beats, the element icon and the land's name in its colour on the plate, and the plate flies into the minimap's name plate; the other four lands, which have no ornaments of the owner's, play the plate alone. Nothing comes up under the minimap any more. The four strips loaded behind the world's loading screen and went when you left it (about 15.7 MB while you were in the world). Since v2.3.5003 (the owner: "Yes do it", docs/specs/pictures-256.md §7) a land's strip is held only while you are in that land or near enough to walk into it: none in BroTown, one out in a land, two at most near the commons' edge; it loads seconds of walking before its land, so the banner is the same on screen. A strip somehow still on its way when you cross is waited for up to 2 seconds, then the land plays the plate alone, its icon and name, as the four lands without art do: never a broken picture. `?banners=all` holds all four, as before.
- The element icons are the element itself (v2.3.3609: "Some zones (like wind) have a strange rune representing the element. It should just be a pure icon of the element (like wind lines or something)."): the wind is three gusts of wind, the storm a lightning bolt, the stone a boulder, drawn in code in the style of the other seven (tools/ui/draw-element-icons.py). They were a spiral rune on a grey triangle, a purple star and a cracked floor tile.
- **Monsters are small red dots, grouped** (v2.3.3986, from the review the owner took: "smaller enemy markers, potentially grouped at low zoom, to eliminate the current clusters of oversized red skulls", and danger marks "visually distinct from the skull used for outlaw status"): one 5 px dot with a dark edge for each 10 px cell of the box that holds monsters, 7 px for three or more; a boss is a small horned skull, never the plain skull No man's land's outlaws wear. They were a 12 px red skull each (v2.3.1810, "Monsters should be skulls or something indicating danger"), so a pack was a pile of skulls. The dungeons' and the farm's small box keep the skull.
- No element icon has a shape behind it (v2.3.3907: "None of the elements should have shapes behind them. Just the element itself."): the flame is a single flame (it sat in a gold-framed red diamond), frost a six-armed snowflake (on a blue hexagon), water one curling wave (on a blue disc), dark a swirl of purple-black smoke (a three-lobed blob), light a golden burst of rays (an 8-point star badge), flora a sprig of leaves (a vine drawn on a big leaf). All ten are the owner's own hand-made set, one sheet from the prompt in ART-WISHLIST (kept in `assets/icons-source/owner-2026-11-02/`), cut by `tools/ui/cut-element-sheet.mjs` to 256 x 256 on 256 colours; the drawings in code (`draw-element-icons.py`) stay as the fallback.
- Marks on the minimap: you (a chevron the way you face), other bros (green), monsters (red dots, above), camps, passes, gates and landmarks, roads and the river as clean lines, and resources as small glyphs tinted by tier ("Show nodes on minimap", v2.3.3012). Townsfolk get no mark of their own in the Wheel, only their QUEST PIN when they have one: the same gold "!", grey "?" or green check as over their head, in the owner's pictures ("Make the quest icons appear on the minimap and map too", v2.3.3525). One just off the box shows smaller on its edge, toward him, as a party member does: from where you arrive the box holds about half the town. On the world map too, at every zoom, bigger and named with the buildings, and "has a quest" in its key.
- The quest's way: a gold road from you to a star where the quest leads, held at the box's edge when the spot is off it, so the box always says which way (v2.3.2990, v2.3.2992: "just rely on the gold road on the minimap of where to go").
- A NEW star is shown (v2.3.3872, the owner: "they miss the star on the minimap pointing them where to go. Maybe you can make a giant star appear above the minimap and shrink down to just catch attention each time there's a new star?"): a gold star about four times the minimap's comes up over the box and shrinks onto it in a second, once each new target, never again for the same one (docs/specs/star-cue.md).
- The star follows the quest you TRACK (v2.3.3522: "I tapped track quest in quest menu but no golden stars were in minimap (I had two active quests - one from beast master and the other from mayor bro)"), and every quest leads somewhere: its land, its resource, a building's door (the Woodworker for traps, the Land Office for the dungeon), where the nearest land's monsters begin, a building you have not been in, or its giver when it is done. Standing where it leads, there is no star.
