- The way home: when town is off the box, a house badge rides the box's edge pointing at it ("the world feels hard to navigate without losing your sense of position relative to the town center", v2.3.3023). It steps aside while the quest star already leads to Mayor Bro in town.
- Your party in blue, ringed so it reads on every land's colour, and riding the box's edge toward a member who is off it; on the world map at every zoom, named (v2.3.3277, the owner's "Yes do suggested").
- Every building in town drawn where it stands, roof-coloured, a shut one grey, a notch at its door ("Also all the buildings in town should show on the minimap.", v2.3.3146).
- A dungeon's way in is named on the world map with what it is and its levels, as a camp and a pass are: "the Goo Grotto / dungeon · Lv 1–5" (v2.3.3540, with the starter dungeon near town; the owner: "I value SIMPLICITY and INTELLIGIBILITY"). Only a mouth the server opens is called a dungeon; the Mayor's "Clear any dungeon" leads its gold road to the Goo Grotto.
- **A dungeon built for a party says so before you walk in** (v2.3.3573; the owner: "I want the goo grotto dungeon to be very difficult without a party of 2-3 people"). The Goo Grotto is level 16 whoever comes in, so the world map reads "the Goo Grotto / Lv 16 · 2–3 players", and its Enter button has "Lv 16 · for 2–3 players" in small type under "⚔️ Enter the Goo Grotto". The map's line leaves "dungeon ·" out: a map label is drawn only where it clears the labels before it, and "dungeon · Lv 16 · 2–3 players" lost to the stage's name at the zoom you reach the Grotto at. Inside, the top bar says "(Lv 16)" and every plate says Lv 16 in a danger colour. Nothing else warns you: no window, no "are you sure" (docs/specs/grotto-party.md).
- Each building wears its picture there (v2.3.3522): "Have icons representing each building on each building in the minimap/compass." The picture its Enter button wears, 14 px on its footprint (a shut one grey), and on the world map up close at its door, over its name. The Land Office wore the game's parchment map (it had borrowed the Feed & Seed's barn, and two barns on a map say nothing) until v2.3.3678, below. They load behind the Wheel's loading screen (about half a MB) and go when you leave it.
- v2.3.3614 made each a white symbol on a dark grey disc (the owner: "It blends in with the building visually"). Since v2.3.3630 each is its picture IN ITS OWN COLOURS again, DRAWN BIGGER THAN ITS BUILDING: the owner, "instead of the white minimap icons you should just do the colored icons but oversized so they aren't competing with the buildings color and size". 20 px, its see-through margin cut so each fills its square alike, ringed in a thin dark outline; the same pictures on the world map. Don't suggest the white discs again, or shrinking them back to the footprint's size.
- Since v2.3.3678 ONLY THE PICTURES, NO BUILDING SHAPES: the owner, "I also want the building shapes behind the town icons to go away so only the icons are left". Each picture sits on the middle of the ground its building stands on; a building's terracotta footprint and door notch are drawn only while its picture has not loaded, so the town is never blank. And the owner asked for every picture to be checked ("not all of them make sense"): four changed, each to a picture the game already shows elsewhere -- the Gem Works a gem and chisel (its grinding wheel was a muddle at 20 px), the Feed & Seed the sprout its own window wears (a barn says "farm", not a seed shop), the Land Office the barn its own window wears (a map on the minimap read as "open the map"), the General Store a shop's awning and coin (the balance scales read as a court). The Enter button wears the same pictures.
- North is always up and neither map turns; a gold N on a charcoal bead on the minimap's frame and a compass on the world map say so (v2.3.3065; the bead's colours the slim frame's since v2.3.3986). The lands are named by where they lie.
- In BroTown the world map opens ON THE TOWN (v2.3.3522): "When you're in town make the expanded map zoomed in on the town so you can see which building is labeled where on the map." Every building is on the screen, named at its door with its picture; a name that would run into its neighbour's goes on two lines. In town ◎ is the town's view too. v2.3.3898 (the owner: a player "opened up the minimap and was stuck on the old minimap that looked really zoomed out and didn't show the town. When you're in the town it should always be zoomed in enough to see the town"): home is the town OR its commons (the top bar reads "BroTown" on both); the worker's "where am I" counts only when it is fresh (its last answer can be where you died, so a respawn in town opened the whole Wheel); before the buildings are known the map opens on the town's middle at the buildings' zoom and switches to the town's view when they come, unless the player has moved the map. Anywhere else it opens on YOU, up close (◎'s zoom), never the whole Wheel at zoom 1 (the town a ~6 px speck); one rule for the opening and ◎ (`firstView`).
- **The world map's marks are little pictures of what they mark** (owner: "the legend icons for the map are terrible. Those all need different colors and shapes that actually more closely align with what it's describing ... where monster is red dot, fish is white dot, etc", v2.3.3780): a house the town, a tent a camp, two peaks with a gap a pass, an arch a gate, a flag a landmark, a horned head a monster, a fish, a tree, a rock with a fleck of metal for ore, a little person a bro. The SHAPE says what it is, so it reads without telling colours apart; the colour agrees (a resource keeps its tier's tint: a tree's leaves, a fish, the ore's fleck). The key draws the very same shapes (src/ui/mapGlyphs.js). Kept as they were: the quest star, the "!" quest pin, the road and the river, your party's blue mark.
- The world map is full screen, with "BroTown" and "You are in …" across the top, the ✕ top right, the compass top left, +, − and ◎ (back to you) bottom right, and a key bottom left that says "Zoom in for more". Sideways the key keeps to the margin left of the Wheel, in rows, so it no longer runs across the south gate (UI review, v2.3.3481). Drag, pinch or mouse wheel to move and zoom.
- More appears as you zoom ("Make it so if you tap the minimap and zoom in you can see more details.", v2.3.3057): first the eight lands' names and the quest's road and star; then the other bros, the stages, level bands, camps and passes, resources and the monsters near you (the game is only sent the monsters around you, v2.3.3318), bridges and Bro Pond; up close, every building in town named at its door. A label only appears where it clears the ones already placed, so a crowded spot drops a name instead of piling them up.
- Far from you, zoomed in, the land is drawn as sharply as round you, with finer pieces loaded only while shown (v2.3.3319).
- Dungeons and the farm still use the older 52 px box, sized by an earlier owner ask, "shrink the minimap size by 50% but increase the size of the icons on it", and "Make it 20% transparent." (v2.3.2247).
- On a computer M opens and closes the world map; the minimap wears the M beside its tap-me mark (v2.3.3407).
- **Your own pin** (v2.3.3879, the owner: "add the ability to drop a pin to the map for where you want to go after opening it ... You just need to also have an easy way to remove it afterwards"): a tap (a click) on open ground in the world map drops a pin there; a tap on it, or "Remove pin" at the top of the map, lifts it. A plain tap did nothing on the map before, so it is safe; a drag, a pinch or a long press drops nothing. Kept on this device, never sent to the server (docs/specs/map-pin.md).
- **Two pins, blue and red, with a line to each** (v2.3.4045, the owner: "Add a line path when you drop pins on your minimap ... If there's already 2 pins on map ask if you want to remove one of the pins (give choice of which to remove - blue or red)"). The purple pin and the Town Hall board's red and blue pins are ONE pair now: a dropped pin takes a free colour, blue first, and the board's "Red pin" / "Blue pin" buttons fill their own; with both up a tap asks "Replace which pin?" (the map-pin cards' section). A line runs from you to each in its colour: solid on the minimap, to the pin or, when it is off the box, to its mark on the edge with a small arrow past it; dashed on the world map (the quest's gold road is the dashed gold one, so a pin's line is never mistaken for it: different colours, and only the quest's has a star). One chip at the top of the map for each pin up ("Remove pin" for one, "Remove blue" and "Remove red" for two), and "your pin(s)" in the key in their colours.
- On a computer the key is sized from the play column, not drawn at the phone's 11 px in a window-sized map (v2.3.3830, the judges' pass found it small at 1440 x 900): the column a computer gives the phone's layout over the phone's 390, so 1.26 times at 1440 x 900, 1.5 at 1920 x 1080, as before at 1280 x 720, never past twice; its words and pictures grow together. Phones never change (docs/specs/layout-nits.md).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- Where you are was printed under the box (v2.3.2966), then on the top bar (v2.3.3009), then on the plate under the box (v2.3.3108).
- A thin frame on a slightly see-through box (until v2.3.3009), then a thick 7 px slate-and-brass frame with corners of 8 (until v2.3.3986).
- The same 132 px box on every screen, a computer's too (until v2.3.3986).
- A 12 px red skull for every monster on the Wheel's minimap (v2.3.1810 until v2.3.3986).
- The town was one white square (until v2.3.3146), then plain footprints (until v2.3.3522), then footprints under the pictures (until v2.3.3678).
- The world map always opened on the whole Wheel, the town a dot, and ◎ stopped short of the buildings' names (until v2.3.3522).
- The world map's marks and key were dots and simple shapes: a red dot a monster, a near-white dot a fish, an orange diamond ore, green dots both a tree and a bro, a square the town, a triangle a camp, a diamond a pass, purple and teal dots the gates and landmarks (until v2.3.3780).
- The star ignored the quest you tracked, and half the quests had no star until handed in (until v2.3.3522).
- The world map zoom stopped at 12 (28 since v2.3.3057, further on today's longer Wheel).
- Party members were the same green dots as everyone, and the world map drew no other players until zoom 1.5 (until v2.3.3277).
- A quest path drawn on the ground (put away in v2.3.2992 for the minimap's gold road).
- The land banner: the owner's animated ornaments either side of the plate for Frost Ridge, Flame Fields, Wind Dunes and Verdant Wilds, and the plate alone for the other four (v2.3.3024), until every land's scene from the owner's icon sheet (v2.3.3481: "Icon picks: 1b").
- The land's scene stood over the banner's plate, half the banner wide (v2.3.3481) -- "new land emblems look bad enlarged (revert or show below minimap)" -- then the plate alone and the scene small under the minimap for a few seconds (v2.3.3511), until the owner asked for the older banners back (v2.3.3609).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Enlarging the minimap on a phone: the owner kept its 132 px there and gave the plate room inside it (v2.3.3108); only a computer's is bigger (v2.3.3986).
- A thick frame, or a skull for every monster on the Wheel's minimap: the owner chose the slim rounded frame and the grouped dots (v2.3.3986).
- The land scenes, over the banner or under the minimap: the owner tried both and chose the older animated banners ("The older ones were better.", v2.3.3609). Four lands have no ornaments of the owner's and play the plate alone; ornaments for them would be new art from the owner.
- A rune or a framed glyph for an element: the owner wants the element itself.
- Putting the stage's name on the plate, or text under 11 px there.
- A road on the ground in place of, or next to, the minimap's gold road.
- Showing every label at once on the world map: the zoom-gated labels are what keep it readable.
- Opening the map on the whole Wheel while you stand in town (the owner asked for the town).

**Still open:**
- The land scenes are small on the sheet (about 190 px wide); shown at their own pixels they are about 64 CSS px wide on an iPhone. A bigger emblem would need the owner's new, bigger pictures, never these stretched.
- No arrow at the edge of the screen for a party member off it; the minimap's edge mark does that job for now.
- On a computer the world map covers the whole window, a little wider than the game's column (UI review, v2.3.3407).
- The buildings' map pictures are the Enter button's small icons; a set drawn for a map (bolder shapes at 14 px) would read better.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/WORLD-MAP-PIPELINE.md, docs/specs/minimap-look.md, docs/specs/wheel-north.md, docs/specs/party-on-maps.md, docs/specs/desktop-keys.md, docs/specs/reach-every-button.md, docs/specs/owner-icons.md, src/rendering/systems/wheelMinimap.js, src/rendering/systems/minimapRenderer.js, src/ui/WorldMapOverlay.jsx, src/ui/zoneBannerOverlay.js, src/ui/landEmblem.js

## The map pins' cards
Screens: map-pin-replace, map-pin-arrive, map-pin-remove

**What it is for:** Three small questions about your two map pins (blue and red): which one a new pin replaces, whether to lift one you have walked up to, and whether to lift one you tapped on the minimap.

**Decisions, and why:**
- The owner's words (v2.3.4045): "When you get near the pin (in game) make a window pop up asking if you want to remove pin. A no just makes it stay up until you drop a new pin and get close to it again. To turn off the pin just tap it on minimap and choose remove pin. If there's already 2 pins on map ask if you want to remove one of the pins (give choice of which to remove - blue or red)."
- **Replace which pin?** Both pins up and a tap on open ground in the world map: a small card under the map's top chips with Blue and Red (each filled in its colour) and Cancel, and a white ghost pin where you tapped, so the card says what it is about. A second tap moves the ghost. The world map itself stays usable round it (no scrim).
- **You reached the blue pin. Remove it?** Within about 160 game px of a pin: Remove (the brass primary button, Enter on a computer) and Keep (quiet). Keep, or the ✕, leaves the pin up and it never asks again; a new pin in that colour asks afresh ("A no just makes it stay up until you drop a new pin and get close to it again"). It asks only on the way IN: a pin dropped where you stand waits until you have walked off (past about 240 px) and come back. Never during a fight (a blow taken or landed in the last 5 seconds), never while the world map is open, and never over another card: it waits until they are gone, and steps aside if one comes up. Walking off without answering lets it go.
- **Remove the red pin?** A tap on a pin on the minimap asks this instead of opening the world map (Remove, Cancel); a tap anywhere else on the minimap still opens the map, and M still opens it on a computer.
- Placed as No man's land's "Attack?" window: beside the minimap where the strip to its left holds it (sideways, a computer), else under it, centred; out of the thumbs' way and never over the minimap. No scrim, no timer.
- Every button acts on the finger's lift, and the tap's click that follows is eaten (TRAPS §247); Esc presses the ✕, Enter the main button; words that say "tap" say "click" on a computer.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Asking as you drop a pin near yourself: it asks only when you arrive.
- A pathfinding route: the line is straight, as the quest's gold road is (no pathfinding exists).

**Still open:**
- The board's pin buttons (moving to the noticeboard) do not ask before replacing their own colour: a press there is already a choice of colour.

Sources: docs/specs/map-pin.md, src/game/pinSlots.js, src/ui/PinCards.jsx, src/ui/worldMapPins.js, src/ui/WorldMapOverlay.jsx, src/rendering/systems/wheelMinimap.js, src/ui/mobile/NmlAskCard.jsx

## Chat, the bell and emotes
Screens: chat

**What it is for:** Talking to other players: a short line typed or spoken into a box over your bro, shown as a speech bubble over your head and in the World Chat feed for everyone, plus emotes and moves that need no words.

**Decisions, and why:**
- On a phone, chat opens by tapping your own bro; chat left the dashboard's toolbar by the owner's choice (v2.3.1283). On a computer, C opens it. These are two boxes: the phone's sits high over your bro (its bottom a quarter of the way down the screen, clear of the keyboard, and never less than 156 px down, so sideways its top and its lanes stay on the screen: UI review, v2.3.3481), with a tail pointing down at him; the computer's is a bar near the top of the screen with ✕, the line and Send. (The review shows each: the phone's box in the phone pictures, the computer's bar in the computer ones.)
- Lanes as chips over the line: All, Area (everyone in this zone), Whisper (one player, by name, with a "who?" field) and Party (only while you are in one) (v2.3.2139, v2.3.2820). The chosen lane is filled in its own colour and the placeholder changes with it: two cues, because one was not enough in the demo. Whisper is never remembered after a reload, so you never think you are whispering while talking to everyone. Typed /p, /a and /w still work.
- The phone's line wraps and grows up to four lines: "it's tricky to see your full message before sending because it gets cut off" (v2.3.2039). It grows upward, away from the keyboard.
- No Send button on the phone: "Instead of having a send button at all, just remove it. Players can just use their own phones send button and it works." (v2.3.2896). The keyboard's key says "send", and sending closes the box: one line, then back to the game.
- The microphone: "is it possible to have a microphone button on the chat window? It would be easier for people to speak to text and then just send it." (v2.3.2037). It fills the box rather than sending, so a misheard word can be fixed first; it spins while the phone works the speech out ("show a spinning icon while the game processes your speech to text. There's a little delay.", v2.3.2039). It only appears where the phone can do speech to text.
- The smiley beside the mic opens the emotes (B on a computer). Emotes have no button of their own on screen: the owner-approved fun & social plan keeps every new always-on button off the phone, so they live in the chat box (v2.3.3391).
- The emote picker opens on its Moves tab, twelve moves (wave, laugh, flex, shrug, facepalm, cheer; sit, sleep, flop and three dances held until you move), with the 30 emoji and 12 quick lines on the second tab (v2.3.3392). `/wave`, `/sit`, `/dance` and the rest work in chat, and `/roll` and `/flip` are rolled by the server (v2.3.3393). An emoji pops over your head for 2.6 s; a quick line is your speech bubble. Only players near you see them, one a second.
- Speech bubbles last 5 s and are bigger and bolder ("Chat font (like tap on player to chat) should be chunkier and larger.", v2.3.1912). They size to the message, wrap long words ("Make it so that long text wraps into a second line.", v2.3.2896), carry a grey outline and soft shading ("I just want it to pop a little more", v2.3.2823), and point just above the name ("not on their face like it is currently", v2.3.2896). Since v2.3.3858 (the owner, the morning of the showing: "for character messages I'd rather have chat bubbles right above character head and just cover nameplate during chat. Make chat bubble background white and the chat font black"): the point comes down to the top of the head, on the name plate's place, and the plate steps aside while the bubble is up and comes back after it (in a fight the HP bar keeps its place and the bubble stands over it); every bubble is white with black words, a player's as a townsperson's, replacing v2.3.3522's darker player bubble. A whisper draws no bubble, so a private line is never shown to the people standing near.
- Whose bubble is whose (v2.3.3522, replaced by v2.3.3858: every bubble white now): "Make Npc dialog boxes white. Keep players the darker color to differentiate." A townsperson's bubble is always white with black words; a player's is dark with light words: the night-purple pumpkin bubble in Halloween week, a Lantern Slate slate the rest of the year, so the two never look alike.
- The bell is in More now (v2.3.4172, from the review of the dashboard the owner adopted: "The notification bell could also move to More, retaining an unread badge"). Shut, the World Chat feed draws nothing in the world's corner: no bell over the movement stick. The unread count rides the More button and More's Notifications tile; More → Notifications lists the same lines, newest first, and opening it clears the count; its "Show on screen" puts the feed back in the corner, and one tap anywhere on the feed folds it away again ("Make it so touching the world chat window once just closes it ... and you can remove the tiny down arrow minimizing button.", v2.3.2325), into More. Before, the bell was the corner's resting state: "collapse all notifications (chat, special events, etc, into a little notification bell on the bottom left corner above the dashboard)" (v2.3.2155).
- The feed shows sent messages in the lower left, outside the chat box: "Just have the chat appear in the lower left area above the dashboards in a new 'World Chat' section" and "by chat appear I mean sent messages. Keep chat window where it is." (v2.3.2037). It keeps the last 40 lines, newest at the bottom, and cannot be scrolled: it sits over the movement stick, and a scrollable list there stopped players from walking.
- Chat is the bottom layer: "Chat should always be the bottom layer if any menus open up beside it (want it beneath trade menus, player menus, etc)." (v2.3.2280). The chat box closes itself when a trade or other decision window opens ("during trade make the world notifications (chat, etc) UNDER those panels. I couldn't accept any trades because notifications blocked it.", v2.3.2145), and the bell hides under any window (v2.3.3407).
- Muting follows you to every device, and a muted player's lines never reach you. Reporting takes two taps on the player's card (a reason chip), because a one-tap Report beside Mute would be mis-fired on a 390 px phone. A blocked player's chat and emotes are hidden, and since v2.3.3526 their duel challenges, trade requests and party invites never reach you either: the phone drops them, and they run out on the sender's side like an unanswered one (the first ChatGPT review asked; the owner: "Go recommended on everything"). The block list stays on the device; keeping it on the server is the fun & social plan's Phase 4.
- Guardrails you may notice: about five lines in ten seconds, the same public line said once per 30 s, and lines cleaned of invisible characters, piled-up accents, more than 6 emoji and runs of one letter past six ("Broooooo" survives as a joke; numbers are never cut) (v2.3.3396).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The feed lived inside the chat box behind a "World chat" toggle, so messages showed only while typing (until v2.3.2037).
- The feed was open by default (until v2.3.2155); its fold arrow went in v2.3.2325, after "the down arrow makes me think it expands it" (v2.3.2266).
- A 36 px bell in the lower-left corner with the unread count, the feed's shut state (v2.3.2155-v2.3.3940); it moved into More (v2.3.4172).
- The feed's list could be scrolled and blocked the movement stick under it (until v2.3.2123).
- A one-line input that cut long messages off (until v2.3.2039), and a Send button on the phone (until v2.3.2896).
- Emotes had a button only on a toolbar hidden for a long time, and a sent emote drew nothing (until v2.3.3391).
- A "golden tickets left" line in the corner during an event ("you can remove the golden ticket left notification. The event is over.", v2.3.2266).
- Every bubble white, then in Halloween week every bubble purple, the townsfolk's too (from v2.3.3395), until townsfolk white and players dark (v2.3.3522).

**Please don't suggest:**
- A chat or emote button always on screen, or a Chat button back on the dashboard.
- A Send button on the phone's chat box.
- A scrollable or tappable World Chat list, or the feed open by default.
- A mute or silence switch in the bell's corner. The owner asked for one there ("give options to silence all notifications (on bottom left above dashboard of screen)"); three placements were tried and each broke walking or the tutorial, because that corner is the movement stick's touch area. It lives in Settings ("Notifications — silence all of them", v2.3.2147).
- A one-tap Report next to Mute.
- Speech bubbles for whispers, or remembering the Whisper lane after a reload.
- One colour of bubble for everyone: white is a townsperson talking, dark is a player.
- Chat drawn over menus or trade windows.

**Still open:**
- Blocking someone hides their chat and emotes but does not yet stop their trade or duel requests (a later server-side block).
- The emote picker shows all twelve moves; choosing your own set of slots is not built yet.
- Sitting is a squash in code; if it reads as a short bro, one picture of the bro sitting would replace it.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/chat-lanes.md, docs/specs/chat-moderation.md, docs/specs/chat-guard.md, docs/specs/emotes.md, docs/specs/emote-moves.md, docs/specs/dash-tabs.md, docs/FUN-SOCIAL-PLAN.md, src/ui/mobile/ChatBubble.jsx, src/ui/mobile/WorldChatFeed.jsx, src/ui/mobile/notifBus.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/NotificationsPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/ChatChannelChips.jsx, src/ui/mobile/modalGuardBus.js, src/ui/mobile/BottomDashboard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/SettingsPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/ChatPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/EmotePanel.jsx, src/game/chatChannel.js, src/rendering/systems/effectsRenderer.js

## The dashboard: band, five buttons and pages
Screens: hud-band, dash-*, more-*

**What it is for:** The band at the bottom of the screen is the game's one menu, and the world keeps playing above it. Since v2.3.6681 it is two workspaces under one header: **Gear** (the Gear tab: your bag, your bro and your gear side by side; **The Gear tab** below) and the **Menu** (a directory of every other page). Under `?ui2=0` (remembered on the phone; `?ui2=1` returns) it is the row of CLOSE and six buttons described under "Tried before".

**The owner's words, 2026-11-25** (the master specification, with *"You are authorized to begin implementation ... independently resolve ordinary visual and implementation decisions"*): *"Completely retire the old CLOSE / Gear / Bro / Quests / Codex / More persistent tab strip. Use independently remembered Gear and Menu workspaces, a universal directory, one contextual header and actual-history navigation. Deep screens have labeled Back, title, Menu and Close; no duplicate secondary global nav."*; *"Gear and Menu have independent remembered states. Gear opens the Gear workspace; Menu resumes the previous exploration destination. Closing temporarily hides the dashboard without resetting either workspace."*; *"Back follows the actual navigation history (the player's journey), not necessarily the canonical category tree. Close returns to gameplay. Preserve scroll, selections and relevant context on returning from drill-downs."*; *"The Menu shortcut on a deep view opens the directory root; ordinary Menu workspace entry resumes its prior state."*; *"Keep the existing mobile dashboard height"*.

**Decisions, and why (the frame, v2.3.6681, card UI2-frame):**
- **One header where the row was**, the same 52 px (48 on a short screen), built from the kit's header (`src/ui/kit/Header.jsx`): Gear's root `[Close] Gear [Menu ›]`; the Menu's root `[‹ Gear] Menu [Close]`; a page `[‹ where Back goes] its name [Menu] [Close]` (`dash/DashHeader.jsx`). The band's height did not change by a pixel.
- **Back names its target and goes there** (`src/game/dashNav.js`, the one state and its one transition): the page you came from, not its category; "Gear" from the Menu's root; "World" for a page reached from the world (the quest card's page is not under a Quests list you never saw: Back closes the dashboard again). A page's scroll is kept for each visit and given back (`dash/dashScroll.js`, read only at the top of the game's frame).
- **The small Menu on a page goes to the directory's root** and leaves nothing under it (Back from there is Gear). **Menu from play, the closed row or Gear's root resumes** the last page. **Close hides; it resets neither workspace.**
- **The Menu's root is eight equal tiles** (`dash/MenuGrid.jsx`, `src/game/dashDests.js`): Bro, Quests, Social, Ranks, Codex, Homestead, Map, Settings, each the picture its door has today and one word; four across and two down, one row of eight on a phone on its side (82 px of page). No tile larger than another (*"an equal-weight directory, not a prioritized list"*).
- **The pictures as big as the band lets them be** (v2.3.6784, card UI3-menus; the owner, 2026-11-26: *"Main menu. Keep the four-by-two layout. Enlarge the icons by approximately 25%, reduce the button bevels and shadows, and use the same typography across all navigation buttons."*, *"I'd also retain your existing illustrated game icons instead of replacing them."*, *"without increasing the dashboard height"*). Each picture is the largest the tile holds, capped at 58 px upright and 35 sideways (46 and 28 x 1.25), worked out from the grid's own measured box (`src/game/menuTile.js`, the same ResizeObserver entry that says the page is short: no new layout read); the tile's padding 2 px (the kit's 4 left 56 at 390 x 844). Drawn on the test box: 58 at 390 x 844, 375 x 667 and a computer, 35 at 844 x 390 and 667 x 375; the band 243, 235, 130 and 125 as before. 375 x 667 and 667 x 375 have 0 px to spare at the cap. Bro's six and the hubs draw through the same grid and the same rule; the bevel and the one size of word are the kit's (tag 6780).
- **Every page More reached is still reached**: Life Skills, Achievements and Pets from Bro's own doors, as before; Social, Party and Guilds from Social's small hub; Settings, Notifications, Account, Feedback, About and Journey from Settings' small hub (Journey until step 3 gives it a home under Bro's Feats); Map opens the World map over the game, as More's tile did. Homestead is a small page saying what the Bro row says (plots ready, where the farm is), and nothing to manage from here.
- **Closed, the slim row keeps two buttons, Gear and Menu**, and the handle on the band's edge. Points to spend glow on Bro's tile and climb to the Menu button (a count); a quest ready, the Codex's new pages, requests and the bell's unread mark their tiles, and a dot on the Menu.
- **A computer**: I Gear, O the Menu, and P, J, U, K, L open their pages as before (pressed again, the dashboard closes); a tile wears its key; Esc presses Close.
- **The old hooks** the tutorial, the coach, the guide arrow and the points' flight find are worn by the control that leads there now: `[data-nav="dashboard"]` by Gear's button (or "‹ Gear"), `[data-nav="hero"]` by the Menu button (Bro's tile on the Menu's root).
- **A page grows out of the tile tapped, and shrinks back on Back; never a slide** (v2.3.6682, card UI2-frame PR 2; the card's reading 8). CSS only, transform and opacity on the page's container, 220 ms on the band's own curve (`sheet/motion.js`): `src/game/dashGrow.js` says which motion a step makes and from where, `dash/DashGrowBox.jsx` wears it as a class until the animation's own end, `dash/dashGrow.css` holds the keyframes. The origin is one the code already holds: the tile's cell in its grid (MenuGrid's `from`), else the press's point (the pointer's own event and the kept window size), else the centre; nothing is measured at a tap or in a frame. A change of workspace (Gear and the Menu), the Menu shortcut to its root and the dashboard opened from play grow from nothing (the centre, a small fade). **Back's shrink** is drawn as the page underneath settling from a slight zoom at the place the closed page grew from: the closed page is not kept on screen after Back (no copy of a page is kept to animate). Presses are never held: a page asked mid-motion starts its own. `?dashgrow=0` and the phone's reduced-motion setting give none.
- **The band's handle hides and keeps both workspaces** (v2.3.6682): tapped on an open page it is Close (dashNav's `fold`), never "to the bar" then the fold, which reset Gear's own pages. Under `?ui2=0`, today's handle.

**Decisions before the frame (they hold under `?ui2=0`, and the band's own ones hold for both):**
- **Desktop HUD polish (v2.3.4263, owner: "add the short list of shortcuts to the extra dashboard area on the right", "Add the BroTown logo centered on the top bar", "Make the desktop version of the minimap somewhat larger").** (1) On a computer under the one band the band's empty right side holds `BandKeys` (KeyboardHintsPanel.jsx): the essential keys as slate key caps and words in two columns, a "Controls  H" heading that folds it, "More keys" at the foot (the strip's own choice); the floating strip and chip are not drawn then (H still toggles). (2) The top bar's middle holds the title screen's BroTown lockup (`public/ui/topbar-logo.png`, 256 colours, warmed with the XP bar's icons), in the world only, no touches (the 1.2 s hold still reaches the title under it); the XP bar takes the centre while you earn (the logo fades), TEST MODE draws over it. (3) The minimap is 1.35x on that computer (`wheelMiniK`, WHEEL_MINI_BAND_K), the phone's window across so the marks grow with the box; the old side column keeps its 2x.
- **A computer's band is 480 px, not 840 (v2.3.4243, owner: "make the container areas smaller (like they appear on mobile) they don't need a bunch of extra dark space").** `BAND_ASSEMBLY_MAX_W`: the nav row and the panels are one centred block a big phone wide, so the panels hug their cells; Bro keeps two columns there.
- **Buttons are pictures, named by a badge (v2.3.4243, owner: "blown up to fill the full button space ... name labels on top of the picture similar to the badge you have for counting quantities").** Each picture is trimmed of its empty margins (`NAV_TRIM` in NavRail.jsx, the opaque box of the 256 px file) and scaled to the button's whole face; the word is a `.bt-item-qty` badge at the bottom centre (gold when open). The key cap stays top-left, the red count top-right. Sideways, where the buttons are too narrow for words, the picture alone shows (aria-label as before). CLOSE is unchanged.
- **Never more than a third of the screen** (owner directive, v2.3.1258). The painted world stays the brightest thing on screen; the band is a quiet slate tray.
- **At rest the band is the bag** (v2.3.1653, owner: "make the dashboard view the main bag view and move the equipped view to be merged with the character overview ... the combat stats stay on the right in their own column"). It shows 4 slots across and 2 rows, with a sliver of a third row under a fade, and it scrolls (v2.3.1654). Worn gear moved to the Character page.
- **The slots are big on purpose** (v2.3.1648, owner: "the slots and info displayed currently don't meet a minimum size where users who can't see at smaller sizes struggle with it").
- **Three combat cards: Melee, Bow, Magic.** HP, defense and stamina already show on the world's bars (v2.3.1648, owner: "The hp, defense, and stamina/energy can just be reflected in total hp, energy points"). A card is an icon, "LV n" and a bar. A word label was tried and was cut to "M…", the same for Melee and Magic. The exact XP is in a popup on tap (v2.3.2131, owner: "get rid of the xp numbers in the 3 combat skills and put them as some kind of pop up when you tap on it"), and the popup's button goes on to the Points tab (the owner's "popup, then Build on a second tap").
- **CLOSE, then the five, each its picture and its word** (v2.3.4172). The owner drew the row (the two mockups in the repo root, 61C354F2-F323-4048-921E-654578D99AD6.png and D474F679-6893-4890-823A-3BC96F817184.png): CLOSE at the left, then Gear | Bro | Quests | Codex | More, each an icon and a word, the open one gold-bordered; "Keep the 5 buttons". The words show wherever they fit at 11 px or more, from each button's real width: under the picture on an upright phone (58 px each at 390, 52 at 360: "Quests" beside its picture needs about 70), beside it from 86 px (a tablet, a computer's one band), and the pictures alone only where a word cannot fit (a 320 px phone, 44 px each; the old side column, `?oneband=0`, about 30). In the one band (the default since v2.3.4152) a phone on its side and a computer have the room for the words beside the pictures. Bro shows your own face. Rectangles because a circle's corners take no taps (v2.3.2321, owner: "I want to see what rectangular shaped buttons with rounded edges looks like"). Wide for tapping (v2.3.2320, owner: "make the 5 dashboard navigation buttons wider to increase tap surface"): 44 px tall on a phone taller than 720 px (40 on a shorter one, identityRowHeight's rule), 44 px wide or more, the house rule a 44 pt target. Icon-only before (v2.3.1637, the owner's mockup then: "just the size of the icons").
- **Lit is a brass tint and edge, and badges are only for something to act on** (v2.3.1296). The owner had the lit button's extra arrow chip removed (v2.3.1649). Badges are: unspent points on Bro, which also glows (v2.3.3004, owner: "when you level up make the character tab do a light flashing effect ... until all points are spent"), or a plain dot there for an unseen life-skill level-up (the Bro card's Life Skills row carries it too); a quest ready to hand in; new codex pages; and on More the bell's unread count (v2.3.4172). The owner removed the Bag's dot (v2.3.1315).
- **No handle on the band's edge: the row's own button folds it** (v2.3.6697, card UI2-grip). The owner, 2026-11-26, after trying the new controller on their iPhone: *"New controller looks good but remove the pull handle on the dashboard. It has an open button making pull handle redundant. Also pull handle implies stretching when dashboard is really just minimized or expanded"*. So upright the band's top edge carries nothing (no tab, no bar, no tap area over the world): the controller's shell meets the band's edge, and a tap where the tab stood reaches the world or the weapon pill under it. Under the new frame the header's **Gear** (folded) and **✕ Close** (shown) fold and unfold it (exactly dashNav's `fold`: Gear as it was left, Close keeping both workspaces); under `?ui2=0`, OPEN / CLOSE at the row's left. Those controls wear the fold's hook `data-dash-fold` (`min` / `open`), so the harness, the coach's rings, the guide arrow, the gear flash and the rewards' flight still find the fold (`dash/bandGripSwitch.js`); the coach's "Tap the handle" became the sideways lesson's own "Open your dashboard". `?bandgrip=1` brings the handle back for one phone, and then it alone wears the hook, as before. Held by `tools/guards/band-grip.test.mjs` and `mp-bandgrip`.
- **CLOSE at the row's left** (v2.3.4172, the owner's drawing of the Bro page has it on every screen). With a page open it closes the page and folds the band; at rest it folds; folded it reads OPEN and unfolds. 56 px wide, as tall as the buttons.
- **A brand-new player's band arrives folded** (v2.3.2495, the owner's tutorial request), with a ring on the control that opens it (since v2.3.6697 the header's Gear, or OPEN under `?ui2=0`; the handle until then): "Your bag, your gear and your stats live down here. Open your dashboard." The rest of the tutorial waits for that one tap. Opening any page unfolds the band (v2.3.2119), and so do Diego's shop and a trade, which work through the bag's own tiles (v2.3.3399).
- **One height for every page** (v2.3.1638, owner: "shorten the expanded view to be the exact same size as the default dashboard view"). A page takes the bag row's place, so the world you see never shrinks. The cost was accepted on purpose: every page scrolls inside about 191 px (on the QA phone).
- **You can play with a page open.** Walking and fighting never close it; turning the phone does (v2.3.2157).
- **Gold is in the top bar** (v2.3.2320, owner: "Move gold amount display to very top right on the top bar that lists the zone name"). Only one gold count is ever on screen, because two counts disagree the moment one lags.
- **Sideways is optional and has no band** (owner: "Landscape would be an optional view."; v2.3.2168, owner: "You can actually remove that whole bottom length bar now"). At rest the only dashboard control on the world is a 44 px ▴ chip. It opens a column beside the world, never over it (owner: "No I don't want an overlay over the world", and "You should be able to play the game with the menus open. That's the idea.").
- **The sideways column is the bag turned upright,** 2 across and 4 down (v2.3.2165, owner: "This would make it equivalent to the portrait view of the bag. The additional room goes to screen space of the world"). Its buttons show only while it is open (v2.3.2176); the owner weighed the extra tap and chose it. It takes the side away from the iPhone's Dynamic Island (v2.3.2174, owner: "The iPhone has a punch hole that's awkward since it goes right through the menus"), and the owner checked both rotations: "The mapping is correct" (v2.3.2177).
- **On a computer each button wears its key** in a small key cap: I (Gear: the band at rest), P (Bro), J, K, O, and M for the map (v2.3.3407, owner: "just put the letter next to the icon or action in a tasteful way"); L opens the Life Skills page under Bro, and the Bro card's Life Skills row wears the L (v2.3.4172). Pressing again closes. B was already the emotes' key, so the bag got I. Phones never show the letters.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- **Reversed by the owner's master specification of 2026-11-25** (the words above; the frame, v2.3.6681), each kept whole under `?ui2=0` until the build's step 10 deletes the old row:
  - **Six buttons since v2.3.4393: Gear, Bro, Quests, Ranks, Codex, More.** The owner, 2026-11-09: "I want to reorganize the 'quests' section and make room for a new 'leaderboards' button on the dashboard (I think this is already in the 'more' button)." The Leaderboards left More for a button between Quests and the Codex (the two things you do, then the two things you look up; More stays last). The room came out of the buttons: 48 px each at 390 where five were 58, 45 at 375, 44 at 360 (CLOSE is 48 px wide under 375 so those stay a thumb's 44), 38 at 320. The words stay under the pictures down to 45 px (every iPhone; a 360 px phone shows the pictures alone: "Quests" is a pixel too wide for a 44 px button). On a computer its key is U. Sideways the dock's buttons are 26 px again, so the dock is the same 214 px.
  - **Five buttons: Gear (lit at rest), Bro, Quests, Codex, More** (v2.3.4172). The owner: "I want to consolidate the dashboard area as much as possible (limit lots of buttons to switch between different context)", with his table of what each holds: Gear the bag, the figure, what you wear, stats and gold; Bro your picture and player card, combat stats and skills, points, quests completed, achievements and their points; Codex the bestiary, the items and discoveries; More the map, social, settings and account. Asked, he chose five: Quests keeps its own button ("the quest journal is the main 'what do I do next' help for new players, so it stays one tap away") and Life Skills move inside Bro (its Skills tab). This replaces six buttons (Dashboard, Character, Quests, Life Skills, Codex, More), each once the owner's ask: Quests (v2.3.1651, "I think those are the main buttons people will use"), Character (v2.3.1654), Life Skills (v2.3.1655), Codex (v2.3.3215). More stays last, because it is the overflow. The buttons keep their ids (Gear is `dashboard`, Bro is `hero`), so tests and the coach still find them.
  - **Nothing in the row ever moves.** The buttons keep one spot at rest, with a page open and with a page drilled into, so a thumb that knows a button always finds it (v2.3.1637b).
  - **No title bar on main pages** (v2.3.1350, owner: "remove the headers — it's redundant"); the lit button names the page. A drilled-into page has one back button, "◂ SETTINGS". Asked whether to make the six buttons narrower or move the arrow out of the row, the owner said "Move back arrow" (v2.3.3215). The title always shows whole.
- A full-width bottom toolbar of six labelled buttons under three wells (Bag / Loadout / Build, v2.3.1240), then a rail down the left (v2.3.1637), then the row at the band's top (v2.3.1642).
- A BAG / EQUIPPED / COMBAT row of three columns (v2.3.1636), then two panels (v2.3.1653); six combat squares, then three cards (v2.3.1648).
- An identity strip (your portrait, then a summary of name, XP and gold) that the owner found "way too busy". It was cut piece by piece (v2.3.1848–1857), and its last piece, the gold, went to the top bar (v2.3.2320).
- A taller sheet that rose over the world (v2.3.1311), and a third "compact" state (retired v2.3.1350).
- Swipes on the band (retired: too ambiguous over menus); the fold as a ▾ arrow; round buttons; a Bag button; Friends and Chat buttons (Friends is under More; chat opens by tapping your own character).
- Six buttons with a Life Skills button and the OPEN / CLOSE pill in the row (v2.3.3215-v2.3.3940): the owner's consolidation made it five and a handle (v2.3.4172). Icon-only buttons (v2.3.1637-v2.3.4172): the owner's drawing gave them their words back, and CLOSE its place at the left.
- **The slim handle on the band's top edge** (v2.3.4172-v2.3.6697), from the review the owner adopted: "remove the large OPEN/CLOSE button taking up a full navigation slot. A slim, centered handle at the top of the dashboard would communicate that the panel can be expanded or collapsed". A 36 x 5 bar on a small tab of the band's slate, a 64 x 20 tap area standing 16 px over the world. CLOSE came back beside it the same week, and the owner removed the handle on 2026-11-26 (above: "pull handle implies stretching when dashboard is really just minimized or expanded"). Kept whole under `?bandgrip=1`.

**Please don't suggest:**
- A page taller than the band, or one that grows over the world. That also moves the buttons under the thumb.
- Round shapes for the frame's tiles; a Bag button; a persistent row of buttons or a second row of tabs under the header (*"no duplicate secondary global nav"*); a tile bigger than the others on the Menu's root.
- Narrower buttons to make room for something. The owner moved the back arrow instead.
- Gold back on the band, or a second gold count.
- Swipes on the band; taking away CLOSE (the owner drew it); a handle, tab or bar on the band's top edge (the owner removed it: *"pull handle implies stretching when dashboard is really just minimized or expanded"*).
- Worn gear, HP / defense / stamina, XP numbers or skill names back on the band.
- Sideways: a menu over the world, buttons on the world at rest, or flipping the Dynamic Island mapping.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/design/UI_MASTER_SPEC.md (card UI2-frame), src/game/dashNav.js, src/game/dashDests.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/DashHeader.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/MenuGrid.jsx, docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, docs/UI-BIBLE.md, docs/specs/dash-tabs.md, docs/specs/landscape-mode.md, docs/specs/monster-codex.md, docs/specs/desktop-keys.md, docs/specs/damage-number-size.md, docs/BACKLOG-TRIAGE-2026-09-14.md, src/ui/mobile/BottomDashboard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/NavRail.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/BandGrip.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/DashColumns.jsx, src/ui/mobile/sheet/sheetGeometry.js, src/ui/mobile/sheet/IdentityStrip.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dashMinBus.js, src/ui/mobile/dashboardPanelBus.js, src/ui/mobile/QuestCoach.jsx, src/styles/game.css

## Bro: one page (v2.3.6782, card UI3-bro)
Screens: ui2-bro-landing, ui2-bro-character, ui2-bro-titles

**What it is for:** managing your character from ONE page. The owner, 2026-11-26 (`docs/design/UI_FEEDBACK_2026-11-26.md` part 2): *"I don't think you need separate Bro and Character landing menus anymore. Moving the identity information into the persistent HUD eliminates much of the reason for having two screens. I'd turn the Bro menu into a unified character management dashboard."* Its mock-up: *"‹ Menu / Character / Combat / Equipment / Life Skills / Feats / Pets / Journey / AP 415 / Quests 0 / Clan: Party of 3 / Guilds: View ›"*; *"Titles could live within Feats. Weapon levels would live within Combat. Clan and guild management could link directly into Social."*; *"This eliminates the large redundant portrait, combat level banner, three weapon level buttons and separate oversized informational boxes."* Part 3: *"Supporting information ... Flat, aligned within shared containers. No individual heavy borders."* Their standing rule: *"keep my game content over the mockup stuff"*. This CHANGES the two sections below for yourself (they are kept as what was).

**Decisions, and why (sheet/BroLanding.jsx, sheet/BroStrip.jsx; the rules src/game/broHub.js BRO_HUB_DOORS, broStrip, broSelfPage):**
- **Six raised tiles, the destinations:** Combat, Equipment, Life Skills / Feats, Pets, Journey (three by two upright and on a computer, one row sideways). Character left the grid (the HUD has who you are); Journey (lifetime records, its own picture `journey.webp`) came in from the passport's door, and More's Journey road is Bro's now.
- **ONE flat strip under them, what you have** (the kit's facts, `?uikit=1`'s third weight): AP, Quests done, Clan (or "Party of 3"), Guilds ("Skill guilds") and your title ("No title equipped" until you wear one). Each entry is its label over its value and a door to its page (Feats, Quests, Party, Guilds, Titles) with the kit's chevron, flat: no bevel, no border of its own, 44 px tall so a finger can take it. A long value takes two lines rather than smaller text. A value on its way is the pulsing placeholder, never a 0. The game's words stay over the mock-up's ("Quests done", "Skill guilds").
- **The title entry is interim:** Titles opens from it until card UI2-feats takes Titles into Feats ("Titles could live within Feats") and removes it.
- **Not drawn for yourself:** the portrait, the name, the combat level, the three weapon doors. Combat's hub has the weapon levels: the Bow level is two taps from Bro (Bro, Combat).
- **The room (measured on the test box, mp-ui2bro):** the landing gave up its own 4 px pads; upright the strip is about 46 px and the six keep the rest (about 118 px at 390 x 844, 114 at 375 x 667, above the grid's one-row turn at 110), so the pictures shrink from 46 to about 30 px. Sideways the strip sits beside the six, as tall as they are; on the narrowest page (667 x 375) Quests, Clan, Guilds and the title are their pictures' doors (44 px, their words their labels for a screen reader); AP keeps its words.
- **Character is ANOTHER player's page** (Ranks' tap, step 9): their face, name, levels and public lines, no Equip, no Journey. A push of Character for yourself, or of any page id Bro does not know, draws this one page, never your old passport and never a blank.

## Bro under the new dashboard: six doors (v2.3.6683, card UI2-bro)
Screens: ui2-bro-landing

*v2.3.6782: for yourself this is now "Bro: one page" above; kept as what was.*

**What it is for:** Bro answers "who am I" and sends you to the detail by a named door. The owner, 2026-11-25, with the master specification: *"Bro landing: Six buttons only under contextual header. Character, Combat, Equipment / Life Skills, **Feats**, Pets. Remove the redundant player/tap banner."*; its part 4: *"Feats is the approved replacement for Accomplishments. No player portrait or tap banner above the Bro landing grid. For another player, identify the inspected owner in the header or a compact contextual label without adding a large banner. Place full identity on Character."*; and *"Newer Character reference sets the premium blue-metal feel; Bro should match."* Their standing rule: *"keep my game content over the mockup stuff"*. Under `?ui2=0` the section below (today's three pages) is Bro.

**Decisions, and why (sheet/BroLanding.jsx; the rules src/game/broHub.js):**
- **Six equal kit tiles from the Menu's own grid** (dash/MenuGrid.jsx): three across and two down upright and on a computer, one row of six on a phone on its side (the page is about 63 px inside there). Each is its picture and one word; nothing above the grid (no portrait, no banner, no page marks). Measured on the test box (Chromium, no graphics chip): at 390 x 844 the band stays 390 x 243 and each tile is about 125 x 96; at 375 x 667 three by two; at 844 x 390 one row; on a computer three by two; every tile 44 x 44 or more, nothing clipped, nothing to scroll (mp-ui2bro).
- **The pictures are the game's doors' own:** Character `panel-self` (the inspect glass), Equipment `panel-loadout`, Life Skills `nav-lifeskills`, Feats the achievements medal, Pets the paw. Combat has no door picture of its own: the melee lane's sword (`combat-melee`); `panel-weapons` is an anvil, which reads as smithing at a tile's size. A Combat picture of its own belongs on the list of missing art.
- **What each door opens until its own step:** (v2.3.6685: Combat is its hub now, two sections down; Points is the hub's Shared door) Character today's three pages under the header (PR 2's passport replaces them); Combat today's Combat page alone, no marks, with **Points** beside the weapon picker (glowing while points wait; it opens the Bro page's Points window, and Back returns to Combat); Equipment the Gear workspace (as Progress's Equipment door did; the Menu button comes back to Bro); Life Skills, Feats and Pets today's pages under the header (Feats keeps its own inner words until its step).
- **Nothing of today's Bro is lost** (broHub.js BRO_PROGRESS_ROADS): Life Skills, Pet, Achievements (Feats) and Equipment are tiles; Points is on Combat; Titles and Clan are on Character (v2.3.6684: the passport's Titles door and its Clan line); Homestead is the Menu's tile.
- **The points glow** sits on the Menu button, on Bro's tile (the frame's marks) and on Combat's tile, with the count; Life Skills' "new" dot on its tile. Another player's Bro (built, nothing opens it until step 9) shows none of those, and names its owner in one compact line ("Sunburn's Bro").
- **A door the worker does not offer** (Feats without achievements, Pets without the pet book) keeps its place, greyed, and a tap says why ("Not open on this server yet").

**The screen's pieces (the kit, `?uikit=1`):** Zones (identity, summary, deeper doors, actions at the bottom; an empty zone gives its room back), the row of actions (each in its slot; one that is off stays, disabled, its reason under it from the same rule that refuses it; the pending spinner; "Not confirmed yet" when no answer came), the empty state (absent, locked, loading: never a 0), and the one confirmation (only for the costly or irreversible; a routine action never asks). Their rules: src/game/screenZones.js, src/game/pending.js.

## Character, the passport, and its Titles (v2.3.6684, card UI2-bro PR 2)
Screens: ui2-bro-character, ui2-bro-titles

*v2.3.6782: the passport is now ANOTHER player's page; yours is "Bro: one page" above, and Titles opens from its strip.*

**What it is for:** who you are, at a glance, and a named door to each detail. The owner, 2026-11-25: *"Character: RPG passport; large portrait and adjacent combat level, name/title, supporting summaries. Not a combat-stat dump."*; the master specification's 4.1: *"Prominent portrait, name, equipped title and combat level. Supporting content: Sword, Bow, Staff levels; clan and guild affiliations; achievement points; quest completion count; titles ... Link to the detailed destinations rather than duplicating all their content."* Reference 01 is the feel and the order of importance, not its thick gold rim, heavy shadows, every panel raised, or its numbers. Their standing rule: *"keep my game content over the mockup stuff"*. Under `?ui2=0` Character is today's three pages (the section below).

**Decisions, and why (sheet/CharacterPage.jsx; the rules src/game/broHub.js broPassport, titleActions):**
- **Three rows upright, in the screen's zones:** the identity (the still face as tall as the row, the name, the title in italics or "No title equipped", the combat level under them, right beside the face; the Titles and Journey doors at the row's end); Sword, Bow, Staff (each its picture, word and level, a door to today's Combat page on that weapon, with the points waiting on it as a count and the warm glow); Clan, Guilds, AP, Quests done (each a door). The identity row takes what the two 44 px rows leave: on the test box 72 px at 390 x 844 (the face 72 px), less at 375 x 667. Only what opens something is raised; the name, title and combat level are recessed (a fact).
- **The values are the game's:** the combat level by its reader (prog3CharLevel: the best weapon's), never worked out again; the title only when it is earned (the name of the achievement); Clan your party ("Party of 3"; its tag and name once it is an alliance or was your clan; "No clan" in none), a door to the Party page; Guilds reads "Skill guilds" (the ten every player is in) and opens the Guilds page, no guild name invented; AP the worker's total, a door to Feats; Quests done your phone's count, a door to Quests. A value not arrived yet is a short pulsing bar, never a 0.
- **Not shown:** the two worked-out labels ("Sword Specialist"): their rules are the owner's to set (the master notes' F5); the identity row closes up without a gap.
- **Sideways** (63 px of page): one row, the face 56 px or so, the combat level as its own small block beside it. On a page narrower than 760 px (a 667 x 375 phone) Clan, Guilds, Titles and Journey cannot hold their words, so each becomes its picture's door (44 px; its word is its label for a screen reader): the card's rule, "the line becomes a door, never smaller text". The weapons show their level by the picture alone there.
- **Journey** (lifetime records) moved here from the Settings hub (one row of dashDests.js): it was told "moves under Bro at step 3".
- **Another player's passport** (built; nothing opens it until step 9): the same lines, named as theirs in a compact label where Journey would be; no points, no Journey (your phone's own records), Quests done "Not shared" when the worker does not have it, and no Equip.
- **Titles** (its own page under Character, Back to the passport): your title and how many you have earned at the top; the titles you have earned, newest first, the worn one marked "Equipped" (a bounded list: it scrolls inside, sideways across); **Equip** and **Remove title** in the row of actions at the bottom, each in its place, off with its reason beside it ("Already your title", "No title on"). Changing a title is routine: no confirmation. The pressed button spins while the server answers and the other is held; no answer within 3 s reads "Not confirmed yet" and stays held until the server's word arrives (src/game/pending.js; the specification's Q84). Without achievements on the server the page says so (locked); with none earned, how to earn one.

## Combat: the hub and a weapon's page (v2.3.6685, card UI2-combat PR 1)
Screens: ui2-bro-combat, ui2-combat-weapon

**What it is for:** one page a weapon, its DPS the headline, and its points spent through the preview the owner already has; the pools told apart exactly as the GAME holds them. The owner, 2026-11-25: *"Combat: Three independent weapon channels plus shared attributes. Dedicated weapon screen with level/XP, DPS, supporting stats and six attributes. Shared points/EHP belong to shared screen. Existing allocation preview opens on selection, not a permanent panel."*; the master specification's 4.2: *"Each weapon opens a dedicated screen (not a persistent weapon selector). Return to the Combat hub to switch."*

**Decisions, and why (sheet/CombatHub.jsx, sheet/WeaponPage.jsx; the rules src/game/combatHub.js, src/game/progression.js):**
- **The hub is four doors** (reference 03's structure; the kit's finish): Sword, Bow, Staff in a row, each its picture, level, a continuous XP bar and "n points"; Shared Attributes under them with the shared count. ONE vocabulary: Sword, Bow, Staff (the points screen's Melee and Magic stay under `?ui2=0`). Pictures: the passport's weapons; Shared wears the Points tab's gold bust (`points/character.png`): Combat and Shared have no door picture of their own (missing art).
- **Every count is the worker's pool**, by one rule (combatHub.js combatPools, which the Points window now reads too): a weapon's is its own points plus the free ones banked before channels existed (those are spendable anywhere, so they are in every count); the shared count is the shared pool plus the free ones. Nothing on the hub says how points are earned: the specification's "3 shared points a weapon level" is not the game's rule (shared points come with the character level, the best weapon's).
- **A weapon's page, in the room** (172 px at 390 x 844, measured on the test box): the level and its XP, now and needed, with the one true line a level gives ("Each Sword level gives 3 Sword points", the game's number); DPS the headline (raised, a chevron: a tap opens its breakdown, the stat tree, below; v2.3.6685 opened DPS's explainer), and beside it, smaller, Damage, Attack speed, Crit chance and Range (the Bro card's own strings; Element is no part of DPS, so it is not among them); "Attributes" with "n Sword points to spend" and the six, two rows of three, each its points of its cap. No preview on the page, no plus buttons, no weapon picker, no tabs: Back goes to the hub.
- **A tap on an attribute opens today's preview, unchanged**: it moved whole out of the Points window into `sheet/statWindow.jsx`, so both open the same window (the stepper at 0, before and after, the DPS row, the fight, the lane tabs with each pool's count, Spend, the window staying after it). The pool a Spend draws on is the page's weapon, named on the page and on the window's lane tab before the press. With no points it opens and reads, Spend greyed with its reason.
- **Sideways** (63 px): one row, the level, DPS, the six as 44 px doors (their word and points; no picture). The four supporting stats need about 900 px of page beside the six: narrower (every phone) they are one door, "All stats", never smaller text.
- **Shared** opens the Shared page (below, v2.3.6686); in v2.3.6685 it opened the Points window on its Shared column.
- **Another player's page** (built for step 9; nothing opens it yet): no counts of theirs, and the preview reads only (InfoPopup's read-only mode: no stepper, no Spend, no lane tabs).

## Combat: the stat tree and the Shared page (v2.3.6686, card UI2-combat PR 2)
Screens: ui2-combat-tree, ui2-combat-tree-deep, ui2-combat-shared

**What it is for:** a way down from DPS to its reasons, one layer at a time, and the shared attributes on a page of their own. The owner's master specification, 4.2: *"Tap a headline metric to see its breakdown ... Use authoritative existing combat calculations, not hardcoded illustrative numbers."*; 3.2: *"Show one layer at a time."*; the owner, 2026-11-25: *"Shared points/EHP belong to shared screen."*

**Decisions, and why (sheet/StatTree.jsx, sheet/SharedPage.jsx; the rule src/game/metricTree.js; the numbers gameSystems.js calcDisplayDps' `out`):**
- **Every number is the game's own.** The tree is built from the very lines that work out the DPS the page shows (calcDisplayDps fills an optional third argument with its parts; its callers get the same number to the last bit), with the same inputs as the page's DPS row. A guard joins every layer again by its operator and holds the root to the headline (tools/guards/metric-tree.test.mjs).
- **One layer a page.** DPS's tap opens layer 0: the headline (its number, "Sword DPS"), one line saying in words how its rows join into it, and its rows as tiles (two rows of three upright; one row sideways). DPS = Damage a swing ÷ Seconds a swing ÷ the display scale; Damage a swing = Average hit + Crits add; Average hit is halfway between the Least and the Most hit; a hit is its Hit base × the weapon's spread, to a whole number; the Hit base is (Weapon base + Level damage) × Power × Tier × Grade; Crits add = Crit chance × (a critical hit − the average hit); a critical hit is the greater of average × crit multiplier and most × the crit floor; Seconds a swing = base swing × attack speed × weapon pace (+ the staff's cast). The join is said in words because a contribution is not always a sum. Each layer is a frame page (TREE_PAGES), so Back returns layer by layer to the weapon's page; the header says DPS on each.
- **A row is raised when it opens something** (a layer with reasons of its own, chevron; the worn weapon, a door to its item card, today's card until Gear's item page) and sunk when it is a reason: it names its source, "game rule", "4 Power points", "Sword level 6".
- **Damage inside the tree is the game's own count**, which the screen shows divided by 5 (the display scale): the root line says so, and the scale is its own row, so the sums are true. Element is no part of DPS, so no row is.
- **The Shared page** is a weapon's page's shape: Max HP the headline (the health bar's number; a tap explains it), Defense beside it (the Bro card's own row), then "Attributes" with "n shared points to spend" (the worker's shared count) and the seven, four and three (upright under 520 px of page their pictures go, never their words). A tap opens today's preview on its Shared tab. **No effective-health number**: none exists in the game and its formula is the owner's to define (the master notes' F1); the headline's place can take it on the owner's word.
- **The points rule is the worker's**: a shared stat draws the shared pool or the free remainder, never a weapon's points (server/src/prog3.js, v2.3.2592). The card's older line (a weapon's points buying body stats) is retired in the code, so nothing here offers it.
- **The passport's weapon counts now read the hub's rule** (combatPools), so the two screens agree for a character with free points (PR 1's flag).

## Combat: the hub and the one template (tag 6783, card UI3-combat)
Screens: ui2-bro-combat, ui2-combat-weapon, ui2-combat-shared

**What it is for:** Combat shows three big weapons with big levels, and points only when you have some; every weapon's page (and Shared) reads top to bottom as level, then how it fights, then what you can spend, in ONE template, with the six in view at once. The owner, 2026-11-26 (`docs/design/UI_FEEDBACK_2026-11-26.md` parts 4 and 5): *"Combat. The three weapon cards have too much empty space and surprisingly small weapon icons. Make the weapons visually dominant, enlarge the level numbers and only show available points prominently when there are points to spend."* *"Weapon attributes. Currently the messiest menu. Eliminate the left-side Attributes column, consolidate the combat statistics into one summary strip, and give the six attributes a full-width three-by-two grid."* *"This is how I'd consolidate the Sword menu specifically, with Bow and Staff following the identical template ... Same information, but with a much clearer progression from level → performance → attributes. When points become available, the attributes can receive a small gold plus indicator and the available points counter can become gold."* Their standing rule: *"keep my game content over the mockup stuff"*.

**Decisions, and why (sheet/CombatHub.jsx, sheet/attrTemplate.jsx, sheet/WeaponPage.jsx, sheet/SharedPage.jsx; the rules src/game/combatHub.js: pointsShown, pointsTone, attrPlus, weaponPageFold, hubArtSize):**
- **The hub's pictures are as large as the room leaves**: upright the weapons' row is what Shared's 44 px leaves (122 px at 390 x 844), less the word, the 24 px level, the 3 px bar and the count's room: 48 px pictures (32 before), 44 at 375 x 667; the words' column keeps its height with or without a count, so nothing moves. Sideways the picture sits beside the words, 45 px (40 at 667 x 375; 26 before), the name and the count on one line, the level under them. Shared keeps its 30 px bust (26 sideways): its words are long.
- **A count is drawn only while there are points** (gold, the door glowing); at 0 NOTHING is drawn: the count stays in the door's data and in its words for a screen reader ("Staff, level 1, 0 points").
- **ONE template** (`sheet/attrTemplate.jsx`): Sword, Bow and Staff are the same component with no branch on the weapon, and Shared draws the same pieces. Row 1 the level and "x / y XP" (gold, a 3 px bar), with the one line a level gives where it fits; row 2 the summary strip: DPS the kit's STAT (sunk, large, "120.5 DPS ›", a tap opens its tree), then Damage, Attack speed, Crit chance, Range as FACTS in one container (two by two on a phone, one row of four on a page 560 px wide); row 3 the full-width line, "Attributes" on its left and the game's count on its right ("3 Sword points to spend"), gold while there are points and muted at 0, then the six full width, three by two. While there are points each of the six wears a small gold "+" (text, no art); a tap on any of them opens its preview, points or none.
- **The game's words over the mock-up's**: "Crit chance" and "Attack speed" (the mock-up's "Critical" and "Speed"), "n Sword points to spend" (its "n points available": the game has no such words); the numbers as the game prints them (no commas: "4393 / 7333 XP").
- **The room, upright**: the four bands need 172 px exactly (the level 18, the strip 44, the line 14, the six 90, three gaps of 2), which 390 x 844 holds. 375 x 667 has 168, so there the line folds onto the level row's right end (the same words, the same gold, never smaller text, never a scroll); the road line ("Each Sword level gives 3 Sword points") then leaves the row, as it does sideways, and is read in the bar's label for a screen reader. FLAGGED: the owner's to judge.
- **Sideways**: one row, as before: the level, DPS, the four facts where 900 px of page hold them (else one door, "All stats", never smaller text), then the line over the six in a row. A computer plays the upright template.
- **Shared in the same form**: Max HP the stat (a tap explains it), Defense a fact in the strip's container (no box of its own), then the line with the shared count and the seven, four and three, each its gold "+" while shared points wait.
- **Another player's pages** (step 9): no counts, no "+", the preview read only.
- **The guard** (`tools/guards/combat-hub.test.mjs` C13 to C20, each dodge planted and seen red): the left column back, "0 points" drawn on the hub, DPS or Max HP raised, a fact in a box of its own, a second template, the six out of view at 390 x 844, a tile not opening its preview, the "+" with no points.

## Bro: your pages, the Points window and Equipment
Screens: dash-bro, dash-bro-combat, dash-bro-bow, dash-bro-progress, dash-bro-points, dash-bro-equipment

**What it is for:** The Bro tab is you (the Character page until v2.3.4172): since v2.3.6600 a showcase of your character in three pages, each whole with no scroll. The owner, 2026-11-25, with four mockups: *"I also want to redesign the bro character menu area. ... I want it so that it shows "pages" where all the information needs to be displayed together (instead of scrolling little by little) so all related information is shown at once. The biggest improvement is that the Bro tab becomes a showcase of the player's character rather than a long directory of features. Players see the big picture first and can investigate the details whenever they want."* The OVERVIEW (where it opens): your portrait, name, title and level; the weapon in your hand in four numbers; your three weapon masteries; one row of your progress. COMBAT: every combat stat of the weapon you pick, at once. PROGRESS: your counters and the doors to everything else about you: Achievements, Life Skills, Points (the window where you spend the points each combat level-up gives), Equipment (the Gear tab), Homestead, Pet, Titles and Clan. `?bropages=0` gives back the one card of v2.3.4172 (the decisions below that name the card, the panels or the scroller are about it).

**Decisions, and why (v2.3.6600, the pages; card BRO, sheet/BroPages.jsx, the rules src/game/broPages.js):**
- **Pages, not a scroll.** The upright page is about 185 px tall (measured: 184 at 390 wide, 180 at 375; the band keeps today's height, the owner: "No don't change todays dashboard height"), so each page is sized to it: the mockups' contents in the game's Lantern-Slate look (the dashboard's dark wells, brass for what is picked or waiting, the spec's 10 px caption the smallest text upright). The mockups themselves still scrolled (01 ran on into 02, 03 into 04): that scrolling is what the owner wanted gone, so 03 and 04 are ONE page and the masteries and progress fit the Overview.
- **Getting about: taps only.** A strip of three page marks at the top of each page (Overview, Combat, Progress), the open one lit; on the two under the Overview its mark reads "‹ Overview" and is the back chip (mockup 03's). The Overview's combat numbers are one button ("Details": Combat on the weapon in hand), a mastery tile opens Combat on its weapon, the progress row opens Progress. No sideways swipe in this part. The page you were on is kept while the Points window or a page under Bro is open; a fresh visit (the band closed, or the Gear tab) opens the Overview.
- **The Overview.** The portrait in a gold frame, your name, your title ("No title equipped" plainly; a tap: the title picker, as the ribbon was), and "LV N" in a brass chip. NO XP bar (the owner: "no general combat xp"; the top bar shows no character number a bar could read). While points wait, a gold "N points" chip under the level glows (v2.3.3004's light) and opens the Points window: it carries data-section="Build", so the first level-up's coach ("Tap Points") and the points' flight point at the page that is open, not at a door on another page. Combat overview: DPS, Damage, Defense of the weapon IN YOUR HAND (not the one picked on Combat), and Gear worn "n/6". Weapon mastery: Sword, Bow, Staff with "Lv N", the one in hand gold-edged, "Equipped" under it only when that slot holds a weapon (an empty slot says "No sword equipped" on Combat); the XP flight from a kill lands on these tiles (xpLanding), as it landed on the card's. Your progress: one row, achievements n/33, lifetime kills, achievement points.
- **Combat: all thirteen at once.** The picker (Sword | Bow | Staff, the combat skills' pictures), then Offense (Damage, DPS, Attack speed, Range, Crit chance, Crit damage, Special damage, Element power: four across), and Survivability (Defense, Armor, Dodge, Resist) beside Mobility (Move speed) in one row. Each cell is the label over its number, without the mockup's overlap; a tap opens the stat's explainer. The numbers are the card's (sheet/broStats.js); the mockup had Armor, and the game has it. The weapon's XP ("68 / 455 XP", or "Fully levelled") sits beside the Offense heading, where the card's tiles showed it for 4 s on a tap.
- **Progress.** The three counters over the achievements bar (a tap: Achievements, as the AP and the Achievements tile did), then every door of the card as tiles two across, in the mockup's order (Life Skills, Titles, Homestead, Pet, Party or Clan, Points), then the two it left out (Achievements, Equipment). Each opens what it opened. The clan line of the card's left panel is the Clan door.
- **Side by side from 600 px.** A phone on its side (the one band's page about 78 px tall), a tablet on its side: the three pages stand side by side, no marks. Under 150 px tall is the DENSE cut: no headings, one-line cells ("Crit 1.0%"), short labels (Dmg, Def, Speed), the doors three across without their second line, the spec's 9 px metric label the smallest text. Upright on a tablet and on a computer the page is 480 px wide (the band's centred box), so one page shows at a time there, as on a phone.

**Decisions of the card before it (v2.3.4172, now behind `?bropages=0`):**
- **Three panels, as drawn** (v2.3.4172). The owner, of the first card (his own inspect card, below): "I'll need to change the layout of the bro tab, it reads like an old PC menu." Then: "I added what I want bro tab layout to look like on GitHub. One different thing is the xp bar near profile portrait (no general combat xp)" (the drawing: D474F679-6893-4890-823A-3BC96F817184.png in the repo root). And on the portrait the top-left frame lost: "character portrait are fine it's just too much screen space for persistent thing stuck on game screen ... I'm wanting the portrait in the bro section". So: LEFT, you: the game's own headshot in a gold frame, your name, "Lv N" and no XP bar (there is no general combat XP: your level is your best weapon's); the title you wear as a gold ribbon, or "No title" plainly; your clan (castle, name [TAG], or "No clan" / "No party"); your AP with the star; two tiles, Achievements n / total and Lifetime Kills. MIDDLE: the weapon picker (since v2.3.4172 the three combat-skill tiles, below), then "Combat Stats" for that weapon. RIGHT: the doors, a chevron each. No full-body figure.
- **The stats are the weapon you pick, in the game's own numbers** (sheet/broStats.js). The picker starts on the weapon in your hand and stays where you left it until the band closes. Damage, DPS, Attack Speed (swings a second, the auto-attack's own period: your Speed points, the bow's draw, the staff's cast, an attack-speed amulet; it is still the held attack's number, so a flicked bow, which may shoot a quarter sooner since v2.3.4396, shoots faster than it says: docs/specs/manual-combat.md), Range (the reach the stat demo fights with, in tiles), then the Equipment window's own pills worked out as if that weapon were out: Crit Chance, Crit Damage, Special Damage, Element Power (the weapon's element, then its lane's power), Defense, Armor, Dodge, Resist, Move Speed. The last five are shared, so they read the same under every weapon. An empty slot says "No bow equipped" and dashes, never another weapon's numbers. The drawing's numbers were placeholders: the game's units are kept (Defense in percent, Crit Damage "+N%", Element Power "+12", not a percent). Each row is a tap to its explainer.
- **The three combat skills pick the weapon** (v2.3.4172). The owner on #1386, first: "Make the weapon icons above the combat stats larger and just reuse the icons used on the 3 primary combat skills" (the Sword | Bow | Staff buttons took the Gear tab's pictures, bigger); then: "Move the 3 primary combat skills out of the gear menu and put them into the bro menu." So the buttons went: the Gear tab's own Melee, Bow and Magic tiles (one component, dash/SkillTiles.jsx) stand over "Combat Stats", and a tile IS the weapon's button. Each is its big picture with "Lv N" ABOVE it, not under: the Gear tab's order (picture, then level) reads like a weapon lying in the bag, which these are not. A tap picks that weapon's stats (its tile gold-edged, the pressed look of the Bro page; the others plain) and turns that tile into its XP bar, "68 / 455 XP" (the numbers the top bar's XP bar reads; past 9,999 "12.3K"), for 4 seconds, then its picture again, still picked; a second tap puts the picture back sooner. Only one tile shows its XP at a time. The tiles are 72-80 px tall and a third of the panel wide (66 px at the narrowest), touch-action: manipulation; a mouse clicks them the same.
