The XP that flies from a kill still lands on the Gear tab's tiles (xpLanding); Lane A takes the tiles off the Gear tab, and moves that landing, in its own change.
- **The doors** (v2.3.4172): Achievements ("n of total"), Life Skills (the drawing's "Attributes" row: the game has no STR / AGI / INT, so the slot went to the page that moved under Bro; "Total level N", a dot for an unseen level-up), Points ("N to spend", gold and glowing while N > 0, v2.3.3004's light: "until all points are spent"; "All spent" otherwise), Equipment ("n of 6 worn": it opens the Gear tab, v2.3.4151, the way the Gear button does), Homestead ("N plots ready": there is no farm window away from the farm, so a tap says it is reached from the Feed & Seed in BroTown, and the row has no chevron), Pet (its name, or "No pet": the Pets page), Titles ("N unlocked": the Achievements page, opened on the row you would pick from, the title you wear or your newest, where "Use as title" is), Clan (the clan's page). In the left panel the clan line, the AP, the ribbon and the Achievements tile are doors too. Achievements and Titles show only where the server grants achievements.
- **The shape is the page's own width, the height is today's** (the owner: "No don't change todays dashboard height"). Measured from its box, never the window: 600 px and wider (a tablet, a computer's band in its centred box of up to 1200 px (840 since v2.3.4182), a phone on its side in the one band), the three panels side by side, each its own scroller; 340-599 (an upright phone, 378 px of page), you beside the doors and the stats under them, one scroller; under 340 (the old sideways side column, `?oneband=0`, about 220 px), all three stacked. It needs no wrapper of its own: inside the band's centred box (v2.3.4152's `data-band-box`) it fills the box and no more. A phone on its side shows the least: the one band's page there is about 70 px tall, so the card shows each panel's first lines and scrolls. Upright the doors sit beside you rather than under the stats because the page is about 190 px tall there: Points is the third door, on screen when the page opens, where the coach's "Tap Points" and the big arrow can point at it; the stats are a scroll away. A taller box just shows more.
- **Points is a window over the card** (v2.3.4172): the Points screen, framed, under a title row with a close (Esc on a computer) that comes back to the card, the weapon you picked still picked. The Equipment window (your figure, your gear, the stat families) is still there, but only for the roads that ask for it by name: since the Gear tab the card's Equipment door opens Gear, which holds what you wear. Achievements, Life Skills, Pets and the clan are pages pushed under Bro, their back chip returning to the card; every old road to Achievements or Life Skills (the L key, the top bar's medal, a card's title chip) lands there.
- **The tabs are words** (two tabs, Equipment and Points, from v2.3.3773 until v2.3.4172's card: owner: "I also think I want 'journey' to be put as its own icon in the more/other apps menus. That way the two main ones, equipment and points, stand out further as just two tabs."). Journey, your records, was the third tab; it is More → Journey now (below, and the More page's notes). The tabs became words in v2.3.1847 (owner: "for the 3 tabs on the character menu I think I'd prefer text"). "Build" became "Points" (v2.3.1849, owner: "instead of build name it points"), so the word matches its badge of unspent points. The windows' title row stays pinned where the tabs were (v2.3.2600, owner: "make sure the cells don't slide above the headers"). On a computer, P opens the card.
- **Equipment: you, your slots, your vitals, left to right** (v2.3.1653, owner: "move the equipped view to be merged with the character overview so the equipped slots are grouped on the left and the player stats are shown on the right"). You are as big as fits, on the left (v2.3.1841, owner: "I want the character on the character menu to display larger and be in the left side"), facing southeast (v2.3.3577, owner: "make the character face southeast instead of southwest"; southwest until then), in your armour. You hold the weapon in your hand (v2.3.1914; the owner: "When different weapons are equipped like pine bow and staff the character preview doesn't update").
- **A tapped slot's card opens where the vitals were** (v2.3.1843, owner: "It's fine if the card opens over where the vitals are"), so nothing moves or gets cut off. Its name and CHANGE button sit on its frame (v2.3.1844, owner: "put it on its own card. Like the GREATSWORD and CHANGE are the thick border of the card").
- **Every stat is a pill: picture, name, value** (v2.3.3053, the owner's mockup: "Add pill design for hero equipment menu stats"). OFFENSE is outlined in gold, with DPS beside its heading; PLAYER in blue; HP, EN and MP are coloured pills that are the bars themselves. All fourteen read the same way the Points tab does, so the two never disagree. Tap a pill for what it does, with your own value (v2.3.2131, owner: "more pop ups for things users want to learn more about on the character equip menu"). The gold and blue are an exception to the UI rules licensed for this tab only.
- **Two words differ from the mockup on purpose:** "Elem Power +12" (a power number, not a percent), and Attack Speed as "+25%".
- **The Equipment tab scrolls, with a bottom fade.** Fourteen readable pills need about 330 px of a page about 150 px tall. The first screen still shows you, your gear, your vitals, and OFFENSE with DPS and Damage.
- **The stats are in families, an accordion (v2.3.3605).** The owner: "each major combat resource grouped under families ... even HP to show what's contributing ... Accordion style family pills so it can all display without scrolling down". Damage and Defense are pills under the bars; the HP bar is the Health family and the EN and MP bars the Energy family (a tap opens them). An open family covers the figure row, its rows two to a line, and its header shuts it. Health shows its parts (base, level, Max HP points, gear, anything else the worker's bar holds), which always add up to the bar. Don't split them back into fourteen pills: that is what scrolled.
- **Points: every stat on one screen** (v2.3.2683, the owner's mockup: "I want the menu under the points tab to look like this and use these color glyphs instead"). The top row is the six weapon stats (Range, Power, Speed, Luck, Special, Elemental), behind a gold frame of the three weapons. The bottom row is the seven character stats (HP, Defense, Mana, Stamina, Dodge, Move Speed, Resist), behind a gold frame of your character.
- **The two frames are the owner's Points art** (v2.3.3559, a sheet and a mockup: "On the character points screen swap out the old style icons with these. It should look similar to the second screenshot mockup but it won't have the shaped backgrounds behind the icons. Also the character icon should have the up arrow instead of the 4 point star"). The weapons' frame holds three rows, sword, staff and bow -- the owner's thicker icons, sent after the first cut ("Actually swap out just the icons for these slightly thicker ones I think these will look better at that tiny scale for sword, staff, and bow", v2.3.3559) -- each with its count on its right and a carved seam between them, a dark line over a faint warm one with nothing on it (the owner's pick of seven dividers drawn on the tab: "Carved seam is the best", v2.3.3559); the character's frame holds the gold bust with the up arrow, its count under it (the cell is taller than wide: side by side both would be tiny). No shape behind any icon. The two frames are one width, one over the other, as in the mockup; sideways the frames' column is a little wider (1.45 of a cell) so the weapons are 13 px, not 9, and SPEED and STAM write short there like the others. The spend window's lane tabs wear the same pictures (docs/specs/points-heads.md).
- **Two kinds of points** (v2.3.2592, owner: "for every point earned through one of the 3 combat channels, you earn one 'shared' point too"). A weapon's points buy only that weapon's stats; shared points buy only the character stats.
- **Your level is your best weapon's level** (v2.3.3538, the after-review plan the owner agreed on 2026-10-21: "one level that means one thing"). A new character is level 1. Every name plate, the dashboard's "Lv", the Combat rank and the "Shared — Level N" heading show that one number. Each weapon level-up still pays that weapon 3 points. The 3 shared points come when your level rises, so a weapon below your best pays none. Max HP is 200 + 50 a level, so a new character's 250 and every one-weapon bar are unchanged. Every existing character's points were handed back once to spend again.
- **No frame round the heads** (v2.3.3691, owner: "in the points menu the 3 primary combat skills and the character icon shouldn't have the bronze frame around it because it's not self-contained it's supposed to represent the whole row of different skills you can apply points in"). The two gold frames of v2.3.3559 are gone: each head is its row's label, sitting in the row's own tray, its icons, counts and carved seams where they were. Don't put a frame, plate or box back round a head; the row's tray is what groups it with its cells.
- **A cell shows what you have put in, and a frame's count shows what is left.** The cells' numerals are white (v2.3.2692, owner: "try making the outline of all the number stats ... white"), zeros dimmed, and each cell is outlined in its glyph's colour (v2.3.2687, owner: "I'd rather have the cell outline be whatever the main icon color is"). There is one count per weapon and one for the character (v2.3.2689, owner: "One number on each combat type icon (melee, bow, staff) then just one for the character"), in the Points sheet's numerals since v2.3.3559: gold while that pool has a point to spend, grey at 0 (the sheet's own colour rule), and the exact number, never "99+". The counts glow and swell while points wait (v2.3.3052, owner: "glow and grow and shrink if there are still points that need to be allocated"), slowly: a 3.2 s breath (v2.3.3559, owner: "If there are unallocated numbers waiting to be spent make them slowly grow then shrink"); a grey 0 stays still. The Character button and the Points tab glow too (v2.3.3004).
- **The top row follows the weapon in your hand** (v2.3.3051, owner: "So if you have melee equipped show the points you've allocated for melee. If bow show for bow"). Its row is lit in the weapons' frame (it sat on a brass plate until v2.3.3559: a shape behind an icon). The weapon cell was a label, not a button (v2.3.2684, owner: "The weapon icon row is not meant to be button ... It's a tab in the confirm window. This should be for every allocable stat").
- **...and a tap on a weapon's row shows that weapon** (v2.3.3665, owner: "the points page needs a way to change between views between melee, bow, staff. It's stuck on whatever weapon you have equipped but there should be a way to toggle between the 3"). The three rows in the gold frame are a switch: tap the sword, staff or bow and the six cells show its points, its row lit. Changing the weapon in your hand brings the view back to it. This is why the v2.3.2684 rule no longer holds for the rows: then a tap on them would have done nothing that the [+] window doesn't. Each row is a third of the frame, about 55 x 21 px upright: small for a thumb, but a miss only shows the next weapon. Don't add a separate Melee/Bow/Magic switch above the grid: the frame already lists the three, and the sheet has no height to spare.
- **One window explains and spends** (v2.3.2597, owner: "having the confirmation window be the same as the informational window would be better. It tells you what it does and asks you to confirm point at the bottom"). It is short on words (v2.3.2696, owner: "It's a lot of words but good information. What's a better solution to make the user understand without reading a manual?"): one line, big numbers, a bar of how far the stat is toward its maximum, and two chips, "Weaker vs. higher levels" and "Details".
- **[-] n [+] [Spend]** (v2.3.2695, owner: "make it so you can add points more quickly using +/- buttons then confirm spend button to the right of it"). It never offers more than you have or the stat can take. With nothing to buy, the button stays and says why (the owner's "grayed out with that explanation"), and so it does while the connection is down (v2.3.3034).
- **The count starts at 0, and [+] adds every point** (v2.3.3577, owner: "when there's only 1 point left to add in your stat point allocation screen, still enable adding the last point using the plus button. Right now it looks disabled"). It used to start at 1, so with one point left [+] was already at its most and greyed. Now [+] adds each point, the last one too, and Spend reads "Add points with +" until it has something to spend. At 0 the window still shows what one point buys.
- **No stat has a cap** (v2.3.3578, owner, asked why one stat could only take a certain number of points: "Remove stat cap."). [+] stays live while you have a point to spend, whatever the stat holds, and the labels say "4 points" where they said "4 of 10". What stops one stat running away is what each point buys: the curve, and the 10% floor under Dodge and Defense. Don't suggest a per-stat limit again.
- **The window stays after Spend** (v2.3.3522, owner: "After spending a stat allocation point keep it on the menu confirmation window instead of closing it to return to the points main screen."). Spend reads "Spending…" until the server answers, then the same window shows the new value and the next point's before-and-after, with "✓ 3 points spent" under the button and the stepper back at 0 (at 1 until v2.3.3577). With no points left the button says why, as above. If no answer comes in about 3 s, Spend comes back.
- **Closing the window touches nothing under it** (v2.3.3560, found on the Points tab; the owner: "Yes begin the fix"). Its ✕, the dim round it and "Got it" close it as the finger lifts, and that tap's click, which the browser sends a moment later to whatever the window covered, is eaten: on a phone the ✕ of a shared stat's window lies over the minimap and used to open the world map. On a computer the minimap's invisible map button used to lie over the ✕ itself. The gear picker's ✕ and the sale notice work the same way (docs/specs/tap-through.md).
- **The scene is a real simulated fight** with the meadow slime, using your own character, weapon and points (v2.3.2979, owner: "shows real simulation of the hits against a slime monster"). "Now" plays above and "+n" below, at the same time and on the same dice (v2.3.3002, owner: "live side by side simulations of the before and after effects"; asked how, the owner chose stacked). Your bro moves with the world's own animations (v2.3.2986–2991), and Max MP has a scene too (v2.3.3008).
- **The numbers match the bars.** Max HP shows the pool's new total, as its bar will (v2.3.3050: the owner saw "48hp for 6 points" when spending made it "actually 37"). DPS has two decimals (v2.3.2525, owner: "Decimals are fine for tuning combat skills"). Shared stats show no DPS line (owner: "Remove the line about DPS on this window for all shared points").
- **Reset points (free)** at the foot of the grid gives every placed point back, so you can try a different build (v2.3.3541, the after-review list: "Today, points you spend are spent for good. After the change one button gives them back"). Two taps: the first arms it and says what it does ("Tap again: every point comes back"), the second within 4 s resets; left alone it goes back. It shows only with a point placed, and the server refuses it in a fight or within a minute of the last one, saying so over your bro. No second window: the button's own words are the confirm.
- **Your first level-up is shown, never chosen for you** (v2.3.3551, owner: "Leave tips for first natural level up. I don't want players choosing xp to train in for weapons they haven't used yet. Go with your guided level up."). The first time points wait and none has ever been placed, the coach rings the way in, one step at a time, with your own count: the handle, then Bro, then Points (OPEN, Character and the Points tab until v2.3.4172), then the grid's top row ("Top row: 3 weapon points for your weapon's stats. Bottom row: 3 shared points for any stat there. Tap a stat to see what it does."). Your first Spend adds "Not sure? Reset points (free), under the grid, gives them all back." under "✓ 1 point spent". It waits out a fight, runs past the Mayor's tutorial (a first level can come after it), and is gone for good once a point is placed or its card is tapped. Left unanswered it steps aside, and after three times it stops. It never picks a stat, and XP still goes only where you fought.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- Stat boxes, then list rows (v2.3.1890, owner: "every stat is being treated as its own card ... switch to a character-sheet/list format"), then no icons (v2.3.1883b, owner: "Don't use any icons to represent the stats to save room"), then pills with icons (v2.3.3053): the newest ask, paid for by the scroll.
- Icon-only tabs (v2.3.1657; words again in v2.3.1847).
- Three tabs, the third Journey (v2.3.1847-v2.3.3772); Journey moved to its own More tile (v2.3.3773).
- **Accuracy on Journey** (v2.3.4545; the owner: "I want somewhere that stats keep track of 'accuracy' (maybe for all 3 weapon types)"): under the six record cards, three cells in the cards' own dress with no icon (the number is the picture): the weapon, the percentage, the shots; one column sideways as the cards are; one muted line saying what counts ("Basic attacks that hit an enemy. Specials are not counted.") and "Your last 200" once there is more behind you than those. A dash before the first shot, never "0%". Drawn only against a worker that counts (`caps.accuracy`). Not on the unreachable Stats page (TRAPS §319), not on the inspect card (the default put to the owner on 2026-11-10 and gone ahead with: each player's own, and the owner's totals; not shown to other players, not a leaderboard).
- Two tabs, Equipment and Points, opening on Equipment, under a Character button (v2.3.3773-v2.3.3940).
- Five tabs, Me, Points, Skills, Achievements and Equipment, opening on Me, a plain player card (the first cut of the owner's five buttons, in the same change, never shipped): the owner wanted the inspect card instead.
- Your own inspect card (the first card of v2.3.4172, never shipped): the Inspect card filled with your data, its "Spend points (N)" stuck to its foot, your stats as pills under the six slots. The owner: "it reads like an old PC menu"; his drawing replaced it.
- Points layouts before the owner's grid: a list (v2.3.1660), a grid (v2.3.1668), weapon lanes one open at a time (v2.3.2176, owner: "I earned a point. Where do I want to spend it?"), columns opening sideways (v2.3.2592–2594), and a 2x2 you drill into (v2.3.2597).
- A big [+] with a separate "are you sure" window (v2.3.2595); made-up, exaggerated scenes (v2.3.2222); before and after shown in turn; "N PTS" / "N SPENT" on every tile.
- Spend closed the window back to the grid (until v2.3.3522).
- The three weapons fanned down a diagonal, each with a blue badge, and your portrait with its badge (v2.3.2685-v2.3.2692), sideways a 7 px brass pill; replaced by the owner's Points art (v2.3.3559).
- The Points sheet's own finer-lined sword, staff and bow (the first cut, v2.3.3559): the owner swapped them for thicker ones that read at 12-17 px.
- A gold rule with a small diamond in its middle between the weapons, the Points art's first cut (owner: "remove the diamonds on the little separators ... It just muddles the nice crisp image differentiating the 3 combat types", v2.3.3559). Tried in its place, on the tab: the plain gold rule, a one-device-pixel gold line, the UI's divider colour, a flat gold line and no line; the owner chose the carved seam (docs/shots/points-heads-dividers.webp).

**Please don't suggest:**
- A scroll on the Bro pages, or the pages run on into each other, to fit more (the owner: "instead of scrolling little by little"); more on a page means less on another, or the dense cut.
- An XP bar by the portrait, or a level bar made from the weapons' XP: there is no general combat XP.
- Dropping the pills' pictures, or going back to list rows, to save room. The scroll is the agreed cost.
- Icon-only tabs, or calling Points "Build".
- Putting Journey back as a tab: the owner moved it to More.
- Life Skills or Achievements back on a button or tile of their own: the owner moved both into Bro.
- A tab row on Bro, an XP bar by the portrait (the owner: "no general combat xp"), a full-body figure on the page, or an "Attributes" (STR / AGI / INT) row: the game has none.
- A tappable weapon cell, or choosing the weapon anywhere but the window's tabs.
- A second confirm window, a points-left count on every cell, or before and after shown in turn.
- Closing the window after a spend (the owner asked for it to stay).
- "Element Dmg +12%" in place of "Elem Power +12", or an item card that pushes the character or vitals around.
- Shapes behind the heads' icons (the owner: "it won't have the shaped backgrounds behind the icons"), or your portrait back in place of the bust with the up arrow.
- A diamond or any other ornament on the seams between the weapons, or a different divider there.
- Thinner, finer-lined or more detailed weapon icons in the frame: they are drawn 12-17 px, and the owner chose the thick-outlined ones for that size.

**Still open:**
- Sideways at rest the Bro button is folded away behind the ▴ chip, so its glow cannot be seen. Lighting the chip is noted as "a one-line follow-up if wanted" (v2.3.3004).

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/dash-tabs.md, docs/specs/hero-sheet-pills.md, docs/specs/one-level.md, docs/specs/points-reset.md, docs/specs/levelup-tips.md, docs/specs/progression-v3.md, docs/specs/relative-points.md, docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, src/ui/mobile/sheet/HeroExpanded.jsx, src/ui/mobile/sheet/BroCard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/sheet/broStats.js, src/styles/game.css

## Your portrait: a Hemi Bro
**OFF since v2.3.4049** (owner, 2026-11-03: "Sorry but I don't like the pfp"): your
portrait is the game's own headshot again everywhere; `?hemiface=1` shows the
Hemi Bro below for one tab. Its two review screens went with it. The
townsfolk's dialogue busts are not this and stay Hemi Bros.

**What it is for:** Your face on the Character button, in the strip with your name, in the level-up medallion and on every other player's card: who you are, at a glance.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **It is a Hemi Bro** (v2.3.3999, owner: "For profile picture art I want it in the style of the original hemi bro NFT's as much as possible with the custom stuff added on to it"). Hemi Bros, the NFT collection the game is named for, is 3,333 busts of 32 x 32 pixels turned to the viewer's left on a flat background. Your portrait is such a bust of your look, built from the collection's own trait art, by the same rules as the townsfolk's dialogue heads: your skin, hair, hat, beard, eyes, eyewear and shirt (its colour and pattern) are the collection's pieces recoloured or combined, never redrawn.
- **Bald under a hat, the ear below the brim.** The collection wears hair OR a hat; a band (a bandana, the headphones) and what perches (ears, horns, a crown, the halo) sit on the hair.
- **The background is one of the collection's seven flat colours**, the one furthest from your shirt colour, so the bust never melts into it.
- **Crisp, never smoothed**: each of its 32 x 32 pixels is a flat block, drawn pixelated wherever it is shown.
- **What the collection has not got wears the nearest piece** (wavy and long hair, the light bulb, the 3D and white glasses, one-eye, demon and alien eyes; docs/specs/hemi-portrait.md lists each with an art brief). The monkey keeps the game's own portrait. Worn armour is left out.

**Please don't suggest:**
- A drawn portrait of the 3D figure (the game's own headshot, before v2.3.3999): the owner asked for the collection's style. It is still one switch away (`?hemiface=0`, or the Test panel's "Your portrait as a Hemi Bro").

**Still open:**
- The art for the looks with no match yet.

Sources: docs/specs/hemi-portrait.md, docs/specs/npc-creator-looks.md, src/rendering/hemiBust.js, src/rendering/hemiLook.js, src/rendering/hemiBustCore.js

## Quests under the new dashboard (v2.3.6691, card UI2-quests)
Screens: ui2-quest-landing, ui2-quest-page, ui2-quest-rewards, ui2-quest-road

**What it is for:** open Quests and see the quests you are on; tap one and see what you have done and what to do NOW, with Track and Show on map beside it, and nothing of what is still to come. The owner, 2026-11-25, the master specification's part 6: *"Landing page shows **active quests first**, as direct selectable rows/destinations. No tiny dropdown or three-dot pagination. Daily quests and Completed quests are secondary destinations. Keep long lists scrollable within the dashboard."*; *"Individual quest shows **journey first**, with completed steps and the current objective visually dominant. Current task includes relevant progress and contextual actions such as Track or Locate when supported. Future journey is open-ended: do not reveal future steps, total step count, or whole-quest completion percentage. A neutral indication that the adventure continues is sufficient for an ongoing quest. Completed quests show actual completed history. Story and Rewards are separate accessible destinations. Preserve existing quest logic and data; do not rewrite quest narratives based on wireframe examples."* Their standing rule: *"Yeah my game content over the mockup stuff"*.

**Decisions, and why (dash/QuestsLanding.jsx, dash/QuestPage.jsx, drawn by dash/questViews.jsx; the rules src/game/questPages.js questLanding, journeyToNow, questActions, questOpens):**
- **The landing is rows**, under the one header ("Quests"): the giver's face, the quest's name, its count ("0/4") where it counts, a green "Ready" once it can be handed in, the gold star on the one you track, which comes first (the journal's own order). The rows scroll inside their zone; the doors under them never scroll away: **Daily** (only when the game shows its daily rewards card today), **Completed (n)**, and **On offer (n)** when someone has a quest for you (today's picker's second half: nothing is lost). No tabs, no page marks, no picker (card QP's tabs and three marks are what part 6 rules out).
- **Nothing in hand** is the kit's empty state with the game's own line, and the same doors; the worker's answer not in yet is the kit's placeholder rows, never "no quest" (Q84).
- **A quest's page** (header "Quest"): the giver's face, the name and its state; the steps behind you (a green tick, their words, what the giver handed over) in a zone that scrolls and opens on the last; then **NOW**, framed in gold and never behind a scroll: the thing's picture, what to do, its count and bar, the place; then one quiet line, **"The adventure continues"**. A step still to come is never drawn; the hand-in is not drawn until the quest is ready, and then it IS the now; there is no "Then: ..." line, no count of steps, no percent.
- **Its actions** sit at the bottom: **Track** (a quest in hand only; at once, no confirmation, no pending mark: it is the phone's own; turned on, the quiet line says for five seconds what it does, "Your map's gold star follows this quest") and **Show on map** (only where the game can show the road; absent, never greyed, where it cannot). **Rewards** is a raised door beside them, not an action: its page shows the completion rewards as the game's tiles. **Story is left out**: the game has no story text for a quest, and the door comes with the text.
- **A done quest** (from Completed) is its whole history: every step done, the hand-in done, its Rewards door and the journal's own "Next: ..." line (what to do now after it); no Track, no map. **An offer** (from On offer): NOW is the talk to its giver, Show on map where the game maps it, nothing past the talk.
- **Back follows the history**: Rewards goes over the quest's page, so Back returns to the quest and Back again to the landing at its scroll. The pinned quest card's road lands on the quest's own page with its way home ("World"): the quest's page no longer hands over to the tab (dashNav.js HANDS_OVER is empty).
- **Sideways** (about 63 px of page) the landing's doors sit at the rows' end, and a quest's name and steps stand on the left with NOW and its actions beside them. A computer plays the upright layout.
- **Text colours are the kit's** (v2.3.6691, the kit's R9 in `ui3-weights.test`: a `color:` reads a text token or a meaning the guard knows, and it knows no done or full): a full count and "Ready" read gold, an in-progress state ivory, a done state and the steps behind you muted (the green tick's disc stays); before, they were three hand-written greens and blues. A picture's words changed colour, nothing else moved.
- **Under `?ui2=0`** today's pages (card QP's QuestPages.jsx; `?ui2=0&questpages=0` the older list beside the quest), unchanged.

**Flagged for the owner:** the game has no story text for a quest (writing it is content); "Daily quests" in the specification is the game's daily rewards card (the door says "Daily"); the quest that comes next ("Then: talk to ... for ...") is read as future journey and hidden while a quest is in hand.

## The quest log
Screens: ui2-quest-landing, ui2-quest-page

_v2.3.6691: the history of the Quests screens before the new dashboard (under `?ui2=0` today's pages, played by mp-questpages); the new pages are the section above._

**What it is for:** The quest journal (v2.3.3944). It answers, at a glance, which quest you are on, what it asks, where to go, who you have talked to, what you were handed and will be paid, and what to do after it. Taking a quest and handing it in happen only with its giver, in the world.

**Since v2.3.6620 (card QP): the tab is three pages, each whole with no scrolling.** The owner, 2026-11-25, of the Bro tab first: "it shows "pages" where all the information needs to be displayed together (instead of scrolling little by little) so all related information is shown at once ... Players see the big picture first and can investigate the details whenever they want"; then of this tab, with three mockups: "Same idea here I want the quest menu redesigned to this". The band keeps today's height (owner, earlier: "No don't change todays dashboard height"): about 190 px on an upright iPhone, so each page is built to that (mp-questpages measures 390 x 844 and 375 x 667). The rules are `src/game/questPages.js` (one field, `view`, one transition); the pages `src/ui/mobile/dash/QuestPages.jsx`; `?questpages=0` gives back the page below.
- **Page 1, the quest** (it opens first): the row **Active (n) | Daily | Completed** with the three page marks at its end; the **head** (the giver's face, the quest's name, "IN PROGRESS · Mayor Bro", Track); **what to do now** (the thing's picture where the bag has one, the words, the count and its bar: a tap opens the journey); **where** (the pin, the place, Map, the whole row the Show on map road it was); **Rewards** in one row of tiles (gold, the points, the items with their pictures: a tap opens page 3). The tabs are the owner's own mockup's row (the "no tabs again" below was of v2.3.1298's tabs, which hid the quest itself; these hold one each of Daily and Completed, and the quest is the page).
- **Page 2, the quest journey**: the head, then the steps as a line: done green with a tick (talking to the giver, with what he handed over by its pictures: the mockup's "Received"), the step in hand gold with its place and count, the steps to come grey ("Hand it in to Mayor Bro"). Whole for up to four steps; a longer one scrolls, opening on the step in hand.
- **Page 3, rewards and what is next**: the head with the count, **Completion rewards** as tiles, and the **next quest** (its giver's face, its name, who and where, a lock and "Available after <this quest>"), from the same table the old NEXT line read (the giver's chain, then the tour). Where nothing names a next quest, the journal's own NEXT line.
- **Getting about**: the marks (a tap each), the "what to do now" block, the rewards row; on pages 2 and 3 the top row is the page's name and the marks (the mockups draw no tabs there); the Quest mark or the Active tab is page 1. Taps only.
- **Several quests in hand**: the head is the picker's button (a ▾ after the name): Active (the tracked one first), then Available, as the old list's pills; a tap opens that quest's page 1. The picker, Completed and Daily are lists and may scroll.
- **A quest opened by name** (the pinned card's "Open quest") lands on its page 1 in the tab, not on a page of its own: a pushed page's header would leave it no room.
- **Track** says what it does for five seconds where the status line was ("Your map's gold star follows this quest.").
- **Wide** (the page's own box 600 px or more: a tablet, a phone on its side) the three pages stand side by side under one head, no marks. On a phone on its side the band is about 80 px tall, so there the head and the tabs share one row and the whole scrolls, as the old page did.
- **Left out of the mockups, because the game does not know them**: a step's "West of town" and "Received" words of its own (the place is the journal's "Earth Forest, west of BroTown"; what was handed over is the giver's own list), the anvil picture (the next giver's face stands there).
- **The words written in the old page but not on the pages** (each is still one tap away or said another way): REWARD's "Paid by Mayor Bro when you hand this quest in" is the tiles' tooltip, under the heading "Completion rewards"; WHO's face and "Talked to Mayor Bro ✓" are the head and the journey's first step; "1 more person has a quest: look for a gold !" is not drawn (the picker lists every offer).

**Before the pages (v2.3.3944 to v2.3.6620), and still the page behind `?questpages=0`:**
- **A menu since v2.3.4394: your quests are a list of pills, the open one beside it.** The owner, 2026-11-09: "The quests page actually needs to be reorganized as a useful menu interface. Right now everything scrolls too long and it's not a good quest management system. It needs to be able to help you navigate active quests, next steps, locations, etc easily on a mobile device." The v2.3.3944 journal was one column: the points, one whole quest (about 700 px of it), the daily card, then three lists, in a band about 190 px tall. To reach a second quest you scrolled past all of the first. Now it is the Leaderboards' shape, which the owner had just approved ("This is looking good"):
  - **The list** (left, scrolls by itself, each pill a 44 px target): **Active**, **Available**, then **More** (Done with its count, Daily). A quest's pill wears the pin its giver wears in the world: the grey "?" while you work on it, the gold ring and tick once it is ready to hand in, the gold "!" for one on offer; the quest you track wears a brass star. Done wears a green tick, Daily the prize wheel.
  - **The open quest** (right) is a head and then one card an item. The owner, of the first cut: "the quest detail menu is still pretty messy. I like clean separation between each menu item. Like the 'make 3 box traps at the woodworker' and the '0/3 box traps' line beneath it all run together ... display the profile picture of each NPC beside the quest. Think of the best way to arrange the layout so that mobile users can see the most pertinent data at a glance."
    - **The head**: the giver's face, the title, the status ("In progress", "Ready to hand in"), and the **Track** star (44 px).
    - **NOW**: what to do, a card under its own brass word.
    - **The count and its bar**: a card of its own. A number you glance at is never the tail of a sentence; it turns green when you have it all.
    - **WHERE**: the place, led by a map pin, and the whole card is the Show on map button (a brass "Map" chip at its right end says so). Where a thing is and how to get there are one thing to a player.
    - That is the order a player asks in (what, how far along, where), and all of it is on screen at once on a 390 px phone with no scrolling (mp-questjournal measures it: about 177 px of the band).
    - A scroll below, the same cards for WHO and the steps, GIVEN TO YOU, REWARD and NEXT.
  - **The giver's face is on his quest**: in the head, and on the quest's pill in the list, with the quest's state as a small mark on the face's corner (his pin, or the brass star of the quest you track).
  - **Tried and changed (v2.3.4394, the same day)**: Map and Track as two buttons in the head and NOW, the count and WHERE as plain lines under it ("messy", the count ran into the sentence); "Mayor Bro · In progress" under the title (cut to "In progr…": the face says who); the word WHERE inside its card (it made the place three lines beside the Map chip, and the card no longer fit the screen: a pin leads it instead).
  - **One tap moves between quests**; the list never leaves the screen and no page is pushed.
  - **Done** opens the points your quests have paid and each quest handed in with its +5 AP; a tap opens that quest, "Done quests" comes back.
  - **It still opens on the quest you are on** (the tracked one, else the newest in hand, else the first on offer). A quest you pick stays open until it changes hands (taken, handed in), when the page goes back to the one you are on.
  - **Sideways** (a band about 80 px tall) the pills are one row over the quest.
  - "★ Tracked" is not written in the status line any more: the lit Track button says it, and the line stays one line.
- **It opens on the quest you are on, in full** (v2.3.3944, owner: "Make the 'quest' button in the menu actually useful. Right now it's just a confusing menu with barely any utility and it's not very easy to read. Make it a place that makes it clear which quest they are on, what's required, where to go, who was talked to and what to do next, etc. show rewards preview, what was handed during the quest too."). The quest you track, else the newest one you took, else the next quest someone has for you ("Your next quest"). The first playtest's trouble was "After finishing mayor bro quest that awards sword and shield it wasn't apparent what to do next", so after a hand-in the page opens on what is next.
- **A quest's page is six plain parts, top to bottom** (the words: `src/game/questJournal.js`, `docs/specs/quest-journal.md`):
  - **NOW**: the one thing to do (a quest with steps: its current step, the objective under it), the count ("0/4 Slime Remains") and a bar, brass while you work and green once it is all there (green means done here: never green for "in progress"). Ready: "You have it all: hand it in to Mayor Bro."
  - **WHERE**: the place in words ("Earth Forest, west of BroTown"; "At the forge, in BroTown"; the giver and where he stands for a hand-in or an offer), and **Show on map**: the world map, with the gold road on that quest (a quest in hand is tracked first, as the road follows the tracked quest).
  - **WHO**: the giver's face and name, "Talked to Mayor Bro ✓" (from the quest being taken: nothing records a conversation), and each step with ✓ when done and ▶ for the next (v2.3.2820: players would "get stuck on the quest for cooking 2 fish").
  - **GIVEN TO YOU**: what the giver handed over for saying yes, in every state. Before, it showed only while the quest was on offer, so the page forgot the sword and shield the moment you had them.
  - **REWARD**: gold, XP, the items with their pictures (v2.3.1710, owner: "Quest item thumbnail rewards are not shown in the quest panel until after you accept the quest") and "+5 Achievement Points", with "Paid by Mayor Bro when you hand this quest in" (v2.3.1704, owner: "The quest UI is a little confusing what's rewards for the next quests vs what's rewarded for the current quest").
  - **NEXT**: one line of what comes after: the giver's next quest in his chain, else the town tour's next step ("Then: talk to Blacksmith Bro at the forge for “Heavy Metal”."), else a quest still in hand; and how many others have work ("look for a gold ! on your map").
- **Achievement Points from quests** (at the top until v2.3.4394; at the head of Done since, where the quests that paid them are listed) (owner: "Also keep track of achievement points gained from each quest (make each quest give them)"). Every hand-in already paid +5 (server `quests.js`, `QUEST_AP_REWARD`) and no quest screen said so; now every page's REWARD says "+5 Achievement Points", every Done row "+5 AP", and the total is at the top. The number is held to the worker by mirror-audit.
- **(Until v2.3.4394) One scroll, no tabs**: the page it opens on, then Active (the tracked quest first, then the ones ready, each row with a 48 px ★ Track star), Available (who has a quest for you and where: "Farmer Bro, at the Feed & Seed"), Done. The three tabs (v2.3.1298) hid two thirds of the answer behind a tap each. On a wide band the page and the list sit side by side, from the panel's measured box, never the window's.
- **Every offer is listed** (v2.3.3944). One offer at a time (v2.3.1681, owner: "must finish one to begin another") was about old givers who were not in the world; the owner's tour (v2.3.3525: "mayor bro is the only quest available for the first quest then after completing that other NPCs begin having quest icons appear above their head") opens the whole town after the Mayor's first quest, and the maps already show every gold "!". The tour's suggestion is listed first. Before the first quest is handed in, the Mayor's is still the only one.
- **Readable on a phone** (owner: "not very easy to read"): body text 15-16 px, section labels 13 px brass capitals, the sheet's own high-contrast ink, no tiny chips; mp-questjournal checks nothing on the page is under 13 px.
- **Track leads the way** (v2.3.3522, owner: "I tapped track quest in quest menu but no golden stars were in minimap (I had two active quests - one from beast master and the other from mayor bro)"). The ★ on a row, "☆ Track this quest" on a quest's page and Show on map all set the tracked quest, which the minimap's gold star, the world map's road and the pinned quest card follow. The page says so: "Your map's gold star follows this quest."
- **No Accept and no Turn In buttons here; the owner removed both.** "Disable turning in quest rewards (completion) through the quest pane. It's getting messed up" (v2.3.1704). "the landscape quest module lets you accept a quest from the menu (this was disabled a while ago and should remain disabled)" (v2.3.2195): accepting from the menu skipped Mayor Bro handing over the starter weapons.
- **The Quests button's badge** lights only for a quest ready to hand in, never for an offer.
- **The pinned quest card** (top left of the world) shows the active quest's title and next step (the tracked one, when you track one: v2.3.3522), with the count at its right when the quest counts ("3/5", v2.3.3918), and a tap opens that quest's page (v2.3.3918; it folded until then, v2.3.1714). The green check means ready only, with "Done! Go back to <giver>" (v2.3.3918). The card is src/ui/QuestCard.jsx, its words src/game/questCard.js, its top `var(--bt-hud-top, 56px)` so a frame above it can push it down. Its scroll picture turns into a painted green check once you have everything (v2.3.3048, owner: "Make the quest notification turn to a green checkmark if you have everything you need to complete the quest").
- **The giver's head says the same:** a gold "!" pin for an offer, a still grey "?" pin while he waits (never green: owner, v2.3.3336, "Green implies something is done"; grey, the owner's pick, v2.3.3481), and a gold ring with a green check when you can hand it in.
- **The way to go is the gold road on the minimap** (v2.3.2992, owner: "I think I want to remove the footsteps and just rely on the gold road on the minimap of where to go"). So the "Quest path" switch (v2.3.2896) is hidden along with the road on the ground.
- **The Daily rewards card, under the quest page and above the list** (at the top until v2.3.3944, which moved it down so the page opens on the quest you're on) opens the Daily Rewards window (v2.3.3140). It is here because this is where you look when deciding what to do next; the code notes the owner wants the HUD kept clear, and More's grid is full. Since v2.3.3400 it says "free spin ready" or your streak (owner: "Remove the daily quests they're randomly appearing as complete and it makes no sense to me", then "Yes drop the season"). Nothing opens at login (owner: "Personally I find the login page with the chest intrusive").

**Tried before, and changed:**
- One long feed (before v2.3.1298); three tabs, Active · Available · Completed, with 12-13 px rows (v2.3.1298 until v2.3.3944); a Turn In button and an XP picker on the quest page (until v2.3.1704); an Accept button there (v2.3.1665–2195).
- Every old giver's quests, then Mayor Bro sorted first (v2.3.1669), then the giver filter and one offer at a time (v2.3.1681 until v2.3.3944).
- Rewards as text only (before v2.3.1710); the handed-over kit shown only while on offer (v2.3.1710 until v2.3.3944); no achievement points anywhere on these pages (until v2.3.3944); the giver's quoted words on the quest's page (v2.3.1673 until v2.3.3944: his face stays, in WHO; his words are in his dialogue).
- A footprint road with its switch on this page (v2.3.2896, hidden v2.3.2992).
- Daily quests and a season behind the card (v2.3.3140, put away v2.3.3400); a scroll emoji and a text "✓" on the pinned card.
- Track only sorted this list (v2.3.1298 until v2.3.3522): the maps and the pinned card never read it.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Accept or Turn In buttons in the journal.
- Quests from givers who are not in the world, or offers before the Mayor's first quest is handed in.
- Tabs that hide the quest, or small text and chips (the pages' row of three, the owner's 2026-11-25 mockup, holds Daily and Completed; the quest is the page).
- Daily quests or a season again, a daily-rewards button on the HUD, or a chest at login.
- A road on the ground instead of the minimap's gold road, or green for "in progress".

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/plan/cards/QP.md, src/game/questPages.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/QuestPages.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/questParts.jsx, docs/specs/quest-journal.md, docs/specs/quest-guidance.md, docs/specs/daily-rewards.md, src/game/questJournal.js, src/ui/mobile/sheet/questJournalModel.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/QuestsPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/QuestDetailPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/QuestJournalPage.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/DailyRewardsCard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/sheet/questModel.js, src/ui/BroTown.jsx

## Life skills
Screens: dash-bro-skills, dash-bro-skill-detail

**What it is for:** Your ten life skills at a glance, and a detail page for each. Since v2.3.4172 they are a page under Bro ("Lifeskills move inside Bro", the owner's five buttons): the Bro card's Life Skills row opens it, its back chip returns to the card, and L on a computer opens it. The detail gives your exact XP, what one hit can be now and at the next level, which resources still pay full XP, and what the next levels open.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A home screen of ten app tiles, all on one screen** (v2.3.3522, owner: "Make the lifeskills menu look more like iPhone apps instead of elongated pills"). Five across in two rows: a rounded square tile tinted by the skill's own colour with its painted icon big in it, the name under it, the level on a chip at its corner, a thin green XP bar along its bottom edge, and the brass dot on a skill with a level-up you have not looked at. A press shrinks the tile, as a phone's home screen does. This REVERSES the owner's own earlier ask, "make the expanded view pill shaped so they all fit without scrolling" (v2.3.1312b); its other half, all ten on one screen with no scrolling, still holds. Sideways three across, and the column scrolls (it has height to spare and no width: two pills a row once cut every name to one letter, v2.3.2173).
- **A fixed order that never re-sorts:** the five gathering skills (Woodcutting, Fishing, Mining, Farming, Trapping), then the five crafting skills (Cooking, Smithing, Woodworking, Gem Cutting, Enchanting). Spatial memory beats recency (v2.3.1296). The group headings went so all ten fit (v2.3.1337, owner: "fit on one screen without scrolling"); the order still keeps the groups apart.
- **No "coming soon" cells** (v2.3.1312, owner: "they consume space, make the game look unfinished, and force six narrow columns").
- **The detail opens inside the same page, at the same height,** with "‹ All skills" to go back (v2.3.1312). It shows the level, an XP bar, and your XP against what the next level needs; **Each hit** and **At Lv n+1** (Mining, Woodcutting, Fishing, Cooking), e.g. "1 (80%) · 2–3 (10% each)" at level 3; **Full XP** and **Less XP** (the three gathering skills); **Unlocked / Next unlock / Later** from the game's real tables; a **Benefit** line only where a real formula backs it; **Found at**; and one sentence on how the skill earns XP. Farming, Trapping and Enchanting have no level table, so no ladder is made up for them.
- **No Track, Find Trainer or View Recipes buttons.** Nothing in the game backs them yet, and a button that only pretends to work was left out on purpose (v2.3.1312).
- **The page prints odds because of the hit ladder** (v2.3.3229, owner: "after the first level you get a 10% chance of hitting 1 number higher ... the number 1 will eventually get replaced by the 2 as more 10% chances get added with each level"). It is slower than before under level 10 and faster above it.
- **Full XP / Less XP, because XP fades 5 levels below you** (v2.3.3251, owner: "Xp should begin decaying starting at 5 levels below your current level. It should always be most efficient to harvest or fight things within your current level"). For example, at Mining 11: "Full XP — Titanium Ore", "Less XP — Black Steel Ore 28% · Iron Ore 15% …".
- **Levels cost twice the XP since v2.3.3090** (asked "Should life-skill XP slow down like combat XP?", the owner said "Yes"). No level was lost: the bar reads half as far along, and every "+n XP" shown stayed true.
- **A skill is never shown at level 0** (v2.3.3041); new skills start at 1. **The Life Skills dot** means a level-up you have not looked at, never ordinary XP, and opening the page clears it.
- **Resource levels go in steps of five** (v2.3.3038, owner: "I'll change tier level requirements in levels of 5 ... black steel now requires a mining level of at least 5. Fishing clownfish required fishing level 5").
- **In the world the requirement is shown quietly** (v2.3.3059, owner: "a grayed out icon above whatever the resource is ... would be a good cue ... I just don't want the screen to be too busy with text though"). Each resource wears only its tool's picture, grey when your level is short, and only the nearest one within 260 px says its name and level. Tapping a locked one swings three times, popping a 0 each time under "Requires Fishing Lv 5" (owner: "you can still show zeroes popping"). Fish show no rod, only a name plate when you are near (v2.3.3146, owner: "Remove the fishing icon above fish but leave the proximity based nameplate in place").
- **The harvest bar is green, over the resource, and reads "7/10"** (v2.3.3035, owner: "I want resource harvesting bar to be green and to appear above the resource, not the player head. It should also list the numbers on the bar"). For mining it hangs under the rock (owner: "For mining you can put the bar beneath the ore"), because above the rock it covered the miner.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- A 3-column card grid (v2.3.1286); group headings (v2.3.1312, removed v2.3.1337); two "SOON" cells (v2.3.1296, removed v2.3.1312); the detail as a separate page (until v2.3.1312).
- Ten pills, two to a row (v2.3.1312b until v2.3.3522), one column sideways (v2.3.2173).
- Hits rolled evenly from 1 to your level (until v2.3.3229). The old timer-based Benefit line shows only against a server that rolls no hits.
- A locked tap that said "Need Mining Lv 5" with a beep (v2.3.3038); a name-and-level pill over every resource (v2.3.3040).
- The harvest bar over your own head (v2.3.3027).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Track / Find Trainer / View Recipes buttons, or "coming soon" skills.
- Re-sorting the skills by level or by last use, or headings that push skills off the first screen.
- The pills again: the owner asked for app tiles (v2.3.3522).
- A name-and-level label over every resource, or a rod over the fish.
- A plain refusal in place of the try you can watch fail, or a ladder invented for Farming, Trapping or Enchanting.

**Still open:**
- The owner set black steel and clownfish at level 5 and said "I haven't thought the rest out yet, game is still a demo". Trout's and the woods' levels follow the same steps of five and are recorded as "the owner's to change".

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/hit-ladder.md, docs/specs/xp-decay.md, docs/specs/wheel-resources.md, docs/specs/gathering-hits.md, docs/specs/pace-and-difficulty.md, src/ui/mobile/dash/SkillsPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/sheet/skillsModel.js, src/data/lifeSkills.js

## The Monster Codex
Screens: dash-encyclopedia, dash-codex-monster, dash-codex-loot, dash-codex-item

**Under the new dashboard (v2.3.6693, card UI2-codex; `?ui2=0` keeps the page below):** the owner, 2026-11-25, with the master specification (part 8): *"Codex is a complete encyclopedia, not a knowledge-unlock or discovery-gated system."* Q73: *"Use illustrated entity cards, with artwork as the dominant visual and a small amount of essential information that depends on the entity type."*, *"Avoid a dense database-style directory as the general collection layout."* Q74: *"In the monster example, the actual animated monster sprite, name, short description, level/element and essential combat stats are visible on the first screen. Subject destinations summarize their own contents and expand into dedicated detail views."* Q75: *"Show item artwork, explicit item name, rarity and exact drop probability together. Selecting an item opens universal item inspection. No dice-roll numbers, probability progress bars or unnecessary drill-down by rarity."*, *"Do not assign illustrative percentages to actual items, round very small positive probabilities to zero, or imply mutually exclusive drops unless the engine works that way."*
- **The collection** (dash/CodexPage.jsx, the kit's Ui2Collection; its rules src/game/codexView.js): the kinds in one row at the top, Monsters chosen; Items, Resources, Townsfolk and Lands keep their place with "Not in the Codex yet" (their pages are content still to write), Pets with one door, "Your pet journal" (the pets' own collection, never copied). Mechanics and Furnishings are left out: the game has nothing of either. A monster's card is a raised door: its picture first and largest (48 px; 72 on a computer), its name, its element (icon and word) and land, your kills, NEW. Three across; the cards scroll inside their zone; sideways the kinds are a column at the left and the cards one row that scrolls sideways.
- **The game's rule stays** (the master notes' F4, the owner's to answer): a page opens at one kill, read in ONE place (codexView.js `codexMet`). Before it: the shape, "???", "Not met yet" and its land; its page is locked, with no doors.
- **A monster's showcase** (dash/MonsterShowcase.jsx, the kit's Ui2Showcase; src/game/entityInspect.js): the frame's title is its name, Back says Codex. The monster large in its idle strip on the left; four doors on the right, each with its summary: Combat (its special), Loot (its count of drops), Habitat (its land and levels), Story (its elder form); under them its essentials flat in one strip: levels, element, type, your kills, and Strength "By level" (no monster has a health or damage number of its own: its level is its land's). Sideways the three lie in one row. Combat has today's stage (you, its attack, its special), what it does, its element's effect and the tip; Habitat the land, both stages' levels, the way from BroTown, its dungeon; Story its tale and its elder form.
- **Its art loads when its page opens and is freed on leaving** (the plan's step 6, moved from the Info tab): the showcase and Combat each hold the stage's strips while drawn, and the frame draws only its top page. One monster at a time: at most 12.0 MB decoded on the test box's sums (the Mummy and its skeleton: each file's width x height x 4), the same strips today's Info tab holds.
- **Loot is plain rows** (src/game/lootRows.js, made from the one drop table): picture, name, type, ONE chance (the table's percent, never "0%" for a chance above nothing; "Every kill", gold with its amount), a gear piece "rolls a grade". Under them, once, the roll's true words ("One roll a kill: at most one of the drops with a chance" where the worker rolls once a kill; "Each drop rolls on its own" where it does not) and the four grades with their shares. A row opens THE one item page Gear opens, read-only ("In the Codex"): "Drops from Mummy 0.2%", that it rolls its grade, and a door Found from (every monster that drops it, with its land). The iron greatsword is named by the bag's own reader ("Iron Greatsword"), so the Codex and Gear say the same.

**What it is for:** A page for each kind of monster, opened in full by your first kill (v2.3.3209). It says what the monster is and does, shows it fighting your character, lists every drop and the roll that drops it, and says where it lives. The owner: "After you kill a monster you learn more about it, what it does, where it's from, elemental type, special attack, (make up a backstory) etc. it'll be another dashboard section that just displays information."

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Everything opens at the first kill** (owner: "I want all the facts revealed after just 1 kill"). Before that, a card is only the monster's shape and "???".
- **Its own button, fifth of six** (v2.3.3215, owner: "Are you able to fit the codex as another dashboard button?"). Its badge counts pages with something new, and More's Codex tile opens the same screen.
- **The list fits without scrolling** (v2.3.3215, owner: "is there a way to display more data without needing to scroll down so much?"): nine small cards (picture, name, land, kill count), three across, two sideways. The Codex's other tabs (Codex / Materials / Zones) stay hidden while they are empty, as they are today; they are kept, not deleted.
- **A page follows the owner's mockup** ("This is just a mockup with fake items but the structure is correct"): a short header (portrait, name, kills, and badges for levels, element and creature type), then three tabs, Drops, Info and Locations.
- **Creature type is by body** (owner: "more along the lines of humanoid, beast, (some word along those lines that means flying creature, bug-like creature, swimming creature)"): Humanoid, Beast, Aquatic, Ooze. They replaced Elemental, Goblin, Undead, Golem, Slime, Aquatic and Spirit. The snowman is a Beast (v2.3.3908, the owner: "Change snowman from humanoid to beast"); it was a Humanoid.
- **The buttons ride the name's row** (v2.3.4035, the owner: "Move the 'all monsters, drops, info, and locations' row of buttons to be in the same row it talks about the monster title (In this case 'Fire goblin') that will give much more room on mobile to see more of the drop table etc."): the pinned bar is ‹ All, the monster's name, then Drops, Info and Where, each an icon and a short word, 40 px tall. Under it, a shorter header: portrait, kills and badges. Where the bar is narrow (a 320 px phone, the sideways column) the buttons are their icons alone so the name keeps its room; a long name wraps to two lines in the bar.
- **Short, and it scrolls** (owner: "Stay short and scroll"). The tabs stay pinned, with "‹ All monsters" as their first button. A page opens at its top, a tab's content lands just under the tabs, and closing an item card brings back the row you tapped.
- **The drop table has a near-black panel** (owner: "The drops table needs a different background so the information can stand out more from the dashboard background. Maybe black?", then "Make the codex section that has the table more readable with the background"), with striped rows, a heading band, bigger type, and long names that wrap instead of being cut.
- **The bars get the room, each a share of 100%** (owner: "Wrap text for the item name and increase the usable area for the bar size. Looks like the chance column reserves too much space"; "All drop chances should be a single value. Also make the percentage bars out of 100%. The 10% bar almost looks as long as the 100%").
- **A ROLL column, with dice in its heading.** The owner first suggested "a 'roll-based system' where you see dice and number", then: "Actually 'roll' column with a number range and drop chance percentage in parentheses might be better". So rows read like "612–1,611 (10%)", and gold and remnants read "Always (100%)". A key under the table explains it.
- **One roll a kill, with ranges that never overlap** (owner: "Shouldn't these numbers not overlap each other? ... That way a player hunting for something can also be looking for a roll number within that range"). A kill rolls 1 to 10,000; each drop owns its own numbers, the rarest starting at 1, so a kill drops one of them at most. Every kill showed its roll over the monster with the Gambling Den's dice: a grey "7,431" when nothing more drops, or a gold "640 Frost Shard".
- **Since v2.3.3616 a kill shows its DROPS, not its roll** (owner: "Instead of showing the dice roll for each drop I think it would be better to show each drop in columns ..."), and **since v2.3.3627 as lines of words, not columns** (owner: "I just want the name of the item to float upwards with quantity in parenthesis and the color of the font indicating rarity. So normal would just be white. So coins (5) rising and disappearing in white, slime remains (1) rising beneath that message"). One line a drop, "Coins (5)" over "Slime Remains (1)", at a message's size, each under the one before, rising off the monster and fading; the colour is the rarity (white for every kill's drops, the shard green, the rare gem blue, the nugget orange, gear its grade, purple until picked up). No pictures and no rarity words: the owner turned the columns down. The roll stays on this page. Don't suggest putting the dice back over the monster, or pictures beside the lines.
- **No random weapon drop** (owner: "Remove 'a random weapon' drop", then, of the whole game: "Remove it from the game").
- **A row opens the item's card:** picture, name, type and base rarity, what it is, FOUND FROM, and BASE DROP CHANCE with its numbers ("drops on a roll of 2–21 out of 10,000"), plus the four grades for gear. Its X goes back to the row.
- **Info's stage:** you on the left and the monster on the right, on a strip of its land's colour inside a dark frame, with nothing drawn outside it (the owner had seen them "in a layer above the container they're supposed to be in"). It plays the attack, and every other round the special with the world's word (SLAM!, LUNGE!), wind-up and warning circle, so the page teaches the tell the fight will show. A toggle shows the elder form.
- **The stage's animations load when Info opens and go when it closes.** The owner chose this over holding about 20 MB all session: "I think loading only when you open the info tab". The portrait stands in until they arrive.
- **Locations** gives the land and its levels, the elder form's levels, the way there from BroTown (which gate, which road), and the land's dungeon.
- **A first kill raises a NEW MONSTER card for 6 s,** with "Tap to see it in the Codex ›" (v2.3.3336, owner: "Make the new codex entry for monsters as a 'new monster' announcement instead of a new codex entry and have it jump to the codex area"). It has no dimmed backdrop, so it never stops a fight.
- **The table cannot drift from the game.** It prints the server's own drop rates, and a check fails if the two differ.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- (v2.3.6693, under the new dashboard) the ROLL column: each drop's numbers of the kill's 1-to-10,000 roll with the Gambling Den's dice in its heading, a bar a row, and the Codex's own item card. The owner's specification turned dice and bars down (Q75): they stay only under `?ui2=0`.
- Facts opening at 1, 3, 10 and 25 kills; a tall page drawn like the mockup; 122 px cards, a 78 px header and tabs that scrolled away (before v2.3.3215).
- A wide chance column with 44 px bars; bars scaled by powers of ten; each drop rolled on its own, with every range from 1; a "random weapon" row.
- "New in the Codex: Snowman" floating over your head (before v2.3.3336).
- A stage standing on the dashboard's slate.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Facts revealed gradually over several kills, or a page taller than the sheet.
- All the monsters' animations on the loading screen. The owner chose loading on the Info tab, and the spec says: "Do not move them onto the loading screen."
- Separate rolls per drop, overlapping ranges, log-scale bars, or the random weapon back.
- A dimmed backdrop or pause for the NEW MONSTER card, or showing the empty Codex / Materials / Zones tabs.

**Still open:**
- The Winged and Insectoid types wait for a monster that flies, or a bug.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/monster-codex.md

## The bag and item cards
Screens: dash-bag, item-card

**What it is for:** Everything you carry and are not wearing, shown on the band at rest. Since v2.3.4151 the bag is the left panel of **The Gear tab**: a tap picks an item and the middle panel shows it; the card below is where its Sell and Drop sheets open.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The bag lives on the band; there is no bag page** (since v2.3.1653–1654). Sideways, the owner called the old small-slot bag page "a legacy view that needs to retire" (v2.3.2173).
- **Eight slots in view, then it scrolls:** 4 across and 2 rows, with a sliver of a third under a fade (v2.3.1654, owner: "make the bag slots scrollable downward same as bag view was"), and more rows below (v2.3.1704, owner: "you can add two more rows of inventory space (it's just scrollable so it shouldn't change the look)"). There is no "N / 32" counter, because the bag has no real limit (v2.3.1285). An empty bag is a grid of empty squares, not a message (v2.3.1639, owner: "remove empty bag message placeholder").
- **What you wear is not in the bag; what you take off comes back to it,** a worn cape included (v2.3.2143; the owner, twice: "after equipping it it still stays as an icon in your inventory"). **Order:** anchored (⚓) items first, oldest anchor top-left, then everything else newest first (v2.3.1070).
- **Filter chips on their own row** (v2.3.1652, owner: "put the filters on their own header row above the inventory slots"): All, Weapon, Armor, Consumable, Crafting. They are pictures only, with no tray behind them (v2.3.1650). Each has a small funnel mark except All, the one chip that clears filtering (v2.3.2183, owner: "Just add the filter icons to everything except the bag button"). Sideways they run down the bag's side (v2.3.2168), with bigger icons (v2.3.2184, owner: "actually increase the size of the filter categories ... there's room to").
- **Food sorts under Consumable with the potions; there is no sixth chip** (v2.3.3130). A bought bottle lands there too (v2.3.2145, owner: "fix the potion landing in the bag after buying it").
- **Any crop can be eaten raw** (v2.3.3537, the after-review plan the owner agreed on 2026-10-21: every crop gets one use a player can guess). The card says "+10 HP when eaten" (up to +48 for a Farming 20 crop: half what a fish of the same level heals) and has Eat. Crops stay under Crafting, since recipes and the order board use them. Quick eat never picks a crop, so one tap in a fight can't use up the carrots meant for Zoom Juice.
- **Pictures, not emoji:** the owner's painted item sets, including 53 more since v2.3.3281 (seed packets, crops, compost, dishes, the box trap, hardened woods, essences; owner: "Here are the art assets from the 5 other prompts"). Each item has one picture everywhere it appears: the card, trades, the auction house, the order board.
- **The bag's bow is the pine bow; the menu's is the newer one** (v2.3.3571, owner: "revert the inventory art of the bow back to the original one (pine) ... I only wanted it to replace the menu art for the bow"). The bag, the item card, a drop's reveal and loot on the ground draw the pine bow; the Equipment slot, the weapon button and the player card draw the owner's card 11 bow from its own file. The copper ore and the pickaxe are the owner's own painted pictures since the same version ("change the art style of the copper ore and the pickaxe to this").
- **A plain item has one quiet grey edge.** Category colours (weapons blue, armour green) were taken out at the owner's correction, because they looked like a rarity the item did not have (v2.3.1228).
- **A piece's grade is its edge** (v2.3.3142, owner: "So rare is blue, elite is orange, godly is prismatic", the pets' colours). Rare and elite get a 2 px edge in their colour; godly gets a turning rainbow ring, the only always-moving thing the UI allows. On the Character page's card the grade is the name's colour, never a word (v2.3.1847, owner: "I'd rather have the font color of the name of the item represent rarity. For normal items it will just be white"); a godly name is rainbow text. A rare-or-better drop gets a short reveal, then is shown "in font color of its rarity tier" (v2.3.1925).
- **Small touches.** The stack count is a small badge, bottom right (v2.3.1249). Now and then one item moves as it would (a potion sloshes, a fish flops, metal glints, logs lie still), and a new item pops in (v2.3.2815, owner: "I'm looking for a liveness pass. Basically making things move a little in a way that makes sense for whatever object it is"). Only one moves at a time, nothing loops, and it is off under reduced motion.
- **Diego's shop and a player trade use this same bag** (v2.3.2059, owner: "Your existing bag should become one half of the shop interface. Don't open another inventory or cover it ... Tap my inventory -> sell it"; v2.3.2149, owner: "change the player to player trade menu to be like the shopkeeper trade menu where it just attaches to the player bag"). In the shop a tile shows what he pays (↓ when his pile is deep); in a trade, how many are offered. Either one unfolds a folded band (v2.3.3399). Either one also brings the bag itself to the front as it opens: a band left on another page (Bro, Quests...) goes back to the bag, upright and so on every screen with the one band (the trade v2.3.4877; the shop v2.3.4878, the owner: "give Diego's shop the same fix"; the old side column keeps the shop's own rule, v2.3.3407).
- **The item card opens beside the tile you tapped** (v2.3.210). It has a small ⚓ pin in its header (v2.3.2476: pinning only moves the item within your own bag, so it stays out of the action row; it looks 26 px, and its press is 44 px, v2.3.3481); the picture, name and stats; for gear, a green or red line against what you wear (v2.3.1313); a caption; and the actions (Eat, Drink, Light fire, Claim, Equip / Unequip, Sell, X).
- **Sell opens a price sheet inside the card:** "Sell in the Auction House", how many, your price, "It leaves your bag now and comes back in a week if nobody buys it", then Put it up / Back. There is no suggested price; the seller sets it. The card moves up as it grows, so the sheet never opens off screen (v2.3.2612, owner: "tapping 'sell' on an item currently goes nowhere"). A piece that can never be sold keeps a greyed Sell and says why (v2.3.2551).
- **Closing the card:** tap outside it, or X (Esc on a computer). It also closes with the page that opened it (v2.3.1698): a card once outlived its page and covered Mayor Bro's Turn In button.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- A Bag page with Items / Equipped tabs (v2.3.1326). The tabs went in v2.3.1639 (owner: "remove 'items' and 'equipped' buttons since equipped is visible from the dashboard view"), and the page in v2.3.1654. A 3x3 quick-bag with a "+N" chip.
- Filter chips cut for room (v2.3.1645), restored (v2.3.1649), then given their own row (v2.3.1652).
- Category-coloured edges; purple for elite and gold for godly (until v2.3.3142).
- A big loose count number; the anchor as a full action button; emoji for seeds, crops, dishes and the trap (until v2.3.3281).

**Please don't suggest:**
- A separate inventory screen, or a second copy of the bag inside the shop or trade window.
- Category colours, or the word "Rare" in place of the grade's colour.
- A sixth chip for food, a funnel on All, looping animations in the bag, or a suggested sell price.
- Making the item card scroll. Sideways, a scrolling card pushed its buttons under the backdrop, so a tap meant for Sell closed the card (v2.3.2612).

**Still open:**
- The card is not yet laid out for a short sideways screen, and its content can spill below it. A code note says this stays "until this card is laid out to fit a short viewport, which is a different change" (v2.3.2612).

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/landscape-mode.md, docs/specs/bag-icons.md, docs/specs/crop-food.md, docs/specs/armor-grade-look.md, docs/specs/rarity-reveal.md, docs/specs/quest-guidance.md, docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, src/ui/mobile/dash/InventoryPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/ItemDetailPopup.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/BagFilterChips.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/bagFilterBus.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/bagModel.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/bagLife.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/DashColumns.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/common.js, src/ui/mobile/sheet/HeroExpanded.jsx, src/ui/mobile/BottomDashboard.jsx

## The Gear tab
Screens: gear-*

**What it is for:** The resting dashboard, what the bag button opens: your bag, one card about you or what you tapped, and your gear side by side, so you can see what you carry, what you wear and what a change would do without opening anything (v2.3.4151: the owner's design of 2026-11-02, laid out as the owner's second mockup of 2026-11-03). Pictures: docs/shots/gear-tab-overview.webp (your bro), gear-tab-item.webp (a material), gear-tab-gear.webp (a weapon compared), gear-tab-armour.webp (a rare chest compared), gear-tab-more.webp (the "⋯" open), gear-tab-slot.webp (an upright phone, all of them), gear-tab-desk.webp (a computer, 1280 x 800), and inside the one band (Lane B's PR #1410, every screen's dashboard now) gear-tab-oneband-desk.webp (a computer, 1280 x 720) and gear-tab-oneband-sideways.webp, made by `node tools/qa/mp/run.mjs gearshots` (v2.3.4191).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A ✕ on every pick's card, and spare armour stacks (v2.3.4233, owner: "add an 'X' close button on the top right corner of the detail view"; "Armor should be able to stack right?").** `data-gear-card-close` (data-esc, 32 px, over the card's corner) puts the pick down: the ring goes, the card is the bro again. Identical spare shields and armour (same kind, material, quality, hardness, all revealed; `groupSpare` in GearTab) are one cell with a count; display only, Equip and Drop act on the first piece; weapons never stack.
- **The bag scrolls, with shadows; the coins are an object (v2.3.4233, owner: "the shadowed effect for additional inventory slots ... scroll within the inventory container downwards" and "allow the gold amount to be tapped on to have its own item detail in the second pane").** The bag shows every slot, empty ones too, with at least one empty row past the last item; a soft shadow lies on its bottom edge while more slots are below and on its top edge once scrolled (`data-gear-bag-shade`). The middle card still never scrolls. The coins cell is a button: tapped, it wears the bag tiles' blue ring and the card says Gold (coin picture, the amount, one line on what it buys, no buttons); tapped again it goes (`gearTabBus.tapGold`).
- **Three panels: the bag | one card | "Equipped", left to right, as the second mockup draws them** (owner, 2026-11-02: "inventory on left with filter on top ... the furthest right tab has all the equipped items"; 2026-11-03: "I uploaded a different mockup for the gear menu"). Dark slate panels with thin borders and rounded corners, the card framed in brass. Laid out from the box the tab is given, never the device. Across: the two grids share ONE square size (below), the card takes the rest, never under about 120 px (at 360 px wide the card is 120 px and the squares their 34 px floor; at 390, 132 px and 36 px). Too narrow for that (the sideways ~222 px column): the bag and the gear side by side on top, the card the full width under them.
- **The bag shows nine: three square slots a row, three rows in view, the rest by scrolling** (owner: "Inventory slots too cramped. Changed to 9 visible slots"). Each piece's count at its corner, empty squares filling the view; a taller or wider band makes the squares bigger.
- **On a computer, and across any wide band, the tab never stretches** (owner: "The stretched middle panel looks silly on computer. I like what you did the landscape mode on phone"). It first capped itself at 560 px, centred (v2.3.4151). Since Lane B's one dashboard assembly (PR #1424: the button row and the open tab one centred block, at most 840 px, a sideways phone's band) it fills that block as the Bro tab does, and has no cap of its own (v2.3.4191): at 1280 x 720 the squares are held by the row's height (three rows: about 45 px), so once the card would be more than twice a grid panel the three panels share the block equally, about 276 px each as the Bro tab's thirds, each grid centred in its panel at its own size and its head lined up with it (a phone is untouched). Filling the block with the card alone gave a 530 px card between two 149 px panels. Against #1386's earlier 1056 px box the card had stretched to about 740 px, which is why the cap waited for the 840 px block. The sideways phone is left exactly as it is.
- **Inside the one band (Lane B's dashboard for every screen, PR #1410)** (owner: "The new universal dashboard system will need to adapt to the new redesign we're adding for 'gear'"): on a computer the band's row is the upright phone's height and wider: the squares grow to the row's height and the tab fills the band's 840 px assembly. On a sideways phone the open band leaves the row about 78 px tall, too short for three rows: there both grids lie in ONE row of nine (the bag's nine in view, the nine cells in their order), still one size, and the card goes compact -- your bro at its left with the name and the bars beside him; a picked thing's name, its stars, one line of numbers or words, and its gold button. It still never scrolls (mp-oneband measures it).
- **The Equipped cells are the bag's squares' size, on every layout** (owner, 2026-11-03: "Make equipped slots as big as the slots you're using for inventory"): one size for both grids, as big as the card's room allows and never under 34 px. On the sideways column (~222 px across) two grids of three side by side cannot both be 34 px, so both are 32 px there: the rule is that they shrink together rather than differ.
