- **The card is ONE card, no tabs** (the owner adopted: "The middle panel should function as one contextual information card, without tabs"; the first mockup's LOOK | STATS | DETAILS tabs are gone). With nothing picked it is your bro; with something picked it is that thing's card.
- **With nothing picked the card is your bro, full-body** (v2.3.4191, owner, 2026-11-03: "I want the character preview in the middle panel by default (actually showing what he's equipped) like how I had it in the previous character layout ... Keep the profile picture in the bro tab. This one just put the character preview in middle"). He is the Character page's own figure (`CharacterView`), wearing exactly what is equipped and holding the weapon in your hand, facing southwest. Three arrangements were built and pictured; the owner picked the first: "<your name> Lv N" on top, the bro centred and as large as the card allows, the HP, MP and EN bars below him. No headshot here (the profile picture stays on the Bro tab's button).
- **One energy bar, "EN".** The owner's words allowed a separate stamina bar only if stamina and energy are different resources in BroTown. They are not: the game has one pool (`stamina`), which every screen calls energy, "EN" (VitalBar's label), so there is one bar. HP is on the display scale, as on the Character page.
- **The three combat skills are not here** (v2.3.4191, owner: "Move the 3 primary combat skills out of the gear menu and put them into the bro menu"): Melee, Bow and Magic, with their levels and XP bars, are on the Bro page (Lane B, PR #1386). The XP that flies in after a gain lands on that skill's Bro tile while the Bro tab is open (its number held, then counting up), and otherwise in the top bar's XP number, as a kill's does: never floating and fading in place (v2.3.4191, the orchestrator's call on the owner's "have the xp jump down into whatever combat skill earned the xp").
- **No "Combat Power".** The first mockup drew one (a single 3,842); the game has no such number, and a made-up total would be the one number on the page that nothing else in the game agrees with.
- **The card never scrolls** (owner: "try to fit data in middle panel compact but making good use of space without needing to do any scrolling"), and nothing in it is under 11 px. mp-geartab measures every state at 390 x 844 and 360 x 780 (and sideways and on a computer): the card's scrollHeight never past its height, no text under 11 px.
- **A picked thing's card, as the owner's item mockup draws it** (v2.3.4191, owner: "I want the item selection menu to look more like this (larger pills) ... Remove the 'greatsword common' type subtitles"). Its picture in a frame, beside its name in its grade's colour. The name is never cut (v2.3.4191: the type of sword is in the name, which is why the subtitle went): it has the head row's whole width, two lines, and a name that still needs more is set smaller, down to 10 px (the name alone; everything else keeps 11 px), with words never broken inside. The longest in the game, "Worldbreaker Greatsword" and "Black Steel Greaves", fit at 360, 375 and 390 px and on a computer (11 px at 360, the picture 30 px there). Under the name, in place of the old "Copper · Greatsword" subtitle, its grade as STARS (owner: "a simple star system ... one star - white for common, two star, blue for rare, etc"): the game's own four grades and colours (`QUALITY_COLOR`), normal one white, rare two blue, elite three orange, godly four gold; "Qty N" beside them for a stack. A weapon adds a row, "Hardness" with five marks and "N/5" (owner: "a basic number line hardness 0/5"; a weapon's hardness is 0 to 5 on the worker's ladder). Then up to four large stat pills, two to a row, each its picture (the Points window's and the Character page's own stat icons; owner: "each large cell 'damage' 'DPS' was accompanied by icons. There should already be icons made for all of these stats"), its word (DAMAGE, DPS, SPEED, RANGE; a shield's BLOCK, ENERGY, DEFENCE; armour's DMG CUT; three letters where a pill is narrow) and its number, with the change beside it or, where they do not fit side by side, under it (never a number cut short). A thing that is not gear shows, in place of pills, what the game says it is for, at most two lines ("Smelt 1 into a Copper Bar at the Blacksmith"). The second mockup listed "Uses" (Smithing, Crafting, Refining); the game keeps no such list, so the card says only what the game really knows.
- **ONE gold button, the rest behind "⋯"** (v2.3.4191). At the card's foot one big gold button, the thing's main action: Equip, Unequip, Reveal, Eat, Drink, Light fire (the item card's own words for each, rather than one "Use"). Everything else (Drop, Pin, Inspect) is behind a "⋯" beside it at the foot (v2.3.4191: it sat top-right and left the name about 45 px, so "Copper Greatsword" read "Copper Grea..."), a list over the card that its ✕ (Esc) shuts, so the card keeps its size. A stack with nothing to do here (an ore) has no gold button, only its "⋯" at the foot. All of it is the one action list in one function (`itemActions.js`), shared with the item card.
- **No Sell in the Gear tab** (v2.3.4191, owner: "Just remove the 'sell' option for now. I don't think this is a good feature to have next to equip button"). The item card (opened from a shop or a trade) keeps its own Sell; only the Gear tab leaves it out.
- **The card wears the pick's blue** (owner: "somehow connect the middle panel (in gear) to whatever the active attention item is (blue outline). Maybe the middle panel can have blue outline itself or even a blue background"). While a bag piece or a gear slot is picked, the card's border is the blue ring's own colour (one constant, `GEAR_SEL_BLUE`, that both read) with a faint blue wash; with nothing picked it is the brass-framed overview. Gold stays the weapon in your hand.
- **The equipment is a 3 x 3 grid under "Equipped":** Sword, Bow, Staff / Chest, Legs, Cape / Shield, Amulet, Gem, the mockups' order (owner: "3 columns ... Sword, bow, and staff across the top ... no helmet ... add gem slot (this will give your weapons elemental abilities)"). Boots, gauntlets, belts and rings do not exist in the game, so nothing was taken out. Each cell shows its piece with the grade's edge, or the faint silhouette when empty. The Gem cell shows the gem or gems set in the weapon in your hand, and follows you when you switch weapons. The weapon cells are plain: their picture and, for the one in your hand, its gold (owner: "remove the points badges and thin xp lines from those"); the level and the XP are on the Bro page.
- **An empty Equipped cell is the owner's framed tile, and the base shirt is not armour** (v2.3.4199, the owner: "from now on don't show the shirt in the chest slot. It's too confusing and makes the chest placeholder unclear. It's intended for torso armor."; "I've just now added new placeholder art ... for unequipped slots in the equipped item section"): the nine tiles (sword, bow, staff, chest, legs, cape, shield, amulet, gem; steel with gold corners) are cut from the owner's sheet into `public/icons/bag/equip-*.webp` (128 px, 256 colours, plain images like the old slot ghosts), and an empty cell is its tile filling the cell: no border, background or radius of the cell's own, so the tile's frame is the only frame (the blue pick ring still shows). A filled cell is unchanged (the item's picture, the hand's gold). A gem cell with no gem set shows the gem tile. The chest cell is filled only by torso armour (the quest Iron Torso, or a chest gear layer); the cosmetic shirt (`equip.shirt`, no armour stat) leaves it empty. Same in the upright grid, the sideways one-row form and the computer band: one cell component.
- **Blue is what you tapped, gold is what is on** (owner: "tapping on an item highlights it in blue to show active press but highlighted yellow shows what's 'on'"). The weapon in your hand wears a gold edge and a soft gold glow, as the mockup draws it, and the cell or bag piece you tapped a blue ring outside it, so both can show at once. v2.3.4191: a rare piece's thin 2 px blue edge was the same family as the pick's 2 px ring, and in a bag of rare armour four squares looked picked; the pick is now a brighter sky blue (#8CC4FF), 3 px, set off by a 1 px dark gap and wearing the soft glow the card's outline wears, while a rarity keeps its thin edge.
- **Tap a cell: the bag filters to what fits it, the card shows what is in it** (owner: "when you tap on an equip slot your inventory gets filtered to whatever item category was tapped on ... The option window after tapping on an equipped item would just be unequip. The middle character pane can automatically change to display the stats of the highlighted weapon and maybe the name and description"). Its picture, its name in its grade's colour, its stars, its numbers and ONE gold button, Unequip (the playtest: "just have one button for unequip instead of 'change'"). The cape's Unequip takes it off your back; the amulet has none (the worker's unequip for the amulet slot has nowhere to put it, so it would be lost), and the Gem cell says "Gems are set at the Gem Works". An empty cell says "No bow yet. Pick one from your bag." Tapped again, the cell is put down and its filter goes.
- **Tap a bag piece: the card compares it** (owner: "current versus prospective stats with green increases and red decreases before equipping it"): each pill shows the new number with the change against what you wear inside it, a green ▲ +N, a red ▼ -N, or "--" for the same (hovered, "old → new"). A stat the worn piece lacks, or an empty slot, counts as 0: the green gain over nothing (a rare chest's 21.5% damage cut over a plain shirt is "▲ +21.5"; v2.3.4191, the orchestrator's call); armour by its damage cut, a shield by its block, read through the same numbers the item card and the Character page use. Its gold button rides at the foot of the card so the pills never push it out of reach. Equipping a weapon fills its slot; which weapon is in your hand stays as it was (unless your hand was empty).
- **The filter is one pill, "All Items" with a funnel and a chevron, your coins beside it, as the second mockup draws them** (owner: "a tiny accordion style pill button instead that just says 'filter' on it ... and expands downward into a bigger menu that includes things like sword, chest armor, leg armor, ore / metal bars, crops, fish, etc."). Its menu drops down over the bag panel only, the other two panels never move, in groups with a count each, a group you hold nothing of greyed. The rules adopted against getting stuck: the active filter always shows on the pill ("Fish") and the × beside it clears it in one tap; a filter a cell set clears itself after an Equip or Unequip and when the cell is put down, while one you picked stays until you clear it; a filter with nothing in it says "No capes in your bag" with a Show all button; picking something never changes the filter.
- **Your coins sit beside the filter as a bag slot does** (owner: "Make gold icon as big as it can fit in that space and just put a badge over the number of gold (or whatever you currently do for item quantities)"): the coin picture fills its box and the amount is the bag slots' own count badge, a big purse said short as the top bar says it ("12.4K"). With a filter on in a narrow bag, the funnel steps aside so the filter's word fits. The weapon bag's "weapons 3/8" heads the filter menu.
- **On a computer the weapons are the keys 1, 2 and 3** (v2.3.4191: the tab's own weapon bar is retired; the one band's rail keeps its weapons). A key takes that weapon up and the worker hears it (the fix that made the keys send the pick to the server stays), and the gold ring moves to its cell. Phones switch weapons with the button under the movement stick, as before.

- **A "?" piece (see A mystery piece in the bag) is the same here:** tapped, the card shows its name, "Rare or better", Reveal as its gold button and Equip behind "⋯" (Equip reveals first), and no stars or comparison, because they would give its grade away (v2.3.4151, v2.3.4191).

**This reverses, on the owner's new design of 2026-11-02:**
- "a Bag button" (in **The dashboard** and **The bag and item cards**): the bag button opens this tab, and the bag is one of its three panels.
- "Worn gear ... back on the band" (in **The dashboard**): what you wear is the tab's right-hand panel.
- "an item card that pushes the character or vitals around" (in **Your character and the Points window**): here the middle card is meant to change to show what you tapped, beside the bag and the gear, and back again when you put it down.
- The five filter chips, the 2-across sideways bag and the item card on every bag tap: the bag's tap now picks the item and the card shows it; the item card is still where Sell's price sheet and Drop's how-many open.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The overview's three skill tiles (Melee, Bow, Magic, a tap showing the XP bar for 4 s) and, on a computer, the tab's own weapon bar (v2.3.4151, 2026-11-03): the skills moved to the Bro page and the bar gave way to the keys (v2.3.4191). The item card's "Copper · Greatsword" subtitle, its numbers as small rows with the change inline, and its row of buttons (Equip wider, Sell, "⋯"): the owner's item mockup made them stars, large pills with the change inside, one gold button and "⋯" (v2.3.4191).
- Three arrangements of the bro in the card (v2.3.4191): the name, figure and bars stacked and centred (picked); the figure at the left with the name and bars beside it; the figure behind the bars. The second lives on in the short sideways band, where there is no room for a stack.
- The second mockup's first build (v2.3.4151, 2026-11-03 midday): skill pills with XP bars, a gold glow and a tap to the Points window; the card's numbers in long rows that scrolled; the gear cells smaller than the bag's squares. The owner's notes the same day made the tiles icon-and-level only, the cells one size and the card fit without scrolling.
- The first mockup's middle (v2.3.4151, 2026-11-03 morning): LOOK | STATS | DETAILS tabs, your bro in LOOK, and a STATS page of Character Stats and the weapon in hand's box. The owner's second mockup made it one card without tabs or a figure; the bro came back as the card at rest (v2.3.4191), with no tabs.
- Four bag squares a row at 34 px: "too cramped".

**Please don't suggest:**
- A filter that changes the bag without saying so on the pill, or a filter row of icons again.
- A bag tap that opens a card over the tab; Change in place of Unequip; a picker list of everything that fits a slot.
- Tabs in the middle card, a headshot in place of the full-body bro, the skill tiles back in the Gear tab, a "Combat Power" (or any other made-up total), a second stamina bar beside EN, a "Uses" list the game does not keep, a card that scrolls, or gear cells a different size from the bag's squares.
- Sell back beside Equip, a second big button, or the type-and-grade subtitle back under the name.

**Still open:**
- The band's height is the dashboard's (Lane B's), kept as it is: on an upright phone the panels are about 185 px tall, and every state of the card fits it.
- Sideways the squares are 32 px (two grids side by side in a ~222 px column), under the 34 px floor elsewhere.
- On a computer the column is tall, so under the nine squares, the card and the grid there is empty panel.

Sources: src/ui/mobile/dash/GearTab.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/gearTabBus.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/itemCategory.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/gearStats.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/itemActions.js, src/ui/mobile/sheet/CharacterView.jsx, src/ui/mobile/sheet/heroStatPills.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/common.js, src/ui/mobile/sheet/VitalBar.jsx, tools/qa/mp/mp-geartab.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-gearshots.mjs

## A mystery piece in the bag
Screens: bag-unrevealed

**What it is for:** A rare-or-better drop you have not opened yet. It waits in the bag as a "?" until you choose to reveal it (v2.3.4012, owner: "Mystery item launched automatically. Should go to inventory then have reveal option").

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The tile is the reveal's own picture:** the item's painted art as a dark silhouette, a "?" over it in the rare blue, and the rare-blue 2 px edge. Rare blue because the pile only ever promised "rare or better", the same promise as the drop line and the pile's shine (v2.3.3746). No new art: a filter on the picture already loaded and one character of text, so it costs no memory.
- **You know what it is, not how good it is.** The tile and the card keep the item's name and kind (an Iron Torso is an Iron Torso); only the grade is hidden, as on the ground (rarity-reveal.md).
- **The card says "Rare or better" in blue and has two buttons: Reveal (the brass one) and Equip.** No damage or "% damage reduced" line, because that number would read the piece as a Normal. No Sell, Drop or Salvage until it is revealed.
- **Reveal closes the card,** so the reveal is seen. It is the same short, non-modal reveal as before (2 s a stage): the world keeps playing.
- **Equip reveals first,** then puts the piece on when the grade lands. What you wear is never a secret: your armour's grade shows on your body.
- **The tile stays a "?" until the reveal lands,** even though the bag already knows the answer, so the grade never shows under the spin.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Revealing on pickup again (that is the switch `revealbag: false`, for emergencies), or a modal reveal.
- A different colour for the "?" than the rare blue, or the word "Rare" on the tile.
- Selling or trading a "?" from this card: that is a separate plan (docs/specs/sealed-gear.md).

Sources: docs/specs/reveal-in-bag.md, docs/specs/rarity-reveal.md, docs/specs/sealed-gear.md, src/game/revealBag.js, src/ui/reveal/UnrevealedMark.jsx, src/ui/reveal/RevealOverlay.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/InventoryPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/ItemDetailPopup.jsx

## The More page and its pages
Screens: dash-more, more-settings, more-pets, more-social, more-journey, more-account, more-notifications, more-about, more-feedback

**What it is for:** The overflow: one grid of every page that has no button of its own on the row. Its pages hold the world map, social, settings, your pets, your account (log out, switch character, your Login Key), the notifications, and a way to talk to the developers.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Eleven tiles since v2.3.4393:** the Leaderboards left for a button of their own on the row (**The Leaderboards**), as Quests and the Codex did. Six and five upright, the twelfth cell empty.
- **The owner's More** (v2.3.4172): "More: map, social features, settings, account, character switching", and from the review he adopted, "Switch character / logout: More → Account" and "The notification bell could also move to More, retaining an unread badge". Twelve tiles: Map (opens the World map, the minimap's), Social (friends and messages), Party, Guild, Pets, Journey, Leaderboards, Settings, Account, Notifications (with the unread count, as on the More button), Feedback, About. Quests and the Codex left (their own buttons), Life Skills and Achievements too (pages under Bro); Feedback and About, rows inside Settings, got tiles. "Leaderboards" and "Notifications" break onto two lines where six across has no room, as "Achievements" did.
- **Account: the door first, then the key** (v2.3.4172). "Log out" (or "Switch character" when the device holds more than one bro) opens the same confirm window as the top bar's door, with its words and its one-bro rule ("Switch bro?" / "Leave the world?"), then the Login Key card and the form to continue another character.
- **Notifications** (v2.3.4172): the bell's lines, newest first, a page you can scroll (it is inside the dashboard, never over the movement stick), "Show on screen" for the World Chat feed in the corner, and where to silence them all (Settings).
- **One compact grid, every choice on one screen** (v2.3.1645, owner: "for the More shrink down the icons so I can see all the choices in a smaller format for the space"). Twelve tiles, six across (until v2.3.4172: Quests, Life Skills, Pets, Friends, Clan, Guild, Achievements, Journey, Codex, Ranks, Settings, Login Key). The tiles grew to fill the band for legibility (v2.3.1648), and six across stopped an eleventh tile starting a third row (v2.3.3120); the twelfth (Journey, v2.3.3773) fills the second row upright and the last cell of the two-across list sideways and on a computer, so no row was added. Sideways it is two across, because labels were cut to "Qu…" (v2.3.2173). Each tile's live status line now lives only in its tooltip, so that all the tiles fit.
- **Quests, Life Skills and Friends are here too** (v2.3.1641, owner: "add skills, quests, and friends to more panel"); they lost their only way in when the row shrank. The Codex tile opens the Codex's own page (v2.3.3215).
- **Login Key is a top-level tile** (v2.3.2038, owner: "is the character key retrievable once inside the game?"). It had been three taps deep behind a gear, though it is the only way back to a character. It was called Login Key, not Account, because that is what the card and the start screen call it; since v2.3.4172 the tile is Account (the owner's review: "Switch character / logout: More → Account"), its tooltip still naming the key and the key card on its page. The Settings row to it stays, for people who learned it there.
- **Settings** is a list of 44 px rows: Audio with Music and Effects sliders (v2.3.2820); Landscape menu side, Auto / Left / Right (v2.3.2177; three equal chips on one row, sideways too, since v2.3.3481 -- they wrapped two and one, Right alone looking like the bigger choice); silence all notifications; Login Key; replay the controls tutorial; "Play full screen — hide the browser bar", only in iPhone Safari's browser (v2.3.2159, owner: "there needs to be some kind of instruction on the game itself on how to do this"); Feedback; About; and a red Restart character. Choices show as chips with every value visible, never as a dropdown. The debug switch shows only in developer mode, and the Quest path row is hidden with the road on the ground (v2.3.2992).
- **Restart character has its own confirm screen** (v2.3.1347, an owner playtest request) that says what is deleted forever and what is kept. It reconnects and tries again rather than failing (v2.3.1424, owner: "restart button never works — says it can't right now").
- **The notification mute is in Settings, not where the owner first asked** (v2.3.2147, owner: "give options to silence all notifications (on bottom left above dashboard of screen)"). Three placements in that corner were tried, and each took drags from the movement stick or broke the tutorial. The code's verdict: "A mute switch is worth less than being able to walk."
- **Pets** (More → Pets, or the farm's Pet House; v2.3.3120–3123). Each pet shows its portrait, name, working level (capped by your Trapping), kind, "With you", Golden and Big badges, an XP bar and what its land ward softens. A tap gives Take out / Put away, Rename and Release; Release asks first, "Release … for good?". Naming was the owner's ask ("let people name their pets"): 2–16 letters, numbers, spaces, - or ', and no emoji, which crash iPhone Safari in outlined text. Then come trade status, Sell at the Auction House, and More room for the Pet House ("Pet House: room for 10 more pets, 1,000 gold", the price the server's; it said "10 more places" until v2.3.3481), confirmed once; that row leads with the owner's picture of a pet house, the one thing on the page you can buy (their icon pick 6, v2.3.3481). The empty page states the real odds, "About one try in a hundred at best" (owner: "leave the odds exactly the same for everyone"), and those words come before More room, so a new player learns how to catch a pet before being offered room for more (v2.3.3481; with pets, More room stays above the list). A Journal shows all 18 kinds and stages, uncaught ones as shapes. Every button waits for the server's answer.
- **Friends** (server friendships since v2.3.1323–1324, asked for by a ChatGPT review: requests, messages, unread badges). Friends / Requests tabs and "N online". Add Friend says honestly how it works, "Tap another player anywhere in Bro Town to view their profile and add them as a friend", with Share Invite Link; there is no add-by-name. Each row shows a ring (green online, amber away after 2 minutes, grey offline), name and level, the zone or last seen, an unread count, and a ••• menu: Profile, Message (mutual friends only), Invite, Remove, Block. A declined request is never announced.
- **Ranks: one board per skill** (v2.3.1671, owner: "a traditional high scores system that ranks each skill... I want all of the lifeskills to be added on the high scores too"): Combat, Melee, Bow, Magic, Kills and every life skill, using only numbers the server counts. Gold and AP went, because they were the phone's own claims. AP came back in v2.3.3527 as the server's own number: an AP board after Kills while the server grants achievements, "1,234 AP", only players with some. Your own row is highlighted, and the top three ranks are brass. Every board but Kills is a level and says so, "Lv 3" (v2.3.3481; Combat is the name plate's level -- since v2.3.3538 the best of the three combat levels, it was the three added up); Kills is a plain count. The page is headed Ranks, its tile's word (v2.3.3481; it said Leaderboard); the Town Hall's row and the profile's button still say Leaderboard.
- **Journey is your records, with a tile of its own** (v2.3.3773, owner: "I also think I want 'journey' to be put as its own icon in the more/other apps menus. That way the two main ones, equipment and points, stand out further as just two tabs."). It is the Character page's old Journey tab, moved whole: six records, Kills (the server's count), Deaths, Lifetime Gold, Lifetime XP, Duels Won and Deepest Zone, three across in two rows upright; sideways they stack in one column so the labels fit (v2.3.2176, owner: "labels that are still cut off"). Under them, once a level has been written to Hemi, a link to its transaction ("Click to view the transaction" on a computer). Its tile's line reads "N kills · N duels won". Achievements keeps its own tile beside it. It replaced a travel log that nothing in the game or the server ever wrote, so it only ever showed "Your journey is just beginning." under the owner's treasure map (their icon pick 9, v2.3.3481; the picture stays on disk).
- **The Login Key page** shows your key as eight numbered words, with Copy (v2.3.3355, the owner's choice: "8 short words"); the words go four across where they fit (two by four sideways), so all eight and Copy are in the first view, and Copy takes its row as "Copy Login Key" -- the joined key's field showed it cut short ("slept-hash-") and is kept out of sight only for Copy's fallback when the clipboard refuses (v2.3.3481; an old short key keeps its field). It says: "This Login Key is the only way to get your character back on a new phone or after clearing your browser. Save it somewhere safe. There is no email recovery." Below it, a form continues another character with its key. The key is checked with the server and confirmed on a "Continue as your Lv N character?" preview, so a wrong key can never wipe this device's key or quietly make a new character.
- **About** (v2.3.2820; a demo audit found no privacy notice, rules or credits anywhere a player could see): what the game keeps and why, each line matching something the code really stores; the rules (players 13 and over); credits for the AI tools and sound creators; and a Send feedback button.
- **Feedback** (v2.3.2820): Submit has a 500-character box, then "What kind?" and "About" chips, and a Send that says honestly whether it arrived ("Thanks — it's on the board"). The box comes first so it is in the first view; under the chips it started below the fold everywhere (v2.3.3481). The kinds are Bug, Balance, Remove, Add, Improve and Praise; Improve said "QoL", gamer shorthand, until v2.3.3481. Browse is the public board, Newest / Most liked, with up-votes that a second tap takes back. Before v2.3.2820, every report was lost. Under Send, a footnote (v2.3.5821, the owner, 2026-11-17, on routing reports: ordinary bugs stay on this board, security holes go privately): "Found a cheat or a security hole? This board is public, so please report it privately on GitHub instead.", the link opening GitHub's private report form in a new tab, the same address as the site's security.txt (site-headers guard). A footnote, not a line above the box: the box stays first in view. No "tap" or "click" in it, so it reads the same on a phone and a computer.
- **Clan and Guild tiles:** clans are parties now (v2.3.3321, owner: "skip clans altogether and just go from party to alliances"), and the Clan page says so, with an "Invite a player" button ("Find a party" until v2.3.3481: it opens the player list, where you invite someone, and the server refuses an invite to anyone already in a party, so it could never find you one). Guild shows your rank in each skill guild, set by that skill's level (v2.3.3066).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- A status-hub More page with headings, two-line captions and three card shapes (v2.3.1299), which scrolled with two or three choices in view. It became the grid (v2.3.1645).
- Six tiles, then nine, then eleven, then twelve; the Login Key folded into Settings (v2.3.1291) and brought back out (v2.3.2038).
- Journey as a travel log nothing wrote (v2.3.1299-v2.3.3772), and from v2.3.3527 its tile given to Achievements; now it is the records page and both tiles are there (v2.3.3773).
- Feedback that went nowhere and a "coming soon" Browse tab; a leaderboard that never fetched anything ("No leaderboard data yet." for everyone); friends as a one-way list kept on the phone; a Clan page telling players to type developer code.
- The mute floating over the bottom-left corner, tried three ways (v2.3.2147).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Any control that is always on screen over the bottom-left corner: it is the movement stick's area.
- The Login Key only inside Settings.
- Captions under every More tile, or headings that push tiles off the first screen.
- Gold or AP boards from the phone's own numbers, a per-player luck rule for trapping, or emoji in pet names.
- A debug switch for players, or a Quest path row while the road on the ground is put away.

**Still open:**
- The About page is "a plain-language starting draft written from the code, not legal advice; the owner signs off on the wording (and the age line) before it is relied on".

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/trapping.md, docs/PET-TRAPPING-PLAN.md, docs/specs/owner-icons.md, docs/specs/friends.md, docs/specs/account-login.md, docs/specs/login-keys.md, docs/specs/landscape-mode.md, src/ui/mobile/dash/MorePanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/SettingsPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/PetsPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/SocialPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/LeaderboardPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/JourneyPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/AccountPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/NotificationsPanel.jsx, src/ui/account/AccountKeyCard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/AboutPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/FeedbackPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/ClanPanel.jsx

## Achievements
(Today's page, under `?ui2=0` only since v2.3.6695: mp-achievements plays it there. The screen `dash-bro-achievements` shows Feats now: see "Feats".)

**What it is for:** Points for completing the game (v2.3.3527). The owner: "Achievement points are something I'd like in the game. You get it by completing the game. Some of them are through super rare achievements. Although not as rare, an arena winner should be included in that." Twenty-three achievements, each earned once: seven common, nine rare, the arena's one and six super-rare, 7,470 achievement points (AP) in all. v2.3.4036 added ten more, the rare fish eaten (see "Rare fish"): an albino of each of the five fish, common, 5 AP, and a large one, rare, 10 AP -- each row's own points, as no tier pays 5 -- so 33 and 7,545 AP. The server decides every one; the page only shows what it says. The owner answered the plan's six questions "Go recommended on everything" (docs/specs/achievements.md "Decided").

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The page is under Bro** (v2.3.4172, the owner's five buttons; it was More's tile, v2.3.3527). The Bro card's Achievements row ("Achievement Points: N", your latest three badges) and its AP open it as a page pushed under Bro, with its drilled header (the AP, the count and the title ride it) and a back chip to the card; every old road to it (the top bar's medal, a card's title chip) lands there.
- **A tier's points: common 10 AP, rare 100, super-rare 1,000, and the arena's 500 between them** ("although not as rare"). Each tier wears the armour's grade colours (v2.3.3142): common grey, rare blue, the arena orange, super-rare a rainbow, as the edge and words of its points pill, never a fill.
- **The arena pays its 500 AP once a character.** The first win earns the Gladiator achievement; a later win pays its gold and counts on the row ("×3"). Paid on every win (v2.3.3481), a second account in a two-player bracket made 500 every few minutes; the owner: "I don't want anyone exploiting the achievement points from it". Main paid it once under a mark of its own first (v2.3.3488); a champion paid that way gets the achievement at their next login, with no second 500. The arena window says so.
- **The super-rares are shown before they are earned:** named, with how to earn them, and a lock, never "???". Players hunt what they can see.
- **A big one is a moment, a common one a line.** Rare, the arena's and super-rare get a banner over the world (the achievement's picture and "Gladiator · +500 AP" on a plate in the tier's colour, about three seconds, never in the way of a tap) and the arena champion's fanfare. A common one is a gold line in the chat, "Achievement: First Blood (+10 AP)". The banner waits for a loading screen to lift, two take turns, and it moves down below a NEW MONSTER card that is up at the same time (a kill can raise both). On a computer it is centred on the game's column, not the window.
- **What you had already done counts, quietly.** At the join the server grants what it can see you did before achievements existed (a kill, a combat level, the story, the codex's nine kinds, a pet) as one chat line and no banners, so a returning player is not buried in fanfare.
- **A duel won and a No man's land kill are common, once each.** A friend's second account can hand you one; at 10 AP that is the owner's accepted trade.
- **Journey's tile became Achievements:** the same place on More, the owner's medal its picture (the journey scroll until the badges came). On a narrow tile the label breaks "Achieve-ments" onto two lines rather than lose its end; the tooltip says "N of 23 earned". The title and the rarest box on a player's card open the page too.
- **The page:** your AP in gold and "9/23", and your title under them, on the page's own header row, right of "◂ ACHIEVEMENTS" (sideways, where that row has no room, on a line of their own under it). Then the chips All, Common, Rare and Super-rare (the arena's row is under All and Rare), and one 48 px row each: picture, name, a one-line clue ("Clear all 3 Wheel dungeons") and how far along you are where the server already knows ("best Lv 23", "4/9", "1/3"), the points pill, and the day you earned it ("Oct 19") or a lock, with "×N" on the rows that count. A tap opens a row in place: its line shows whole ("Clear the Great Cave, the Foundry Dome and the Buried City · 1 of 3 cleared"), and on an earned row the title buttons. One row open at a time. The page's last line says where your AP came from, "Your 1,850 AP: 1,840 from achievements, 10 from quests, guilds and more", because story quests (+5 each) and the guild ladder already paid AP. Sideways the side column is about 238 px: the chips go two by two, a long name takes two lines, nothing scrolls sideways.
- **Two rows in view, not one** (the owner's ChatGPT review, 2026-10-19: "Keep the system; compact the overview, not the world"). The page first had three lines of summary and the chips over the band's 191 px, so it opened on about one row. The summary moved into the empty right side of the header row, the split to the page's end, and the title buttons onto the opened row, so the page opens on two whole rows and part of a third. The band's height did not change. Every finding was checked against the code before anything changed; see "Tried before" for what was not built.
- **Your title** (the card's placeholder "Fishing Master" until v2.3.3527): an earned row, opened, offers "Use as title"; the one you wear has a small gold tick by its name and, opened, says "Your title" with "Remove title"; a row you have not earned never offers it. With none chosen the header says "No title yet: tap one you have". The card's title chip and rarest box open the page under More, with its header (a page opened on its own had no name and no way back). Each tap asks the server and the buttons wait for its answer ("Saving…"), so the page never shows a title the server did not keep. Ten quick changes, then one every 10 seconds. The card's rarest box is worked out by the server, never chosen: the rarest one you have, by tier and then by the game's own odds.
- **Each achievement wears its own painted badge** (the owner's two sheets, 2026-10-19). The owner chose painted badges over ChatGPT's flat code-drawn stand-ins ("Wait for painted ones"), so the 23 match the painted icons on More and the bag. Each is cut at 150 px, three times the card's 50 px rarest box, the biggest place one is drawn, and dimmed until you earn it. Sheet 2's twelfth picture, a medal, is the page's own icon on More's tile, framed like its neighbours so it is drawn their size.
- **AP is a score.** Nothing spends it and nothing takes it back. The number everyone else sees (the card, the player lists, Ranks' AP board) is the server's own, written over whatever a phone claims.
- **Left out on purpose:** life-skill levels (the guild ladder already pays AP for them), owning Hemi Bros (buying, not playing), and logins or streaks (not "completing the game").

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The phone kept 32 "badges" of its own that nothing showed and the server never knew, and a rare item drop added AP on the phone alone; both went in v2.3.3527.
- The arena's 500 AP on every win (v2.3.3481).
- An AP board from the phone's own numbers, taken off Ranks in v2.3.1671; the board is back with the server's number.
- The first page (v2.3.3527, before the review): three summary lines over the chips (AP, the split, the title sentence), rows that wrapped their whole "how to earn it" (Delver's took three lines) and "Use as title" on every earned row. It opened on about one row.
- Each row borrowed a picture the game already had (the melee icon on three rows, the pets icon on three), until the owner's badges (2026-10-19). ChatGPT's first answer to the badge prompts was a set of flat vector stand-ins drawn in code (its image tool did not follow the prompt; it said so itself); shown them in the game, the owner waited for painted ones.
- From the owner's ChatGPT review (2026-10-19), NOT built: a "Closest first" sort button (only six of the 23 rows have progress the server sends, so it would reorder those six for a button in the crowded chip row), and a smaller banner type (its 14 px name and 13 px points would make the name smaller than its 16 px today). The super-rare rarest box's purple fill became a rainbow edge on the card's slate, as the review asked.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Hiding the super-rares as "???", or a banner and fanfare for every common one.
- The arena's 500 AP on every win.
- AP counted on the phone, anything that spends AP, or taking AP back.
- A seventh button on the dashboard's row: the owner put its door on More, in Journey's place.
- Achievements for life-skill levels, owning Hemi Bros, or logging in.
- Code-drawn badges in place of the owner's painted ones.

**Still open:**
- The four tiers' frames (an optional third sheet, docs/ART-WISHLIST.md "Achievements"): today the tier is the points pill's colour and the edge of the banner and the rarest box.
- The banner's plate is drawn in code, with no art of its own.
- What the Journey page becomes: nothing writes a journey, and with achievements on its tile is gone.

Sources: docs/specs/achievements.md, server/src/achievements.js, src/data/achievements.js, src/game/achievements.js, tools/ui/cut-achievement-badges.py, src/ui/mobile/dash/AchievementsPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/AchievementBanner.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/MorePanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/LeaderboardPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/buildings/PartyPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/PlayerProfilePanel.jsx

## The Leaderboards
Screens: ui2-ranks-board

(v2.3.6692: this is today's page, now drawn only under `?ui2=0`; under the new dashboard see "Ranks under the new dashboard" at the end, whose states replaced dash-leaderboard, leaderboard-picker, leaderboard-skill and leaderboard-spin.)

**What it is for:** Who is the highest level, who has the most kills, who took the biggest pot from the daily spin, who weighed the heaviest trout. It has its own button on the dashboard's row ("Ranks") since v2.3.4393. The owner, 2026-11-09: "make room for a new 'leaderboards' button on the dashboard (I think this is already in the 'more' button). But the leaderboards should be expanded and easier to navigate from mobile and include the character profile pic and level next to each record. Also I want to have the 'daily spin' biggest wins and losses records. Additionally is it possible to have stand-in (deleted character) rows if the record was made by a deleted character? Maybe still include their profile pic and level but just grayed out with the deleted label over it." And on 2026-11-14, with two mockups and an art sheet (`assets/ui/leaderboard/`): "I'd also like something more like this for leaderboards (mockup and art assets attached)", and of the second mockup, "This pic is better mock representation". And on 2026-11-25, with one more mockup (`docs/plan/status/assets/ranks-mock-2026-11-25.jpg`, made with ChatGPT), right after asking for the Bro and Quests tabs in pages ("all related information is shown at once ... Players see the big picture first and can investigate the details whenever they want"): "Also I prefer changing the leaderboard style to something like this".

**Decisions, and why:**
- **One board at a glance since v2.3.5503** (card LB part 2, the owner's 2026-11-25 mockup; `RanksPage.jsx`, its rules `src/game/ranksPage.js`): four tabs across the top, each its picture and word, the open one on the gold bar -- **Power** (the sheet's sword: Overall, Melee, Bow, Magic, Level), **Stats** (the Bro tab's shield: the eight stat boards), **Records** (the sheet's gold trophy: Kills, AP, Daily spin, Coin flip, Biggest fish), **Skills** (Mining's pickaxe: the ten life skills). Under them one row: the **picker** (the open board's picture and word and an arrow; a tap opens the tab's boards as a list over the table, 44 px each, its ✕ pressed by Escape), "RANKS" between the laurels, and a quiet tag "All-time leaderboard" (no board is a daily one: the Daily spin's records are its biggest pots ever). Then the heads (#, Player, a "?" on Overall only -- the only board today's page explained -- Lv, the board's own word), the rows (the only part that scrolls), and **your row pinned at the foot**, framed in gold: your face, "<name> · You", "Rank #N" and your value, or the mockup's dashes off the board ("Rank #—"; your own face drawn from your look). A tab opens the board you last looked at in it. Every board of the list before is under a tab; a board the page does not know goes under Records, last.
- **One value column**, the board's own number, as the mockup draws it: the Power on Overall, the weapon's level on Melee, Bow and Magic, the level on Level (no second "Lv" chip there), a stat's points, the kills, the AP, a skill's level. Overall's three style scores and the weapons' Total are on the page before (`?rankstyle=0`), not on this one.
- **The band keeps its height** (191 px upright at 390 x 844, 187 at 375 x 667, 82 on its side), so the world stays in sight: the rows take what the tabs, the picker, the heads and your row leave, sized so the first three (gold, silver, bronze) always show at once upright (`ranksFit`). On its side the tabs, the picker and your row (its rank and value) share the one top line, the heads are left out and two rows show; the picker's list is then one line of boards scrolled sideways. The mockup's five rows want a taller band: the owner's call.
- **The records' boards** (the spin, the flip, the fish) are today's boards inside the new frame (their wins / losses switch and the fish's kinds on their first line); your row is pinned under the spin's and the flip's.
- **`?rankstyle=0`** gives back the page below (the list down the left, the podium), which the rest of this section describes.
- **The look is the owner's mockups'** (v2.3.5501, card LB; `docs/specs/leaderboard-look.md`). The second mockup sets the layout; the owner's sheet gives the art, cut into pieces by `tools/ui/cut-leaderboard.mjs` (v2.3.5500), each at 2 picture px a CSS px on 256 colours.
- **In the dashboard's band, as every page is.** The world stays visible above; everything is sized from the band, never the window.
- **"RANKS" between two laurels heads the list**, not "LEADERBOARDS" (the second mockup): the list is 108 px wide, and the button on the row already says Ranks.
- **A list of boards down the left, the board beside it** (v2.3.4393). Before v2.3.4393 seventeen boards were one row of chips scrolled sideways: six showed on a phone and nothing said there were eleven more. You scroll the list with a thumb, one tap changes board, and the one showing is lit. The list and the board each scroll by themselves. Each board is a 44 px target.
- **The order is the second mockup's.** Overall (the crown tile) leads and opens first (v2.3.5502: the worker has the power index, `caps.power`, card PW; a worker without it shows no Overall). Then Melee, Bow and Magic with the sheet's sword, bow and staff tiles, then Level. The first mockup's five stat boards (Might, Fortitude, Vitality, Agility, Mind) were retired stat names; in their place (the owner, through the planning session: "You can add more stats to leaderboard if you think it's worth it") a **Stats** group under Level ranks the points put in each shared stat, Defense, Max HP, Dodge, Stamina, Resist, Max Mana and Speed, and the weapons' damage points as **Damage** (never "Power", which is Overall's), each with the Bro tab's own icon and word (v2.3.5502, card PW's boards). Then the sheet's gold divider and every board there was before: Kills, AP, **Records** (Daily spin, Coin flip, Biggest fish) and **Skills** (the ten life skills). Nothing was dropped. The page opens on the list's first board.
- **The pills are the sheet's pill bars**, the plain one, and the gold one on the board showing; the word is lit gold on it. Each board keeps its own picture (the level-up star, the sword and skull of Kills, the achievements' medal, the prize wheel, a gold coin, a trout, each life skill's icon,. Words of eleven letters ("Woodworking") are 11 px so they stay whole; "Smithing" is the Life Skills page's word, because "Blacksmithing" does not fit.
- **The headings (Records, Skills) headline their sections** (v2.3.4394, the owner: "Why do the menu titles look dark and subtle?"): brass, 13 px, heavy, a brass rule under each (`railKit.js` `railHeading`).
- **The podium**: the first three on the sheet's shields, silver 2 on the left, gold 1 in the middle and raised, bronze 3 on the right, each with the face in the shield's field, the name on the ribbon (its letters shrink to fit, never under 11 px, then it ends in "..."; the table's row has it whole) and the board's own value under it as the row prints it, a plain number ("Lv N" on the Level board only: one "Lv", the name plate's), the Power on Overall, "1,234 kills", "1,234 AP" or a stat's "12 pts", so the shields read in the board's order. It is scaled to the board's width and height so the table's head and three rows stay in sight under it (the second mockup's four rows). Where that would make it smaller than 0.6 of its size (a phone on its side) it is not drawn: the table alone.
- **The table**: on the weapons' boards and Level, #, Player, Lv, Total and the sword, bow and staff columns. Total is today's number, the three weapons' levels added up (its head's tooltip says so). On **Overall** (v2.3.5502) the column is Power, a rating (the owner, 2026-11-14: the control character 3,000, about 200 at level 1 to 4,700 at level 40), written in full ("4,712"), and the three columns are each style's score, the row's own (`powerIndex`), short in their 24 px ("2.7k"); a character with no index yet reads "-", never 0. A tap on "Power?" in the head (on an upright phone, the "?" at the end of the Player head) shows the owner's own reading of the rating under it, "400 points ahead = 10× likelier to win" (a computer's pointer gets it as the tooltip too). The weapons' boards and Level keep the levels, the numbers they are ranked by. A stat's board shows the points under the stat's name. On every other board: #, Player, Lv and the record ("1,234 AP", a kill count, a skill's level under the skill's name). The column a board ranks by is the bright one.
- **Whole names on an upright phone.** The board beside the list is about 250-265 px wide on an upright iPhone, too narrow for a face, a name, Lv, the Total and three more columns (a first cut left a 375 px phone about 16 px for the name). There the weapons' boards and Level show Lv and either the Total or the three weapons, never both; the head's columns after Lv are one button that swaps them (Melee, Bow and Magic open on the weapons, the one the board ranks by; Level on the Total). The name then has about 105 px and takes two lines before it is cut. A computer, and a phone on its side, show every column.
- **The level is a small pill after the name** (v2.3.4393), the name plate's: so a board that ranks something else never shows two numbers that both read "Lv". A skill's level is a plain number under the skill's name; on the Level board the pill is the record.
- **Rows 1-3 sit on the gold, silver and bronze bars with the laurel medals** (both mockups), a crown by the first name; from fourth place, the plain number on the band. Your own row wears the plain bar below the first three, and a light ring round its metal bar in them, so you find yourself in fifty rows. The records' boards (the spin, the flip, the fish) use the same medals and bars.
- **No pages.** The board scrolls inside the band (fifty rows, the server's limit), as every board did. The first mockup's "Page 1 / 10" would add a row of buttons to a band that shows four rows; scrolling is the band's own way.
- **The medals shine and sparkle**: v2.3.4393's code for the trophies (the owner: "Add a cool code based effect for shine or slight sparkling"), moved onto the medals. A band of light crosses each one, cut to its own shape, and small stars wink on its laurel. Nothing moves under Reduce Motion, and a deleted character's medal is grey and still.
- **Where the page is too narrow or too short for the two side by side** (a phone on its side, a band about 80 px tall) the pills are one row over the table, the headings small between them and the divider a thin line, the board showing scrolled into sight.
- **The face is a Hemi Bro bust drawn from the look the server kept with the record** (the Ace board's rule, v2.3.2619: a look, never an image). It draws for a player who is offline; a row with no look shows the name's initial on a tile.
- **Daily spin: Biggest wins / Biggest losses** on the board's first line (two buttons, never two more rows in the list). A win is a pot paid; a loss is a pot lost to a double or nothing. The line under the name says how: "No doubles", "3 doubles", "On the 2nd double". Both spins count. **Coin flip** is Ace's own hall of fame (v2.3.2619), with the same two buttons.
- **A deleted character keeps its row** (v2.3.4393): grey, the face desaturated and dimmed under a small DELETED stamp. It holds its place until someone beats it. On the Ranks boards it stays 7 days, as a player who stopped playing does; on the Records boards until it is beaten.
- **"Ranks" on the button and the title, "Leaderboards" for its name** (the tooltip, the screen reader's name, these notes).
- **The page opens on the board you last looked at** while the game is open.
- **The art loads with the window**, not behind the loading screen: the pieces are asked for as the Leaderboards' own script loads (the first time the window opens), as the trophies were. They are files in the page, shown only while the window is open (`docs/MEMORY-MAP.md`, "a window's own art, bigger than an icon").

**Tried before, and changed:**
- One row of chips scrolled sideways (v2.3.1671 to v2.3.4354): see above.
- The boards as a list down the left beside the board, a podium of the first three over the table (v2.3.4393 to v2.3.5502): the 2026-11-25 mockup's tabs and picker replaced them (v2.3.5503); `?rankstyle=0` still shows them.
- A tile under More (until v2.3.4393).
- The owner's trophies for the first five places, 40 px each, in the rank's place (v2.3.4393 to v2.3.5500): the new sheet's laurel medals for the first three replace them, as both mockups show; from fourth place the plain number.
- The dashboard's own quiet pill with a brass rim (v2.3.4393 to v2.3.5500): the sheet's pill bars.
- A list 122 px wide (v2.3.4393 to v2.3.5500): 108 px, so the table's columns fit beside it on a phone.
- "Taken as it landed" and "Lost on the 2nd double" under a spin row: cut at 390 px. Shortened.
- A 10 px DELETED stamp: it ran over the rank and the name. 9 px, on purpose.
- (First cut of v2.3.5501) a podium drawn at the board's full width: on a phone it left one row of the table in sight. It is scaled to the board's height too.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Tabs across the top as well as a list down the side: since v2.3.5503 the tabs and the picker ARE the list (the band is about 190 px tall on a phone, and every fixed row costs a row of the table).
- Pages as well as the scroll: see above.
- "#1" written beside the medal as well: the medal says it.
- A glow or blur filter behind the medals: the shine is transforms and opacity only.
- A board fed by the phone's own numbers: every board here is the server's (v2.3.1671).
- Hiding a deleted character's record: the owner asked for the stand-in.
- (v2.3.1232's "brass is an accent, never a slab" is set aside here by the owner's own mockup: the first three rows on their metal bars.)

**Still open:**
- A "you are #37" line pinned under a board when you are below what it shows.
- When each record was set ("3 days ago").
- The picker's "Remove from this device" does not delete a character, so its rows stay live; only a restart makes a stand-in.

Sources: docs/specs/leaderboards.md, server/src/boards.js, server/src/leaderboard.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/LeaderboardPanel.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/BoardRow.jsx, src/game/spinBoard.js

## Rare fish: the weigh-in card and Biggest fish
Screens: fish-weigh, fish-board

**What it is for:** Rare catches and a reason to weigh them (v2.3.4036). The owner: "Add a 1% chance of catching an albino version of whatever fish you're trying to catch. Consuming adds a one time 5 achievement point bonus. Also add a 0.1% chance of catching 'large' whatever fish you're fishing. Consuming adds 10 achievement points (one time). It also heals double. Doing an 'inspect' will roll for finding the weight of the fish. Include leaderboards for largest caught types of fish (triggered by inspection)." The server rolls the catch, the weight and the boards (docs/specs/fish-variants.md); these screens only say what it decided.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The catch says it, in the popup over your head:** "An albino Trout!" (pale pink) or "A LARGE Salmon!" (gold), with the collect chime, beside the usual "+Trout". A plain fish says nothing new.
- **A rare fish is its own item, so it never hides in a stack:** "Albino Trout" and "Large Trout" are separate tiles from the plain trout, and stay so cooked ("Cooked Albino Trout"). They sit under the bag's Fish chip like any fish.
- **No new art (the owner's lane decision).** In the bag an albino is the species' own picture washed pale with a little pink (a CSS filter on the bag tile only), and a large one wears a small brass "L" in the tile's top-left corner (the anchor owns top-right, the count bottom-right). The item card and the weigh-in card are drawn over the world, where a CSS filter is the iPhone grain hazard (TRAPS §42), so there the name says "Albino" and the picture is plain. The ten achievements wear their fish's bag picture until the owner paints badges.
- **Inspect is one more button on the item card** (beside Eat on a cooked fish, Sell and Drop), a quiet secondary button, on any fish, raw or cooked, only against a server that weighs (caps.fishweigh). Lane A is rebuilding the card around one shared action list: Inspect belongs in it.
- **Each caught fish can be weighed once.** A fish you bought or were given has no catch to weigh, and the card says so plainly: "You've weighed all your Trout." A cooked fish weighs its raw catch. Weights are the owner's ranges in kilograms (Minnow 0.05-0.2, Clownfish 0.1-0.4, Trout 0.5-3, Salmon 2-8, Pike 2-12; a large one 3 to 5 times the biggest plain one); under a kilo they read "0.12 kg", above it "2.4 kg".
- **The weigh-in card sits where the achievement banner does** (centred on the world, under the minimap upright, near the top sideways), so it never covers the stick or the attack buttons, and passes every touch but its own. "This Trout weighs 2.4 kg", then "Your heaviest Trout yet!" or "Your best: 3.1 kg", "· #3 on the board" in gold when it took a place, and "· 2 more to weigh". It stays about nine seconds, or until its ✕ (Esc on a computer) or Records. 44 px targets: the ✕ and Records stack at the card's right edge.
- **Since v2.3.4393 Records opens the Leaderboards (their own button on the row) on Biggest fish at that species**, and each fish row has the face and the level it was weighed with (**The Leaderboards**).
- **Records opens More, Ranks, on the Biggest fish tab at that species.** The boards are a chip of Ranks, the last one, only against a server that weighs, rather than a page of their own: they are rankings, and Ranks is where rankings are. Five species chips; the ten heaviest each, one row a player (their best), "#1 Name Albino 2.4 kg", your own row highlighted, the top three ranks brass, as every board on the page.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- (New in v2.3.4036.)

**Please don't suggest:**
- A weight for every fish shown without Inspect: the weigh-in is the owner's "triggered by inspection".
- Weighing the same catch again, or a fish that was bought: one catch, one roll, or the boards are a reroll machine.
- A CSS filter on anything drawn over the world.

**Still open:**
- Painted badges for the ten rare-fish achievements, and painted albino and large pictures (both would replace the filter and the "L").

Sources: docs/specs/fish-variants.md, server/src/fishlog.js, src/data/fishVariants.js, src/game/fishWeigh.js, src/ui/panels/FishWeighCard.jsx, src/ui/mobile/dash/FishBoard.jsx

## A window that could not open
Screens: lazy-cantopen

**What it is for:** The windows a player opens now and then (ranks, the codex, achievements, settings, the clan, feedback, the buildings' windows, a player's card...) load their code the first time they are opened (v2.3.3717, on for everyone since v2.3.3736: the owner, "I think including the new memory change work by default would be better"). A game left open across an update asks for the OLD version's code, which the site no longer has. The window then says so instead of staying blank.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Plain words, and what to do:** "Couldn't open this" over "Reload the game to get the newest version." The fix is always the same (the page again brings the new version's code), so the card says it, and its Reload button does it.
- **The game plays on under it.** A deploy can land while the judges play: nothing stops, the world keeps moving, and only that one window is missing.
- **Close puts the window away too** (its own close, so the game is not left "busy" behind a window with no ✕). Opened again, it tries again.
- **One try more first, unseen.** A dropped connection is the other way a window's code can fail; it is tried once more, a moment later, before the card shows.
- **The middle of the play column**, in the game's dark-and-gold card, with Reload (`data-enter`) and Close (`data-esc`) for a computer's keys.
- **A window that breaks while it is open uses the same card** (v2.3.5593, the red team's RT-3 S3): "This window hit a problem" over "Close it and open it again, or reload the game." Before it, one bad value in one window (a feedback post whose name was not words) blanked every screen of the game, because nothing caught the error. Now the window's guard catches it, the card shows (`data-lazy-cantopen="threw"`), the game plays on, and the error goes to the crash feed. Opened again, the window is drawn afresh. The words differ from the update card's because the fix differs: here closing and opening again is usually enough.

Sources: src/ui/lazyPanel.js, src/ui/lazyPanels.js, docs/specs/worker-code-diet.md, docs/specs/feedback-guard.md, tools/qa/mp/mp-lazyfail.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-panelguard.mjs

## The Town Hall's board and the map pins
Screens: building-townhall, building-townhall-page, building-townhall-action

- **It is on the square's noticeboard now (v2.3.3899).** The owner: "Remove the town hall menu for now. It's too confusing when trying to turn in mayor bro quests. It keeps popping up and it'll confuse players. You can move the menu to the sign post to the left of the town hall near the 3 street lamps." That post is the noticeboard (the catalog's "Bragging board") in the square's north-west corner, beside the lamps. A tap on it opens the board; standing at it shows "Read THE TOWN BOARD" (E on a computer). The Town Hall's door opens nothing, and Mayor Bro talks instead of opening it. The window is headed "Town Board". This reverses v2.3.3258 (the Mayor as the hall's keeper) and v2.3.3766 (the board opening as you enter the hall). Found by its kind at runtime (game/wheelNoticeboard.js); the window and its screens are the same.

**What it is for:** One place that says everything there is to do in the world and where it is, for a new player (and a judge) who does not know the game yet. The owner, 2026-10-28: "at the town hall it was just a big menu board of all the things to do (dungeon, gambling, arena, woodworking, etc) with a page that describes what each thing does and pin drop you can put on the map to go to the one you want (two pins can be active at once - red pin and blue pin). When you "enter it" it brings up the board."

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The board is the Town Hall's window (v2.3.3766)**: a grid of tiles (picture and name) under eight plain headings: Quests, Fight, Dungeons, Explore the lands, Make, Money and trade, Gambling, People. The dungeons were under Fight until the owner's word (v2.3.3779): "it should be classified by 'dungeon' not by 'fight'"; Fight keeps the Arena, No man's land and Duels. Then (v2.3.3788) the owner: "'Gambling' should be another category. I don't necessarily agree with the categories you have. Also list the level ranges below the dungeons (e.g lvl 10+ for goo grotto) ... (quests should've be another one)". Quests comes first: the Mayor's story (pinned on Mayor Bro), the townsfolk's quests (no pin: six people, the gold "!" over a head says who), the Guild Hall's ladders and Lil Bro's race; Gambling has the Gambling Den and Ace's coin flip (backing a fighter stays on the Arena's tile). The owner will think the headings over again; until then only these three changes were made.
- **The owner's review of the board (v2.3.3801)**: the owner ran the board past ChatGPT and adopted its review. The headings, in this order: Quests, Explore the Lands, Dungeons, Fight, Craft & Grow (was Make), Trade & Bank (was Money and trade), Gambling, Town Life (was People). Lil Bro's Race moved to Town Life ("racing is recreation, not a quest"); the Guild Hall stays in Quests. A Skateboard tile in Town Life (with the worker's boards), its button's own picture.
- **A line under every tile's name (v2.3.3801)**, small: what it is in a few words, its numbers from the code ("Entry: 100 gold", "10 skill guilds", "Grow 16 crops", "45% chance to win"); a land its compass and element from the world's plan ("West · Flora", "SW · Venom"); a dungeon its levels (below). The Bank says "Gold & gear overview", or "Store gold & items" (and its page the vault's words) once the server has lane L's vault (caps.bank). It fits its tile at 390 and 320 px upright, sideways and on a computer (mp-townboard).
- **A page with no one place has ONE action button (v2.3.3801)** in place of the pins: Town Quests "Open Quests" (your Quests page); the Skateboard "Ride now", only where you may ride (BroTown's safe ground), which puts you on the board and the window away; No man's land none.
- **Badges, from real state only (v2.3.3803)**: a tile says so when something waits there, in its top-left corner (the pins have the right): Mayor's Story and Town Quests a count of the quests offered now, on the gold of the "!" pin -- read from the very marks over the townsfolk's heads that the maps' quest pins read, so the board never disagrees with the street -- and the Gambling Den "Spin ready" while today's free spin is untaken (the server's daily state, what the Den's wheel reads). Nothing static: no badge when there is nothing to say.
- **The foot (v2.3.3801)**: the Leaderboard and the World map, and under them two small shortcuts, Codex and Journey, each opening its page of the dashboard.
- **A dungeon's tile says its levels under its name (v2.3.3788)**, small: "Lv 10+ · party of 2–3" for the Goo Grotto (the level it is for and the party it is built for), "Lv 26+" for the Great Cave and the Foundry Dome, "Lv 41+" for the Buried City (the lowest of the place's levels). Read from the dungeon tables and the world's plan, never typed on the board; it fits its tile upright at 390 and 320 px, sideways and on a computer (mp-townboard). Only what is built and reachable today, and only what this server opens (the Hotel, Lil Bro's race, the Beastmaster and each dungeon wait for their server's word), so no tile leads nowhere. The list is `src/data/townBoard.js`.
- **Every tile its own picture, from the game's own art (v2.3.3779)**: the owner, "use better icons for each one" (four dungeons wore one gate, two coins and a land's element looked alike). A dungeon wears its boss (the blue slime, the Amethyst Golem, the Storm Slime, the Gilded Mummy, cut small from the monsters' art in their colours); the Arena its gate (the arena's own art, the one with "ARENA" on it); No man's land the skull; Duels the crossed swords; each land its scene (the owner's pick of 2026-10-19, shown nowhere else since the land banners went back), drawn wide; Diego, Ace, Lil Bro's race and Pets their townsperson's head; each building its map icon (the one its Enter button wears). The new pictures are small palette pictures, 256 colours at most (`tools/ui/bake-board-icons.py`); no two tiles share one (town-board.test 1l). Every picture fills about the same height: the game's UI icons keep a wide clear margin, so they are drawn 1.45 times larger, and one with fewer pixels than it is drawn with goes pixelated, crisp rather than blurred (the orchestrator: the small icons looked "half-empty next to the lands").
- **A land's name is the world's own**, read from the world's plan, never spelt on the board, so a land renamed in the plan is renamed here too.
- **A page per tile**: what it is and what you do there, what you get, the level or cost (numbers from the code, never invented), and where it is in words ("In BroTown, north of the square"; "West of BroTown"). Then two big buttons, Red pin and Blue pin.
- **Two pins, red and blue, both up at once**: pressing one puts it on this place, moving it from wherever it was; pressing it again here lifts it ("Lift red pin"). A tile wears its pins in its corner and in its border's colour. They stay until lifted, per character, on the device (the orchestrator's recommendation; the owner can change it).
- **The pins on the maps**: on the minimap, the pin's point on the place, or (off the box) a smaller pin riding its edge on the line to the place, as your party does, so the minimap always says which way. On the world map, at every zoom, over the gold road, and at the screen's edge when off it.
- **Places are found where they stand**, never written down: a building's door, a townsperson, a dungeon's way in, a land's first stretch. A pin keeps the board's name for the place, so it can never point at a stale spot.
- **No man's land is a tile without pins** (the orchestrator's call): it is every land past the safe ground, not one place, so its page says what it is (fights between players, the skulls, what you lose) and "Where: everywhere outside BroTown's safe ground", with no pin buttons. Its level words stay general while its levels are being changed.
- **The Leaderboard and the World map stay**, as two small buttons at the board's foot: the trophy case and the painted map were the owner's earlier choice (v2.3.3258).
- On a computer the words say "click" and the board flows into more columns in the wide layout.
- **The layout pass (v2.3.3802)**: the owner ran the board past ChatGPT, and we took its LAYOUT advice (lane C did the words).
  - **A nav strip** sits under the header: one row of chips, one per heading, in the board's order. It stays at the top of the window while the board scrolls. A tap scrolls to that heading, and the heading in view wears the gold.
  - **A compact header**: the Town Hall's picture cropped to the sign and the map (100-124 px upright), so Quests and the first tiles show without scrolling. A sideways phone has no room picture, as every window.
  - **A wider window** on a computer and a sideways phone: up to 800 px, never wider than the play column, up to 85% tall on a computer.
  - **Columns by the board's own width**: 3, then 4 from 540 px, then 5 from 720 px. An upright phone keeps 3.
  - **Even tiles**: every tile in a section is one height, its line (a dungeon's levels, or a tile's subtitle) at its foot, 11 px and muted.
  - **One 52 px art box**: each picture is scaled by what it draws, so a townsperson's head and a land's scene look the same size, neither stretched.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Pins written as map coordinates, or a pin per townsperson's quest (the quest pins already do that).
- A third pin colour, or pins that clear themselves, without the owner.

**Still open:**
- A pin cannot yet be lifted from the map itself, only from the board.

Sources: src/data/townBoard.js, src/ui/panels/buildings/TownBoardPage.jsx, tools/ui/bake-board-icons.py, src/game/mapPins.js, src/game/mapPinPlaces.js, src/ui/panels/buildings/TownBoard.jsx, src/rendering/systems/wheelMinimap.js, src/ui/WorldMapOverlay.jsx, tools/qa/mp/mp-townboard.mjs

## Building doors and the Enter button
Screens: near-door

**What it is for:** In BroTown nobody walks into a building. You walk up to the foot of its steps, a small "Enter" button comes up, and a tap opens that building's window over the street.

**Decisions, and why:**
- Doors were added because the old town's portal was removed (v2.3.3025), which left the forge, bank, shops and auction house unreachable. Owner: "Push to main. Then after that add doors." (v2.3.3032). Each door opens the same window the old town's building did.
- The button comes up when your BOOTS are within 140 game px of the foot of the steps: the ground you stand on, not your waist. Doors on a street are 600+ px apart, so the nearest one wins. About one and a half steps from the porch is where it feels like being at the door (v2.3.3032).
- It reads "Enter" (small, capitals, dimmer) over the NAME ON THE BUILDING'S SIGN, beside the building's icon: "Enter SALOON", not the old town's label "TAVERN" (v2.3.3032).
- It is two short lines on purpose. It sits a little left of centre, in the free stretch between the notification bell and the jump control, and is never wider than that stretch. Since v2.3.3799, with Jump south-west of the disc in that strip, it stands just above Jump instead (8 px over its top), between the move stick and the disc with 8 px to each, 150 px at most (a longer name wraps; on a 320 px phone it is small and drops its picture); a prompt that has its own place (the Workshop's, the bed's) keeps it. A one-line pill with the signs' long names (GENERAL STORE, AUCTION HOUSE) ran under the JUMP button (v2.3.3032). The JUMP button was put away from v2.3.3105 to v2.3.3680 (a tap on the right stick jumped) and is back since v2.3.3681.
- The button goes away the moment you tap it and stays away while the window is open. Owner: "get rid of the vendor pop up button after you tap it because it's staying on even when you're in the vendor marketplace menus" (v2.3.2617). Walking away and back brings it back. It (and every other prompt over the band: the bed, the workshops, a dungeon's mouth, TRAP) also stands aside while any other window is up, such as your Inspect card, a player's card or a duel challenge, and is back when it closes (UI review, v2.3.3481: sideways "E ENTER BANK" lay on the Inspect card and took its taps).
- It opens when the finger LIFTS, not when it presses. Opening on the press let the same tap fall through and close the window it had just opened. The cost is a short delay on a phone, accepted because a door is not a combat control (v2.3.2617).
- A tap on the right stick also enters: the stick shows a DOOR picture at a door. Owner: "...chat bubble for speaking [to NPCs], door for entering door" (v2.3.3105). Next to a character, the stick talked to the character first (Diego and Ace stand beside their doors) until v2.3.4105: now the stick and E always enter, whoever stands beside the door, and a tap or click on the person talks (the owner: "Make talking to a npc player and entering a building always two different things").
- On a computer the button shows a small E key, and E opens the door. It ALWAYS shows at a door (v2.3.4105): from v2.3.3868 a townsperson in talk range swapped it for "E Talk to <name>". The keyboard-keys strip lies in the black margin beside the game (v2.3.3526); where it is drawn in the corner instead, it steps aside while the button is up, because it used to lie over the button and take the click (v2.3.3407).
- A building with nothing behind it shows a caption, not a button: its name and "Shut for now", so nobody takes the door for broken. It takes no touch (v2.3.3032). None is shut today: the Hotel, the last, opened in v2.3.3539, when the server could pay for its rest; against an older server it still says "Shut for now".
- The halls use the same button with their own picture: the Guild Hall, Post Office and Sheriff's Office (v2.3.3066), the Town Hall (v2.3.3258) and the Hotel, the owner's bed (v2.3.3539).
- KEEPERS: talking to a building's keeper opens his building the same way (a tap on him, E beside him, or a tap on the right stick; since v2.3.4105 only the tap or a click on him), after any quest he has. Blacksmith Bro opens the forge. Mayor Bro opened the Town Hall once he had no quest for you, until v2.3.3899: he says the next step now, and the board is on the square's noticeboard ("Read THE TOWN BOARD"). The owner picked this over walking inside: "Yes I like your idea of interacting with the building's NPC instead of having the building interiors" (v2.3.3258). Every door still opens too.
- Diego (his shop) and Ace (his coin flip) keep their own windows and stand beside their buildings. Standing at the General Store's door does NOT open Diego's shop: he stands 135 px away, and his shop opens within 90 px of HIM (v2.3.3032).
- At the Town Hall there is no button since v2.3.3899 (its board is on the noticeboard). Before that, it was not showing when you arrived. A new character starts 108 px south of the door, so the button comes up as you walk to Mayor Bro, whose dialogue is drawn above it (v2.3.3258).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- Until v2.3.3032 BroTown's buildings were pictures with nothing behind them. The four new buildings all said "Shut for now" until three halls opened (v2.3.3066); the Town Hall became a fourth (v2.3.3258), and the Hotel a fifth (v2.3.3539).
- A one-line "Enter <NAME>" pill ran under the jump button (v2.3.3032).
- Until v2.3.2617 the button stayed up after its tap, drawn on top of the window it had opened.
- The Saloon's door opened the gladiator arena (from v2.3.881). Since v2.3.3258 it opens the Saloon's own window, and the arena is reached from the Sheriff's Office.
- The Assay Office's door is gone with the building, so there are eleven building doors (v2.3.3259, the Gem Works).

**Please don't suggest:**
- Walking inside the buildings. The owner chose keepers and the room behind the menu instead (v2.3.3258).
- A wide one-line Enter pill (it runs under the right-hand controls), or a button that stays up while its window is open.
- Opening on touch-down instead of on release.
- Opening Diego's shop or Ace's flip from a door. They are the characters' own windows, opened by talking to them.
- A "Talk to <name>" button in the door's place, or E or the stick talking to someone at a door (v2.3.4105: entering and talking are two different things).
- A blurred or frosted background behind the button. The game's UI rule is no blur, because of iOS Safari (a Lantern Slate hard lock).

**Still open:**
- Only two keepers exist. The other eleven buildings wait for the owner's pictures of their keepers (two for the Gem Works: a cutter and a setter). Once a building has its keeper, its Enter button could go, if the owner wants it to.
- The townsfolk stand still in BroTown (in the old town they walked).
- The Enter button is meant to be small and to fit its stretch. The UI review lists many buttons under Apple's 44 px touch guide; they were left as they are for the owner, and none is unreachable.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/wheel-doors.md, docs/specs/wheel-halls.md, docs/specs/building-rooms.md, docs/specs/desktop-keys.md, docs/specs/reach-every-button.md, docs/specs/jumping.md, docs/TRAPS.md (§88), docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, docs/UI-BIBLE.md, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/styles/game.css, src/data/wheelBuildingDoors.js, src/game/wheelTownDoors.js, src/game/desktopControls.js, docs/ART-WISHLIST.md

## Building windows: the room behind the menu
Screens: building-*

**What it is for:** Every building in BroTown opens the same kind of window over the street. It is a card with the owner's painting of that building's room as its background and the building's menu written over it. Nobody walks inside.

**Decisions, and why:**
- No interiors to walk around in. Owner: "Yes I like your idea of interacting with the building's NPC instead of having the building interiors. Have it open up as backgrounds behind the different buildings menus instead of literally going into a building" (v2.3.3258). The room pictures are the owner's own ("Ok wire these up").
