- **The portrait is a door** (the magnifier) to the **Inspect card**, built to the owner's second mockup ("This is the direction I'm going"): name with the Hemi Bro checkmark, LV, achievement points, the Hemi Bro count (owner: "It would be cool to list the number of hemi bros owned somewhere on their profile", v2.3.3255), quests, title, clan or alliance tag, the three combat levels only ("not the full Points menu"), the six equipment pictures with no words, kills, the Homestead, and a dock (Invite to Party · Trade · Duel) that stays on screen while the rest scrolls under it. "Magic", not "Staff": asked, the owner chose "You can do magic." Your own Inspect card (the review's `inspect-self` picture, the one way to show it without a second player) has no dock: there is nobody to invite.
- **Marks on a name:** the Hemi Bro checkmark means the wallet owns one or more Hemi Bros (or a verified worn Bro), and everyone sees everyone's (v2.3.3255; no contract and no transaction, as the owner asked for a "small surface area"). An alliance (20+ active members) or a former clan shows `[TAG] Name` on every member's name plate and on the card (v2.3.3313, v2.3.3321).
- **Trade is a drawer joined to the top of your bag, not a centred window.** Owner: "change the player to player trade menu to be like the shopkeeper trade menu where it just attaches to the player bag" (v2.3.2149), then "Yes bring the trade into the drawer too" for all six of its states (v2.3.2280). It has no dark backdrop on purpose: that would cover the bag, and the bag is where you pick items. A tap on a bag tile offers one; each row has − / + (owner: "it only allowed me to put up one of the fire goblin remains ... neither of us understood it well", v2.3.1752). So the bag must be on screen: as a trade opens, the band leaves whatever page it was on (Bro, Quests...) for the bag (owner: "Previously I had it to where you could just tap your inventory items to put up items. Now it only has predetermined pull buttons", v2.3.4877).
- **Whose pile is whose:** on a computer the two piles stand side by side, yours on the LEFT and theirs on the right, on the live drawer and the Confirm screen (owner: "I'd prefer a side by side layout of your trade window on left and other person trade window on right. Top to bottom (current) is more confusing", v2.3.3856). Where the drawer is under 520 px (an upright phone's, about 378) a lane would be 180 px and the gold buttons no longer fit, so there they stack and yours is the bottom pile, as on the receipt (owner: "This way it's consistent across all 3 trade windows", v2.3.2282). `?tradeside=1` / `?tradeside=0` force either for one tab. Their items sit on light grey, yours on light brown, the owner's own colours (v2.3.2294), and each pile carries that trader's face (v2.3.2292). Item pictures and a gold icon beside the amounts (v2.3.1755).
- **Gold:** the owner's ladder +1 +5 +25 +50 +100 +500 +1000, which ADD (v2.3.2286); a − button flips them to subtract (v2.3.2288/2292); a field takes an exact number. A chip you cannot afford is greyed, never quietly smaller.
- **Ready, then a Confirm screen:** once both are ready, a stripped-down "you give / you receive" summary that both must accept again. Owner: "This second screen is arguably the single best anti-scam feature ever added to a trade system" (v2.3.1754). Nothing on it can change the trade. Items are counted, not given a value: the game has no price table, and an invented number on an anti-scam screen is worse than none. 5,000+ gold needs Accept tapped twice (owner: "large currency amounts trigger an explicit confirmation to catch typos"). The totals are bold, asked for twice (v2.3.2296, v2.3.2299).
- **The receipt** shows both piles, folds away what you sent, then flies what you got into your bag (the owner's idea, v2.3.2297). No trade can be confirmed while either of you is fighting a player (v2.3.3183).
- **A trade request** arrives as the drawer "<name> wants to trade" with Open trade / Decline. It has no ✕: Decline is the answer, and it tells the other player (v2.3.2280). Its title leads with the owner's handshake (their icon pick 3, v2.3.3481), as the duel challenge card leads with its swords, so both request cards say what they are before you read them; the trade's other drawers have no picture. Sideways the dashboard's ▴ steps aside while it is up, as it lay on the left end of Open trade (UI review, v2.3.3481); in a live trade the ▴ stays, as it opens the bag. An older one-way "gift" card is kept only so an older server still works; today's game never sends that kind of request.
- **Duel challenge:** a small centred card. "Duels are consensual. No wager, no item loss, no reputation penalty." (the server made those promises true, v2.3.1121); with a stake it reads "Duels are consensual. No reputation penalty. The loser pays the wager. No item loss." (UI review, v2.3.3481: it used to speak of a wager on every challenge, "Loser pays wager (if any)"). Tapping the dark backdrop does NOTHING (v2.3.2289): it is nearly the whole phone, a mis-tap must not throw away a duel and its wager, and the challenger always hears Accept or Decline. A challenge sent from a player's card has no stake, so the card's wager line ("winner takes all") shows only when there is one, which today is never.
- **Party invite:** a small card at the top centre, above the dashboard so Join can always be tapped (v2.3.1966). It goes away when the invite runs out. Leaving a party takes two taps, since parties now last until you leave (v2.3.3284). In a party, the roster is a small chip that a tap opens (v2.3.3768: "Your party on the screen", below).
- **No man's land: attacking is a choice** (owner: "I want attacking other players to be intentional", v2.3.3253). A tap on a player you could fight, or one of your shots or swings passing through them, raises a SMALL window: "Attack <name>?", their level, the red skull with a tiny (i), and Attack / Party / Ignore (the owner's words). **A tap's window has Trade too** (Attack / Party / Trade / Ignore in the same small window, each button 57 px wide; v2.3.4988, the owner: "Also add trading to the character menu when you tap another player"): No man's land begins just outside town, so out there a tap on a player opens this window and never the player card, and without it a trade could only be started on the town's safe ground. Trade does exactly what the card's Trade does (the same request; no aim, no skull). It is only on the window a TAP opens: a stray shot's or a swing's window asks about a fight, and "Hit back" is about someone attacking you. Until you choose Attack, your blows never hurt them. It sits at the TOP, under or beside the minimap, with no dark backdrop (it can come up mid-fight). Attack then lasts: they are your foe until a minute passes with no blow either way (v2.3.3268). Hitting back the player who attacked you goes through the same window (owner: "keep the behavior of needing to confirm the "attack" button that pops up in the window first before any damage is registered to either player", v2.3.3528): your swing, shot, bow line or tap at them asks "Hit back <name>?" with the WHITE skull you carry instead of the red one (hitting back gives no red skull), and after Attack your blows land on them with or without a lock. Their attack asks again even if you once pressed Ignore on them, once per bout of fighting. A group that attacks you needs ONE window (owner: "If the 2nd, 3rd, etc people already approved "attack" at you you can immediately hit back at each of them after that", v2.3.3530): once you have pressed Attack on any window, everyone who is attacking you joins that fight with no window of their own, and hitting back any of them gives no red skull. And you are white the moment someone chooses to attack you, before any blow, whether you fight back or not (owner: "Whenever someone attacks you in no man's land you always get a white skull above your head ... Doesn't matter if you attack back or not", v2.3.3530): the window's Attack is the attack, starting both skulls at once, and the badge's explainer says so.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The trade window's ornate painted kit (v2.3.2290) was replaced by a flat one (v2.3.2291). The owner said the first read as "too many boxes within boxes" and "deviates a lot from the theme I'm using for the dashboard".
- A bag inside the trade window, and every small explainer line ("remove all the tiny info disclaimers and explainers", v2.3.2293), are gone.
- The card's TP button is gone (owner: "remove it", v2.3.1744); so is Threat, the non-consensual fight (owner: "remove the option to kill other players for now", v2.3.1917). Trade used to be the one gold button; the mockup gives all four tiles one weight.
- The Attack window first sat just above the No man's land badge: there, Attack lay where a left thumb steers and Ignore over the attack button.
- Held sideways, the Attack window was centred in the strip left of the minimap, which put it right over you, its foot a few px above your head, and 2 px INTO the space over you once the top bar grew (v2.3.3765). Since v2.3.3830 it slides left, off the middle where you stand, when it would come down that low; it cannot go higher (the top bar) or shorter (its buttons are already under 44 px) (docs/specs/layout-nits.md).
- Hitting back was one tap with no window (v2.3.3253 to v2.3.3528), and a swing or shot at your attacker did nothing at all; the owner found they "can't attack back unless they lock on target" and asked for the window's Attack before any damage from either player.

**Please don't suggest:**
- A dark backdrop or tap-outside-to-close for the trade drawer, or moving trade back to a centred modal.
- Letting the duel card's backdrop dismiss or decline it.
- Putting your own pile on top, or giving both trade piles one colour.
- Frames around each trade row or more nested boxes (the owner's "boxes within boxes"); one well per pile is the agreed limit.
- A total "value" for the items on the Confirm screen, or bringing the explainer lines back.
- Removing the player card's corner brackets and ◆ dividers (the owner drew them; a documented exception), or spreading them to other panels.
- HP, mana, crit or fold-away sections on the Inspect card ("Do not turn this into a giant character-stat screen", "Do Not Add an Accordion Yet").
- A one-tap Report, a teleport-to-player button, or a Threat button.
- Telling a player their friend request was declined: declining is kept private on purpose (v2.3.1323).
- Moving the Attack window down near the thumbs, giving it a backdrop, or letting a stray shot hurt a player.

**Still open:**
- Nothing in the game opens your own Inspect card yet: where its button goes is the owner's call (v2.3.2926).
- The Inspect card's Homestead picture is a marked placeholder the owner allowed ("Demo placeholders are acceptable") until that system exists. Its title ("Fishing Master") and rarest drop ("Ancient Ember Crown") were placeholders too until v2.3.3527. Now the title is an achievement the player chose and the rarest is their rarest achievement, both the worker's (docs/specs/achievements.md "The card"). With none, the card shows no title and "Rarest achievement: None yet".
- The owner's painted frame for the trade drawer is in the repo but was backed out; it needs a layout change first (v2.3.2290).

Sources: docs/specs/trading.md, docs/specs/duels.md, docs/specs/owner-icons.md, docs/specs/friends.md, docs/specs/party.md, docs/specs/no-mans-land.md, docs/specs/hemi-bro-count.md, docs/specs/alliance.md, docs/specs/wheel-halls.md, docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, docs/TRAPS.md, CLAUDE.md, src/ui/panels/TradeWindowPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/InspectPlayerPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/PlayerProfilePanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/playerProfile.js, src/ui/panels/PlayerListPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/DuelRequestPanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/IncomingTradePanel.jsx, src/ui/panels/PartyHUD.jsx, src/ui/mobile/NmlAskCard.jsx, src/game/nmlAsk.js, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/styles/game.css

## Your party on the screen: the chip and its list
Screens: party-hud, party-hud-open

**What it is for:** Knowing your party is there and well without it taking the screen, and seeing who is in it, or acting on them, when you want to.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Folded to a chip by default** (owner: "Also is partying unobtrusive right now? Just wanting to make sure the screen isn't cluttered with party stuff", v2.3.3768). The chip sits where the list was (top left, under the status readout and the quest card, clear of the thumb controls): the party mark, "3/4", and a small pip for each friend coloured as their health bar is (green over half, amber, red under a quarter; red-ringed when down; grey when offline). An alliance's or a former clan's tag shows on it too. It is 32 px tall where the open list of four was about 150 px, 40% of a sideways phone's height.
- **A tap opens the list, a tap on its head (▴) folds it,** and the phone remembers which you chose. The list is the one it always was: names, levels, health bars, where a friend is when not with you, the leader's crown, ✖ to leave.
- **It opens by itself when something needs you,** for four seconds, then folds again (unless you had opened it yourself): a friend joins or leaves, goes down, or drops under a quarter of their health. Only on the change, so a friend who stays low opens it once; never for your own health (your own bar says it), and never on the roster you joined with.
- **The leader's kick is two taps,** as leaving is (v2.3.3284): the first turns ✕ into "Kick?", the second kicks. It is only in the opened list, so a stray tap on the chip never kicks a friend.
- Unchanged: the invite card (a decision, so it stays a card above the dashboard), your party's blue marks on the minimap and the world map, the party badge on name plates, and the Party chat lane.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The list stood open the whole time you were in a party and could not be put away (v2.3.1185 to v2.3.3768).
- The kick was one tap on a 24 px ✕ beside each friend's name.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Opening the list by default again, or keeping it open after something needed you.
- Putting the kick or the leave on the chip.
- Moving the chip near the thumbs, or into the top bar beside the gold.

**Still open:**
- Whether a member walking far off (another land) should open the list too: it does not today.

Sources: docs/specs/party.md, CLAUDE.md, src/ui/panels/PartyHUD.jsx, src/game/partyChip.js, src/game/partyRows.js, tools/qa/mp/mp-partychip.mjs, server/test/party-chip.test.mjs

## Harvesting (woodcutting, mining, fishing)
Screens: harvest-near, harvest-windup, harvest-ready

**What it is for:** Gathering wood, ore and fish (cooking at a fire works the same way): walk up to a resource, start it, watch your hits wear its bar down, then finish with a short hand motion on the right button.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The right button is the harvest button.** The owner's directive (2026-09-03): "No resource extraction button in the middle of the screen ... Resource extraction will be detected by perimeter and contextual button will be tapped to begin harvest. The gesture will be performed on that right button ... The gesture cues will be on the right button." At a resource in reach, the button shows the skill's tool picture and no word (the owner's mockup puts pictures, not words, on every control, v2.3.3018). A tap on the resource itself also starts it (owner: "allow tapping on the resource ... (assuming you're close enough)", v2.3.2270), and so does E on a computer.
- **A monster nearby takes the button back** for attacking. Owner: "prioritize attacking nearby monsters instead of the contextual resource extraction (if it comes up)" (v2.3.2270). Once you are harvesting, monsters leave you alone.
- **Quiet labels.** Owner: "I just don't want the screen to be too busy with text" (v2.3.3059). Every resource shows only its tool on a small dark disc, the same 20 px on screen at any zoom. The tool is GREY when your level is too low (the owner: "a good cue that it's harvestable but you're not high enough level yet"). Only the nearest resource, within about 260 px, adds its name and the level it needs (red if you are short). A fishing spot shows no rod at all, only that name plate when it is the nearest (owner: "Remove the fishing icon above fish but leave the proximity based nameplate in place", v2.3.3146): the fish swimming in the water already say what it is.
- **A resource you have no tool for is not drawn at all.** It is not on the minimap and does not block your walk (v2.3.3145; owner: "there are invisible areas that block movement near the town").
- **Trying one your level is too low for:** you sit down and swing three times. Each swing pops a "0", with "Requires Fishing Lv 5" (or Mining, or Woodcutting) in red, and then it ends by itself (owner: "show zeroes popping as they try to harvest the resource with the message that it requires whatever level", v2.3.3059).
- **The hits:** each swing knocks a number off the resource, landing on the blow you see. The bar is GREEN, sits OVER the resource (a tree's crown, the school of fish), and reads "7/10". Owner: "I want resource harvesting bar to be green and to appear above the resource, not the player head. It should also list the numbers on the bar" (v2.3.3035). It is the size of your own HP bar, and your name plate and HP bar are put away while you harvest. Owner: "Ticks for the resource extraction is too hard to see. You can make it as large as the normal hp bar and just hide the player name plate and health bar during extraction" (v2.3.3027).
- **Two bars hang UNDER, on purpose.** The rock's: the miner stands behind his rock, and a bar above would cover him (owner: "For mining you can put the bar beneath the ore"). The campfire's: the cook leans over the fire, so above it is his head. A huge tree's bar and label stay no more than 300 px above its foot, so the pinned quest card never hides them. Fishing is quiet until the end: each hit is a nibble, a number and the bar, with no splash and no sound (the owner: "reeling is the ONLY splash moment", v2.3.1445).
- **Hits grow with your level.** The owner's hit ladder (v2.3.3229): "after the first level you get a 10% chance of hitting 1 number higher". Each resource tier takes about twice as long as the one before, at the level it opens (owner's ask, v2.3.3251). Resources also grow in size by tier, small copper to a huge obsidian boulder, maple and pike (owner: "huge fish, ore, and trees at the end", v2.3.3345).
- **At 0 the character STOPS and waits for you,** frozen, with no time limit. Owner: "Once it reaches the limit, the character is supposed to stop animating until you perform the correct gesture". The empty bar pulses gold to call for it. The button teaches the motion: a mini picture of the tool sits at the start of its track and flashes, chevrons point the way, a streak of light runs the motion, and one word says what to do (WAIT during the hits, then PUMP, CHOP, REEL or FLIP). The character's frames follow your thumb, at your speed (v2.3.2760).
- **The gesture takes about 1.5 seconds** at a quick pace. Owner: "reduce resource extraction time during gesture by 75%" (v2.3.3036). On a phone it is made on the button only; a finger on the world does nothing.
- **On a computer** (v2.3.3407; owner: "I don't think you can harvest lifeskills right now on the computer"): the right button comes back for a harvest, with a line over it such as "Drag ↔ or hold E". The mouse can make the motion anywhere on the world, or E held does it for you. A held key draws no path, so it is never graded "perfect"; the mouse is graded like a thumb.
- **The bro looks like himself while gathering:** his own skin, trousers, boots, shirt print, tattoos and size (owner: "It needs to stay consistent", v2.3.3146; tattoos v2.3.2854). Other players gather in their own colours (v2.3.3314).
- **Each wood is its own tree, and each metal its own rock,** the same picture in all eight lands (owner: "the log art ... would need to be the same across all 8 lands"). The land shows only in small effects drawn in code: snowflakes, embers, petals and so on (v2.3.3345, v2.3.3402). A tree whose crown covers you turns see-through. A mined-out rock plays a crack and breaks into pieces of its own picture (owner: "Add cracking sound when the ore splits", v2.3.3040; own pieces v2.3.3345).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- A mid-screen extraction button with the gesture made in the middle of the screen. Removed by the owner's directive (v2.3.2245).
- A progress bar over your head (v2.3.2514), removed (owner: "you can remove the status bar above the player head now since the HP-like bar will replace that", v2.3.2956). Then the resource's bar drawn over your head (v2.3.3027), then over the resource, green and numbered (v2.3.3035).
- Labels went from a tool emoji, a tier dot and tiny tips, to a full pill with tool, name and level over every resource (v2.3.3040), to today's quiet discs (v2.3.3059).
- The gesture's length: about 3 s (v2.3.2760, the owner's "require about 3 seconds"), doubled (owner: "Double the amount of time", v2.3.2761), then cut to a quarter (v2.3.3036).
- At "ready" the body used to loop a demo of the motion by itself (v2.3.2384). That went against the owner's freeze, and the teaching moved onto the button (v2.3.2760).
- "Need Mining Lv 5" and a beep (v2.3.3038) became the visible try with zeros (v2.3.3059). "Too far away!" and "You need a tool for that" are gone: such a tap is now a tap on the ground (v2.3.3145).
- "Too far away" came back in v2.3.3990, at the owner's ask ("There needs to be a message that you're too far away if you can't mine and are tapping on the ore. Too far away message should also be with any resource."): a word floating over the resource (any resource, and a farm bed), once a second each, "Too far away: walk closer" on a computer. Unlike the old one it takes nothing: the tap still goes on to whatever it did before, which is why v2.3.3145 removed the old one.

**Please don't suggest:**
- A harvest button in the middle of the screen, or a phone gesture anywhere but on the right button.
- Keeping the character moving while it waits for the gesture (TRAPS §107: the freeze is the owner's design), or a time limit on it.
- A progress bar over the player's head, showing your own name plate and HP bar during a harvest, or moving the rock's or campfire's bar above them.
- Names and levels on every resource, a rod icon over fishing spots, or a splash or sound on every nibble.
- Making the gesture longer again, or a "Too far away" that takes the tap (v2.3.3145; the word since v2.3.3990 only floats).

**Still open:**
- The lumberjack, cook and fire-lighter are separate paintings, so their drawing style differs; eye colour is not on the mining, fishing or those three figures; a cape is left off the three on purpose (it would hang into the ground on a crouch).
- Only five woods grow so far (none past maple).

Sources: docs/specs/gathering-hits.md, docs/specs/gesture-cue.md, docs/specs/desktop-harvest.md, docs/specs/wheel-resources.md, docs/specs/gathering-look.md, docs/specs/hit-ladder.md, docs/specs/harvest-ink.md, docs/specs/tier-trees.md, docs/specs/tier-ore.md, docs/specs/control-redesign.md, docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md, docs/TRAPS.md, CLAUDE.md, src/ui/BroTown.jsx, src/ui/panels/TouchControls.jsx

## The controller (the touch controls' look, `?padskin=0` the old)
Screens: pad-controller

**What it is for:** The owner, 2026-11-25, first with a mockup and four sheets ("Also want a redesign of the controls area to be more like a controller": part 1, v2.3.6640), then, after trying it on their iPhone, a redesign: *"The controller is too large and doesn't connect in a seamless way to the dashboard. The shape also doesn't make much sense. Here's a redesign showing how it should attach to the dashboard. Make the jump button turn into the shield button while in combat. Tapping attack button or shield button again lowers it"* (part 3, v2.3.6641; `docs/plan/cards/PAD-part3.md`). The touch controls read as ONE object that belongs to the dashboard.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **No Sprint button: the left stick pushed past its edge sprints** (v2.3.6644, card PAD part 9). The owner, shown the controller with Sprint floating over its right side (their drawing has no socket for it), asked "Where do you think sprint should go"; told "no button; push the stick to its edge", as the two-button trial already played: "Yeah do that and remove sprint button". On a touch screen, in the usual controls and the trial alike: past 1.25 of the stick's drawn radius it sprints, back inside 1.05 or lifted it stops, at the edge itself a full-speed walk that spends nothing (docs/specs/sprint.md). The button's stamina rim went with it and no meter replaced it: the energy shows as the five blocks under the bro while it is spent. The tour says "Push it past its edge to sprint." A computer keeps the button and Shift; `?rimsprint=0` gives the button back.
- **A full push sprints, reachable from anywhere** (v2.3.6647, card PAD part 12, tag 6647). The owner, 2026-11-27, upright on an iPhone: "the sprint action isn't working correctly because you can't move a big enough distance off screen (in the southwest corner where the joystick is) when moving west or area too close to the edge of the screen. Maybe just make sprint the default behavior when moving until you run out of energy unless you have better idea". The 1.25 line lay past the glass for a thumb landing on the disc going west or south. Now the thumb at the stick's reach (1.0) sprints and below 0.8 of it walks; a part push spends nothing, so a stick held over sprints until the energy runs out and easing off saves it. The reach in the thumb's direction is the drawn radius or the room to the play area's edge less 16 px (the band's top going south), the smaller, never under 18 px; the landing point never moves (a still thumb moves nothing); the walk is full and the knob at its stop at a full push. Out of energy the bro walks on with no flicker; "Not enough energy!" once each time the bar empties. The trial takes the same rule; `?fullpush=0` gives yesterday's 1.25 back whole (docs/specs/sprint.md). Theirs to change: the 1.0 and 0.8, the 16 px margin, the 18 px floor, when the words show.
- **Attack is a stick with a bow or a staff; the sword keeps its latch** (v2.3.6648, card PAD part 13, tag 6648). The owner, 2026-11-27, on an iPhone in the two buttons: *"for melee to stay as it is (attacking and locking towards nearest enemy) but [BOW] and magic need to use the right button that turns into the right joystick again where attacks must be used in that directional rotation with no lock on mechanism. Right now I can run through the [forest] and just hold down the attack button and I'm destroying every monster. It needs to be more skill based than an auto lock on system for ranged attacks"*. So with a bow or a staff in hand the Attack disc is mode 4's held stick on the disc itself: the thumb goes down on it and drags, the knob (the sword picture inside the well) follows the thumb, and past 10 px (the tap's own dead zone) the shot goes the thumb's way while it is held, at the weapon's own pace (the bow still looses only when its line is on something; the staff's bolt still follows the thumb). A tap (no drag past 10 px) is one shot the way the bro faces, on the lift. Nothing picks, locks or turns a shot toward a monster: no yellow or red frame round a monster, no sight line, no lit or ringing disc, and the bow's Special flies along the aim. The plainest honest sign that it is a stick is the knob itself, moving with the thumb inside the steel well (no new picture: the steel skin's rules); the disc wears `data-disc="stick"` for QA. A sword's Attack is today's latch, whole. A weapon swapped with the thumb down ends the hold. The right half of the screen stays the world's taps. `?rangedstick=0` gives the latch back to the bow and staff (game/rangedStick.js; pictures `docs/shots/rangedstick-*.png`).
- **The combo goes at the last target** (v2.3.6650, card PAD part 14). The owner, about 07:05 UTC 2026-11-28, after playing on an iPhone: *"Also for combos it's hard to have to tap the button and aim at the same time. Just make the combo auto-attack the last target. If the line of site is lined up just make the combo be first attack"*. A combo in this scheme is the owner's own word for the specials thrown into a fight (2026-11-23: *"combos of special attacks, jumps, and blocking instead of managing the direction"*; the x1/x2/x3 chain is long gone). With the bow or the staff the Attack disc is a stick and nothing locks, so Special had nothing to fly at: the bow's waited, readied, for a shot's line to land, and the same right thumb had to press Special and then aim. Now Special pressed with the thumb OFF the disc goes at once at the last target: the monster your last landed blow hit (the worker's own echo), if that was less than 8 s ago and it is alive and in the weapon's range. With no last target but the bro's facing lined up on a monster (the same sight pick the sword's latch uses), the special is the first attack, at it. A thumb aiming the disc still wins; with nothing to take Special does what it did. Nothing locks (the stick keeps none). The sword is untouched: its latch already turns its special to the lock. `?comboaim=0` gives the old Special back, and the phone remembers it until `?comboaim=1` (game/comboAim.js; `mp-comboaim`). What a player feels and the owner may change: the 8 s.
- **One shell** (v2.3.6641): the owner's `housing.png` is the only backing: the stick's lobe at the left, a low rail in the middle, the taller lobe at the right, its gold outline unbroken, as wide as the play column, its bottom edge ON the band's top edge with the band open or folded. Drawn as three cuts of the one picture (CSS border-image: the lobes at their shape, the rail stretched), so any width keeps the outline whole. Its empty space takes no touch: the world's taps get through.
- **As wide as the WORLD, not the window** (v2.3.6641, part 4): sideways under the one band the world keeps a capped width between two bars (844 x 390 folded: 752 between two 46 px bars), so the shell is laid out at the canvas's width as the game's resize gave it (`padSkin.js padWidthOf`, told, never measured), and each control redraws when that width moves (a fold, a turn: `padSpot.js usePadWidth`). Laid out at the window's 844 it ran past the world's edge and the right lobe's buttons sat 92 px left of their wells. A top-row touch box (44 px) that rises off the big buttons' boxes keeps its ART in its well: the face is drawn that much lower in its box (`padSpot` dy). Never on a computer (a mouse the main pointer): there the weapon pill and the skateboard's button keep today's places.
- **The owner's sizes** (layout.json, a 920 x 280 reference, scaled to the column and capped at the owner's 430 px phone, never larger): the shell 131 px tall, Attack and the big left button 65, the small wells 37, the weapon wells 40, the stick's base 83. A wider column (sideways) stretches the rail; the lobes and their wells keep to their ends.
- **Touch boxes are not the art**: each at least 44 x 44 px, holding its art, none over another. Under 430 px the art shrinks with the column and the boxes do not, so where two would touch their wells are spread to 45 px between centres and a top-row box rises off the big buttons'. Under about 390 px the lobe cannot hold three 44 px boxes in its top row with the owner's art at its size: that row's art shrinks (to 0.8 at the least) and its boxes stay 44 (what gives on a narrow phone).
- **The wells**: the stick (base always drawn, the thumb at its middle at rest, moved by today's stick code); the rail's three weapon wells in today's order, the same taps and rules as the pill they replace; the right lobe's top row Mobility (Skate or Charge by the game's own rules, never both), the middle well, Special; its bottom row the big left button and Attack (the face of the weapon in hand: sword, bow, staff).
- **Jump becomes the shield in a fight** (the owner's words): the big left button is Block, wearing the shield, exactly when the Block button shows today (ShieldButton blockLive, one rule); Jump moves to the middle well, small, so nothing is lost (where Jump goes was chosen for the owner by the orchestrator). Out of a fight the middle well is empty.
- **The shield is a tap**: it already was (v2.3.2242, game/shieldToggle.js: a tap raises it, a tap lowers it, the guard door its one writer); Attack lowers it (playerActions.js), as do a break, the stamina, death, riding and the rest, unchanged. A guard still up when the fight's rule ends keeps Block shown (shieldButtonLive), so it can always be lowered; then the big button is Jump again.
- **States in CSS, no filter**: pressed (the face 0.96 for 100 ms), cooldown (a dark sector over the face inside its ring, never the rim), unavailable (the face dimmed; the sword's own dim face), active (a quiet ring, opacity only), a control hidden right now its EMPTY WELL (action_socket). No number on a cooldown (the game has none).
- **Whirlwind and Shield Bash in a tier over the right lobe** (v2.3.6642, part 6; the gatekeeper: "Whirlwind and Shield Bash in their old places beside the new Special socket"): the housing as drawn has no free place for a 44 px box (the rail is the three weapon wells end to end, the lobe's top row is full, the stick fills its lobe), so while either shows the right lobe is drawn about 42 px taller (the same `housing.png`, the steep band of its left wall stretched: no new picture) and the two sit in that row: Whirlwind over Special, Shield Bash over the mobility well (over the shield, the big well, whenever Bash shows: the guard is up). Each the top row's size, its box 44, its face the old button's own (the tornado, the bash). Who shows is the old buttons' one rule (abilityStatus: Whirlwind the sword's in a fight, Bash the raised shield's with any weapon); the tier's height and both places are fixed, so nothing moves as one comes or goes, and when neither shows the tier is not drawn (`padSkin.js specialLayout`).
- **Steel, not gold** (v2.3.6645, card PAD part 10). The owner, 2026-11-26, with one new sheet of the whole controller in a steel-blue rim: *"Also swap out the control color with this one. I don't like the gold."* Every picture in `public/ui/pad/` is now cut off that sheet (assets/icons-source/owner-2026-11-26-pad4/, its `layout.json` each piece's box), same names, same sizes, the layout and every button's rule untouched. The lit weapon well keeps a GOLD ring: it is on the owner's own sheet (a glow for "this one is in hand"), while every rim is steel. Not on the sheet and made from it: the weapon wells' icons (the weapon lifted off its Attack face) and the dimmed Attack (the sword's face greyed as pad3's was). The steel housing measures a little roomier than the gold (its right shoulder 10 of the reference further left), so the right lobe's cut moved 400 -> 420 and the tier's stretched rows 70 -> 75 (padSkin.js); the top row's art now stays whole down to 345 px. No switch keeps the gold (the owner's own words); `?padskin=0` is the controls before the redesign.
- **The gold beside it, steel too** (tag 6646, card PAD part 11). Asked which gold was left round the steel controller (the rings on Whirlwind and Shield Bash, the ready glow on Attack and Special, the five energy blocks under the bro), the owner, 2026-11-26: *"Turn steel"*. Each takes the steel sheet's own colours, measured on its Attack face (padSkin.js `PAD_STEEL`): the ready glow is the rim's cold light (DEEBF6), brighter against the steel than the gold was (contrast on the rim's side 4.58, the gold 3.12 and 4.02), not-ready still without it and the dimmed face still the second cue; the tier's two rings are a steel ring drawn in CSS from the rim's light to its dark (not the sheet's socket picture, so their cooldown and disabled greys read as before); the energy strip is a twin of the owner's gold strip with only its gold moved onto the steel (tools/art/steel-strip.mjs; full against empty 9.37, the gold's 7.58). Kept gold, on the owner's own sheet: the lit slot's ring, the stick's arrows, Special's star, the board and the charge streaks. `?padgold=1` gives the gold back for one phone.
- **The art law**: the owner's pieces kept byte for byte (assets/icons-source/owner-2026-11-25-pad3/, then the steel sheet of 2026-11-26 in owner-2026-11-26-pad4/); tools/art/cut-pad.mjs cuts each off the sheet and takes it to 2 px a CSS px at its biggest, 128 colours, 135 kB in all, warmed on the loading screen. Part 1's sheets and pieces are gone.

- **No message over it** (v2.3.6791, card UI3 PR 11; the owner: *"Needs to fix tutorial messages over new controller area"*): the coach's cards, the big arrow's words, the "Next: ..." toasts, the Add to Home Screen card and the controls tour's card keep clear of the housings' boxes by one rule (`src/game/tutorClear.js`; the outline's tops measured off `housing.png`: the stick's lobe 27.8, the rail 146.4, the right lobe 0 of the 280 reference, the tier over the right lobe). A change to the shell's art or its cuts re-measures those three numbers.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The gold-ringed drawn buttons of v2.3.3018, each placed on its own: `?padskin=0`.
- Part 1's two grips (v2.3.6640), a left one for the stick and a right one for five buttons, the middle open: the owner found them too large, not joined to the dashboard, the shape wrong.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Changing the dashboard's height for the shell (the owner said no).
- Scaling the controller up on bigger screens (the owner's "too large").
- Baking states into more pictures where the style sheet can draw them.

**Still open:**
- Buttons with no well (Quick eat, the trap, Element Burst, the TALK/CHOP button) keep their old places and looks; the owner's call where they go. Sprint has none on a touch screen since v2.3.6644 (below); with `?rimsprint=0` it is back over the tier, as part 6 placed it.
- The tier (part 6) is the orchestrator's choice for the owner: the other ways were the two floating over the shell in their old places, or new art. The owner may prefer the tier always drawn for a sword (empty wells out of a fight) to a lobe that grows in a fight.
- A phone narrower than about 390 px draws the top row's art a little smaller (above).
- The mockup's tab row with icons, the top bar and the minimap (out of this card).

## The two-button controls (a trial, `?manual=5`)
Screens: two-buttons, two-buttons-sighted, two-buttons-hold, two-buttons-reach, two-buttons-ctx-tree, two-buttons-ctx-talk

**What it is for:** A second way to fight on a phone, for the owner to try beside today's (the held attack on the right stick). The owner, 2026-11-23: "change the combat mechanics of the right joystick so that there's just an attack and jump button", "when monsters in your line of site (and in range) the combat buttons appear around the attack button", "By combat buttons I mean blocking and special attacks." The idea, in their words: "the majority of combat will consist of delivering combos of special attacks, jumps, and blocking instead of managing the direction or smashing the attack button." It is a trial: nothing of today's controls is removed until the owner has played both (card CT, `docs/plan/cards/CT.md`).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Chosen by `?manual=5`, remembered on the device**; nothing chosen is today's combat (mode 4). Touch only: a computer's keys and mouse are unchanged.
- **The left stick has three zones** (v2.3.6541): a small dead zone, then up to 30% of the disc's drawn radius the bro TURNS and does not walk, past it he WALKS; the disc draws the border as a ring. `?turnzone=<percent>` moves the border for the trial.
- **Attack is a latch, drawn bevelled** (v2.3.6542): raised at rest, pressed in and lit while combat is on. A press with a monster in sight locks it and the weapon attacks at its own rate with no thumb down; a second press, the monster out of range (the sword's 440 held out to 550), its death with no other in sight, your death or a change of place lets go. If another is in sight as yours dies, the lock moves to it. (Changed by tap and hold, below; `?holdauto=0` keeps this.)
- **A tap is one attack; a hold turns auto-attack on** (v2.3.6548, card CT part 15; the owner, 2026-11-24: "I'm also thinking if you hold down the attack button near a monster something signifies that the auto-attack is enabled. Maybe 2 seconds. Otherwise each tap is its own attack cycle. What do you think?", and to the reading below, "Yes do it the way you suggested"): every press of Attack is one attack the moment the finger lands. With a monster in sight the tap locks it (your bro faces it, the line shows) and the lock stays, so the next taps strike the same monster, until it dies, leaves the sight's range or you die; a tap never lets a lock go, and the button stays raised. Hold Attack on a locked monster and a gold ring fills round the button; when it is full (0.6 s) auto-attack is on: the button is pressed in and lit and the weapon fights at its own rate with the finger lifted. Lifting before the ring is full empties it at once: that was a tap. Sliding the finger off the button does the same. A tap while auto-attack is on turns it off (and is not an attack of its own). When the monster dies everything lets go: no lock, no auto-attack, the button raised; the lock does NOT move to the next monster (the owner, earlier: "I want to change the melee behavior so that it doesn't jump to every monster"); tap or hold again. A hold with nothing locked fills no ring: it is one attack. Why 0.6 s and not the owner's 2 s: a fight is short, and two seconds of a thumb held still near a monster is a long time to be hit; 0.6 s is long enough not to happen by a tap and short enough to choose in a fight. `?autohold=2000` tries their 2 seconds (any whole ms from 200 to 3000). The ring is drawn by the page's style sheet from one mark set at the press (no frame redraws it); with no new art and no new sound. Switch: `?holdauto=0` gives the press above back (a tap locks and keeps fighting, again lets go, a kill moves the lock).
- **A press with nothing in sight is one attack the way you face, and a bow's is an arrow** (v2.3.6547, card CT item 8; the owner, 2026-11-24: "I want free form attacks when you press the attack button prior to entering any combat situation. So I want arrows to be able to shoot whenever the line of site is aimed at (instead of the current behavior of only firing when something is in front to fire at like props or monsters)"): the arrow flies the way your bro faces, to the bow's reach, whether or not its line is on anything. One that meets a monster hits it and starts the fight as any arrow does; one that meets nothing is a miss and costs nothing on the worker. Presses keep the bow's own rhythm (an arrow no sooner than its cycle after the last). Everywhere else the bow still waits for its line: the latch's arrows fly at the lock, and today's controls are unchanged. Switch: `?freefire=0` gives the old wait back (a press with nothing in front looses nothing).
- **A sword's press does not carry you to the monster** (v2.3.6547; the owner, 2026-11-24: "I want to change the melee behavior so that it doesn't jump to every monster. Instead you must press an attack charge button to get that behavior."): the press locks and faces the monster and the sword swings where your bro stands; only the left stick walks you in, and a swing that cannot reach misses. The sword dash (the lunge) is the Charge button's (below, v2.3.6549). No switch: in this mode the press already moved nothing when the owner spoke; what they felt was the game between two of the trial's steps on 2026-11-24, when this mode's Attack was still today's press, whose first press lunges.
- **The sight line** (v2.3.6543): while a monster is in your sight (alive, within the weapon's range, in front of you: 45 degrees each side of the way you face) a soft white line runs from your bro to it. It is the bow's own aim stream, so the trial adds no new look. The owner: "The line of site will become visible if it's within target range of the monster (target range will depend on whether melee weapon or ranged weapon is equipped with range being longer)." With a bow out and shooting, the bow's own line is the line (one, not two over each other).
- **The sight line is drawn only in combat** (v2.3.6546; the owner, 2026-11-24, after playing: "it's snapping to a monster target when you run by. It's supposed to be just free movement until you tap attack and it'll lock on", and asked what snapped: "It's only the line drawn that snaps"): before Attack is pressed no line is drawn, so running past monsters shows nothing jumping from one to the next; the sighting still decides, unseen, what Attack will lock, and Block still comes with it (v2.3.6549: the specials in reach, below). Once Attack locks a monster the line runs from your bro to that monster, and it goes when combat ends. Nothing turned the body or the camera toward a monster before the press (a sword walked past one, every frame read). `?sightline=1` draws the line before the press again, for one phone.
- **Attack wears the plain sword** (v2.3.6546; the owner, 2026-11-24: "attack button needs to change icon to general attack icon right now it's a crosshair"): in this mode the latch shows the game's own sword, the Character sheet's Melee drawing the weapon pill already shows, whatever weapon is in hand, raised at rest and pressed in and lit while combat is on. The crosshair is the aiming stick's face (v2.3.4130), and here nothing is aimed by the finger. Today's controls keep the crosshair.
- **A clean right side: Attack and Jump, and each other button at its own moment** (v2.3.6549, card CT part 16; the owner, 2026-11-24: "I want a clean attack and jump button that's it. The special attacks buttons should only appear when a monster is within attack range."). With nothing near, the right side is Attack and Jump only. Each other button comes at its own moment, decided for all of them by one rule (game/thumbSide.js), and none blinks at an edge: each line has an inner edge to come and a wider one to stay, and holds a moment after it is crossed.
- **The specials come in reach** (v2.3.6549): Special, Whirlwind and Shield Bash come only while a monster is where an attack would connect: with a sword the swing's own reach (72 px past the monster's body, x the gear's melee reach; held out to 90), with a bow or a staff its shot range (675 px x the gear's reach), any way you face (your bro turns to strike). They stay 0.4 s after the last moment in reach. Whirlwind also needs a monster locked, as it always has. Switch: `?reachspecials=0` brings them at the sighting, as before.
- **Block at a threat, on its own spot** (v2.3.6549; the owner: "Yes" to Block as its own shield button in a fixed spot, shown at a threat): Block comes while a monster is in your sight (the weapon's sight range, the wider one it always came at), locked, or in reach, and ALSO for 3 s after a monster's hit when none is in sight: a hit from one you cannot see brings Block up. This undoes the earlier "decided for the owner while away" (v2.3.6543: such a hit did not bring Block in this mode). Switch: `?threatblock=0` brings it at the sighting only, as before.
- **Charge, the sword's dash as its own button** (v2.3.6549; the owner: "you must press an attack charge button to get that behavior", and "do the recommended charge button behavior"): with a sword in hand and a monster in sight (or locked) but too far for the sword, a Charge button comes; a press locks that monster if none is (the one in sight at the press, no attack of its own) and dashes your bro to it with the sword's lunge (its stun and damage, the worker's own checks; the same 2.5 s wait between dashes, the button dimmed with the sweep meanwhile). Once you are in reach Charge goes and the specials come. Never with a bow or a staff, never in a fight between players. Its picture is the game's own sword (the weapon pill's) with three bold speed lines, and its word "Charge" on the button's tag (as Special's AIM and ON), so it never reads as a second Attack; no new art. Switch: `?chargebtn=0`, no Charge button. No key on a computer: the two buttons are a touch screen's only.
- **Laid out for a right thumb** (v2.3.6549; the owner: "help position the size and arrangement of the buttons to cater to users using their thumb on that side to do the work"): Attack is the biggest (the owner's own 84 px upright, 94 sideways), low by the right edge where the thumb rests. The others stand on two arcs round it, the way the thumb's tip sweeps: Jump, Block and Special on the inner one, Charge, Whirlwind and Shield Bash one button further out. Each has a spot of its own that never moves when another comes or goes (a thumb learns a place), at least 10 px of air between any two, none under 44 points, all on the right half (the left stick's is the left), clear of the screen's edge, the band, the weapon pill, the minimap and the skateboard's button. The sizes follow the play column (a narrower phone, a little smaller; never under 44). Switch: `?thumbarc=0`, the old places round the disc.
- **Why Block and Special flickered sideways** (v2.3.6549; mp-twobuttons 3c/3d, twice): each button measured again, every 0.2 s, whether a monster was inside the sight's outer line (550 px), with no hold; a monster wandering across 550 between two of those took the button away and gave it back while the sight itself held still. In this mode the buttons' own "a monster near" is no longer a line of its own: the one rule decides.
- **Switched in the Test panel** (v2.3.6544): the row "Controls: two sticks / two buttons" sets the same remembered choice as the address (the installed app has none) and the game loads again into it; back to two sticks is today's default. No key needed, as the side column's row above it.
- **The teaching words follow the mode** (v2.3.6544): on a touch screen in this mode the controls tour says the stick turns, then walks; Attack locks on and keeps fighting until pressed again (v2.3.6548: with tap and hold, each tap is one attack and a hold until the ring fills keeps fighting; when the target falls the fight stops); the sight line finds what you face; Block and the specials come up round Attack while a monster is in sight. The quest coach's move, attack and special lessons say the same in short. Every other mode and a computer read the words they always did.
- **Sprint is the left stick pushed past its edge** (v2.3.6545, card CT item 6; the owner, 2026-11-24: "the sprint button should be moved to the left joystick too", asked how: "Go recommended"): the thumb out past 1.25 of the disc's drawn radius sprints, and it stops once the thumb is back inside 1.05 (the gap stops a thumb resting near the line flickering it on and off) or lifts. It is a hold, like Shift on a computer. At the edge itself the bro walks at full speed and spends nothing, so a thumb resting there never burns stamina by accident. Every end of today's sprint still ends it (stamina gone, the shield, an attack, the water, death, a zone, standing still), and after one it starts again only once the thumb has come back in and gone out again. Too tired: the button's own "Not enough energy!" and denied tick, once a push. While it runs the turn ring is lit gold; there is no sprint button in this mode (nor, since v2.3.6644, in the usual controls on a touch screen: card PAD part 9). Stamina, speed and what the worker is sent are today's. The tour adds "Push the stick past its edge to sprint." Switch: `?rimsprint=0` gives the button back and the edge does nothing.
- **In a fight between players** (a duel, a spar, No man's land) the sight sees monsters only, so Block and the specials stay up as they are today: a rule of a fight between players is the owner's to change.
- **Undone** (v2.3.6549): v2.3.6543 had decided for the owner while away that a monster's hit from outside your sight does not bring Block up in this mode; Block now comes at a threat, that hit included (above). Switch: `?threatblock=0` for the old rule, `?manual=4` (or nothing chosen) for today's controls.
- **Attack always attacks; the thing to do has its own button under it** (v2.3.6550, card CT part 17; the owner, 2026-11-24: "I'm also thinking contextual buttons like mining fishing or interacting with npc should appear below the item instead of having a dual purpose attack button. Attack button always stays attack"). In this mode Attack never turns into HARVEST: its word and sword stay, and a press swings, beside a tree too and during a chop (the chop goes on). One brass button (the game's own button look, 48 px tall, the skill's picture and a word) stands just under the ONE nearest thing: CHOP, MINE, FISH or COOK under a tree, an ore vein, a fishing spot or a campfire, exactly when Attack used to become the harvest button (the node in reach, its tool held, no fight, no monster within 220 px); during the work the same button wears the beat (PUMP, CHOP, REEL, FLIP; WAIT while the hits land) and the strokes are made on it. TALK under a townsperson within 120 px when there is no fight; a tap on it talks exactly as a tap on him does (which still works). **TALK is the owner's pill, not a word** (v2.3.6552, card CT part 19; the owner, 2026-11-25, with a picture: "Your talk button is intrusive use the left icon I attached here instead (ignore the right one)"): a small dark slate pill with a gold rim and a cream speech bubble with three dots, drawn 44 x 30 with no brass plate behind it and no word, in a clear 48 x 48 touch box (named "Talk" for a screen reader). **It stands below his name tag** (v2.3.6553, card CT part 21; the owner, 2026-11-25: "Talk icon below npc"): part 19 hung it where the old TALK button stood, 34 px under his feet, which put it over his name tag and his "1 quest available" line. Now it stands below everything written under him, the name tag and the quest line when it shows, about 6 px under the lowest, centred on him, never over either. Its place is a rule in the labels' own sizes (game/contextButton.js ctxTalkBelow: the tag hangs off his own height, the tag and the line are held one size on screen), never a measure of the page; at the window's foot it is held on the screen. CHOP, MINE, FISH and COOK keep their place, 34 px under the thing. A word on a brass plate under every townsperson read as a shout; a picture the size of a speech bubble reads as an offer. The file is the owner's picture with its drop shadow cut off, 132 x 91 on 48 colours, 3.4 kB (tools/ui/cut-talk-pill.mjs), small enough to ride inside the game's script, so it never loads on first use. The harvest buttons keep their word: a verb on a plate is what to do with the axe in hand. Nothing opens by itself, and doors and the notice board stay taps. A node and a townsperson both near: the nearer one. It hides while a menu is open. `?ctxbuttons=0` gives the old Attack back. It sits on the world (it follows the thing as the camera moves, on whole device pixels so it does not shimmer), above the sticks' touch areas, so a tap on it is never a walk or a swing.
- **A locked bro faces his lock between swings** (v2.3.6551, card CT part 18; what the owner was told: "your bro keeps facing that monster"): a tap's lock is the latch's (src 'auto'), and the facing asked only for a held attack or a tapped lock, so between swings only the hit went to the monster. In this mode a latched monster lock now turns the body, the aim and the walk's stance to it (game/lockFace.js, targeting.js bodyFacesLock); modes 1 to 4 ask exactly what they did, and a townsperson's lock never turns you.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- The right stick became a plain Attack button (v2.3.2242, 2026-09-03), then both sticks came back, the right one keeping its button ways (v2.3.2258): "I want both joysticks back ... BUT I also want the right joystick to keep its contextual button properties that exist now".
- Every attack a swipe of the right stick (manual combat, v2.3.4395 to v2.3.4655), until the owner, 2026-11-13: "I like combat better the old way. You just move the right joystick towards the enemy and it attacks as long as it is held down" (v2.3.5018: the held combat, mode 4, the default today).
- A lock you choose by tapping a monster, taken out 2026-11-09 as "easily botted and abused" (v2.3.4396). The latch locks only what is in your sight, and only with a press of Attack.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Removing today's right stick, moving the old phone tests, or changing a computer's controls: those wait for the owner's verdict on the trial.
- A tap on a monster to choose the target (taken out as "easily botted").

**Still open:**
- Whether the line should differ for a bow and a staff (today both draw the same stream as the sword's line).
- The rim sprint's two lines (1.25 on, 1.05 off) are trial numbers, for the owner's thumb to judge. v2.3.6647 (card PAD part 12): by default a full push now sprints (1.0 of the reach on, 0.8 off, the reach the drawn radius or the room to the edge less 16 px, never under 18); the two lines are `?fullpush=0`'s.
- The hold's length (0.6 s; the owner first said "Maybe 2 seconds") and whether a tap's lock should show more than the line (today the button stays raised until auto-attack is on): for the owner to judge on the phone.
- The turn band's width (v2.3.6546): on an iPhone 14 Pro upright the dead zone ends at 4.5 px, TURN at 11.25 px and the rim is at 37.5 px (sideways 5.4, 13.5 and 45), so TURN is about 7 to 8 px of thumb travel. The owner, 2026-11-24: "directional facing (no movement) isn't working with left joystick based on press distance". `?turnzone=50` or `?turnzone=60` widen it for the owner to try; no new default without their word.

Sources: docs/plan/cards/CT.md, src/game/stickZones.js, src/game/sight.js, src/game/latch.js, src/game/controlVisibility.js (blockShown, specialsShown, chargeShown), src/game/thumbSide.js, src/game/thumbArc.js, src/ui/panels/ChargeButton.jsx, src/rendering/leaves/effects-sight.js, docs/specs/manual-combat.md, docs/specs/control-redesign.md.

## Fighting
Screens: spar-fight, lock-reticle

**What it is for:** What a fight looks like on screen, and why: the damage numbers, the HP bars and name plates, the status chips, and the buttons that come up in a fight. The screenshot is a fight with a sparring clone, the owner's own way of testing fights between players.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The monster you lock on wears a thin pulsing reticle** (v2.3.3876: "Show some kind of lock on reticle if you double click a monster to lock on"; v2.3.3973: "Thin the reticle when you lock on target so it's just a thin larger line that grows and shrinks while on target. Right now it's tighter and has a thick border."): four thin orange corner lines, a box a little roomier than the body, slowly growing and shrinking, the orange chip over its head as before. **And when a monster comes into your swing's reach** (v2.3.3973: "Whenever a monster first comes in range for melee attack this same reticle should appear too. It'll help you to know when you're within distance for the first melee attack."): with a sword in hand and no lock you chose, the same lines, softer, stand on the nearest monster inside your melee reach for as long as it is; the reach is the reach ring's own, so the lines mean your first swing will land.
- **Damage numbers are big, and one size on every screen.** Owner: "Damage numbers for players and monsters needs to be about anywhere from 1.5-2x bigger", so they are 1.75x (v2.3.3033). They used to grow and shrink as the dashboard opened and folded; offered the choice, the owner said "Yes do fixed size instead" (v2.3.3192).
- **Messages are a plain hit's size** (owner: "Make the messages in the game like "no mana" and other things like "quest complete!" the same font size as the combat damage numbers", v2.3.3192). Long ones wrap into even lines and stay inside the screen; they never shrink. A crit is the one thing bigger.
- **A mark after every number:** your weapon (sword, arrow, bolt) on your hits. On a hit you take, the monster's element (snowflake, flame, wind, leaf...) or a heart. Owner: "with the elemental icon after the damage number similar to how the sword has sword icon if melee damage, arrow icon if bow damage" (v2.3.3026). A hit on you rises over your bar, not your face. A dodge says "Dodged", never "-0". Hits between players look the same as hits on monsters: yours in amber with your weapon's mark, theirs on you in red with the heart, "Blocked!" with the stamina it cost (v2.3.3263).
- **The numbers are a fifth of the real ones.** The owner found the combat numbers too big to read at a glance on a phone, and chose a display-only change (v2.3.2520). A hit shows how far the bar dropped; a kill shows the whole blow. A landed hit always reads at least 1 (owner: "I just want damage rendered during combat to be at least 1 damage (no decimals)", v2.3.2525). One blow shows the same figure on every screen (v2.3.3263).
- **Monster name plates** came from the owner: "Give monsters a name plate with their name and level beneath it similar to how the player has their name plate" (v2.3.1918). They were rebuilt to the owner's mockup (v2.3.2513), then softened because the owner found them "too harsh -- the contrast is too high" (v2.3.2530) and "a bit too large and distracting" (v2.3.2590). The plate sits over the head and turns INTO the HP bar for the monster you fight. Owner: "I want them to be above the head and disappear (change to hp bar) when taking damage ... This makes it way less distracting" (v2.3.2571).
- **The plate's ring colour is the danger:** the monster's level against the skill of the weapon in your hand. Green Low, yellow Near, orange High, red Danger, and GREY when it pays reduced XP, 5+ levels under (owner: "I like the idea of gray nameplate for reduced xp add that", v2.3.3251).
- **The monster's element after its name** (v2.3.3906, the owner: "After name of monster on nameplate it lists elemental icon type."): name, the element's icon, then the level, the plate growing by the icon. The icon is a 64 px copy of the element icon (`elem-<name>-64.webp`, `tools/ui/make-elem-small.py`), never the 256 px one, which shimmers when shrunk to plate size; it loads behind the loading screen with the other small marks. A monster with no element shows no icon.
- **No words announce a monster's move** (v2.3.4026, the owner: "Remove all text announcing what attacks or things the monsters are doing. It's lame."): no SLAM!, POUNCE!, LUNGE!, ROAR!, CHARGE!, SWEEP!, ENRAGED!, DRAIN!, Summon! or BURSTING! over a monster or a boss. The ground ring, the wind-up, the shake and the sound still tell you. What happens to YOU still shows (a miss, IMMUNE, "Dazed!", "Frozen!"), and the bestiary's stage still names its moves: it is a reference page.
- **A monster hitting you fills its plate bright red** (decision D4), so you can tell WHICH one is hitting you. That warning was deliberately left loud when the rest of the plate was softened. A brief "!" pops over a monster that notices you (owner: "put a brief exclamation point over their head to cue you in that you're being targeted", v2.3.2295).
- **A dungeon's boss is marked** (v2.3.3556). The owner had never played a dungeon ("This will probably need work to get right"). The tour found the boss looked exactly like the monsters before it, with the same picture, size and name, and about their health. Now:
  - it is HUGE, about twice your height, its HP bar with it (owner: "Make boss slime huge"; the first cut was half again as big); you hit it where it is drawn, and it turns see-through while you stand behind it;
  - its plate says "Boss Blue Slime" in bold on a deep red, kept the plates' size on the screen;
  - the deep red is darker than the alarm's bright red, which still takes over while the boss hits you, so the two never mean the same thing.
- **The Goo Grotto's giant slime splits** (v2.3.3558). Owner: "a giant slime that explodes and spawns small (normal size slimes) like 8-10 maybe but shrinks in size afterwards ... 3 rounds of that before it finally just becomes a normal slime then after you kill that you defeat the dungeon". On screen:
  - its HP bar wears a thin dark mark at each split still to come (75%, 50%, 25%), so you can see where it will burst;
  - at a mark it swells for 1.6 s, a blue slime's own warning, with no words over it (v2.3.4026: "BURSTING!" went with every monster's move words, below), and can't be hit; then it goes off with the blue slime's shockwave and sound, and 10, then 9, then 8 slimes fly out of it in an arc;
  - it shrinks a step each time (x4.59, x2.76, x1.66 a slime), so its last form is a normal slime's size that still wears its "Boss" plate;
  - killing that last form is the win: every slime it threw out that still stands pops with it, with one splat sound for them all, and the clear card comes up.
- **An ordinary blue slime warns you only by swelling** (v2.3.3568). It swells for 1.6 s before it goes off, with no words over it. Offered "BURSTING!" over it, as the giant slime shows, the owner: "No warning for the bursting I think it's more fun to discover that".
- **A slime's blast breaks what stands near it and leaves soot** (v2.3.3679). The owner: "It would be fun to make the slime explosions destroy any nearby props (trees)" and "It would also be funny if the slime explosions left black soot explosion mark on the ground for a while". In the world, every tree and prop whose ground lies within the blast's radius shatters, as if hit enough times, and is mended after the usual 3 minutes; a building in reach only starts to smoulder where the blast met it. A black scorch with streaks and flecks of char lies where it went off for a minute, fading over its last 15 seconds, the giant slime's splits too. Not a warning ring: the owner had a flat disc the size of the blast taken away (v2.3.2226, "remove code drawn impact areas for slime death"); the soot is the mess left behind, ragged, under every figure and everything standing.
- **After a dungeon's clear, a card counts down** (v2.3.3556): "Dungeon cleared! Pick up your loot · out in 27 s", with Leave now. It sits where the race clock goes, under the minimap, out of the thumbs' way. A clear used to take you out after 3 seconds, the boss's drop left on the floor. The way out at the bottom of the arena still works, and Esc does not leave (it is not a window to close). The top bar says the dungeon's one level, "the Great Cave (Lv 28)", where it read "(Lv28-28)".
- **The clear's card opens the boss's chest** (v2.3.3565). The owner said "Yes" to "the boss drops a chest with sure rewards ... The clear's XP would go into the weapon you used". Under the card's first row, the chest opens just after the clear's fanfare, its lid and coins heard as it does: since v2.3.3566 the owner's chest, shut then open, the same one that lands in the arena where the boss fell (it was the old daily chest's picture). Beside it are its lines, each with a small picture: "+60 gold", "Flora Shard ×3", "Rare Gem" (one time in ten), "+245 Sword XP". The card stays where it was, and the codex's "New monster" card steps down under it rather than over the chest. The chest stands in the world too since v2.3.3566 (docs/specs/dungeon-art.md). Since v2.3.3567 the Grotto around it moves, at the owner's ask ("a bit more animated for the water effects and such. Maybe a little Breeze for the ferns?"): its slime drips and bubbles, its torches burn, its mushrooms' spores rise and its ferns sway, all still under the QA calm switch (docs/specs/grotto-life.md).
- **A death in a dungeon keeps your bag** (v2.3.3556). Offered "your pile waits outside at the mouth, or you keep your bag in a dungeon", the owner: "Keep your bag in dungeons". "Your bag is safe" shows under "YOU DIED", only when the server says it kept the bag. A red skull from No man's land still loses what that rule takes.
- **Another player's HP bar is the size of yours** (76 x 22, with an 18 px number). After the first spar the owner said: "the enemy Hp bar was too small (the numbers were tiny in their hp bar) should be the same size as mine though" (v2.3.3267). It shows over their plate in a fight and stays a few seconds after the last hit.
- **Status chips** (Chilled, Burning, Stuck, Dazed, Soaked, Poisoned; and from players' gems, Slowed, Fractured, Thorned) show an icon and the seconds left, on a dark backing: see-through chips were hard to read on the Wheel's bright sand and snow (v2.3.2996). Each status is also drawn on the body: frost at the feet, flames, goo, stars round the head. It began with the owner's ask: "using a snowflake icon for instance when hit by a snowman's snowball and slowing down for a second" (v2.3.2996, the other four elements v2.3.3014).
- **Only the sword picks its own target:** the nearest monster (or clone) within about 220 px. The bow and the staff never do. Owner: "For ONLY melee (sword) I want to keep the auto targeting behavior ... You must tap on the monster for auto-targeting behavior to take effect" (v2.3.2258). A target you tapped stays yours until it dies or leaves the fight (v2.3.2513). A subtle ring at each monster's feet shows who the attack button can reach (owner: "there should be a subtle visual perimeter around each monster that shows you when your contextual attack button can engage", v2.3.2251): red on a monster you tapped, brass on the one that is just the nearest; small carets mark the others.
- **The attack button in a fight:** it shows the weapon in your hand (owner: "when attacking it should show the weapon type depending on what weapon is used", v2.3.3105) and turns warm (owner: "only during combat it changes color (like to orange) keeping its transparency", v2.3.2264). Its face nearly vanishes while a monster is under it (owner: "Attack button sometimes covers monster", v2.3.2263, v2.3.2472).
- **The bow only shoots at something.** It waits until its sight line crosses a target, then fires at once; the sight line stops at what it will hit, and arrows fly 3x faster (v2.3.2473). A special swiped while the line is empty is held, and the Special button shows it with a brass ring and the word AIM rather than a message (owner: "the player does not need telling every time; they swiped, they expect a shot", v2.3.2543).
- **The shield button** appears in a fight when you carry a shield (owner: "its own shield button that appears below the right button during combat"). It sits alone at the attack button's lower left, "as a mental separation for combat purpose" (v2.3.2562). It stays up until you attack, tap it again or run out of stamina (owner: "Instead of dropping the shield at first hit I want it to keep being held", v2.3.2248), and it works with the bow and staff too (v2.3.2472).
- **Special and Whirlwind** sit above the attack button on a diagonal, spaced so one thumb does not press the other (owner: "Spec and swirl need to be on the right joystick", v2.3.2574). They moved a row up for the Sprint button: "Above, fights move up" (v2.3.3106). Whirlwind disappears out of combat (the owner's call after playing it, v2.3.2561). Special greys out and says "No mana!" rather than hiding: a button that vanishes cannot be learned (v2.3.2472).
- **Against a player** (No man's land, or a clone) the shield and Special buttons are up for every weapon while the fight is within about a screen, even before the first blow (v2.3.3267, v2.3.3268). The quick-eat button (over the movement stick, showing the food it will eat) appears only while you carry food that heals and are missing health; in any fight you get one bite every 15 s (v2.3.3260).
- **No man's land skulls** sit over the name plate, in the owner's skull art: red on a player who attacked another, white on one who was attacked, 20 minutes of play each (v2.3.3058, v2.3.3279). The badge with the skull and the ring number sits just above the dashboard (owner: "make the skull level (no mans land) appear above the center of the dashboard", v2.3.3107). Since v2.3.3268 it sits just left of the shield button, no longer on top of it.
- **Sparring clones** (owner: "I wish I could test PvP somehow ... Maybe this is better to add to the admin window", v2.3.3265): up to three copies of you (sword, bow, staff), Dummy, Easy, Normal or Hard, fighting by the real player rules. A knockout heals you where you stand ("Knocked out! Healed"), and nothing is lost. After the owner's first playtest (v2.3.3267) your weapons treat a clone like a monster (owner: "attacks need to hit the clone even when you aren't locked on target"), and Easy became a real beginner (owner: "The bot was tough to be beat even on easy").
- **The staff's special swings widely**, sometimes very low. That is the owner's design: "staff, but with high variance (can have lowest damage hits)" (v2.3.2849).

**Tried before, and changed:**
- Monster plates hung under the feet (v2.3.1918). They moved over the head and became the HP bar (v2.3.2571). Plate sizes went up (v2.3.2154, v2.3.2262) until the owner said "Nameplates are now way too large", then came down (v2.3.2265), were rebuilt to the mockup (v2.3.2513), and were softened (v2.3.2530, v2.3.2590).
- Messages were about 57% of a number's size, and both changed size with the dashboard; now they are one size (v2.3.3192).
- The shield first dropped after one block (v2.3.2242); now it is held (v2.3.2248). A double-tap-and-hold guard for bow and staff (v2.3.2446) was retired and the button brought back (v2.3.2472).
- Special and Whirlwind went to the left stick (v2.3.2562) and came back to the right (v2.3.2574). Whirlwind greyed out of combat (v2.3.2542), then was hidden (v2.3.2561).
- An amber pointer on monsters chasing you was removed (owner: "Yes remove the pointer marker", v2.3.2923). A "Lining up..." message on every held bow special (v2.3.2473) became the Special button's AIM state (v2.3.2543).
- Another player's HP bar was a monster's small one (44 x 13, a 9 px number) until v2.3.3267; the old Easy clone beat a beginner who throws no specials every time.

**Please don't suggest:**
- Numbers or messages that change size with the zoom, messages smaller than a plain hit, or long messages in smaller type (they wrap).
- Hit-stop (a freeze frame on each hit): it was left off as the owner's call (v2.3.2200).
- Auto-targeting for the bow or staff, or letting a nearer monster take a target you tapped.
- Letting the bow fire at empty ground, or a pop-up message each time a bow special is held.
- Dropping the shield after a block, or bringing back the double-tap guard (TRAPS §78).
- Greying Whirlwind out of combat instead of hiding it, or moving Special and Whirlwind to the left stick.
- Muting the red "attacking you" plate, putting plates back under the feet, or bringing back the amber pointer.
- Painting a boss's resting plate the bright alarm red: that red means "hitting you right now".
- A separate health bar for each of the giant slime's forms: its one bar and its marks show the whole fight (v2.3.3558).
- Words over an ordinary blue slime as it swells: finding it out is the fun (v2.3.3568). Or over any monster's move, the giant slime's included (v2.3.4026).
- Shrinking other players' HP bars, or making the staff's special steadier.

**Still open:**
- Against players, an ordinary hit's knockback and the bow volley's burn are not applied yet: the owner's call (v2.3.3264).
- Clones walk through buildings and water, since the server knows no walls. On your screen an arrow may stick in a wall while the server still lands it on you.

Sources: docs/specs/damage-number-size.md, docs/specs/monster-statuses.md, docs/specs/first-30-minutes.md, docs/specs/sparring.md, docs/specs/combat-feel-pack.md, docs/specs/control-redesign.md, docs/specs/no-mans-land.md, docs/specs/pvp-fights.md, docs/specs/one-formula.md, docs/specs/xp-decay.md, docs/specs/bow-sight-gate.md, docs/specs/specials-rebalance.md, docs/TRAPS.md, CLAUDE.md, src/data/gameSystems.js, src/rendering/systems/entityRenderer.js, src/ui/ElemStatusChips.jsx, src/ui/panels/ShieldButton.jsx, src/ui/panels/SpecialButton.jsx, src/ui/panels/TouchControls.jsx, src/game/targeting.js, src/game/playerActions.js

## Dying: the death card
Screens: death-card

**What it is for:** Saying that you died, what killed you, and where you are going, for the five seconds before the worker brings you back in BroTown, and saying it again in the chat once you are back.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A card, for the five seconds** (v2.3.3817). A judge's pass on a computer: a level-1 bro died in the first quest's slimes and "just reappeared in BroTown"; none of the captures showed a death. A death showed only things that pass: the body's fall, a shake, a half-second dark flash, the sound and a floating "YOU DIED" like a hit number. Now a small card in the middle of the play area says "You died", "Killed by Blue Slime" (the monster's name as its plate says it; in No man's land the player's name, and what they took once the worker says so), and "Back in BroTown in 5" counting down.
- **At the top, never over you** (v2.3.3841). The owner: "The 'you got killed by' placard needs to be positioned in a different spot (maybe towards the top) because I totally missed my exploding skeleton death." In the middle it covered exactly where the bro bursts into bones. It now takes the place of No man's land's "Attack?" window (cardPlace): beside the minimap on a computer and a sideways phone, under it, centred, on an upright phone; slid off the middle when it would come down near your head. It comes in quickly (150 ms, fading and settling) so the eye goes to it.
- **A strip across the top, not a stamp** (v2.3.4035). The owner: "The you died by snowman message should span the entire top strip of the game screen. It looks a bit silly as a stamp-like screen near the top." It is a dark red band directly under the top bar, as wide as the top bar (the play column: the whole phone upright, the world's own width sideways and on a computer), with a thin lighter edge, sliding down out of the bar in 150 ms: "YOU DIED · killed by a Snowman · back in BroTown in 5" on one line where it fits, two on a narrow phone and a computer's column (the head and who, then the rest). It covers the top of the minimap for those five seconds; the bro, in the middle, stays clear, so the bone burst is seen.
- **It never takes a tap or a click**, and has nothing to close: a notice, not a window. A dying player's thumbs are still on the controls, and the card goes by itself with the respawn.
- **One line in the chat once you are back** ("You were killed by Blue Slime, and came back in BroTown."), so a glance away, or a screenshot that missed the five seconds, still finds it.
- **"BroTown"**, never "town" or "the Wheel": the place you come back to, as the top bar names it.

**Tried before, and changed:**
- In the middle of the play area (v2.3.3817): it hid the death itself.
- A 264 px rounded card beside or under the minimap (v2.3.3841): "a bit silly as a stamp-like screen" (v2.3.4035).
- The floating "YOU DIED" over the body alone (kept: it is the death's moment; the card says the rest).

**Still open:**
- Whether a death should cost anything outside No man's land and a red skull is the owner's call; the card says only what the worker did.

Sources: docs/specs/death-card.md, src/game/deathCard.js, src/ui/mobile/DeathCard.jsx, tools/qa/mp/mp-deathcard.mjs.

## The owner's card: the economy (the Test panel)
