Screens: dev-econ-card

**What it is for:** The owner's daily look at the game's economy, without a session or a terminal: how much gold and how many of each item the game made and destroyed each day (the day's book's `econ` part, counted by the gold and bag doors as each road runs), the room's own counts beside it (card A's `room` part), and the economy's five numbers of the grand plan's step 10.4. Only the owner sees it: a box of the Test panel, keyed with the admin key (v2.3.5093, card E PR 12).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A box of the Test panel, not a window of its own**, below the smoothness table (card A's `ReportTable`): the owner's tools live there, the panel's own Close closes it, and nothing a player sees changes. Drawn only when "Show the economy" is tapped, so opening the panel reads nothing.
- **Read through the book's ONE admin route** (`GET /dev/daybook`, card A's): the smoothness table and this card read the same answer; there is no second way to read the book.
- **"Made" and "destroyed", never "minted" and "burned"** on screen: the owner reads plain words. A move between players or gold the game holds (the bank, a market order, the mail) is neither, and the card says so in its first line, so a busy trading day with nothing made reads as it is.
- **A number nothing counts yet says "not counted yet"**, with what it waits for, never a 0: a 0 for unexplained gold is the reading the owner wants to see, and must never be shown by default. The five numbers are always listed in the plan's order, so the card's shape does not change as each one gets its count.
- **Today, the last 7 or the last 30 days**, newest first, each day its own block: the same choices as the smoothness table beside it.

- **The numbers' sources, all read, nothing new written** (the planning session's reading, 2026-11-14): unexplained gold and the share of new gold to the richest come from the once-a-day economy count (`metrics:`), which now carries the world's one sum (`server/src/worldsum.js`) through its own paged walk; gold made a player-hour from the book (gold made, and the players' seconds added at each leave); the share moved one way for nothing from the transfer ledger (`xfer:`). The card reads them through its second route, `GET /dev/econcard`. A number that cannot be counted yet (one daily count so far, no play today, no gold moved) says why in its place.
- **Unexplained gold in red when it is not 0**: it is the one number that means something went wrong.
- **A character's restart is added back** (the gatekeeper's sitting 10): a restart deletes a character's gold together with their own count of what they made, outside the gold door, so the owner's own restarts from the picker read as red copies and losses. The server books the difference in the day's book as the restart happens (`econcard.js econResetBook`, riding the book's own writes), each daily count says how much of its day's it had seen, and the card adds back the restarts between the two counts. When a restart falls before the days the card loaded, it says so beside the number.
- **Gold made a player-hour counts the sittings still open**: the book counts a sitting's seconds when it ends but its gold as it is made, so a reading inside a long sitting was far too high; the route adds the open sittings' seconds of today.
- **The count is not instant**, and the card says so under the number: the daily walk reads 200 records a slot over a few seconds, so gold moving between two pages then can show as a little unexplained, either way.

**Still open:**
- Cases waiting, for card Q.

Sources: src/ui/panels/EconCard.jsx, src/game/econCard.js, server/src/econcard.js, server/src/worldsum.js, server/test/econ-card.test.mjs, server/src/daybook.js, docs/plan/cards/E.md (PR 12).

## The usage meter (the Test panel)
Screens: dev-usage-meter

**What it is for:** The owner's watch on what the game costs to run, after turning the six cost switches on (2026-11-17: "I think turn all of them on then we monitor usage cost"): the rows the server wrote and read and the messages it sent, today (UTC) and since it last started, with an estimate of the day's writes against the plans' included rows. Only the owner sees it: a box of the Test panel, keyed with the admin key (v2.3.6001, card CS PR 2).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A box of the Test panel, below the economy card**, drawn only when "Show usage" is tapped: opening the panel reads nothing, and nothing a player sees changes.
- **Counted in the server's memory, never written** (the card: no storage write for the meter itself), read through one admin route (`GET /dev/usage`). So it starts again at an update, and the box says since when it counts: "counted since 09:12 UTC (the server started then)".
- **The estimate is of rows written only**, the number the plans' included usage is told in (free about 100,000 a day, paid 50 million a month), spread over the part of the day counted; no estimate from under ten minutes ("Too early"). Reads and messages are shown as counts, not priced.
- **Cloudflare's own page is named as the truth** (Workers & Pages, brotown-server, Metrics; Billing), right under the estimate: the meter is a guide, not the bill.
- **The six switches each get a line in plain words** (what its rows are), today's rows written and read, and the way to turn any off (Live flags, Set a switch): the owner turned them on to watch them.
- **The biggest kinds first** (`rpg:`, `oplog:` ...), six of each: the names are the storage's own, the owner's way to ask a session "why so many `rpg:` writes?".

**Still open:**
- A day's total is lost at an update (memory only): Cloudflare's Metrics keep the whole history.

Sources: src/ui/panels/UsageMeter.jsx, src/game/usageMeter.js, server/src/usagemeter.js, server/test/usage-meter.test.mjs, tools/guards/usage-meter-page.test.mjs, docs/plan/cards/CS.md (PR 2).

## The Test panel sets a switch
Screens: dev-set-switch

**What it is for:** Setting any live switch to a value from the owner's phone, with no terminal: the owner is never asked to run a command (CLAUDE.md §1). The two that matter most: `newersave` off right after a dashboard rollback (OPERATIONS, "Rolling back"), and `max_players` to hold the world to fewer seats (v2.3.5602, card OPS PR 3).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A row under Live flags, not a window**: the list above already shows each switch and its Clear, so setting one sits beside where it then appears. The list is shown again after a set.
- **The usual switches as chips, each with its words** (`src/game/ownerSwitches.js`); any other lower-case name may be typed, and the server judges it (its refusal shows in the panel's message line). A guard holds every chip to a row of the switch table, so a chip never names a switch that is gone.
- **On/off as two choices, a number as a number**: an on/off switch offers no number field, a number switch a whole number in its range; anything else is not sent.
- **Two taps, the panel's own pattern** (as Forget and New key): the first shows, in the message line, what setting it will do, in plain words; only the second sends. A typo cannot change the live game on its own.
- **The same POST as the panel's other switch taps**, with the admin key: no second way in.

**Still open:**
- The worker's four old camelCase switches are not offered (they keep the lower-case rule's exception on the server); none is an owner's recovery.

Sources: src/ui/panels/SetSwitchRow.jsx, src/game/ownerSwitches.js, src/ui/panels/DevPanel.jsx (its flags section), tools/guards/owner-switches.test.mjs, docs/plan/cards/OPS.md (PR 3).

## The Test panel looks after the feedback board
Screens: dev-feedback-owner

**What it is for:** Taking down a post anyone can read on the feedback board, and sweeping the board (posts too big for it and old pace rows out, its counts made again), from the owner's phone, never a terminal (red team RT-3's #1878 routes; v2.3.5603, card OPS PR 4).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The board's newest 20, each with its own Take down**, not a box for a post's id: the owner sees the post on the board, never its id. The list is the board's public one, read only when "Show the newest posts" is tapped.
- **Two taps, the panel's own pattern**: the first says, in the message line, which post (who, about what, its start) or what a sweep removes; only the second acts. Take down's chip turns red while it waits.
- **A 404 is told apart in words**: the Feedback object answers a plain 404 both to a worker without these routes and to a key it does not take, so the row asks the panel's own admin route quietly and says which ("this server can't take a post down yet" or "your admin key does not work on this server"). Either side can ship first.
- **The sweep's numbers in words**, every number the reply carries: one the row does not know by name is said by its own, never dropped.
- **The admin key outside /api/admin goes through the panel's `callOwner`**, the same key and timeout as its `call`: one way the owner's key leaves the page.

Sources: src/ui/panels/FeedbackOwnerRows.jsx, src/game/feedbackOwner.js, src/ui/panels/DevPanel.jsx (callOwner), tools/guards/feedback-owner.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-feedbackowner.mjs.

## The Test panel silences from a chat report
Screens: dev-feedback-owner

**What it is for:** Silencing a player from the report on them, without a terminal: their chat reaches nobody, and they still play. Between Kick (back at once) and Freeze (kept out). Card MOD's #1897 route, the admin key (v2.3.5605, card OPS PR 6).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Under each chat report, beside its Freeze**: the report is where the owner meets the trouble; a silence is answered there, with the same two taps as Freeze.
- **Four lengths, never a typed number**: a minute, an hour, a day, until lifted. The first tap says, in the message line, who and for how long, and that they still play.
- **The running silences are read with the reports** (one more request on "Show chat reports"): a silenced player's report reads "Silenced until 14:32 UTC" (a day and a time when it is not today's) with a Lift, in place of the four.
- **Times in UTC**, as the silenced player is told them (card MOD's spec): one clock for both.
- **A 404 is told apart**, as the feedback rows do: "this server can't silence anyone yet" (a worker without the route) or the key.

(Its screenshot rides the Test panel's own screens: the report needs a second player, which the UI review's single page has not.)

Sources: src/ui/panels/SilenceButtons.jsx, src/game/silenceOwner.js, src/ui/panels/DevPanel.jsx (chat reports, callOwner), tools/guards/silence-owner.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-silenceowner.mjs.

## The Test panel renames a character
Screens: dev-rename

**What it is for:** Renaming a character without a terminal. Card MOD's #1907 made names permanent (the phone can no longer change one) and gave the owner the only rename, behind the admin key (v2.3.5611, card OPS PR 12).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Two places, one row**: under each chat report, after its Silence (a name is often what was reported), and in the Character section as "Rename my character".
- **Closed until asked**: a single Rename chip, so a report's row stays short; it opens to the new name, Rename and Cancel.
- **Staff word only on your own**: the chip that sends `force` (a name like "Owner" that reads as the game or its staff) is offered only on the owner's own row; a report's row never sends it.
- **Two taps**: the first says, in the message line, who becomes what and that everyone sees it; the second sends.
- **The reply in words**: the old name and the new, whether they see it now or next time they play, and that the boards follow at their next update; a refused name says why in the rule's words (no emoji or symbols, 20 letters at most, reads as staff).
- **A 404 is told apart**, as the silence does: no saved character with that id (a guest has none), a worker without the route ("this server can't rename anyone yet"), or the key.

Sources: src/ui/panels/RenameRow.jsx, src/game/renameOwner.js, src/ui/panels/DevPanel.jsx (chat reports, Character), tools/guards/rename-owner.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-renameowner.mjs.

## The Test panel keeps its key for the session

**What it is for:** The admin key opens every lever in the Test panel. It was kept on any device it was typed on, for good: a borrowed phone, a shared computer, a preview build's own address (v2.3.5718, card SEC PR 19).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **For this session by default**: the key is kept in this tab until it closes (sessionStorage is per tab: a new tab asks again; gatekeeper Z40's review), so typing it once on someone else's device leaves nothing behind.
- **A tick, "Remember on this device"**, off unless ticked, above **Save key**: on the owner's own phone it keeps today's way, so the owner types the key once.
- **The line under the button says which**: "Kept in this tab until it closes, unless you tick Remember on this device (your own phone only)".
- **Nobody is logged out by the update**: a key remembered before this version is still read, as remembered; **Forget** clears it from both places.
- **The button is "Save key"**: "on this device" was true only with the tick. The QA tools tap the new words.

Sources: src/ui/panels/DevPanel.jsx (the admin key), src/game/adminKeyStore.js, tools/guards/browser-secrets.test.mjs.

## The login wheel (free daily spin)
Screens: login-spin

**What it is for:** One free spin a day for the whole ACCOUNT (the device, up to ten characters), waiting behind the top bar's FREE DAILY SPIN button (v2.3.6560: no longer offered by itself at login). It is a gift, not a game: the gold amounts are printed on the wheel, one press of SPIN, a few seconds of anticipation, then "+N gold" and the coins fly to the Gear tab's gold.

**What is on it:** The green "FREE DAILY SPIN" plate on top; the wheel (100 Gold is about half of it, then 200, 300, 500, 1,000 and a thin 10K sliver: the segments' angles ARE the odds); a pointer at the top that never turns; one big SPIN button (Enter on a computer; the words say "Click" there); a close X that is held while the wheel turns. After the spin lands the prize is only a POT the worker holds, and the window turns into the owner's DOUBLE OR NOTHING? panel (the plate, a 50/50 wheel with a skull half and a coins half, a green YES and a red NO), as many rounds as the player wants: a win doubles the pot and asks again (the slot machine pays out), the skull empties it (the buzzer) and says so; NO (or the X) collects. The pot is paid by itself past 100,000.

**Decisions, and why:**
- It never opens by itself (v2.3.6560, card LS). The owner, 2026-11-24: *"the daily spin popup wouldn't leave me alone. Can you disable the immediate pop up feature when logging in for that and just leave it as a button in the top right?"* It used to open at login whenever a spin waited, and again a second after it was closed without a spin. Now the glowing button at the right of the top bar is the only way in; the game still asks the worker once at login so the button knows to glow. `?loginspin=1` brings the old offer back for one phone, once a sitting (closed, it waits for the button); `?loginspin=0` never by itself, like the default.
- On that `?loginspin=1` road it never opens over a loading screen or over a new character's first steps (the welcome, the five first steps, a plate or a coach card): the host waits for `worldReady` and for `onboardingBusy` to clear, and a brand-new character's spin waits for them.
- Double or nothing is the owner's own call ("as many times as they want"): the worker rolls each 50/50, a double tap is never a second roll, and a player who simply leaves is paid what they held.
- A FREE DAILY SPIN button (the green plate, scaled to the bar) sits at the top bar's right end while the account has today's spin: it opens the window anywhere; SPIN and YES turn only on safe ground (BroTown and its commons) and otherwise say "Spin in a safe area" (NO always collects). The wheel is large and centred in every layout.
- Closing it without spinning keeps today's spin (the button stays bright) for later that day: nothing is written until the spin is pressed.
- The wheel turns by CSS on one picture; the pointer is a separate fixed layer, and the wheel's picture has the pointer's place filled from the rim elsewhere so no hole turns past it.
- The window is dark and full-screen over the world, not a card: the art is the screen. The sound is the whole spin (4 seconds) and ends with its last click as the wheel stops.
- Each wheel is a lit picture over its plain-rim twin, turning together, the lit one slowly fading out and in (the lights dim and brighten); the spin has a wind-up, a long eased stop, a flicking pointer, a burst of coins sized to the prize, a pop and count-up of the gold; double or nothing has a drum-roll, a gold glow on a win, a red flash and shake on nothing, and "Exactly 50/50 every spin" always on it. All of it is transforms and short Web Animations, still under reduced motion.
- The pictures are 256 colours, fetched into the cache behind the loading screen, decoded on opening and dropped on closing.

**Still open:**
- Whether a missed day should stack (the owner said one a day) and whether 10K at 2.5% is the right jackpot: the angles are the art's, the owner's call.

Sources: CLAUDE.md, docs/specs/login-wheel.md, server/src/loginspin.js, src/game/loginSpinWheel.js, tools/guards/login-spin-offer.test.mjs, src/ui/mobile/LoginSpinWindow.jsx, src/rendering/loginSpinPreload.js, tools/qa/ui-review/states.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-loginspin.mjs

Under it a "Your plots" heading and the bed-status line, then the tabs **Buy** and **Deliver** (were Seeds / Orders; same ids), each under a short heading ("What you can buy", "What you can deliver for gold"). Nothing removed. Pictures: `docs/shots/feedseed-before.webp`, `feedseed-after.webp`.

The name and Lv sit on the picture's top edge on a soft dark fade, so they stay in view; the title, party and AP rows follow under it and the panel scrolls inside today's band height. While something is picked, the rest of the Gear tab steps back: the other bag cells, the other equipment cells and the empty squares drop to 30% strength (no CSS blur: iOS); the picked cell and the card stay bright, every cell stays tappable (picking another switches), and a tap on the veiled space outside any cell, button or the card puts the pick down. The card's ✕ pulses three times as it appears, then keeps a soft blue glow (still, under reduced motion). New things on the screen, all small, gold-edged and never over a card: the big arrow at the band's CLOSE button; a 56 px E key with "Press E to collect!" over the character at a harvest's window (and the arrow at the pad); a toast under the top bar when the window is missed; pulsing rings on the wanted trees and an arrow on the screen's edge when they are off screen; "Click the monster to attack" over a near monster and a sword cursor; the COMBAT TIPS card under the top bar after the first kill. ## Manual combat draws nothing (v2.3.4395; the game's combat since v2.3.4396)

Spec: `docs/specs/manual-combat.md`. The game's combat since v2.3.4396 (`?manual=0` is the old one for one device; `?manual=2` the same with a slingshot bow: pull back, let go). The controls tour, the phone's arrow at the special's button and a computer's COMBAT TIPS say the new way. A sword's and a bow's every attack is a swipe (a stroke, a flick, a computer's click); a staff is as it always was (it fires while the thumb is held), so there is nothing to wait on and NOTHING is drawn for it: no gauge, no bar, no ring. The one thing a player sees is the special's button reading ON while a special is armed for the next attack. Three indicators for an earlier hold-and-release version were built and turned down by the owner in one night: on the attack disc ("The thumb covers the indicator and the indicator itself is too subtle"), a ring round the character ("too obnoxious having it circle the player"), a bar over the joystick ("I don't like it. Especially for bow and staffs"). Please don't suggest: a build-up or cooldown indicator in any of those places, or hold-and-release for the bow or staff.

## Readiness on the weapon: the sword's feel (v2.3.4800; the timing bar removed)

Not a screen: it is drawn in the world, on the sword (spec: `docs/specs/sword-feel.md`).

**What it is for:** how far your swing has come back, and the moment a strike is perfect, shown ON THE BLADE: dull right after a swing, brightening over its cycle, a glint running down the edge in the perfect strike's window. The cut's arc follows the thumb (forehand, backhand, flick, sweep) and its contact is sized by its weight.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The timing bar is gone.** The owner, 2026-11-11: "Yes remove the timing bar. It needs to be something else entirely" -- after asking to "think about the 'feel' of the combat" instead of copying other games. It stood over the left joystick from v2.3.4625 to v2.3.4656 (the owner's sword and arrow, grey filling gold, a glint and a frozen copy); before that, north of the right one. Its pictures were the owner's own sheet, kept on disk (`public/sprites/timingbar/weapons.png`). Gone with it: `TimingBar.jsx`, its CSS, its preload, `?timingbar`, `mp-timingbar`, the review's `timing-bar` and `timing-bar-glint` pictures.
- **On the weapon, never a gauge** (the owner's brief): the thumb never covers it, it is where the eye already is in a fight, and it needs no place on the screen.
- **Placeholders, minted** (the combat-feel-pack pattern): soft white pictures tinted in code, in a world layer of their own; the owner's own drawings can replace them by name.
- `?swordfeel=0` is the sword without it on one device.

**Please don't suggest:** a gauge, bar or ring for readiness anywhere on the screen (four were turned down: on the attack disc, round the bro, over either joystick); hit-pause, a combo counter, a charge attack, tap-to-lock (the owner's do-not-re-propose list).

Sources: src/game/weaponFeel.js, src/game/swordFeel.js, src/rendering/systems/weaponFeelFx.js, src/data/weaponFeelSound.js, tools/qa/mp/mp-swordfeel.mjs

## The new dashboard kit (v2.3.6680, card UI2)
Screens: ui2-kit

**What it is for:** The one kit every screen of the new dashboard is built from (the owner, 2026-11-25: "Establish a centralized UI design system before migrating individual menus"), and a page to SEE it at the phone's real size: `?uikit=1`. Not a screen a player meets yet; the live Bro tab is unchanged.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **One source of numbers.** `src/game/ui2Tokens.js` holds every colour, size, radius, depth, the shine and the motion; the kit's style sheet (`src/ui/kit/ui2.css`) carries them in a block written from it, held equal by `tools/guards/ui2-tokens.test.mjs`. A change of the look is a change in that one file. Everything is under `.ui2`, so nothing that exists today changes.
- **The slate: the game's own, turned toward the benchmark's blue.** The game's eight colours (band-top `#26383E`, sheet, raised, well, deep, brass, highlight, ivory text) turned 12 to 15 degrees toward blue and a little deeper, at the same lightness; brass and the ivory text to the digit. The benchmark (reference 01) is the feel; its thick gold rim and heavy text shadows are not copied: the rim is 2 px, the shadows 1 px.
- **Raised and recessed never read alike, shine or no shine.** A control you press is raised (a broad top-lit gradient, a light top edge, a bevel and a drop shadow); information is sunk (a dark gradient and an inner shadow, no drop shadow). The gloss is one token (`--ui2-shine`, `?ui2shine=0` puts it out) and the difference holds without it.
- **Selected is pushed IN, not tinted.** Its light inverts (darker at the top), its shadow goes inside, its gloss goes; the footprint is the same. Pressed is momentary: a pixel down, a shorter shadow.
- **Five light action faces, ONE dark label.** Gold routine, green accept, blue guidance, red destructive only, silver dismiss; the label `#141D22` on all five, worked out at 5.4 to 1 or better on every stop of every face, pressed and glossed included (the suite). Red is lighter than the game's danger red so its pressed stops pass.
- **The game's grades** on the rarity slot: Normal, Rare (`#5B99DE`), Elite (`#E8893A`, a lighter metallic top edge), Godly (the game's five-colour prism, still: no loop); the stars are the non-colour cue, the grade's word goes to a screen reader. The suite holds them to `dash/common.js`.
- **The letters: Source Sans 3** (tag 6785, the owner, 2026-11-26: *"swap out the typography (font style) on all menus to be same as the 'logout' font"*): the kit's token is the Log Out button's face, 400 and 700, the sizes unchanged (a sans at the same px reads a little smaller: the owner judges from the pictures); Merriweather is no longer asked for, and `?ui2look=0` (yesterday's colours) wears today's letters. `menu-font.test` holds every font family of the kit, `dash/*.css` and `sheet/*.css` to the token. What follows is the serif's choice, as it was:
- **The serif: Merriweather** (400 and 700, SIL OFL, Google Fonts like the game's other fonts): drawn for screens, a large x-height that holds at 11 px, lining and tabular figures (`font-variant-numeric`). Turned down: Cinzel (capitals only: no sentence reads in it) and EB Garamond (hairline serifs and old-style figures that blur at 11 px on a phone). It loads behind the kit page's own "Loading the kit", only where `.ui2` draws. Raleway, asked for by the page and used by nothing, is no longer asked for.
- **Sizes at 390 x 844:** the band 390 x 243, its header row 52, the page under it 191 (175 inside the kit's own pads); a Bro tile 119 x 84 with 46 px art; header controls 44 tall; the least text 11 px (a count badge 10).
- **What the real size cannot hold:** sideways (the page is 66 px tall at 844 x 390) the Character sample drops Clan and Guild and folds the portrait to 56 px; the four summaries need the upright page's second row.
- **Three weights, and only three** (v2.3.6780, card UI3-kit). The owner, 2026-11-26 (`docs/design/UI_FEEDBACK_2026-11-26.md`): *"the biggest problem with the new BroTown menus is everything is competing for the same level of attention. Almost every piece of information has its own large beveled button, even when it isn't an actionable or particularly important piece of information"*, and part 3: *"Navigation buttons: Raised, beveled, prominent. Reserved for destinations and actions. Important statistics: Recessed surfaces with large numbers. No heavy beveling. Supporting information: Flat, aligned within shared containers. No individual heavy borders."* So: **raised** (`Ui2Tile`, the header's controls) is a destination or an action, always a button with a handler; **the stat** (`Ui2Stat`) is sunk, its number large (24) and gold, and wears the chevron ONLY when it opens (the mock-up's *"120.5 DPS ›"*: a button), a div with none when it opens nothing; `size="pill"` is one line, a picture and the number (a balance); **facts** (`Ui2Facts`, `Ui2Fact`, new) are label and value rows in ONE sunk container, the label muted left, the value right on one column, no border, background or shadow of their own; a row that opens is a button with the chevron, still flat; `dir="row"` lays them side by side (a strip). `tools/guards/ui3-weights.test.mjs` reads the code's syntax tree and the sheets, and fails a raised face on a thing that cannot be pressed (its dodges planted in the suite). Three were on main in other cards' files and wait on its shrinking allow list: Weapon's DPS and Shared's Max HP, still raised (card UI3-combat), and the item page's doors, drawn as stats (the next job after Gear's PR 3). The plate "Three weights" on `?uikit=1` shows them side by side.
- **The owner's palette** (v2.3.6780). Part 6: *"I would keep the navy and gold aesthetic. It works particularly well with your new control panel. The dashboard's muted blue-gray background is what makes it feel somewhat disconnected"*, with six values, each the kit's to the digit: the page and the frame's middle #14232F, the raised face's middle #223D53, the well #0E1D29, the text #F1F0E9, the gold #EAC474, and #4B6275 as the LINE every edge reads (`--ui2-line`; never a surface: muted and gold words would read 3.7 and 3.8 to 1 on it). The stops around each are a step lighter above and darker below, every one holding the words at 4.5 to 1 (the suite). *"gold for progression and important numbers, and other colors only when they communicate something meaningful"*: the guard allows a text colour from the text list, or a meaning only on its selector (a grade, better and worse, a count). As a control's edge the line is 2.5 to 1 on the base (1.8 on a raised face), under the 3 to 1 asked of an edge: kept, as theirs, and flagged.
- **Softer bevels and shadows, one type** (v2.3.6780). Part 4: *"reduce the button bevels and shadows, and use the same typography across all navigation buttons."* The bevel 2 to 1 px, the drop shadow .42 to .30, the soft drop .28 to .16; every tile's word and header control reads one size, `--ui2-fs-nav` (13, bold). Raised still reads apart from sunk with the shine off (mp-uikit 6).
- **`?ui2look=0`, yesterday's look to compare** (v2.3.6780): remembered on the phone (`bt_ui2look`), `?ui2look=1` returns; the kit page's header carries the link. Its values are `UI2_WAS` in the tokens, and the suite rebuilds main's whole block from it, so a colour changed and left out of it is red.

Sources: src/game/ui2Tokens.js, src/ui/kit/ (ui2.css, the pieces, Facts.jsx, shine.js, UiKitPage.jsx, sampleData.js), tools/guards/ui2-tokens.test.mjs, tools/guards/ui3-weights.test.mjs, tools/guards/lib/raisedFormsKnown.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-uikit.mjs, docs/shots/ui2-kit-*.png, docs/shots/ui3-kit-*.png.

## The kit's roles, and the five pieces V17 named (v2.3.6840, card UI4 PR 1)
Screens: ui4-kit-roles, ui4-kit-tab, ui4-kit-stepper, ui4-kit-inspect, ui4-kit-compare, ui4-kit-overlay

**What it is for:** The first step of the owner's menu redesign, V17 (2026-11-27, `docs/design/v17/MASTER-IMPLEMENTATION.md`): every piece of a menu says what it is by a role its code names, so later steps can make a fact, a thing to inspect, a way to go somewhere and a commitment LOOK different (the owner: *"Clearly distinguish passive facts, inspectable facts, navigation/preparation, selected controls and commitments throughout every menu. Do not make information compete with actions"*). Not a screen a player meets: the plates on `?uikit=1`. Nothing on a screen changes its look in this step.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Nine roles, one table** (`src/game/uiRoles.js`): V17's table (V17:37-47) with the game's names: `info` (a fact to read: never a button), `inspect` (a fact that opens), `go` (a destination, or getting something ready), `selected` (the chosen tab or filter), `adjust` (a setting, a number, a cancel, a close), `commit` (does the thing), `danger` (cannot be undone), `disabled` (with its reason), `slot` (an item's place). V17:49: *"Use explicit semantic role props at component creation (not runtime regex/classification as in the prototype)"*.
- **The prop is `intent`, not `role`.** `role` is the browser's own accessibility attribute, which the kit already passes through (Bro's strip is a `role="group"`; a tab must say `role="tab"`). `intent` is V17's own word (V17:157, *"explicit intent props"*).
- **Yesterday's props still work, mapped in ONE place.** A stat or a fact with a handler is `inspect`, without one `info`; a tile is `go`, or `selected` when chosen; an action's colour says its role (gold and green commit, blue goes, red is danger, silver adjusts); off is `disabled`. Before, the guess was written in each piece (`typeof onClick === 'function'`); now each piece asks `ui2Role()`, and the guard (`tools/guards/ui4-roles.test.mjs`) fails the guess written again. The calls on main when this came, made with no intent, are counted on `tools/guards/lib/rolesKnown.mjs` (78; the count only falls): a NEW call names its intent.
- **Each role's look is TODAY's.** An action made with an intent and no colour wears today's face for that role; the role is written on the piece (`data-u2-role`) and no style sheet reads it yet. V17's look for the roles is the next step, behind its own switch (`?v17look`), so the owner can compare.
- **The five pieces V17 names that the kit lacked** (V17:49's list), in today's look, none yet used by a screen: the **Tab** (a real tab row, the chosen one pushed in, its word always written: never colour alone; Left and Right move along it); the **Stepper** (the trade window's of v2.3.1752, *"A STEPPER, NOT A GUESSING GAME"*: one fewer, the number with what you have, one more, each end off at its end; the neutral dismiss face, V17:43 *"not gold merely because interactive"*); the **stat you inspect** (a stat that always opens: its chevron, or the word Details); the **Comparison** (before, after, and the difference signed AND in words, "+5 better", "no change": V17:49 *"Positive/negative deltas use signed text and color; never color alone"*; whole numbers written whole, V17:7); the **Overlay** (a town window's frame: its header and ✕ fixed, its body scrolling under them, as the building windows keep their ✕ in the corner since v2.3.3522; it does not place itself: V17:63's sizes are *"candidate budgets"*).
- **What it does not do:** no screen is moved to the new pieces (the trade window keeps its stepper, Ranks and the bag their tab rows, the buildings their window); no colour, size or word on a screen changes.

Sources: src/game/uiRoles.js, src/game/kitRules.js, src/ui/kit/ (Tab.jsx, Stepper.jsx, InspectStat.jsx, Comparison.jsx, Overlay.jsx, the pieces that ask ui2Role, ui2.css's last block, UiKitPage.jsx), tools/guards/ui4-roles.test.mjs, tools/guards/lib/rolesKnown.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-uikit.mjs (11), docs/shots/ui4-kit-pieces.png, docs/shots/ui4-kit-pieces-sideways.png, docs/design/v17/.

## The kit's roles in V17's look (v2.3.6841, card UI4 PR 2)
Screens: ui4-look-before

**What it is for:** The owner's V17 look (2026-11-27, `docs/design/v17/MASTER-IMPLEMENTATION.md:37-49`) worn by every kit piece through the role it names (PR 1's `data-u2-role`), so a fact, a thing to inspect, a way to go, the chosen one, a commitment and a danger look different everywhere at once. The owner: *"Clearly distinguish passive facts, inspectable facts, navigation/preparation, selected controls and commitments throughout every menu. Do not make information compete with actions"* (V17:9). ui4-kit-roles shows it; ui4-look-before shows the same plate the day before.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **One switch, on by default, remembered** (`?v17look=0` the day before, `?v17look=1` back; `bt_v17look`). The look is ONE class on the kit root (`ui2-v17`, `src/ui/kit/shine.js`) and every rule of it is under that class, so `=0` draws the game as before to the pixel: the PR's pictures of Bro, Combat, the Sword's page and Gear match the base build's except the AP figure and a pulsing hint ring (game state, not look). `?ui2look=0` (the palette before 11-26) never wears V17 on top.
- **V17's colours to the digit** (`UI2_V17` in the tokens): facts recessed #0A1620 with no border; a stat you inspect #0A1620 with the #91A9B9 border; every raised door the flat slate #294963 with its #698397 edge (V17's own examples of navigation include "Power +"); the chosen one the slate with a gold #EAC474 border, a bar under it and its word underlined (never colour alone); adjust and dismiss the neutral slate #203644; commit solid #E4BA68 with #141D22 words; danger #753439 with white words; disabled #20303B with #BCC8CC words, "muted but readable" (a locked tile too: V17's own example is "Locked recipe"). The values V17's words leave open are read from the owner's prototype's last rule for each role, never made up. Every pair holds 4.5 to 1 (`ui2-tokens.test` 15).
- **No third colour.** V17 has no green and no blue: Accept (green) now wears commit gold, Travel (blue) the navigation slate. Flagged for the owner (Q5).
- **Gold leaves stat numbers and chevrons** (V17:49, *"Gold is not a generic primary-navigation color"*): the number ivory, the chevron #BCC8CC (#91A9B9 on the slate reads only 3.85 to 1). A level's number keeps the game's gold (progress, the owner's 11-26 word; V17 does not name it).
- **A point to spend: V17's priority cue, not commit gold** (V17:20): the count's words #F5CF83 bold and the attribute tile's edge #BC9B60, the prototype's `.v17priority`. Flagged (Q7): it is still a warm colour.
- **The smallest word 12 where it fits** (V17:49 metadata 12-13), measured 11 against 12 (`mp-v17look` 3: no scroll grows, no word cut or run past its box, no control leaves the band): Combat and Gear take 12, both ways up. KEEP 11 (`UI2_V17_SMALL11`): Bro (its strip's labels run 2 px past their boxes at 12, so their descenders would be cut, both ways up), the Sword's page (one line, upright), Shared (it holds 12 alone but is the same template as the Sword's page, so it keeps its size), and the stat tree, every layer (its hit base's five reasons scroll at 12 on a small phone on its side, 667 x 375: `mp-ui2combat` 4d). A count badge stays 10.
- **A label alone in its box is centred.** The owner, about 22:20 UTC 2026-11-27, after trying PR 1's preview: *"Looks good just sometimes the labels are left aligned inside text boxes instead of centered I'd prefer centered"*. Under the look a compact tile with no line under it (its picture and word together: the chosen-shelf and tab-like tiles, a page's doors such as "All unlocks") and a stat's number and word (the Sword's DPS, the AP total) sit in the middle of their box. Left as they were, because centring is not plainly meant there and the owner did not name them (listed in the PR with pictures): a fact's row (its word left, its value right: the Sword's Damage and Crit chance, Bro's strip), a tile with a line under it (a list's row: the Feats and Pets doors), a weapon's attribute tiles (picture, name and "0 placed"), a group's title, an empty state's explaining words.
- **What it does not do:** no word, size of the band, layout or rule changes; the item slot keeps its rarity and selection (V17:47); Gear is untouched beyond its doors' colours (card UI4 PR 6 measures it).

Sources: src/game/ui2Tokens.js (UI2_V17, UI2_V17_TYPE, UI2_V17_SMALL11), src/ui/kit/shine.js (v17LookOn), src/ui/kit/ui2.css's last block, src/ui/mobile/sheet/combatPage.css's last rules, tools/guards/ui4-look.test.mjs, tools/guards/ui2-tokens.test.mjs (15-18), tools/qa/mp/mp-v17look.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-uikit.mjs (12), docs/shots/ui4-look-*-pair*.png.

## Bro, the three weapons first (v2.3.6843, card UI4 PR 4)
Screens: ui4-bro, ui4-kit-leveltile, ui4-kit-strip

**What it is for:** The owner's V17 (2026-11-27, `docs/design/v17/MASTER-IMPLEMENTATION.md:55`): *"HUD carries identity, do not repeat portrait and combat level unnecessarily. Bro presents recognizable Sword/Bow/Staff artwork, levels/progress and available points first, then Character stats and Pets, with active weapon damage and health afterward. Distinguish equipped weapon from in-hand weapon."* And the card's purpose in the owner's words: *"Do not compromise readability, available screen space, gameplay visibility or device fairness to accommodate a design. Flag unresolved requirements rather than inventing solutions."*

**Decisions, and why:**
- **One switch, on, remembered** (`?v17bro=0` the six tiles and the strip of 2026-11-26, `sheet/BroLanding.jsx` untouched; `=1` back; `bt_v17bro`). The rules are `src/game/broV17.js`; the page `sheet/BroV17.jsx`; the suite `tools/guards/bro-v17.test.mjs`; the phone test `mp-v17bro`.
- **The weapons first, one tap each.** Each is the kit's new level tile (`Ui2LevelTile`, the go role): the game's picture, `Lv n` (the level keeps its gold), the XP bar, and the points to spend ONLY while there are some, in the priority colour, never commit gold and never a plus (V17:20; the owner, 2026-11-26: *"only show available points prominently when there are points to spend"*). Today a weapon's page was two taps (Bro, Combat, the weapon); now one, and its Back returns to Bro.
- **In hand against worn, in words and a frame** (V17:49: *"color alone must not communicate meaning"*): the held lane is framed (Gear's `is-hand`) and says "In hand"; the others "Worn", or "Not worn" where nothing is in that slot.
- **Then the Shared Attributes door and Pets.** V17 names the first "Character stats"; the game's word stays until the owner says (Q13). Its points follow the same rule as a weapon's.
- **Then, quieter, the held weapon's DPS and Max HP**, flat facts with no button form (V17:38), the game's own numbers (the weapon page's DPS, the health bar's Max HP); no "effective health" (V17: *"Do not fabricate"*).
- **Nothing lost: ONE quiet strip** under them: Equipment, Life Skills, Feats, Journey (the doors that left Bro's first row), then AP, Quests, Clan, Guilds (the same entries as the old strip). It never cuts a word at its edge: where the width does not hold every entry it shows the whole entries it holds and a "more" control (the adjust role, 44 x 44, "1/2") that turns to the next (the kit's new `Ui2Strip`). At 390 x 844 the first page is the four doors, the second the four facts.
- **The Combat hub stays**, reached by its old road (the HUD's weapon bar: Bro, the hub, the held weapon; then Back) and under `=0`.
- **The room.** Upright: the weapons' row takes what the doors' row (44) and the strip (44) leave (95 px at 390 x 844). Sideways ONE row (V17:31); on the narrowest phones on their side (667 x 375, 740 x 360) Pets heads the strip, still in view, because the row cannot hold the three weapons, both doors and a whole strip entry; the two facts lead the strip on every phone on its side. A computer (`?v17wide`'s 840): the weapons, the two doors and the facts in one row no taller than a phone's, the strip whole under it: more entries, never larger tiles or words (V17:31). Bro keeps 11 px for its smallest word (`UI2_V17_SMALL11`).
- **The owner's two tests** (relayed by the orchestrator, the owner about 01:40 UTC 2026-11-28, after trying PR 2's preview of the Combat hub, a weapon's page and Shared: *"Don't like combat menu or primary skill/shared attribute menus. Too confusing for first time player and doesn't use visuals first."*). Bro is the way in, so it is held to both: (a) **what each thing is, and what one tap does**: every tile is the thing it opens (a weapon tile opens that weapon; Shared Attributes, Pets, Equipment... open their pages), and nothing on the page is a step in between; the only facts (DPS, Max HP) have no button form, so nothing looks pressable that is not; (b) **visuals first**: each weapon's own picture leads its tile (44 px upright, the largest the row leaves), its name, level, bar and points under it; Shared, Pets and every strip door wear their game picture before their word; the only lines without a picture are the two numbers and the strip's four counts (AP, Quests, Clan, Guilds), quieter and last.
- **What gave way against V17's own picture:** the Shared door and Pets share their row with the two facts upright (V17 stacks them); the quiet strip pages on a phone; sideways the facts, and on small phones Pets, move into the strip. Every label alone in its box is centred (the owner, 22:20 UTC 2026-11-27: *"I'd prefer centered"*).

## A weapon's page and Shared, the upgrades first (v2.3.6844, card UI4 PR 5)
Screens: ui4-combat-hub, ui4-combat-weapon, ui4-combat-shared

**What it is for:** The owner, 02:10 UTC 2026-11-28, after trying PR 2's preview: *"Don't like combat menu or primary skill/shared attribute menus. Too confusing for first time player and doesn't use visuals first."* The owner's V17 (`docs/design/v17/MASTER-IMPLEMENTATION.md:55`): *"Tap weapon to see actionable upgrades and available points first, then level/progress and actual weapon damage. Use recognizable artwork and readable labels for the six attributes (Range, Power, Speed, Luck, Special, Element). Shared Health, Defense, Mana, Stamina, Dodge, Move speed, Resistance: upgrade choices and available points first, supporting current strength below in portrait or to the right in landscape. ... Upgrade preview shows actual before/after effect and cost, explicitly spend, independent point pools. When no points, still inspect explanation; don't show actionable plus."* And with the design: *"Do not compromise readability, available screen space, gameplay visibility or device fairness to accommodate a design. Flag unresolved requirements rather than inventing solutions."*

**Decisions, and why:**
- **One switch, on, remembered** (`?v17combat=0` the pages of 2026-11-26, untouched; `=1` back; `bt_v17combat`). The rules are `src/game/combatV17.js`; the pages keep their files (`sheet/WeaponPage.jsx`, `SharedPage.jsx`, `CombatHub.jsx`) and draw V17's order through the ONE template (`attrTemplate.jsx` UpgradeGrid); the suite `tools/guards/combat-v17.test.mjs`; the phone test `mp-v17combat`.
- **The upgrades first.** The line "Attributes" with the points to spend at its right (V17's priority cue, never commit gold), then the six (Shared: the seven) as the kit's tile, the game's own picture OVER its name over what is placed. A "+" in the corner only while that pool has a point; at zero no "+", and a tap still opens the explanation. The tile is a way to get something ready (V17's own example of navigation is "Power +"), so it wears the go role; the commitment is Spend.
- **Then the level and its XP, then DPS** (the stat that opens the seven-page tree, unchanged). Upright they share ONE row under the six; Damage, Attack speed, Crit chance and Range sit beside DPS where the row holds them (a computer), else in today's "All stats" door. Sideways one row: the six at the left (16 px pictures over their words), the level and DPS to their right.
- **Shared: the seven with their pictures, then Max HP and Defense** (below upright, to the right sideways), and the GAME's rule for its points where the row holds it: "+3 each level of your best weapon" (the worker: `server/src/prog3.js:26-33`). V17 says three a weapon level; that is not the game's rule while `onelevel` is on, so the menu says the game's and the difference is flagged (Q1). The bare tiles of narrow phones (no pictures under 520 px, which still cut "Max Mana" and "Move speed" at 320 and 360) are gone: every tile has its picture.
- **Spend is the commit**: the preview window (today's, `statWindow.jsx`: the real before and after, the stepper, each pool on its own tab) draws its Spend in V17's commit gold (#E4BA68, #141D22 words) and marks it `commit`, or `disabled` while nothing is added. Only the pages under the switch ask for it; every other window keeps its own.
- **The Combat hub**: V17 draws no hub (its prototype's Combat page says *"Existing Combat functionality lives here ... not yet redesigned"*; V17's Bro is the hub, PR 4). Within the card: Sword, Bow and Staff are Bro's own level tiles (the picture, Lv, the bar, "In hand" framed, "Worn", "Not worn", the points only while some), and the Shared door shows what it opens: the seven upgrades' own pictures under its name, as many as its width holds (all seven on a phone upright; none rather than one or two).
- **The owner's two tests.** (1) *What each thing is and what a tap does, without a paragraph*: every tile is one upgrade, named and pictured; the "+" says a point can go there; the count beside "Attributes" says how many; a tap opens what one point would change and a gold Spend; DPS has its chevron (it opens its breakdown); the level is a plain bar; on the hub each tile is the page it opens (a weapon's picture opens that weapon; the Shared door shows the seven pictures it opens). (2) *Visuals first*: the first thing on each page is a row of the game's pictures (the six or the seven), the words under each; the numbers (level, DPS, Max HP) come after, quieter.
- **What gave way against V17's own picture** (V17's are `docs/shots/v17combat-v17-*.png`): the tiles keep the game's "n placed" line under the name (V17 shows a count only once placed): it is the game's number of what a point there already bought, and a capped stat says "3 / 12". Upright the pictures are as large as the rows leave: 17 px on a 320 x 568 phone, 26 at 375 x 667, 28 at 390 x 844; sideways 16 px. V17 puts the points sideways in the header's spare width; the game keeps them on the line over the six (the header is every page's, not this card's). Shared's rule is not drawn under 360 px wide (no room beside Max HP and Defense without cutting it); the line's count is.
- **Flagged, never settled here:** Q1 (shared points), Q13 (V17's "Weapon upgrades", "Character stats", "Health", "Resistance" against the game's Attributes, Shared Attributes, Max HP, Resist), and NEW: whether the Combat hub should stay beside V17's Bro.

## The one item page (v2.3.6687, card UI2-gear PR 1)
Screens: dash-item-page

**What it is for:** One place that says what an item IS, the same from everywhere (the owner, 2026-11-25, the master specification's part 5: *"Full Details opens the shared item inspector (also used from Codex, equipment, loot, stat breakdowns). Context and permissions govern available actions; read-only inspection for others. Do not duplicate the data model."*). In this first step only Gear opens it; the Codex, Bro's Equipment and the tooltips come to it later (card UI2-gear PR 3), Gear's two panels and the comparison in PR 2.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A page of Gear's own stack.** It is the frame's page `item`, pushed over Gear: the header reads [‹ Gear] *kind* [Menu] [✕], and Back returns to Gear as it was left, the pick still ringed (gearTabBus keeps it). The title is the item's KIND (Sword, Bow, Staff, Shield, Chest, Legs, Cape, Amulet, Item), short enough for the 44 px header beside its three controls; the full name is the page's own, two lines at most.
- **The way in.** PR 1 made the picked card's picture and name the door; since v2.3.6688 (PR 2) the middle card is gone and the door is the quick look's **Details** button ("Gear's two panels", below).
- **Identity:** the picture in the kit's rarity slot (the GAME's grade by its outline, its stars inside), the name, then the grade's word and stars and whose and where ("In your bag", "Worn"; later "Diego's", another player's name). The grades are Normal, Rare, Elite, Godly (`dash/common.js` QUALITY_LABEL); anything else the record holds reads Normal. A "?" piece shows no grade and no numbers.
- **Facts, three across:** the item's own rows (Gear's: a weapon's Damage, DPS on YOUR character by `calcDisplayDps`, Attack speed, Range; a shield's Block; armour's Damage cut), then Hardness, Gems, what it Requires and how many you hold; six at most. "Speed" reads "Attack speed" (the master notes, table 1 line 10). A stack, a material, food: its card's own sentence (what it heals, where it smelts). No item is called better or worse.
- **Actions:** the ONE list (`dash/itemActions.js`), narrowed by context and never widened: none on another player's item, a shop's goods or a Codex entry; Sell only in a shop (never from Gear); the lead (Equip, Eat, Drink, Reveal…) first and full width, the rest beside it. An action you cannot take stays in its place, OFF, with its reason beside it (Q83): Equip on a piece whose requirement is not met reads "Sword level 18, requires 25", the worker's own equip gate's numbers (`getEquipReqLabel`). The faces: gold routine (Equip, Unequip, Eat…), green a claim (Claim, Open), red only Drop, silver Pin and Inspect. Equip and Unequip go back to Gear, which shows the change; Drop hands to the item card's own sheet; Pin stays.
- **Nothing important behind a scroll:** the identity and the action row never scroll; only the facts may, inside their own box. Sideways (the page 66 px inside) the three sit side by side, chosen by the page box's own height (a container query: nothing measures the page).

**What the real size could not hold:** sideways, the facts run in one row that scrolls across when there are more than fit; a third action beside a disabled lead and its reason wraps the reason to two lines.

Sources: src/game/itemInspect.js, src/data/say/itemInspect.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/ItemPage.jsx, itemPageOpen.js, itemPage.css, gearCells.js, GearPanels.jsx (the summary's Details), BottomDashboard.jsx (PANELS.item), tools/guards/item-inspect.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-ui2gear.mjs, docs/shots/ui2-gear-*.png.

## Gear's two panels (v2.3.6688, card UI2-gear PR 2)
Screens: dash-gear-panels, dash-gear-look-bag, dash-gear-look-worn

**What it is for:** managing what you carry and what you wear, and answering "should I wear this" by what happens to YOU, with no tap more than the look itself (the owner, 2026-11-25, the master specification's part 5: *"The new direction removes the permanent middle column, gives slots more space, and distinguishes stored items from worn equipment by layout shape, not color alone"*; *"Keep the source panel in its original location during quick inspection; the opposite panel is replaced with the summary. No shifting the inventory to the other side"*; part 5.1: *"Compare final outcome, not merely raw item modifiers"*).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **Two panels, no middle column.** Inventory LEFT, Equipped RIGHT, each about 189 x 172 px at 390 x 844. The bro's figure and its health, mana and energy bars left with the middle column: those bars are on the world's own screen already (the top bar). The figure goes to Bro's Equipment page (PR 3).
- **Told apart by shape.** The Inventory is a regular, packed grid (three across, 3 px gaps) that scrolls inside its panel. Equipped is a body: three columns with wide gutters, the weapons (Sword, Bow, Staff) down the left, Cape, Chest and Legs on the figure's line in the middle (those cells a quarter wider, a torso, over a dark spine), Shield, Amulet and Gem down the right. With the colours off the two still read apart. Sideways (the page 63 px inside) Equipped is one row of three groups of three, and the bag one row beside it, its filter and coins stacked at its left.
- **The quick look replaces the OPPOSITE panel.** A bag square: the Equipped panel becomes its summary. A worn cell: the Inventory panel becomes its summary. The source never moves (src/game/workspace.js: one state, one transition, `wsPanels` names what each side shows). The picked square wears the kit's SELECTED state: pushed in, its outline kept, a brass notch at its top as the cue that is not colour. The way back: the same square again, or the summary's ✕ (Esc on a computer); the bag's scroll comes back as it was (kept at the top of a frame).
- **The summary is the item page's model cut short** (`itemSummary` of `inspectItem`), so the look and the page say the same facts. A piece you could wear: the comparison in the transformation form (src/game/compare.js): ONE headline (a weapon's DPS, armour's "Damage cut", a shield's Block), "Now" and "If equipped", the change with an arrow and a word as well as its colour, a percentage only from a real baseline, at most two more rows (Damage, Attack time: lower is better), a plain-words line where one number went each way ("More damage per second, slower attacks"); no item is ever called better or worse. The numbers are YOUR character's (src/game/equipProject.js, the Bro pages' readers; `calcDisplayDps` called, never changed). Food, a drink, a material or a fish: its own facts (how many you hold, what it does) and its action. Then **Details** (the one item page) and the one action (Equip, gold).
- **Effective health is not shown.** It is the owner's question (F1); armour leads with "Damage cut", the number the game has.
- **Equip shows at once as today**, and the worker corrects it; the kit's pending mark waits for step 3's `pending.js`.
- **No Sell here**, as on today's Gear: selling is the shop's own window.
- **Close keeps the workspace** (v2.3.6688, the mends; the owner, 2026-11-25: Close resets no workspace): the pick, its look, the bag's scroll and the filter survive Close and open (`workspace.js` 'fold'); only an open menu shuts. Under `?ui2=0` Close still puts today's Gear back (v2.3.4353).
- **A "?" piece** (not revealed yet) says what today's card says: its name, "Rare or better" in the rare blue, the "?" over a dimmed picture, Reveal and Equip; no grade, no stars, no number, no comparison (they would give the reveal away). The kit draws no CSS filter, so the picture is dimmed, not today's silhouette.

- **One socket, thinner label rows, the gold a pill, the bag in whole rows** (v2.3.6784, card UI3-menus; the owner, 2026-11-26: *"Gear. Actually one of your better layouts. Standardize the inventory and equipment slot styling, shrink their headers, and make the gold balance a compact pill. Preserve the side-by-side structure."*; and at 19:35 UTC: *"the inventory slots don't fit neatly on the new menu the third row gets cut off"*). A bag square wears the worn cell's socket (the kit's `u2-slot` and its grade: `BagTile kit`, passed by Gear's two panels only, so the shop, a trade and `?ui2=0` keep today's square), every mark kept (the count, the pin's anchor, the "?", the big fish); the label rows 24 px (were 30), their words 11 px caps, the controls 24 tall with their touch still 44; the gold the kit's stat at pill size (recessed, its coin 16 px, the number gold), not a button. The bag shows WHOLE rows only (`src/game/gearGeometry.js`): the square is sized from the height too, three rows wherever 44 px squares allow, the scroller exactly those rows tall and the scroll snapping to a row. On the test box: three rows of 48 px at 390 x 844 and a computer, three of 47 at 375 x 667, one row of 56 at 844 x 390 and 667 x 375. A square never goes under 44 (the owner's call).

- **The Equipped panel in pale green, its pictures filling their slots** (tag 6698, card UI2-equipgreen; the owner, 2026-11-27, on an iPhone: *"Also on equipped page might the background color a pale green color and make the thumbnail icons fill each slot better like the inventory icons do"*). The Equipped panel's body (never the Bag panel, a look's summary or Bro's Equipment showcase) is a pale sage, `--u2-gp-equip-ground` #C6DCCD, the panel's one colour of its own, defined in `gearPanels.css` (the kit's palest green #B4E8C9 greyed, so it sits calm beside the dark Bag panel rather than a neon mint). Its label row's words take the kit's dark ink (`--ui2-text-on-action`, 11.8 to 1 on the green); a slot keeps its own dark well and border, so an empty slot still reads as a slot. `docs/LANTERN-SLATE-SPEC.md` keeps green for reward and once turned down a "light and airy" palette: the owner's words of 2026-11-27 win for this one panel. A worn item's picture is 94% of its slot, as a bag cell's (`BagTile`), in Gear and in the showcase alike (both draw `WornCell`, which marks the picture `is-fill`); the kit's own `.u2-slot-art` stays 72% (`?uikit=1` unchanged); the empty slot's ghost stays 78%. `?equiplook=0` is the panel as before (the dark well, 72%; `src/game/equipLook.js`). Held by `tools/guards/equip-look.test.mjs` (the token, the ink's contrast, the 94% scoped to the worn cells, the switch) and `mp-equipgreen` (the computed ground, a picture's box against its slot's, both looks). Pictures: `docs/shots/equipgreen-after.png`, `-after-sideways.png`, `-showcase.png`, `-before.png`, `-before-sideways.png`. Bro's Equipment in green too is the owner's to say.

**What the real size could not hold:** (before v2.3.6784) the label rows were 30 px (the filter's control 30 px tall, its touch reaching 44 px); sideways there is no label row (the panels' names are read to a screen reader) and the summary keeps only its headline, Details, the action and ✕ (Details has the rest).

**Tried before:** the three panels with the middle card (v2.3.4151 to v2.3.6687), kept under `?ui2=0`.

Sources: src/game/workspace.js, compare.js, equipProject.js, itemInspect.js (itemSummary), src/data/say/compare.js, gearPanels.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/GearPanels.jsx, gearPanels.css, gearTabBus.js (`ws`), GearFilterMenu.jsx, gearBag.js, src/ui/kit/TwoPanel.jsx, ItemGrid.jsx; tools/guards/compare.test.mjs, workspace.test.mjs, equip-look.test.mjs; src/game/equipLook.js; tools/qa/mp/mp-ui2gear.mjs, mp-equipgreen.mjs.

## Gear, kept, and measured against V17 (v2.3.6845, card UI4 PR 6)
Screens: dash-gear-panels, dash-gear-look-bag, dash-gear-look-worn, dash-gear-look-thing

**What it is for:** the same as Gear's two panels above. The owner, 2026-11-27, with the V17 handoff: *"Some things may be better designed as already in the game like probably the bag menu and character design but maybe not"*; of Gear's side-by-side, 2026-11-26: *"one of your better layouts"*. So Gear is NOT redrawn: V17:57 described what was built. This pass names the roles, mends what V17's small rules and `mp-v17fit` found, and shows the owner V17's landscape idea beside the game's. All of it behind `?v17gear` (on; `=0` the day before, remembered; `src/game/v17Gear.js`, its suite `tools/guards/ui4-gear.test.mjs`).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The roles, named where the pieces are made** (V17:37-49; `src/game/uiRoles.js`): an item's action by its id (Equip, Unequip, Eat... commit; **Drop is the danger**), Show all adjusts the filter, the gold pill is a fact, the item page's doors go. Each is the role its colour already wore, so naming it changes no look. **Details** is the one that changes: V17:40 calls an explicit Details *inspection*, but the kit's Action has no inspect role, so it is `go` (it opens the item page): V17's raised navigation slate instead of the neutral one.
- **The comparison through the kit's Comparison** (V17:49 *"Positive/negative deltas use signed text and color; never color alone"*): each row its number before and after and the difference SIGNED with its word ("+0.4 better"); Gear's rows were an arrow and a number, the word only in a title. The headline keeps the game's own words under it ("25% more damage per second"). The words stay the size Gear's rows shipped (11 px).
- **Full name in Details** (V17:57): the quick look cuts a long name at two lines (V17's "two-line quick-look max"); the item page, where it is read whole, no longer cuts it.
- **Fixed buttons** (V17:57): a disabled action's reason sat UNDER the buttons and pushed them up; it sits above them now. A thing with no action (a material) drew Details across the whole row; Details keeps its half.
- **The place kept on return** (V17:57): already held (`workspace.js`: the pick, its look, the bag's scroll and the filter survive Details and Close). Checked, nothing to mend.
- **The fits `mp-v17fit` found on main:** sideways the Equipped row was 12 px wider than its pane (the three groups' own padding was never counted), so the Gem cell was cut at 667 x 375 and touched the edge at 844: its true width now. On a 320 x 568 phone the body's three rows of 44 cannot fit under the Equipped label row, nor "INVENTORY" beside the filter and the gold: there the two words give way (the panels keep their names for a screen reader) and the controls and cells stay whole. The quick look gives way, in order, the tradeoff line, a fact row, the headline fact, then its picture (the picked cell beside it shows the picture with its selected mark): its name, its buttons and its ✕ never.
- **A computer's 840** (V17:31 *"use extra horizontal space for additional entries, not larger art, text or gameplay view"*): the bag takes as many WHOLE columns as its panel holds, at the square the height already gives (the same rule as its whole rows): 8 across at 1440 x 900, 24 squares in view against 9, never a larger square. Every phone keeps its 3 across.
- **The owner's "labels ... centered"** (2026-11-27 about 22:20 UTC, *"I'd prefer centered"*): the filter's word, alone in its box, is centred. LEFT AS IS, and listed: the panels' names in their label rows ("INVENTORY" beside its controls; "EQUIPPED" alone over its body) are a pane's heading, not a word in a box; a summary's fact rows (the word left, the number right) are not plainly meant.

- **The owner's three words after trying the look on their phone** (2026-11-28 about 02:10 UTC, through the orchestrator; they overrule "kept" for exactly these):
  - *"Remove the coins from bag and slide filter over."* The gold pill leaves the bag's label row and the filter takes its place at the row's end. The purse stays in view on the top bar (`src/game/topGold.js`); a phone that hid the top bar's gold (`?topgold=0`) keeps the pill here, so no screen is left without the gold. On a 320 phone the word "INVENTORY" now has room again.
  - *"Equipped has whiteish background not green."* Not the V17 look's doing: the panel was the same with `?v17look=0` and on main (#C6DCCD under a white sheen draws about #C3D7C9 on the phone, a greyed sage that reads as white). Under `?v17gear` it is a clear pale green (#A6DCB4, its own token; the dark ink on it far over 4.5 to 1).
  - *"Inventory slots are awkwardly spaced on the sides."* The bag's whole columns stood as one block in the middle of their panel, the spare width at its two edges. Now the columns spread evenly, with the same room at both edges and between them. The squares keep their size and count. Wider squares would lose a row (at 390 x 844, 6 in view instead of 9), and a fourth column does not fit at 44 px, so neither was built.

**What the real size could not hold:** at 667 x 375 the sideways quick look of a WORN piece stands where the bag was, 122 px wide beside the nine cells: its name, Details, Unequip and ✕ need about 262 (at 740 x 360 it now holds its name and buttons whole). Mending it would drop a control or shrink the cells or words, so it is LISTED (pictures in the PR); V17's own answer, the bag strip with an Equipped switch, would give it the whole row: the owner's choice.

**Flagged to the owner (not built):** sideways, the game's two panels in one row against V17's bag strip with an Equipped switch (pictures of both at 844 x 390; the switch costs a tap, the strip shows more of the bag); the Jewel against the Gem cell (Q2: kept); Details as navigation rather than inspection; on a computer the Equipped panel keeps half the block, with room to spare beside its body.

Sources: src/game/v17Gear.js, gearGeometry.js, compare.js (a row's `dec` and `unit`), src/ui/mobile/dash/GearPanels.jsx, gearPanels.css, ItemPage.jsx, itemPage.css, src/ui/kit/TwoPanel.jsx (`name`), tools/guards/ui4-gear.test.mjs, tools/qa/mp/mp-v17fit.mjs.

## Bro's Equipment, a showcase; the roads into the one item page (v2.3.6689, card UI2-gear PR 3)
Screens: ui2-bro-equipment

**What it is for:** looking at what you wear, under Bro, without the bag; managing it stays Gear's (the card's reading 8: *"Bro's Equipment door opens a SHOWCASE (the figure and the nine worn cells in the same body shape, no bag; each cell opens the one item page; 'Manage Gear' for yourself, nothing to press for another player)"*). And one item page from every place an item is looked at (the owner, 2026-11-25, the master specification's part 8: *"The same entity should use the same canonical inspection screen across the Codex and contextual entry points."*).

**Decisions, and why:**
- **A page of Bro's own** (`broEquip`): the header reads [‹ Bro] Equipment [Menu] [✕]. Upright: your figure (the portrait Gear's old middle column drew, southwest, still) with **Manage Gear** under it, and beside it the nine worn cells in Gear's body shape. Sideways (a short page): the figure, the cells in one row of three groups, and the button, in one line.
- **The same body, by one table.** Gear's Equipped panel and the showcase lay their cells out by `GEAR_BODY` (src/game/gearShowcase.js), and draw each cell with Gear's own `WornCell`, so the two shapes and the cells' pictures cannot drift apart.
- **A cell opens the one item page**, by the same source Gear's worn cell hands over (`{ R, cell }`), so the same item reads the same facts from Gear and from Bro. Back returns to the showcase. An empty cell, or one the page has nothing for (the Gem cell: the gems are on the weapon's own page), presses nothing.
- **Manage Gear** is Gear's own road (today's `openGear`): the two panels.
- **Another player's showcase** (nothing opens one yet: step 9): no Manage Gear, their name instead, no figure (the figure is drawn from YOUR wardrobe, so a figure under their name would be a lie), their cells read-only on the item page ("Diego's", no actions).
- **The player card's equipment row** opens the one item page too: your slot by Gear's own cell (the weapon: the hand you hold), with its actions; another player's slot read-only, with what their card knows of it (its picture and name; no number is invented), the kind "Weapon" where their card does not say which. The card closes so the page is seen, as its other doors do.

**What the real size could not hold:** the figure is cropped to its middle (about 44% of its square) beside the body; at 375 x 667 it is smaller than at 390 x 844. Sideways the figure is the page's height, about 80 px.

**Not moved here:** the old item sheet (`ItemTooltip.jsx`) is not mounted anywhere since the bag surface left (`InventorySurface`, GameApp's note), so it has no road to move; the Codex's drop card is the Codex card's (UI2-codex); a shop's goods wait for the shop's own step (8).

Sources: src/game/gearShowcase.js, src/data/say/gearShowcase.js, src/ui/mobile/sheet/GearShowcase.jsx, BroCard.jsx (door 'equip'), src/game/broHub.js (broEquip), src/game/dashDests.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/GearPanels.jsx (EquippedBody, WornCell's `tap`), itemPageOpen.js (the showcase, profile and peer sources), src/ui/panels/PlayerProfilePanel.jsx, InspectPlayerPanel.jsx; tools/guards/gear-showcase.test.mjs; tools/qa/mp/mp-ui2gear.mjs.

## Pets (v2.3.6696, card UI2-pets)
Screens: dash-pets-companion, dash-pets-owned, dash-pets-one, dash-pets-release, dash-pets-all

**The owner's words** (the master specification, 2026-11-25, part 4.6): *"Active companion dominates the overview, with actual appearance, identity and relevant supported stats. Secondary destinations: Owned Pets and All Pets. All Pets should reuse the Codex pet collection when available, not duplicate encyclopedia data. No active companion produces an informative empty state. Owner management and other-player inspection use the same underlying components with permissions. Pet leveling/XP, sample species and stats shown in prior wireframes were examples, not confirmed mechanics."* And their standing rule: *"Yeah my game content over the mockup stuff"*.

**What it is for:** seeing the pet out with you and what it really does for you, and reaching the pets you own and every kind there is to catch. Bro's Pets door (and Beastmaster Bro's tap, the farm's Pet House and its key) opens it.

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The landing is the companion** (`pets`, titled Pets): the kit's showcase, the pet out with you large on the left (its portrait, the pet sheet the loading screen fetched: no new art), and a tap on it opens its page. Over its doors its name, then its kind and stage when it has a name of its own, Golden and Big; its XP bar under its name. Two doors, **Owned** ("2 of 30") and **All** ("2 of 18 caught"), each saying what is behind it. Under them, flat: the level it works at (held at your Trapping level: "Lv 30 (35)", the held sentence under it), its XP, its ward ("Softens chills 34%"). One action, **Put away**.
- **No stat is added to a pet.** The game has no pet rarity, attack, health or ability, and the wireframes' were examples (the owner's own words): so the page shows the GAME's, its kind and stage, Golden and Big, its level, XP, ward and land. The specification's Q73 "species and rarity" is read as the kind and stage and the two badges.
- **The empty states keep the doors**: no pets, today's words on how to catch one; pets but none out, "No pet out with you" with "Open Owned and take one out with you"; the record not loaded, today's "not ready" words; the record not here yet, the kit's loading rows (never "No pets yet" as the truth).
- **Owned** (`petsOwned`): the kit's collection with no kinds row (the kit draws none when there are none), the Pet House's More room at its top with its price (asked once, the kit's confirmation: it spends gold; at 120 "as many as it can"), then a card a pet, "With you" on the one out. A card opens the pet's page.
- **A pet's page** (`petsOne`, titled its name): the same showcase without doors, and the row of actions: Take out or Put away and Rename go at once (routine), Release asks once (for good, as today asked twice), Sell only where the store sells pets, off on the pet out and before its hold ends with the trade rule's own words beside it. Each press shows its pending mark until the worker's record moves (every answer of the worker's, yes or no, is a pets_state), and a second press never goes out while the first is out.
- **Release asks first** (screen `dash-pets-release`; the mend of sitting 72, tag 6696): Release gives the row of actions over to the kit's own question in the actions' zone, "Release <name> for good?", Release in red (Enter) and Keep (Esc), as today's page (the kit's Confirm takes its no button's label, `no`); only the second Release sends, through the one pending state as every press. `pet-pages.test` reads the drawing's tree (W1, W2; E1 to E8 planted at the page, each red), so a send with no ask, or a Take out past `press()`, fails there and not only in petPages.js.
- **All** (`petsAll`): the journal, the pets' own collection, in the kit's collection: its two stages are its kinds (Levels 1-20, Levels 21-40), nine cards each, a caught one in colour with its catches and biggest (golden on its picture), an uncaught one its shape with "Not tried" or "Not caught". The Codex's Pets door opens this page, and a card here opens its monster's page in the Codex: neither copies the other.
- **Another player's** (built, tested by the suite, opened by nothing until the player card's step): only the pet out with them reaches the phone, with no name, so their page is that pet's kind, read-only, "Only the pet out with them is shared": no Owned, no actions.

**What the real size could not hold:** sideways (about 63 px of page) the XP bar and the held sentence are left out (the XP fact and the level's "(35)" still say it); a journal card's biggest may be cut short on an upright phone (its whole line is in the card's spoken label).

**Tried before:** today's one scrolling page (v2.3.3120 to v2.3.6695), kept under `?ui2=0`.

**Flagged for the owner:** pets on the farm (the specification's planned Homestead mechanic) and comparing two pets wait for gameplay; another player's whole list of pets never leaves the worker.

Sources: src/game/petPages.js, src/data/say/pets.js, src/ui/mobile/dash/PetsPage.jsx, petsPageOpen.js, petsUi2.css, PetsPanel.jsx (the choice), src/ui/kit/Collection.jsx and Showcase.jsx (a collection with no kinds; a showcase's head; a fact's marks), src/game/codexView.js (the Codex's Pets door).

## Ranks under the new dashboard (v2.3.6692, card UI2-ranks, the build's step 6)
Screens: ui2-ranks-landing, ui2-ranks-group, ui2-ranks-board, ui2-ranks-spin, ui2-ranks-points

**What it is for:** find the board you care about the way you find your own page (Combat, Life Skills, Feats), read who leads and where you stand on one number, and tap a player to see who they are, then come back to the same place in the list. The owner's master specification, 2026-11-25, part 7: *"Mirror character inspection categories for competitive comparison: Combat, Life Skills, Feats, Equipment where valid metrics exist. Actual leaderboard categories must come from implemented rankings, not hypothetical metrics."*; *"Specific leaderboard is a simple aligned standings list: Rank | Player (portrait/name) | metric for this leaderboard. No unrelated combat level when ranking a different skill. Clearly label metric units/category."*; *"Conditional pinned personal standing only when the player's actual row is outside the visible list; no duplicate row when visible."*; *"Tapping a player opens universal character inspection. Back returns to the same ranking category and scroll position."*; *"Consistent layout across leaderboards; subdued rows, consistent spacing, avoid competing gradients and outlines. Prestige accents should not obscure legibility."* Part 1.2.1: *"Explicitly identify another player's character or possessions when inspecting them, including deep screens."* And after playing the new dashboard, 2026-11-26 (docs/design/UI_FEEDBACK_2026-11-26.md part 4): *"**Leaderboards.** Generally good information density. Reduce the metallic gradients across the first three rows while retaining the gold, silver and bronze medals. Highlight the player's own row regardless of rank."*

**Decisions, and why:**
- **The landing is three equal doors** under the one header (‹ Menu, Leaderboards, Close): Combat, Life Skills, Feats, each with the picture of Bro's door of that name, in Bro's order. No Equipment door: the worker keeps no gear board (part 7: "where valid metrics exist"). A group the worker keeps nothing of is left out.
- **A group is its boards as doors**, each the board's own picture and word from today's list: Combat holds Overall, Sword, Bow, Staff (today's Melee, Bow, Magic boards, in the game's one vocabulary), Combat level, and one door, **Points placed**, to the eight stat boards; Life Skills the ten; Feats Kills, AP, Daily spin, Coin flip, Biggest fish, as the worker keeps them. A board no group names goes under Feats, last: none is dropped (src/game/ranksPage.js ranksGroups). Upright five across (ten fit in two rows), sideways one row.
- **A board is a standings list** under a header with the board's name: the heads Rank | Player | the value's unit (Level, Power, Points, Kills, AP, Gold, Weight). A row: the place (the game's gold, silver and bronze medal for 1 to 3, the number below), the face and the name, the one value. **No Lv chip** except on Combat level, where the level is the value: the owner's older wish (2026-11-09, "include the character profile pic and level next to each record") is reversed by part 7 for every board that is not about level (the picture `docs/shots/ui2-ranks-board.png` shows it). The spin's and the flip's sides and the fish's kinds stay the board's own choice, on its top line, kept on Back.
- **Rows 1 to 3 are the same surface as every other row** (the owner, 2026-11-26): the place is told by the medal ONLY; no bar, gradient, tint, metal edge, glow or laurel. Every row is at least 44 px and scrolls inside its zone.
- **The weights** (card UI3, the kit): a row opens a player, so it is a door (the kit's raised face, its lighter bevel); the value inside it is a flat fact; no stat form inside a row. A deleted character's row is flat and quiet ("Deleted character"), not a door.
- **Your own row is marked wherever it stands** (first, tenth or three hundredth): the kit's brass edge on its surface and the word "You" beside the name (never colour alone). While the real row is out of view it is ALSO pinned at the foot wearing the same mark; never a second copy while the real one shows (ranksPage.js youPinned: from the scroll read at the frame's top, the rows' pitch and the list's size from its ResizeObserver, never measured in a tap). Off the board: one quiet line, "Not ranked on this board". Loading: the kit's placeholder, never "nobody is on it" before the answer.
